# Skull Paper - Maintenance Guide & Build Plan

This file is the source of truth for keeping the Skull Paper (`/staking/skullpaper.php`)
accurate. It maps each platform feature to its doc page and the code that defines it,
records verified constants, and tracks what still needs to be written.

**When you change a feature in the code, update the mapped `.md` page in the same change.**

---

## Feature → Doc Page → Code Map

| Doc page (`skullpaper/`)            | Feature                | Primary code |
|-------------------------------------|------------------------|--------------|
| overview.md                         | Mission / artists      | Founding & partner artist lists auto-generated from the projects DB via `{{projects:founding:names}}` / `{{projects:partner:names}}` (names only; no X links/logos in the projects table yet). Narrative prose still manual. |
| staking.md                          | Points, store, craft   | db.php (updateBalances, craft/shatter), skulliance.php |
 CLAIMED ITEMS CLUSTER AT THE BOTTOM. getItemsData() orders by featured DESC, project, name -- right for a catalogue, wrong for a shopper, because it scatters the rows you can do nothing about through the ones you can; a claimed card is dimmed with its buy buttons disabled, so each one mid-list is a gap in what you are reading. store.php partitions after the query, not in the SQL, because 'owned' is resolved per viewer and ORDER BY cannot see it. A PARTITION, NOT A SORT -- and the reason is the server, not stability in principle: PHP's sorts are stable from 8.0 and a usort behaves identically there (measured), but ~20 PHP builds are available on this host and nothing pins which serves the page, and on 7.x an unstable sort scatters the catalogue order inside each half. store-order-harness.php evals the real block and asserts both the output and the shape. | staking-membership.md               | Member/Elite/Inner     | skulliance.php:145-212 (role IDs) |
| staking-daily-rewards.md            | Daily streak rewards   | db.php:806-830 (getDailyConsumable, getRewardTiers) |
| staking-points.md                   | All points               | db.php getProjects (founding ids 1-6 / partner ids>7,!=15) - point tables auto-generated via `{{projects:founding}}` / `{{projects:partner}}` tokens in skullpaper.php; no manual edits needed |
| staking-crafting.md *(new)*         | Craft/Shatter/Burn     | db.php:3947-3990 |
| missions.md (Daily reward)          | Daily reward claim     | getRandomReward()/dailyRewardAnnounce() in db.php, ajax/daily-reward.php, ajax/daily-reward-announce.php, dailyReward() in skulliance.js, the strip in missions-daily.php, daily-reward-harness.php. REPORTED: "a bit slow and the button doesn't say it is processing". TWO CAUSES. (1) discordmsg() is a synchronous curl POST with CURLOPT_TIMEOUT 8, and it sat inside getRandomReward() between the press and the reply that redraws the strip -- every claim waited on Discord, and a bad minute there looked like a hang. IT CANNOT BE DEFERRED PAST THE RESPONSE ON THIS SERVER: cryptcrawlFlushPendingSideEffects() in db.php records three production failures (fastcgi_finish_request, then X-Accel-Buffering plus flush), most likely a proxy buffering the whole origin response. The only thing that works is a SEPARATE request the browser makes after it has painted, so the claim now leaves a MARKER in $_SESSION['dr_announce'] and ajax/daily-reward-announce.php posts it. That endpoint takes NO parameters -- dailyRewardAnnounce() rebuilds the text from the database and the session, so a client can only ask for its own claim to be announced, never choose what gets posted. It clears the marker BEFORE the curl (the 8 second window is exactly when a double-fire arrives) and re-writes SessionCookie afterwards, because skulliance.php serialised the session into that cookie several lines earlier and on an ITP/PWA session the cookie IS the session -- the identical trap ajax/dhc-seen-drop.php documents. Markers older than 900s are dropped, or a tab closed mid-claim announces yesterday as though it just happened. The 7th-day dhcf_award() STAYS inside the claim: it is a database write that belongs in the transaction that earns it, and the reply carries the drop. (2) The button did nothing visible until the reply arrived and then vanished, and nothing stopped a second press -- two claims racing both pass getDailyRewardEligibility() before either increments the streak. It now pins its own width (it is an <input>, so it cannot carry the ::after spinner .ms-busy uses on the deploy buttons), says Claiming..., refuses a re-entry, and restores itself on timeout, transport error and non-200 -- that last branch used to be empty, so a 500 left it reading Claiming... forever. HARNESS NOTE: the structural checks read db.php through token_get_all with comments stripped and strings blanked. The first version searched raw source and found the word discordmsg() in the COMMENT explaining why it had been removed, reporting the fix as un-applied; blanking strings then erased $_SESSION['dr_announce'] itself, so the marker checks use a comments-only copy. It also asserts the extracted function body contains `return $project;` and is under 4KB, because brace matching over a 14,000-line file is only as good as the stripping. |
| (payout crons)                      | rewards.php    | rewards.php is now a JOB TABLE plus a runner, not fifteen independent if-blocks. `weekly=true` runs swaps, bosses, gauntlets, cryptcrawl, skullracer, obscura; `monthly=true` runs missions, streaks, raids, factions, monstrocity, guardians, cryptconquest, dhcfighters, dhcarena. Every single-board flag still works and dispatches through the SAME table, so a combined run and a single run cannot diverge. THE HAZARD CONSOLIDATION CREATES: none of these payouts is idempotent -- several tables carry no rewarded flag -- so a combined run that dies halfway must NOT be re-run. Each job is therefore isolated (a throw is reported and stepped over, not fatal to the rest), the runner prints a per-board manifest, and on any failure it prints the exact single-board commands to retry only what failed. `skip=a,b` resumes without double-paying. ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING in two jobs: skullracer pays 'race' then 'lap' then resets (the reset closes the week by flipping reward=1, so it must follow both), and obscura pays then resets. guardians resets itself inside its own function and must NOT get a second reset. run_reward_jobs() unwinds output buffers back to the depth it found, not to zero -- `while (ob_get_level() > 0)` destroys the caller's buffers too, which swallowed the failure manifest. DHC FIGHTERS now has a payout at all: checkDHCFightersLeaderboard($conn, $monthly, $rewards), 100,000 CARBON, windowed on NOW() - INTERVAL 1 MONTH at BOTH ends so it settles the month that closed and is safe to run late but not twice. rewards-harness.php and dhc-rewards-harness.php cover both with no database. |
| missions.md                         | Idle missions, bulk launchers | missions.php (REBUILT), missions-lib.php, missions-field.php, missions-ladder.php, missions-news.php, ajax/mission-data.php, ajax/mission-launch.php, missions-harness.php, db.php (getMissions, completeMission). THE REBUILD: missions.php owns its markup and three partials that BOTH the first paint and the ajax refresh include, so a repainted list cannot drift from a landed one. missions-lib.php returns data where db.php's six mission renderers echoed markup; none of them is touched, so the page reverts in a line. FOUR THINGS THE OLD PAGE DID, worth not reintroducing: (1) it only called getMissions() `if(isset($_SESSION['userData']['project_id']))` and nothing set a default, so a first-time staker saw an empty box under the words "Available Missions" -- missions.php now picks a project server-side and there is no branch that shows nothing; (2) choosing a mission was a full page POST (each card was its own <form action='missions.php#inventory'>), which re-ran verify.php and needed a 9s fake progress bar to cover it -- it is a drawer now and the loader is gone; (3) Maximise and Balance were two more POSTs for arithmetic the browser has every input for -- both are client-side against LO.threshold, which mission_loadout() computes; (4) the load-out lived in $_SESSION across four mutating ajax endpoints and told the staker to hard-refresh when it desynced. SECURITY: ajax/process-mission-nft.php writes whatever nft_id the CLIENT names into the session and startMission() inserted it unchecked (same for items). mission_launch() intersects the request against the eligible set and re-derives the level lock and affordability. Those old endpoints still exist and are still reachable -- they are simply no longer used by this page. NEVER-RUN NOTICE: mission_frontier() lists unlocked rungs with no missions row at all, and the band renders ABOVE the Daily deployment bar because pressing Start All Free is exactly what takes the crew a new rung needs. THE GATE IS HOLDING, NOT CLEARING. It used to skip any project with nothing cleared -- reasoning that level 1 is the start line rather than news, and that a new account would otherwise see forty of them. The fear was right and the rule was too wide: a staker only HOLDS a handful of projects, and for those the free intro is exactly what they want pointing out, since nothing else on the page says they qualify. mission_frontier() now takes a second count -- NFTs held at all, deployed or not -- and skips a project only when nothing is cleared AND nothing is held. Held is deliberately not the idle count beside it: a staker whose whole roster is out on missions still qualifies. The two queries differ only by the NOT IN, so missions-harness.php's stub branches on that; sharing one fixture would test the new rule against the old numbers. Also note $cleared[$pid] is now a legitimate miss, so it is read with isset() -- display_errors is ON here, and an undefined-key notice prints into ajax/mission-data.php's JSON and breaks the parse. The harness asserts no UNSUPPRESSED notice is raised, which means its error handler has to honour the @ operator or mission_art_url()'s deliberate @filemtime() fails it. IDS ARE A CONTRACT: skulliance.js's completeMissions() reveals outcomes in place via mission-row-, mission-result-, mission-reward-, currency-, consumable-; retreat() uses retreat-button-. missions-field.php keeps all of them. The page also REASSIGNS window.loadCurrentMissions after skulliance.js loads, because the bulk launchers and retreat() both call it -- that is what keeps them working without editing a file nine other pages share. CSS trap hit here: `.ms-mcard-stats span` also matched the nested currency span and made it a flex block, breaking "3,600 SKULL" onto two lines -- use `> span`. missions-daily.php replaces renderDailyRewardsSection()'s seven full-width rows (that renderer also RESET A LAPSED STREAK as a side effect -- mission_daily() keeps that write, in the same order); it keeps the five ids dailyReward() writes into (#reward, #claimed, #progress_bar, #remaining, #claimRewardButton), three of which it sets to display:flex, and that path is what reveals the day-7 DHC trait via DHC_SHOW_DROP. PERFORMANCE, all three found on a real account with 179 missions in flight: (a) the page's <style> was at the BOTTOM, so the browser painted every card under flexbox.css alone -- .nft at 25%, art at natural size -- a full FOUC; it is now the first thing after the includes; (b) mission_active() called getMissionConsumables() per row, i.e. 180 queries before first byte -- one grouped query now; (c) 179 cards is ~270KB of markup and 179 images, so mission_active($conn, MS_FIELD_CAP=24) trims IN-FLIGHT ones only (everything ready is always kept) and mission_active_total() feeds a Show-all button that refetches with all=1. mission_levels() memoises getMissionLevels() for the request -- first paint wanted it five times -- and is RESETTABLE, with mission_launch() clearing it; a plain static passed every launch test and silently broke four frontier ones, which is how the staleness hazard was caught. NEW vs NEVER RUN: only cleared+1 is 'New'. A lower unlocked rung with no missions row is 'Never run' -- that happens when a mission is inserted into a ladder below where the player already is. Only the short 'New' goes on the art (84px wide, already carrying the level chip); the long label goes on the action line. ADMIN CAN INSPECT A LOCKED RUNG, NOT LAUNCH IT. mission_is_admin() (user 1) makes locked cards clickable, keeps their real title and art instead of the '??????' + padlock, and lets ajax/mission-data.php?what=loadout return a locked quest -- needed for checking how a ladder is configured. mission_launch() still refuses a locked quest for EVERYONE including the admin, and the drawer disables Launch with 'Locked - inspection only'. This is the split the old page did not make: getMissions() rendered the submit form for locked missions when the discord id matched, the locked card still carried an onclick that pressed it, and startMission() never re-checked -- so one stray click launched it, and a success there jumps getMissionLevels() past every rung underneath. That is the likely origin of the 'unlocked but never run' rows on this account; missions-probe.php tells that case apart from a rung added to the ladder later. The harness asserts the admin refusal and was verified to FAIL when a mission_is_admin() bypass is put back into mission_launch(). CLOSING THE DRAWER STOPS ITS MEDIA. msCloseDrawer() only set drawer.hidden, so an animated mission kept playing -- with sound, on a loop -- for as long as the page stayed open, because a hidden element is still a playing element. It now pauses every video/audio in the body BEFORE emptying it: a detached media element can keep its audio running until it is collected, so removing the markup on its own is not a fix either. Every close path goes through this one function (the x, the backdrop, Escape, and a successful launch). DRAWER CLOSE IS A ROW, NOT AN OVERLAY. It was absolutely positioned top-right of .ms-drawer-box, which is the same corner iOS paints its UNMUTE BADGE in on a muted autoplaying inline video -- so on a phone, reaching for the sound closed the mission. That badge is drawn by the OS over the video and cannot be out-stacked, only avoided, hence .ms-d-bar: a 44px-tall row above the art. Do not move the close control back over the media. QUEST MEDIA AND MARKUP: mp4 quests play in the drawer with `controls autoplay muted loop playsinline preload=metadata` -- controls is what gives the unmute button, and muted is what lets it autoplay at all (no browser autoplays with sound), so the pair is deliberate; .ms-d-art grows to 320px for video because the control bar eats the frame. Quest descriptions carry real markup (links, <br>) and are rendered through mission_rich_text(), an ALLOW-LIST not an un-escape: everything is escaped, then bare br/b/strong/i/em/u are restored (a tag carrying ANY attribute does not match and stays escaped, safe by construction) and anchors are restored only with an href matching ^(https?://|mailto:|/) -- javascript: and data: are dropped keeping their text, and closers are balanced against surviving opens so no stray </a> is left. Two regex traps, both hit: the tag body cannot be [^&]* because the escaped attribute IS ampersands (use a tempered dot), and the href must be captured lazily to its matching closing entity or a query string full of &amp; truncates the URL. The harness covers both plus script/img/onclick refusals. ITEM-ONLY LAUNCHES NEED AN IDLE ROSTER. startMaxMaxiMissions() launches with 'nfts' => array() by design, but renderMaxMaxiMissionsButton() gates on maxMaxiAvailableNfts() > 0 -- the rule is that holding NFTs BACK is what buys the right to spend an item, and once a project's roster is fully deployed you are locked out of it. mission_launch()'s first cut allowed a boost-only load-out with an empty eligible set, which would have let anyone farm missions off item stock forever; it now refuses when $eligible is empty, and the harness asserts both directions. ITEMS vs CREW (drawer): an NFT on a mission is locked for its duration, so crew+boost over 100 is waste. The old processConsumable() enforced it bluntly -- picking item 1 ran clearSuccessRate() (all NFTs + items 1-4) and over-100 raised an alert. The drawer's first cut lost that: it capped the DISPLAYED number and sent the crew anyway. TWO BUGS IN THE FIRST CUT OF THAT, both reported from real use: every item ran the shed, including Fast Forward and Double Rewards which carry boost 0 (so picking Fast Forward deselected NFTs for nothing); and it shed down to `target`, which is the whale-BALANCING share and sits under 100 when a lot of rate is deployed, so a 75% item on a 24% roster drove the budget to zero and cleared a crew that fitted fine at 99. Items now shed against boostBudget() = 100 - boost, never against target, and only when boost > 0. shedByItem records what an item took so removing it returns exactly that rather than a fresh greedy pick; toggling an NFT by hand deletes it from that ledger so a deliberate choice is never undone, and the tool buttons void the ledger entirely. Now toggling an item calls shedToFit() (drop lowest-rated picks until the rest fits target-boost; lowest first frees the most BODIES for the same rate given up) or fillToFit() on removal; a >=100 item also calls clearOtherBoosts(), which spares ids 5/6 since Fast Forward and Double Rewards are not boosts. `target` holds 100 for Maximise / LO.threshold for Balance / 0 for Clear. Tool buttons still do a clean re-pick via applyThreshold. LADDER STATES: unlocked = always openable (you came to read it and see the art; the drawer disables Launch with the reason instead), locked = '??????' title + icons/padlock.png, as before -- showing real titles spoiled every reveal. Art is object-fit:contain everywhere (cover was cropping commissioned pieces); the drawer is 260px tall and contained. BULK LAUNCHER BUSY STATE: do NOT swap the label for "Working...". It shrank the pressed button 155px -> 120px and slid the next one 35px left -- on a phone the row is wrapped, so a button moves under the finger that just pressed it and reads as a misfire. .ms-busy keeps the label in the layout and paints it transparent, with the platform's own btn-spin over it, so the box cannot change size. Measured both ways: old moved 2 of 3 buttons, new moves none. SECTION NAV: the old quick-menu (5 icons, <=700px only, swapped panels by inline style.display) existed because a staker with a hundred missions in flight could not reach anything. It is a sticky .ms-nav now -- every width, nothing hidden, counts on the tabs, IntersectionObserver for the active state (not a scroll listener: this page can be 200 cards tall). The platform navbar is position:relative on desktop so top:0 is free; under 700px the burger is fixed at top RIGHT, hence padding-right:56px. Sections carry scroll-margin-top so a jump does not land under the bar. missions-harness.php covers the writer and the notice with no database. EMPTY-ROSTER NOTICE SPANS THE CREW GRID: the "every NFT you own for this project is out on a mission" paragraph is rendered INTO .ms-d-crew, a repeat(auto-fill, minmax(62px,1fr)) grid, so it became a 62px column of one-and-two-word lines. `.ms-d-crew .ms-d-desc { grid-column: 1 / -1 }`. Button label is Maximize, not Maximise. THE HEADLINE AND THE FIGURES REPAINT: "4 ready to collect" survived collecting all four, and the In-the-field figure read 200 while the nav badge beside it already said 196. Only navCount() and the Ready chip were ever updated; the <h2> and the other three figures were first-paint PHP that nothing touched again. The data was ALREADY THERE -- both ajax/mission-data.php (what=field) and ajax/mission-launch.php return `overview` and every callback was discarding it. paintOverview(ready, ov) now rewrites #ms-title with the same three-way rule the PHP uses (ready / in the field / send your NFTs out), and the figures through data-fig hooks (active, ready, completed, levels); the Ready chip is CREATED as well as removed, since it only renders when something is ready and a countdown finishing under a refresh is exactly that case. With no overview it falls back to reading the active figure off the page. missions-head-harness.js brace-extracts the real functions and drives them against a DOM stub. Two harness traps hit here: a bare strpos for data-fig passes on paintOverview's OWN querySelector, so the markup check is anchored to `<div class="ms-fig..." data-fig=`; and no_comments() lives in realms-harness.php, not this one. STICKY NAV GEOMETRY: the bar pins at top:env(safe-area-inset-top) and paints the strip above it with an ABSOLUTELY POSITIONED ::before that only shows while .stuck. Two passes to get here. Pass one pinned at top:0 and paid the inset as padding -- that covered the strip (body's padding-top scrolls away, leaving it transparent for cards to ride up through into the clock) but cost a PERMANENT inset-tall gap above the bar at rest, ~59px of nothing between the figures and the tabs on an iPhone. Pass two pays it only when stuck. The ::before must stay position:absolute: it then has no layout box, so the bar is the same height stuck or not and the page cannot jump as it sticks (measured 39 in both states, gap above the nav 0px at rest). The .stuck class is toggled in the scroll-spy's sync(), comparing rect.top against parseFloat(getComputedStyle(nav).top) so the inset is never duplicated in JS. A/B'd at a stand-in 59px inset: with the band off a mission card sits in the 0-59 strip, with it on the nav owns it. Realms solves the same problem with a negative top MARGIN instead, which works there only because its nav is the first thing on the page. body carries padding-top:env(inset) and that padding SCROLLS AWAY, so top:env(inset) leaves a transparent band above the bar that cards ride up through into the clock and the battery. A/B'd in Chrome against a stand-in 59px inset: old rule painted a mission card 20px down the screen, new rule paints the bar. scroll-margin-top still counts the inset -- the bar is the inset TALLER rather than the inset lower, same total. THE CONTAINING BLOCK IS WHY THIS PAGE NEVER SHOWED REALMS' OTHER BUG: .ms-nav is inside .main, a flex child with auto height (measured 3753px against a 1323px .container), so the pin holds the whole way. Moving it out of .main reintroduces the one-viewport unpin, and missions-harness.php asserts it stays inside. SWIPE: missions.php loads swipe-nav.js and calls SkullSwipe.init('ms-nav', {blocked: drawer-is-open}); the gesture clicks the nav link, so the hash jump and the scroll-spy's pin both happen exactly as they do for a tap and nothing here knows where the sections are. See the realms row for the refusal list and swipe-nav-harness.js. Three bulk launchers, all rendered from getMissions() and therefore only when at least one mission is already in flight: startAllFreeEligibleMissions(), startAutoMissions(), and startMaxMaxiMissions() (+ renderMaxMaxiMissionsButton(), maxMaxiAvailableNfts(), maxMaxiProjectId(); ajax/start-maxmaxi-missions.php; startMaxMaxiMissionsAjax() in skulliance.js). Start Max Maxi launches 2 missions per unlocked Maxingo level from MAX_MAXI_MIN_LEVEL (=2) up (project DHCF_MAXINGO_PROJECT), highest level first, each with consumable 1 (100% Success) and consumables 5/6 when stocked. Consumable ids: 1=100%, 2=75%, 3=50%, 4=25%, 5=Fast Forward, 6=Double Rewards, 7=Random Reward. IMPORTANT ordering: the 100% item requires the project's NFTs to be undeployed but does NOT deploy them, so Start Max Maxi must run BEFORE either Start All -- the button hides itself once the roster is out, which is what enforces it. Two per level (not three) is deliberate: level durations differ, so pairs come due on separate days and 2 + 1 from the free run equals the DHC 3/day cap. Level 1 is excluded because it claims instantly and would spend the day's slots at launch; maxMaxiUnlockedLevel() gates both the launcher and the button on that floor so they cannot disagree. STEPPING THROUGH THE LADDER FROM INSIDE THE DRAWER: .ms-d-bar carries prev/next arrows (#ms-d-prev/#ms-d-next -> msStep()), and the LOCK DECISION IS SERVER-SIDE. mission_loadout() returns prev_id, next_id, next_locked and next_open, where next_open = next exists && (!locked || mission_is_admin()) -- the SAME question ajax/mission-data.php?what=loadout asks before it will serve a locked rung, so the arrow cannot be live on something the endpoint then refuses. If the client re-derives it from levels the two can disagree and the arrow opens an error; missions-harness.php asserts missions.php never mentions next_locked at all. next_id comes back EVEN WHEN LOCKED so the arrow renders disabled rather than vanishing ("there is more, you have not earned it"). Neighbours are the NEAREST level either side (two LIMIT 1 subselects in a UNION, indexed on project_id+level), not level+-1, because the ladder is ORDER BY level ASC and a project whose numbering skips must still step card to card; the harness's stub INTERPRETS that SQL (reads the WHERE, the ORDER BY direction and the LIMIT) after a stub that re-derived "nearest either side" from the fixture passed with the real ORDER BY flipped. STEPPING IS A REPLACEMENT, NOT AN OPEN: msOpenDrawer() now calls msStopMedia() (factored out of msCloseDrawer) and clears picked/items/shedByItem/target before refilling the body -- otherwise the outgoing mission's video keeps playing detached (the same trap the close path documents) and consumables picked for one mission ride into the next one's launch. Arrows are 44px like Close, and both are disabled while a step is in flight (LO = null) so a double-click cannot skip a rung. |
| missions-consumable-items.md        | 7 consumables          | db.php:2389-2418, consumables table. FAST FORWARD IS NOT A SHORTER DURATION. Nothing stores one: mission_active() (missions-lib.php) and completeMission() (db.php:1393) both pull the mission's created_date BACK by ceil(duration / 2) days and then count the full duration forward from there, so the wait actually left is duration - ceil(duration / 2) = floor(duration / 2) -- ZERO on a one-day mission, which lands the instant it is sent. That is NOT the realms rule: upgradeRealmLocation() (db.php:8730) and the raid launcher (db.php:9708) both use max(1, ceil(duration / 2)), a floor of one day, and they store the shortened timer. Do not copy one into the other. The Skull Paper used to say "round up if duration is a prime number", which is not what any of this does. THE DRAWER PREDICTS IT BEFORE THE ITEM IS SPENT: effectiveDays() in missions.php repeats that arithmetic so Runs for, Net per day and the launch line move the moment Fast Forward is picked -- previously the item changed nothing on screen and the only confirmation was the countdown after launching. Two traps. (1) `items[MS_FAST_FORWARD] === undefined`, never a truthiness test: Fast Forward's BOOST IS 0, so `if (!items[id])` reads a picked item as unpicked -- the same 0-boost trap that made toggleItem() shed crew for it. (2) MS_FAST_FORWARD is emitted from the PHP constant MISSION_ITEM_FAST_FORWARD, not written as 5 again. Net per day is scaled off the server's own net_per_day rather than re-deriving (reward - cost) / duration, and is marked changed only when the figure actually moves (on a one-day mission the wait goes but what a day pays does not). THE ARITHMETIC LIVES IN TWO LANGUAGES, so it is tested twice from ONE table: $FF_TABLE in missions-harness.php is proved by driving the real mission_active() with a Fast Forward item attached, and missions-fastforward-harness.js brace-extracts effectiveDays/daysText/paintRate out of missions.php and reads that same $FF_TABLE out of the PHP harness by regex -- a copied table would agree with itself forever. Verified: changing the server's ceil to floor fails the PHP harness, and every way of writing the client rule wrong (keeping the half instead of saving it, borrowing realms' max(1,..), falling back to the full duration on zero) fails the JS one. |

Tier table is graded on the loser's rating minus the winner's (dhcf_table_for('raids', gap)), using the pre-raid ratings. Note endRaid() resolves LAZILY when anyone loads the raids list, so it routinely runs in the other player's session -- dhcf_award() parks the reveal modal only when the awarded user_id matches the session user. |

| games-monstrocity.md (theme NFT stats) | Stats for an owned NFT played in a theme | monstrocity_nft_stats() in lib/monstrocity-stats-lib.php, called from ajax/get-nft-assets.php; monstrocity-stats-harness.php | **A THEME CAN BE ANY COLLECTION, AND THOSE COLLECTIONS KNOW NOTHING ABOUT MONSTROCITY** -- no strength trait, nothing to read -- so the stats are INVENTED from the only thing every NFT has, its identifier, and are deterministic so a roster never reshuffles. Traits and rarity are not consulted anywhere; do not "fix" that without deciding what a trait means across 35 unrelated collections. The ORIGINAL Monstrocity collection does NOT come through here: its stats were authored at mint and ajax/get-monstrocity-assets.php reads them off the CIP-25 metadata via Koios, which is why there are two code paths. **THE CARDANO 5-CHARACTER STRIP IS LOAD-BEARING:** monstrocity_nft_seed() drops "asset" from a CIP-14 fingerprint and seeds from the rest, exactly as the inline code always did. Which bytes get hashed DECIDES THE NUMBERS, so seeding from the full `asset1...` string silently re-rolls strength, speed and tactics for every Cardano NFT already in a theme -- the harness pins four captured baselines against precisely that (mutation confirmed: dropping the strip moved 3 of 4). **IT USED TO BE CARDANO-ONLY BY ACCIDENT:** the endpoint gated on `strpos($asset_id, 'asset') !== 0` and `continue`d, so every Solana mint, XRPL NFTokenID and Polygon contract:tokenId was dropped, the theme fell back to the player's Monstrocity roster, and only an error_log said why. The derivation was never Cardano-specific; it is a hash of a string. **SIZE AND TYPE WERE THE SAME NUMBER:** both were `crc32($seed) % 3` over three-element maps, so every Small was Base, Medium a Leader, Large Battle Damaged -- three outcomes instead of nine, and Medium+Leader (100 health, no tactics penalty) was strictly the best card a third of any collection could roll. type is now salted (`crc32('type:' . $seed)`); SIZE KEEPS THE ORIGINAL HASH so no existing NFT changes size, only its type and therefore its health. Asserted as the 9-cell joint distribution, because "some NFT is a Large Leader" passes on a coincidence. Ranges are pinned too (strength 1-8, speed/tactics 1-7, and the exact size/type/powerup words createCharacter() switches on) because an unknown word does not error, it falls through to 85 health. 6 mutations, all caught. |
| games-monstrocity.md (NFT art) | Which URL an NFT character's picture is loaded from | monstrocityImageChain() in monstrocity.php; monstrocity-chain-harness.js, monstrocity-ipfs-harness.php | **AN ABSOLUTE URL IS THE PICTURE, NOT A CID.** nfts.ipfs holds a bare CID for every Cardano and XRPL row but a whole https URL for any collection hosting its own art (Solana's OMEN serves from omenati.com) and a data: URI for an inline SVG. The chain prepended a gateway to all of them, building `https://gateway/ipfs/https://omenati.com/art/01998.jpg` -- every candidate dead, so the card drew the skull while its stats worked fine. getIPFS() in db.php made this split already, which is why My NFTs showed these images and the game did not; this was the one path that never got it. The locally cached copy still comes FIRST either way, because the cache keys on md5(ipfs) and that works for a CID or a URL. **THE HARNESS EXECUTES THE FUNCTION** (lifted into node via new Function) rather than matching source: the bug leaves every string in the function intact and only the returned list is wrong, so no source check can see it -- mutation confirmed, removing the branch rebuilds the exact poisoned URL. The theme's own ipfsPrefixes column is still read by nothing. 4 mutations, all caught. |

| games-boss-battles.md (many collections per boss) | Which collections a boss accepts fighters from | boss_collections table (manual CREATE in monstrocity-schema.md), lib/monstrocity-boss-lib.php, ajax/get-bosses.php, the picker in admin-monstrocity-boss.php, monstrocity.php's roster fetch; monstrocity-boss-collections-harness.php | **bosses.collection_id IS ONE COLUMN**, so canFight tested one policy and the roster fetch posted one policy. Fine while a project had one collection on one chain; now a project holds collections on Cardano, Solana, XRPL and Polygon and a holder on the wrong chain got *"No qualifying NFT characters available for this boss"* for their own project's boss. **MANY, SO A JOIN TABLE** -- same shape and same reasoning as monstrocity_theme_collections. One boss keeps ONE health pool and ONE bounty, which is the point of a boss; the rejected alternative was a duplicate boss per chain with the same art, separate pools and separate leaderboard rows. **THE BOSS'S OWN collection_id IS ALWAYS ACCEPTED**, prepended at read time by monstrocity_boss_policy_list(), so a half-finished admin edit cannot lock a boss's own holders out and **a boss with nothing attached is byte-for-byte unchanged** -- the migration is additive, nothing moves until somebody ticks a box. Duplicates and blanks are dropped from the list because get-nft-assets.php builds one bind placeholder per entry. **NOTHING IN THE CODEBASE CREATES boss_collections** (same as the theme table): mysqli returns false for a missing table without throwing, so the admin warns on the form AND on the save, and -- the part the theme form originally got wrong -- a warning now BLOCKS the post-save redirect, which otherwise discarded it and showed only "Saved." Ticks also survive a rejected save, or an error on the name silently narrows the boss. 11 mutations, all caught; TWO HARNESS SELF-FAULTS WORTH REMEMBERING: the stub first applied the policy-is-not-empty filter itself, so that assertion tested the stub and could not fail for any change to the library (it is a WHERE clause -- a stub cannot run SQL, so it is read from source); and the explicit table guard plus the `if (!$res)` check after the query are belt and braces that BOTH yield an empty read, so dropping either alone is an equivalent mutant and no assertion here can hold it. |
| (platform-wide) | The Skulliance icon in the tab, on bookmarks, on a phone home screen | lib/favicon-head.php, included by header.php, index.php and every standalone page; favicon-harness.php | The icon tags were PASTED into three pages and ABSENT from every page that skips header.php -- which is every standalone game (Monstrocity, match3rpg, Skull Swap, Skull Racer, Crypt Crawl, Crypt Conquest, Realm Guardians, DHC Arena) plus the gallery and error.php. Those are the pages a player keeps open longest and they all showed the browser's blank sheet. **NO PER-PAGE TEST COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT** -- each page was internally consistent; it is only visible by looking at every page at once, which is what the harness does, listing the pages explicitly so a NEW standalone page has to be added deliberately rather than silently inheriting nothing. **ROOT-RELATIVE hrefs**, never a hardcoded host: the login cookie is host-only so www and apex are separate sessions. **theme-color is deliberately NOT in the include** -- it is genuinely per-page (#161616 header, #0e0f13 landing, #07111d homepage) and folding it in would restyle three pages' browser chrome. **homepage.php is deliberately NOT converted**: WordPress includes it from ABSPATH . 'staking/homepage.php' and serves it at the SITE ROOT, so its head is not ours to normalise -- the harness asserts it keeps its own. The include is also verified RENDERED (index-harness.php checks the live HTML), because a PHP include that fatals looks identical to a missing favicon. 6 mutations; one (deleting the closing `?>`) is a parse error rather than a caught assertion, and the realistic form of it -- dropping the opening `<?php` so the docblock prints into the head -- is caught. |
| (platform-wide) | The way out of a standalone game page | lib/game-exit.php, used by monstrocity.php and match3rpg.php; game-exit-harness.php | **WHY IT EXISTS:** game pages have no header.php, so no burger menu, and in PWA standalone mode no browser back either -- without it the player is trapped. It was written TWICE, the second copy's own comment saying it mirrors the first "so the interaction feels consistent", which is how that stops being true. **ON BRAND MEANS .button FROM dist/flexbox.css:** solid #00c8a0, #07111d text, 0.5rem radius, and a HARD offset shadow (0 6px #007a62) that halves to 2px while the button translateY()s into it, so it presses like a key. The old control was a translucent dark pill -- 999px radius, hairline border, backdrop blur -- which shares none of that and read as browser chrome dropped on the game. **COLOUR AND text-align ARE RE-STATED** and that is not redundant: flexbox.css styles bare `a` globally and monstrocity.php sets `a{color:white}` in its own head, so a component that inherits either renders differently on the two pages it exists to unify; hover restates the colour too or the text changes under the cursor. **monstrocity.php HAD #monstrocity-exit IN TWO !important LISTS** (an accent rule making the arrow teal, and "secondary buttons" forcing #e8eaed) -- on a solid teal button that second one is unreadable and !important beats anything the component says, so both were removed and the harness asserts nothing forces a colour back. The button must also stay OUTSIDE .game-container, whose `:where(...)` rule forces #e8eaed with !important. **top is per page** (62px on match3rpg, under the ~51px sticky site-header; 10px on monstrocity) via a CSS variable -- and the harness asserts the CSS READS the variable, not merely that the element sets it: a mutation hardcoding the value passed the first version of that check and would have put the button back over the nav. CSS is emitted once via a static guard. Verified by rendering the component against monstrocity.php's REAL lifted stylesheet in a browser, since the whole risk is cascade. 8 mutations, all caught. |
| games-monstrocity.md (admin chain labels) | Telling same-named collections apart in the Monstrocity admin | mon_collections() in admin-monstrocity.php, mb_collections() in admin-monstrocity-boss.php | A project's collections on four chains often share a name, so the theme checkboxes and the boss dropdown listed indistinguishable rows. **THE CHAIN NAME IS READ FROM THE blockchains TABLE**, via LEFT JOIN, not from a map in the file: the hardcoded `$CHAINS` in admin-collections.php stops at Solana and has never listed Polygon, which is exactly what a second copy of that map would have done again. The filter is LIFTED AND RUN against a minimal DOM, because which rows end up hidden, and the ticked-row rule especially, is invisible to any source check. 5 mutations, all caught. |
| games-skull-swap.md *(new)*         | Match-3 score chase     SEPARATE MATCHES OFF ONE SLIDE. checkMatches() used to return one FLAT set of matched tiles and judge a move by allMatches.size, so it had no idea whether that was one line or three: two separate 3-matches came to six tiles and were treated as an ULTRA FIVE, three threes still counted as one five, and an L counted as a five too. Measured before changing anything. It now collects each run and merges runs THAT SHARE A TILE into groups -- so an L or T is one match of five (shares the corner) while two parallel rows stay two matches -- and returns `groups`. forgeFromGroups() is the single place that decides what a slide forges: each match forges by ITS OWN size (4 -> Carbon, 5 -> Diamond), PLUS one Diamond for every match beyond the first. So 2x3 -> 1 Diamond, 3x3 -> 2 Diamonds, 4+3 -> Carbon + Diamond, 5+3 -> 2 Diamonds. multiMatchStep (200 per extra match) is the points half: landing two matches off one swap used to pay nothing beyond the tiles, which actively discouraged the harder move. DETONATION IS ALSO PER MATCH, not per slide: each match that holds a bomb fires one (Diamond preferred), a match of 4 additionally credits the bombs it wasted, and only a match of 5+ fires every bomb in itself. HARNESS TRAP: the stubs hand-copied bonusScores, so adding multiMatchStep made the new bonus score undefined and totals came out NaN -- silently, in a probe. Both harnesses now read the real table out of the constructor with REAL_BONUSES(). BOMB-IN-A-MATCH RULE, settled over several rounds and worth stating exactly because I got it wrong twice. THE ORIGINAL SINGLE DETONATION FIRES AT EVERY SIZE and everything else is ADDITIVE to it: the bomb the match picked (Diamond over Carbon) goes off first, before any chain it sets off -- verified by ordering, matched bomb then chained stray then forged bomb. On top of that: a FOUR credits every bomb EXCEPT the one that fired, at that bomb's own detonation bonus (Carbon 50 / Diamond 100), and forges the Carbon it earned, which fires after. A FIVE is the ultra match -- every bomb in the line detonates, plus the Diamond it forges, plus bombComboStep per bomb beyond the first. A THREE gets the single detonation and nothing else: no waste credit, by decision. CONSEQUENCE WORTH KNOWING: in a 3-match, one carbon and three carbons score identically (235), because only one fires and the others are not paid for -- skullswap-harness.js asserts that equality deliberately, where it used to assert 'each extra carbon is worth more'. 4befb56d had made EVERY bomb in ANY match detonate; the symptom it chased was real (four Diamonds scoring less than three) and the fix was to change a rule nobody asked to change. That inversion is now covered directly: the harness asserts four Diamonds outscore three. 4befb56d had made every bomb in ANY match detonate on the argument that four Diamonds for 40 points was the worst move in the game; right about the symptom, wrong to fix it by changing the rule. The size gate lives in resolveMatches() (>= 4 reaches handleBombMatches at all) and isUltra inside it (>= 5 detonates). BOTH halves need a test: routing a 3-match into the bomb path fires nothing anyway, so counting detonations alone passes a three that is quietly collecting the four's credit -- skullswap-bombmatch-harness.js asserts the 3-match scores exactly 30. It drives the real Match3Game through resolveMatches (not straight to handleBombMatches) so the router is covered, and node 26 needs Object.defineProperty for globalThis.navigator, which the older skullswap harnesses predate. | skullswap.php, db.php:5019-5136  MEMBERS SKIP THE LANDING: skullswap.php is one file holding both #ss-landing and #game-container, and the landing rendered for EVERYONE with ssPlay() hiding it on click -- so the Play menu, which only exists for a member, served the pitch on every attempt to play. That is the same annoyance 204f87e7 removed for the other five games; Skull Swap was missed because it has no separate *game.php. $ss_straight_to_game sets the two initial display values server-side (not by calling ssPlay() on load, which would flash the landing first) and hides #ss-exit, the landing-only pill -- GO BACK under the board is a member's way out. The board is safe to start visible: `const game = new Match3Game()` runs unconditionally at script end and ssPlay() only ever reveals an already-initialised game. Audited all 14 Play targets; Skull Swap was the only one with this gate.  BOMB-ON-BOMB MATCHES (skullswap-harness.js, node only -- the scoring lives entirely in the browser so no PHP harness can reach it). handleBombMatches() used to clear every matched tile and then detonate exactly ONE of them, and it computed the match-size bomb and threw it away because only the no-bomb path uses it -- so lining up four Diamonds deleted three for 10 points each and was the worst-scoring move in the game. Now every bomb in the match detonates in turn via handleBombDetonation() (which refills between, which is what makes the second and third worth anything), the match forges its size bomb and detonates that too, and bonusScores.bombComboStep pays 150 per bomb beyond the first -- deliberately below one diamond detonation (~780), because the reward should be the blasts. Measured: 1 diamond 1,025 / 4 diamonds 3,845 / 5 diamonds 5,350. THE CEILING MOVED WITH IT: SWAP_MAX_SCORE was 25,000, derived from '25 matches x ~790' which only held while one match could fire one bomb. An ultra costs ~6 of the 25 matches to set up, so 4 is the practical maximum and a perfect game is ~22,400; the cap is 30,000. A cap below a legitimate run does not catch a cheat, it makes save-swap-score.php answer 'error' and the record is gone. The harness asserts the cap stays above a perfect game and below 3x it. STUB TRAPS, both hit: tiles need a truthy .element or clearBoard()/clearRowAndColumn() count nothing and every scenario scores the same; and showerTiles() must really refill. THE LAST MOVE (skullswap-endgame-harness.js). Two bugs, one cause, both reported from a real round: a bomb earned on match 25 never blasted, and the score shown never matched the one saved. resolveMatches() was SYNCHRONOUS -- it returned true the instant it dispatched, while handleMatches()/handleBombMatches() were still running behind a 300ms setTimeout -- and the swap handler did not await it, so matchCount hit 25 and endGame() set isGrandFinale FIRST. Both handlers refuse to forge a bomb while that flag is up (`if (bombType && !this.isGrandFinale)`, `if (forged && !this.isGrandFinale)`), so the final match silently lost its bomb and the grand finale only caught bombs already on the board. Same cause for the score: endGame() ends with saveSwapScore(this.score), and its own `await this.handleMatches(...)` was awaiting UNDEFINED because handleMatches was not async -- so the save read a score the pending handler had not finished adding to. FIX: handleMatches and resolveMatches are `async`, the 300ms is `await new Promise(...)`, and BOTH call sites await -- the swap site (~1693) and the cascade site (~2051). THE CASCADE SITE IS NOT OPTIONAL: an un-awaited async call returns a Promise, which is always truthy, so `if (hasMatches)` would never be false and matchCheckCount would never reset. The harness drives the real class through move 25 both ways and prints the difference (not awaited: no bomb, 80 points; awaited: carbon, 280 shown and 280 saved), and asserts both methods still return Promises -- which is the exact property endGame() depends on. |
| games-gauntlets.md *(new)*          | NFT roguelike          | gauntlets.php, db.php (gauntlet* functions, ~12932-13300), plus gauntlets-harness.php. **THE HAND IS SPREAD ACROSS COLLECTIONS, NOT DRAWN FLAT.** gauntletStartRun() used one `ORDER BY RAND() LIMIT 6` over every NFT the player owns, so a holder of 200 of one collection drew a hand of almost nothing else -- reported from a real run: five of six cards from one collection. IT IS NOT A COSMETIC PROBLEM: gauntletGetEffectiveProjectId() maps core projects 1-6 to THEMSELVES, so every card from one core-project collection shares an effective project and gauntletCalculateWinChance() returns identical odds for all of them. Five same-collection cards is ONE choice wearing five costumes, and it makes Fast Forward -- which exists to let you swap AFTER seeing the opponent -- buy nothing. (Partner projects 8+ get rand(1,6) PER NFT so they do vary; Diamond Skull 15 is always 0/50%. The bug bites hardest on core collections.) gauntletPlanHand() decides how many to take per collection and is PURE -- no DB, no randomness, caller shuffles -- which is the only reason it is testable; gauntletPickHandNFTs() does the two query shapes around it. Collections are dealt ONE PER PROJECT before any project gets a second, because project is what sets the odds while collection is what the player sees. It PREFERS variety, it does not demand it: one collection still yields a full hand, which is the correct answer when there is nothing to vary. TWO QUERY SHAPES, NOT ONE: a GROUP BY to learn the inventory, then one small `ORDER BY RAND() LIMIT k` per planned collection (at most 6). Pulling every row to shuffle in PHP was the alternative and the whole point is players holding hundreds. `$exclude` (already-played NFTs, gauntletGetUsedNFTIds) must be applied to BOTH queries. The final `shuffle($out)` is load-bearing: gauntletGetHand() renders in insertion order, so without it the hand is laid out collection by collection. HARNESS NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the loop terminators are asserted AT THE TOP of gauntlets-harness.php, before any planner call, and the harness exit(1)s if one is missing. Several cases deliberately request more cards than the inventory holds -- exactly what the no-progress break ends -- so with that break deleted the harness does not fail, it HANGS. It hung twice during mutation testing, the second time after the check had been moved earlier but was still below the first unsatisfiable case. Also: the `$size < 1 || !$inventory` early return is a FAST PATH, not a guard (mutation proved behaviour is identical without it), and the intval() on `available` only diverges for a NON-numeric value, which is what the harness uses to pin it. |

| games-dhc-fighters.md (Arena record) | A Fighter's W/L in the Collection | dhc-fighter-modal.php (the tile), dhcgallery.php + ajax/dhc-fighter.php (both LEFT JOIN dhc_arena_fighters with COALESCE -- they feed the SAME panel, so a field in one and not the other is a record that appears only when you arrived one particular way), pinned by dhc-share-harness.js. **THE LABEL IS KNOWN-WRONG AND THE NUMBER IS KNOWN-DIRTY -- SEE dhcarena-winloss-unresolved.md BEFORE TOUCHING THIS.** Three things in one: a "win" is SURVIVAL (`!$fell`), not victory, so a Fighter can take a W in a battle you lost; it blends offence with the defences dhca_defenders() fields automatically, which you never chose and cannot separate retroactively; and dhca_sweep_stale() credits a forfeiter's still-standing Fighters with wins while its state-is-gone branch records nothing at all, so wins+losses does not equal a Fighter's battle count. | games-dhc-fighters.md (where a trait came from) | The "Dropped from ..." line on the reveal and in the X post | dhcf_earned_from() and dhcf_earned_tag() in dhcfighters-config.php, the source/from/tag fields in dhcf_drop_payload(), shareText() in dhc-dropmodal.php; dhc-earned-from-harness.php and dhc-dropshare-harness.js | **THE PAYLOAD IS THE ONLY CHANNEL.** dhcf_drop_payload() is the one shape a drop takes -- the modal reveals it, $_SESSION['dhcf_unseen'] stores it across page loads so a drop awarded on a game page reveals hours later, and the X post is built from it -- so a field it does not carry is a field the share cannot mention. source, from and tag are resolved SERVER-SIDE at award time for that reason, not looked up in the browser. **THE LABEL IS THE PHRASE FOR EVERY GAME** ("Dropped from Realm Guardians"); the three sources that are NOT games need their own wording because "Dropped from Invite a friend" is not a sentence, and the registry's own 'trigger' field is no help -- those are instructions ("clear 12 of the 15 crypts"), not descriptions of what someone did. dhcf_earned_from() falls back label -> key so a source added later still reads sensibly. **THE X TAG IS THE GAME, NOT THE TRAIT'S ARTIST.** Every trait is @MMAXI404's work and the post's tail already says so; x_tag names the account behind the GAME, which only the two specialty games have (@ohh_meed for Drop Ship, @oculuslounge for Oculus Lounge) because the rest are Skulliance's own and the tail already tags @skulliance. **THESE ARE REAL ACCOUNTS AND A WRONG ONE TAGS A STRANGER IN PUBLIC** from a player's own timeline -- both came from the owner, the codebase holds Discord ids for these people and no X handles, and the harness pins the exact strings and the handle shape rather than letting them drift. **THE CLAUSE IS APPENDED AFTER THE TRIM, NOT BUILT INTO THE SENTENCE.** The post is budgeted against X's 280 (a URL always counts 23) and over-long text is cut from the END, so building the sentence with the source already on it would make the provenance the first thing lost -- on exactly the posts about the rarest traits, whose names run longest. The budget subtracts the clause up front instead. The modal shows `from` only: a handle means nothing outside X. | games-drop-ship.md (odds on the loadout) | The survival figures on the Squad panel and in the shop | dropshipThreshold()/dropshipSurvivalPct()/dropshipTypicalRound()/dropshipItemOddsNote() in dropship/dropship-odds.php, the panel in dropship/dashboard.php, the per-item line in loadItems() in dropship/db.php; dropship-odds-harness.php + harness-fixtures/dropship-threshold-original.php | **DROP SHIP RESOLVES THE WHOLE RUN ON THE `run` POST** -- what plays afterwards is a replay of something already decided -- so the loadout screen is the ONLY place a decision happens, and it had no numbers on it. **THE FIGURE IS THE RUN'S OWN MATH:** each round is `rand(0,99) < $threshold`, so the threshold IS the odds; dropship-odds.php owns the single copy and dropship.php calls it to roll while the dashboard calls it to display. Two copies would mean the screen promising one number and the run rolling another, which is worse than showing nothing. **ceil, NEVER round:** survival is strictly-less-than, so a threshold of 65.66 admits 0-65, which is 66 of 100; round() would under-report a Mech Suit's 88.9 as 88. **THE TYPICAL ROUND EXCLUDES Drop Box, Radio and Jet Pack** so it reads as a floor, not a promise -- they only push it up. **THE EXTRACTION IS CHECKED AGAINST A VERBATIM COPY** of the chain it replaced, over all 512 loadouts, with the original preserved in harness-fixtures/ and marked do-not-tidy; it is dead code whose only job is to disagree. Two rules are pinned rather than "fixed": a bare squad beats Heavy armour (the no-armour branch carries the Skulliance promo gear, which stays inline in dropship.php because it is a side effect, not odds), and the suits OVERRIDE armour rather than stacking. **THE SHOP NOTE ANSWERS A DIFFERENT QUESTION** from the panel -- not "how good am I" but "what does this buy me" -- measured against the loadout held, and it is SILENT for anything whose effect is not in the roll (weapons, Radar, Night Vision Goggles decorate an already-survived round); an invented number on a price tag is worse than none. There is no downgrade branch because adding a suit can only move you up the chain, verified over 256 loadouts x 4 suits. **THE SQUAD PANE IS A BUILD GUIDE, NOT A SHOP.** It first shipped ending in "a Mech Suit would make it 89%" plus a footnote about Drop Box/Radio/Jet Pack, which puts a spending decision in front of somebody still assembling a foundation. Items carry their own numbers on the shop listing now; this pane answers what goes in the next slot. dropshipSquadAdvice() needs the ROSTER, not the flags -- the flags answer "is there any Heavy armour", which is all the ROLL needs, but the guide has to know TWO soldiers are wearing it to say which one to drop. Under four slots it recommends the missing tier worth the most (computed, not list order) plus a missing gear type; at four it names the DUPLICATE and the gap ("2 wear Medium, so no one is in Base") and says which to swap -- naming only the gap is useless advice when there is nowhere to put it, and a looser assertion let exactly that mutation through. A complete squad is told so and advised no further. **WEAPONS HAVE A MEASURABLE EFFECT AND THE SHOP NOW QUOTES IT:** weapondelay() pushes the counter forward on the melee chain and the counter at death IS the score (Rocket 5, Flame 4, MachineGun 3, Smoke/Grenade 2, Sniper/Katana 1). **BUT THE WEAPON THAT FIRES IS NOT THE BEST ONE OWNED** -- setWeapons() is a chain of NON-EXCLUSIVE ifs that each overwrite the choice, so the LAST checked wins, and Tactical Katana (+1) is checked last: owning a Rocket Launcher and a Katana fires the Katana. That is REPORTED in the shop rather than smoothed over, and dropshipWeaponDelays() must stay in setWeapons()'s order or the wrong weapon gets named -- the harness reads that order back out of dropship.php rather than trusting the table. Probably a bug worth fixing in the run; left alone because it changes scores. **STYLING TRAP, HIT ONCE AND NOW PINNED:** dist/flexbox.css centres text globally, so a flex child stretched to full width inherits it and the panel shipped with its first row left and every line under it centred; text-align is re-stated, and the accent is the platform #00c8a0 the Squad heading already uses rather than the lime it shipped with. 13 mutations, all caught. |
| launchpad.md (the daily reward) | Claiming once a day, and the streak | getDailyRewardEligibility(), getMaxDateCreated(), getRandomReward() and verifyYesterdaysRewards() in db.php; renderDailyRewardsSection() and getRewardTimeRemaining() in skulliance.php; ajax/daily-reward.php; daily-reward-harness.php | **THE CLAIM WAS REPEATABLE WITHOUT LIMIT FOR FIVE HOURS A NIGHT.** php.ini put PHP on UTC; MySQL stamps transactions.date_created with the host clock, which is Central. Between 19:00 Central and midnight PHP was already on tomorrow's date, so a claim stamped with today's Central date was read as YESTERDAY'S and the button came back. Not once but every time, because the test compared DATE against DATE -- each new claim landed on a date PHP had already moved past, so refresh-and-claim ran as fast as the page could load. Reported as exactly that. **IT WAS NOT THE PHP UPGRADE,** which is the first thing suspected: the 'UTC on this host' measurement in dhcarena-lib.php was committed eleven hours BEFORE the 8.4 switch, so the skew predates it and the exploit is as old as the clock gap. The same skew showed the player digest a day early and put the Next Reward countdown five hours out. **TWO LAYERS, AND THE SECOND IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** db.php now calls date_default_timezone_set('America/Chicago') -- the platform already says it runs on Central ('Thursday at 4pm CST') -- which fixes the whole class including the digest. But php.ini IS NOT IN THIS REPOSITORY and a cPanel version switch replaces it, so eligibility no longer depends on the zone at all: it is `MAX(date_created) >= CURDATE()` asked of MySQL, one clock writing and reading. Same lesson as dhca_crew() and the soldier sweeps. **MAX() WAS NEVER COMPUTING A MAXIMUM:** the query was `SELECT id, MAX(date_created) ... GROUP BY id` and id is the primary key, so every row was its own group and the PHP loop simply kept whichever row came back last -- newest only by luck, with no ORDER BY to say so, and MySQL 8.0 dropped GROUP BY's implicit sort. **A MISSING SESSION USER WAS ELIGIBLE:** no rows meant null meant 'never claimed', and the reward was then credited to user id 0. Now refused, as is a failed query, which used to read as never-claimed and pay out on any database error. **STILL OPEN:** verifyYesterdaysRewards() does not filter `bonus='1'`, so ANY transaction yesterday keeps a streak alive -- a purchase or a mission payout counts as having claimed. Left alone deliberately: fixing it starts breaking streaks that currently survive, which is a player-visible call rather than a tidy-up. |
| games-drop-ship.md (squads build themselves) | The squad engine: what it builds and when it rebuilds | dropship/dropship-squad-plan.php (pure: the plan), dropshipSquadsAutoSync() and dropshipSquadsRoster() in dropship/dropship-squads.php, the call in dropship/dashboard.php after the holdings sync; dropship-squad-plan-harness.php and dropship-squad-autosync-harness.php | **THE PLAYER DOES NOT MANAGE SQUADS AT ALL ANY MORE.** Save, Disperse and Remove are gone; the engine arranges the whole roster and the player only deploys. **A COMPLETE SQUAD IS FOUR ARMOUR TIERS AND ALL THREE ITEMS,** which makes tiers and items interact: a squad holds one soldier per tier, so the three items must come from three DIFFERENT tiers. Ten of every tier builds no second squad if every Medkit is Heavy. That is why a greedy planner is wrong here and why this is worth having. **IT IS A FLOW PROBLEM, NOT A SEARCH.** Soldiers are interchangeable inside a (tier, gear) bucket so any roster is 16 numbers; for k squads the question is whether a 3x4 item-by-tier table exists, which is a max-flow on nine nodes; and by Konig's edge-colouring theorem any such table splits into exactly k squads, so nothing is stranded by an earlier choice. Largest feasible k is provably the maximum, and the harness confirms it against exhaustive search on 3000 random rosters. **TWO BUGS, BOTH THE SHAPE THE DESIGN AVOIDS:** the colouring took any valid squad rather than one that leaves a splittable remainder (fixed by the proof's own rule -- a tier with as many edges left as squads left MUST be in this squad); and handing out soldiers squad-by-squad let a free slot take a Medkit a later squad's plan needed. **THE REBUILD TRIGGER IS OWNERSHIP AND NOTHING ELSE,** and it needs no stored hash or new column: the arrangement is current exactly when every owned soldier is in exactly one squad, so a bought NFT is in none and a sold one leaves a squad short. dropshipSquadsRoster() deliberately does NOT filter active or deceased -- reading either would reshuffle squads every time somebody pressed Deploy or lost a run. **REBUILDING DOES NOT WITHDRAW ANYONE:** soldiers.active is never touched, so a squad regrouped mid-session stays fielded. **SPEED, because it runs on a page load:** 1.1ms for a thousand soldiers, and the common load is one SELECT because the membership test short-circuits it. dropshipPlanMaxSquads() bisects (feasibility is monotone) and the diff asks one yes/no per squad rather than a full maximum. **THE FOURTH SLOT PREFERS None:** only one of each item fires, so a duplicate does nothing here AND is one the next squad cannot have -- see the Medkit note in the run-loop row. dropshipSquadPlanDiff() still exists for the churn-minimising question but nothing in the UI uses it now that the engine is silent. |\n| games-drop-ship.md (per-theme soldier stats) | Where a soldier's armour and gear come from, per collection | dhc2-loadouts.json (generated), dhc2-mapping-draft.php (generates it from dhc-mint-traits.json), dropshipNftLoadouts() in dropship/dropship-grunts.php; dropship-grunts-harness.php | **TRAIT-DERIVED, NOT A HASH, AND NOT FOUR HAND-PINS.** The theme used dropshipStatsFromAssetName() -- a crc32 of the asset name, deterministic and meaningless -- which gave the owner's four fighters two Base tiers, no Heavy and no Medkit. That was patched by pinning those four names, which was a test and handed one holder a squad nobody else could build; retired at the owner's request. The override LAYER stays, because dropshipGruntLoadout() checks it before the Grunt gate and dropshipGruntFilterSql() already handles the stored-column mismatch both ways; only its contents changed, from 4 names to all 226 fighters. **SHORTY VERSE IS MAPPED TOO** (shortyverse-loadouts.json, from shortyverse-mint-traits.json via shortyverse-mapping-draft.php), which covers Dread City AND Filthy Mermaid since both sit on that policy. That collection has NO traits -- a name, a one-line summary, and every token total_supply 1 -- so armour is EDITION SCARCITY, recovered from the asset-name stem (TRUST001/002/003 are three prints of one character; 81 characters exist once, one runs to twenty) and ranked into quartiles. Gear is ENGAGED, which marks a fighter engaged in violence and so decides WHETHER there is a weapon, with the print number deciding WHICH -- nothing in the metadata separates one print from another. DISENGAGED is tested FIRST because it contains ENGAGED as a substring. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT SCORED SUMMARIES BY KEYWORD AND CUT THE COLLECTION FROM 80 COMPLETE SQUADS TO 20**, because ENGAGED appears in 130 of 381 summaries and swamped Melee; a word that common describes the mint, not the character. Now 89 against 80. **THE TABLES ARE KEYED ON ASSET NAME ALONE** and the soldiers row carries no policy, so they must stay disjoint; dropshipNftLoadouts() keeps the first on a clash and the harness asserts no overlap. **NOTHING MAY EVER MAP DROP SHIP (project 1).** Its soldiers rows were PRESEEDED with the collection's own metadata -- that is why dashboard.php gates its rand() on `project_id != 1` and dropshipThemeTraitSource() keeps it on koios, away from the UPDATE in dropshipSyncFromSkulliance() -- and the seed exists ONLY in the database, with no copy in this repo to restore from. The harness asserts no table declares Drop Ship's policy and none carries a `DROPSHIP<digits>` asset. That name test caught itself being wrong first: a bare prefix match flagged Shorty Verse's own DROPSHIPFIELDCOMMISSIONER001. **ARMOUR IS A RARITY-WEIGHTED SCORE** over torso/headgear/arms, log2(1+total/count) so a 1-of-1 counts ~3.5x a common trait, ranked into QUARTILES so a wrong weight shuffles neighbours and cannot empty a tier. **GEAR IS THE LEAN OF THE VALUE NAMES** -- scythe reads Melee, blaster Demolition, recovery interface or companion Medkit; a near dead heat resolves to None, which is a real state the fourth squad slot wants. **IT COSTS THE COLLECTION NOTHING:** 46 complete squads across 226 against 48 under the hash. **REGENERATE, DO NOT HAND-EDIT** dhc2-loadouts.json. **THE ENGINE PICKS THIS UP BY ITSELF** -- dropshipSquadsAutoSync() rebuilds on stat drift as well as ownership, so every holder's squads regroup once on their next load. **A WHOLE HARNESS BLOCK WAS DEAD** and this is how it was found: dropship-grunts-harness.php had `exit($fail ? 1 : 0);` with the next section header concatenated onto the same line, so ~70 lines of override and filter-SQL assertions had never executed and the suite said "all good" while the data changed underneath it. Summary moved to the end of the file; the revived assertions are now derived from the table rather than naming literals, so regenerating the mapping cannot rot them. |
| games-drop-ship.md (DHC2 Fighters fields what you built) | Assembled DHC Fighters as Drop Ship soldiers | dropship/dropship-fighters.php (dropshipSyncFighters(), dhc2FighterStats()), dhc2-fighter-rules.json, the $ds_fighters block in dropship/dashboard.php, dropshipClaimedSoldiers()/updateSoldiers() in dropship/db.php; dropship-fighters-harness.php | **IT IS AN ADDITION, NOT A SOURCE SWITCH.** The first version made 'fighters' a third EXCLUSIVE trait source, which would have turned the real DHC2 Fighters collection off -- a holder would have lost their minted soldiers the moment the theme was switched over. The Fighter pull runs BEFORE the NFT chain and the Koios arm still fires, so a player fields what they bought and what they built. **OWNERSHIP IS CUMULATIVE ACROSS SOURCES AND THAT TOOK A SECOND PASS.** updateSoldiers() detaches the player's whole roster before re-attaching the list it is handed, which is how a sold NFT lets go of its soldier -- correct for one source, and with two it meant whichever ran second wiped the first. The Fighters synced, created rows, attached, and were detached a few hundred lines later by the Koios pass: four NFTs, no Fighters, every query succeeding. dropshipClaimedSoldiers() is a per-request union so each call re-attaches everything reported so far and call order decides nothing. **DISASSEMBLED FIGHTERS ARE NOT FIELDED** (f.disassembled_at IS NULL) -- without it a player disassembles one, spends the traits on a second, and fields both. **THE SYNC ONLY FIRES ON A CHANGE** to the wallet set, the project or the trait source, so a theme switched in the admin needs one reload to populate and dropship-nft-probe.php reports whether it ran. **THE PICTURE IS A RENDERED PNG, NOT A CID.** dhcf_render_fighter() writes to dhcrenders/ and returns an absolute skulliance.io URL, which soldiers.ipfs stores and dropshipSoldierImage() passes through untouched. That made Fighters the first soldiers ever to reach dropshipSquadFace()'s last line -- every NFT theme hits dropshipLocalNftImages() and returns before it -- and dropshipSquadsAll() had never selected s.ipfs. With display_errors on, the undefined-key warning printed INSIDE the src attribute and rendered as a broken image with a file path under it. The column is selected now and the read is isset()-guarded, because the next missing column should be a missing picture, not a page of warnings. |
| games-drop-ship.md (themes) | Achievement, item and badge icons under a renamed theme | dropshipIconSlug()/dropshipIconSrc()/dropshipIconImg() in dropship/db.php, their seven call sites in dropship/db.php, dropship/dashboard.php and dropship/dropship.php; dropship-icons-harness.php | **THE LABEL IS WORDS, THE ICON IS ART, AND THEY WERE THEMED BY THE SAME CALL.** evaluateText() turned "Stormed the Beach" into "Landed the Eon Raider" (right) and the same translation was then used as the FILENAME, so the page requested icons/eon-raider.png, which nobody had drawn. Three broken achievement images on DHC2 Fighters, and the trap was armed for every theme after it: renaming a thing in the admin silently created a demand for a PNG. The themed filename is a PREFERENCE now -- a theme that ships art gets it, a theme that does not falls back to the base icon, and when neither exists the caller draws no <img> rather than a broken one (icons/extra-life.png has never existed). **BOTH SPELLINGS OF THE THEMED KEY ARE TRIED** because the callers disagreed: the scene achievements looked up 'beach', the item ones 'heavy-armor', slugified BEFORE translating. **THE ICONS ARE NOT IN THE REPO** (FTP, like all images), so the harness drives the resolver against a directory it fills itself, one per scenario -- is_file() is cached per request, which is correct in the app and made the first version of the fallback test pass on a cached hit for a deleted file. |
| games-drop-ship.md (the Armory) | Whether the shop renders at all | dropshipArmoryVisible() in dropship/db.php, wrapped round the Armory column in dropship/dashboard.php; dropship-armory-harness.php | **THE SHOP IS PART OF THE GATE, AND IT WAS NOT LISTENING.** Filthy Mermaid refuses a player without a Shorty Verse NFT and Oculus Lounge refuses one without a Disco Solaris NFT plus VIP, but the Armory rendered for everybody -- so somebody refused at the door was shown a shop full of goods for a game they cannot open, priced in a currency they cannot earn. **HOLDINGS ARE PROVEN BY A SOLDIER ROW**: a soldier exists in projects 3 and 4 only because an NFT under that theme's own policy synced off the wallet, and the four free Grunts cannot create a false positive because addDefaultSoldiers() is gated on project 1. **$vip COVERS BOTH DOORS** -- the Discord role and the DISCOIN temporary pass both read 'true' by the time dashboard.php has it (see dropship.php's note on the session flag, which exists so a player who just paid is not refused until they log out), so this function must never re-derive it; the harness fails if it mentions roles or DISCOIN. Project ids again, and deliberately: both gates being mirrored are written that way a few hundred lines above. |
| games-drop-ship.md (sharing a run) | The Share on X button at the end of a run | dropship/dropship-share.php (dropshipShareFacts(), dropshipShareText(), dropshipSharePage()), lib/share-x.php, lib/dropship-public.php, dropshipgame.php, dropship-card.php, the block beside instantReplayButton() in dropship/dashboard.php, prev_score in logScore(); dropship-share-harness.php | **THERE WAS NOWHERE FOR A SHARE TO LAND.** x.com/intent/post cannot attach a file -- the only picture a post carries is the twitter:image of the URL it links, fetched by X's crawler -- and every Drop Ship URL redirects to the login, dropship/index.php included. The crawler got a redirect to error.php, so a shared run unfurled as a bare blue link. dropshipgame.php is the public front door this game never had. **THE URL CARRIES A THEME, NOT A RUN.** ?run=<result_id> was the obvious shape and would have let anyone read any player's score by changing a number; the theme is all the card needs and the placement lives in the post text, which the player writes in their own composer. **THE ART IS THE PICKER'S, REFRAMED.** The five theme heroes are already public (Discord fetches them) but none is 2:1, and summary_large_image centre-crops -- so dropship-card.php places the art whole in a 2:1 frame over a blurred copy of itself. That backdrop is a PROGRESSIVE upscale, 1.6x a step, not dhc-card.php's single jump: 28px straight to 1200 is 43x and leaves visible squares, invisible on a Fighter render whose edges are flat and obvious on theme art that is high-contrast to the corner. Looked at, not assumed. **WHO GETS NAMED** is the owner's rule: whoever the run passed, else the leader being chased, never anyone who beat the sharer. 'Passed' means below the new score and at or above the old one, which is why logScore() carries prev_score forward -- deleteResult() destroys the row before the page renders. A first run (prev null) passes nobody however high it lands. **THE POST IS AN ADVERT THAT CONTAINS A SCORE.** The first version was a report card -- true, and it told a stranger scrolling past nothing about what the game is or that it costs nothing, which spends free reach on a number. Three parts now: result, pitch, call to action. **AND THE BUDGET IS SPENT, NOT TRIMMED.** shareOnXUrl() cuts from the END, so a long theme name would have eaten the call and then the pitch -- the half the rewrite exists to add. dropshipShareText() assembles against dropshipShareLimit() and drops to a shorter pitch instead, so the result and the call always survive. **THE BUTTON MATCHES INSTANT REPLAY**: .button had no .secondary, only .small-button did, so the share shipped as a small pill stuck under a full-width button. The new .button.secondary pins min-height/line-height rather than padding, because the primary is an <input> and this is an <a> and they do not line up by themselves -- measured at 52 vs 54px in a headless render. **THE CALL RECRUITS, IT DOES NOT TAUNT.** Three situational lines ("Come take it off me", "Who's next?") were replaced by one, at the owner's direction: the board is already here, the person worth writing to is the stranger scrolling past. **THE MENTION IS INSIDE THAT SENTENCE**, so shareOnXUrl() now suppresses its own tail whenever the body already names the handle -- two mentions cost 13 of a very tight 280 and read as a handle dangling under the post. Every other game is unaffected: no handle in the body, tail as before. **@oculuslounge AND NOTHING ELSE.** dropshipThemeHandle() copies from the owner's x_tag registry in dhcfighters-config.php and is shape-checked, because a wrong handle tags a stranger from a player's own timeline. @ohh_meed and @MMAXI404 are in that registry too and are PEOPLE -- a trait drop is occasional, a run share fires every time anybody finishes a game, and that volume was not pointed at someone's mentions without being asked. One line each to add. **THE PITCH IS A LADDER**: the owner's wording names the theme, which the result and the call already do, so at 288 against budget it drops to the same sentence without the theme name and then shorter again. Verified at the longest REAL theme name (Filthy Mermaid) on a 41-player board, because a pitch that only fits in theory never ships. **AND THE PITCH IS PER THEME, BECAUSE ONE SENTENCE FOR FIVE GAMES WAS WRONG THREE WAYS.** It read "Oculus Lounge entails a squad of four soldiers ... Free to play, and your first squad is free too": the Lounge is VIP-gated (project 4 in dashboard.php), the four grunts are DROP SHIP ONLY (addDefaultSoldiers() is gated on project 1 -- dropship-grunts.php opens "Every Drop Ship player is given four free soldiers" and says DROP SHIP ONLY twelve lines down, which is how reading the first line and stopping shipped it), and the Lounge has no soldiers at all -- every theme renames what a squad is made of and the dashboard reads that as evaluateText("Troops"). dropshipThemeClaims() in lib/dropship-public.php is the one rule, read by BOTH the post and dropshipgame.php, because the post is what sends a stranger to the page and the two must not disagree. It is keyed on project id, which is the anti-pattern this codebase keeps deleting -- but both gates it mirrors are written that way, and a second opinion about who gets grunts is worse than one hardcoded id in one place. 'Squad' itself is NOT in the theme dictionary; checked, not assumed. |
| daily-nudge.md | The one suggestion a day on the Launchpad | lib/nudge-lib.php (nudge_quiet(), nudge_footprint(), nudge_breadth(), nudge_candidates(), nudge_pick(), nudge_headline()), ajax/nudge.php, the $lp_nudge block in launchpad.php, the `nudges` table; nudge-harness.php | **THE TARGET IS NARROWNESS, NOT INACTIVITY**, and getting that backwards would aim the whole feature at the wrong people. Read off the Activity wall: bigredcrypto is ninth by weighted score with three sources touched, scorba is on 7,057 with four. Ranking by volume would miss every account the owner marked and pester the ones he left alone. NUDGE_NARROW_MAX is 4 of 15, which is where the real gap sits -- every marked account is at 4 or below, every unmarked one at 6 or above (thepriebe, 6, is the nearest miss). The harness transcribes both sets from that screenshot, so it tests against the owner's judgement rather than mine. **ONE SUGGESTION, AND IT MUST BE ACTIONABLE TODAY.** Candidates are ordered by friction and each carries its own availability test: unused traits first (the reason is a number off their own account), then a realm, then things they are already equipped for, then Drop Ship (four free soldiers, no holdings needed), then the browser games. A candidate that cannot answer "why you, today" is not offered -- 2 spare traits does not suggest the assembler, which needs 3, because sending somebody to a page that refuses them is worse than silence. **COSTS NOTHING ON MOST LOADS**: nudge_quiet() is one indexed read and gates the fifteen activity queries, so a player pays once a day and a snoozed player never -- the harness pins that ordering, because reversing it is invisible and expensive. A broad player's result is logged too, or the queries rerun all day to reach the same answer. **DEGRADES TO SILENCE**: no `nudges` table, no nudge; every query is suppressed. **THE ENDPOINT TAKES NO USER ID** (the session decides) and refuses GET, or an <img src> in Discord could snooze somebody for a week. Repeat guard: NUDGE_REPEAT_DAYS 30, so ignoring a card gets you a different one. **NUDGE_GAP_DAYS IS 3 AND NOT 1, AND A SIMULATION IS WHAT CAUGHT THAT.** Daily, the rotation is genuinely varied -- a different card every morning, never a repeat -- and it is still a card every morning for ten days, then a twenty-day drought, then the same burst. The code read fine; the fourteen-day sequence did not. 3 is deliberately NUDGE_REPEAT_DAYS / 10 (ten candidates x three days = thirty), so the rotation comes round exactly as the guard expires and the steady state is one nudge every few days forever.
