The harness runs the full forty days and asserts the SHAPE -- spaced, never twice running, ten distinct in the first cycle, no gap longer than NUDGE_GAP_DAYS -- so setting the gap to 1 fails on a 21-day drought and setting it to 4 fails on the rotation drifting against the guard. **IT STOPS ASKING WHEN THE ANSWER IS PLAINLY NO.** Nothing used to: a player who stayed narrow got a card every three days forever. NUDGE_FATIGUE_SHOWN 10 offers inside NUDGE_FATIGUE_DAYS 60 with zero clicks logs a 'rested' row expiring in NUDGE_REST_DAYS 90. One click of any kind resets it -- the rule exists to tell "has not got round to it" from "is not interested", and a click is the only evidence of the first. This is why nudge_log() records 'clicked': built for measurement, and it turned out to be the off switch. **A SUGGESTION INSIDE THE FOOTPRINT WIDENS NOTHING**, and the Fighter candidate shipped without that guard -- it tested whether the player had the PARTS and not whether they already build, so bigredcrypto (two Fighters, spare traits) was offered "Assemble a Fighter" under a heading reading "Something else to try". Every other candidate was guarded, which is exactly why reading them one at a time missed it; the harness now SWEEPS every target footprint against both generous and empty facts. **THE GATE HAS TO CLOSE ON AN EMPTY RESULT TOO**: with nothing pickable no row was written, nudge_state() kept answering "no recent nudge", and the fifteen queries plus the trait count ran on every Launchpad load until a candidate aged out. A 'none' row closes it and is excluded from the fatigue count, because we did not actually ask. **THE OWNER CANNOT SEE THIS FEATURE**, which is why admin-nudges.php exists: it only speaks to accounts touching 4 or fewer sources and his touches all 15, so the one person who has to judge the suggestions is the one account guaranteed never to receive one. It is also the campaign's targeting list. **IT MUST NEVER WRITE** -- a 'shown' row from the preview would consume that player's next nudge and restart their gap, so opening the page to read the list would silence the entire list, and nothing on screen would look wrong. Pinned by the harness. It calls the same nudge_breadth()/nudge_candidates()/nudge_pick() the Launchpad does rather than re-deriving them, and reads the whole platform through one activity_totals() pass instead of fifteen queries per player (dhcf_available() is per-player, so it is only asked for accounts that qualify). |
| admin only (no player-facing page) | Which lapsed members have already been written to | lib/outreach-log.php, ajax/admin-outreach.php, the To write / Written / Everyone filter and Mark written button in admin-lapsed.php; lapsed-harness.php | **THE POSITION COUNTER IS NOT PROGRESS.** "68 of 344" is an index into the current sort, so it moves the moment the sort changes or somebody signs back in and drops off the list -- without a record the work is not resumable. **MARKING IS EXPLICIT AND SELF-REPORTED.** Opening the modal records nothing; only the Mark written button (or the d key) writes, and it advances in the same press. Nothing on our side can observe whether a Discord DM was actually sent, so this is the owner ticking a box, not the system watching -- the owner chose this over marking on open, which would record anyone merely looked at. **A BATCH IS ONE WRITE.** outreach_mark_many() takes the whole selection and rewrites the file once; a loop of single marks would be one read-modify-write per person and any two that overlap lose each other, which is exactly the data this exists not to lose. The row checkboxes take a SHIFT-CLICK RANGE, so catching up on seventy eight already sent in list order is two clicks rather than seventy eight. After a batch the page reloads rather than patching the rows, because the header counts, the dimmed thumbnails and the To write filter all read the same state and keeping four things in step by hand is how one of them ends up lying. **A FILE, NOT A TABLE**: it is the owner's notes about his own outreach, no player code reads it, and it needed no schema change. **NOT IN dhcrenders/**, which is where it started: that is the render cache and the obvious thing to empty when a picture looks stale, which would delete a record of hundreds of sent messages. It lives in data/, gitignored, and the harness fails if the path moves back into the render cache or if data/ stops being ignored. **NO LOG MEANS NOBODY HAS BEEN WRITTEN TO** -- the inversion would empty the To write list and look like finishing. **THE DEFAULT VIEW IS 'all'** on purpose: filtering by default is the better list but renumbers a pass already in progress. |
| admin only (no player-facing page) | Whether a lapsed member can still be sent a DM | lib/guild-members.php, admin-guild-refresh.php, the Discord column in admin-lapsed.php; guild-members-harness.php | **A DM TO SOMEBODY WHO LEFT THE SERVER CANNOT BE DELIVERED.** Discord answers "send a friend request first" because you no longer share a guild, and there is no way round it from our side -- so the roster is checked before the message is written, and every wasted attempt avoided is also one fewer against an account Discord watches for bulk-DM patterns. **UNKNOWN IS A THIRD STATE AND THE MOST IMPORTANT ONE**: a cache that was never built must never render three hundred members as "left", because the owner would believe it and write them all off, so guild_member_in() returns null rather than a bool and the column says unknown. **A FAILED READ WRITES NOTHING** -- a partial or empty roster marks everybody missing from it as gone and nothing on screen looks wrong; the harness drives 403/500/empty against a local stand-in and asserts the previous good roster survives all three. **ONE BULK CALL, CACHED TO A FILE** (dhcrenders/guild-members.json, paginated 1000 at a time; the harness returns a full first page so pagination is exercised rather than assumed). It needs the **Server Members Intent**, which is a switch in the Discord developer portal, so a 403 names that switch instead of reporting a status code. **REFRESH IS A SEPARATE POST ENDPOINT**: the panel is read-only and a side effect on GET would fire on every open, including a prefetch. admin-harness.php now tells panel pages from action endpoints STRUCTURALLY (redirects and never loads header.php) rather than by a name list, because this endpoint was swept in as a panel and told off for not opening a layout it must not open. |
| admin only (no player-facing page) | Whether the lapsed-member campaign is working | the Came back view in admin-lapsed.php; lapsed-harness.php | **THE PACKAGE IS THE RECEIPT.** A 'returngift' row in dhc_trait_drops is a returning member with a timestamp, granted once per lapse on a sign-in after a month away -- so measuring the campaign needed no new table, no new column and no event logging. Change that source string and the panel silently reports nobody, which is why the harness counts BOTH places it appears (it first checked only that the string was present somewhere, and passed while one of the two had been changed). **THE LOGIN IS THE WIN**, per the owner: somebody signing in after a personal invite is the campaign working whether or not they use the traits, so 'came back' carries the emphasis and 'built a Fighter' is a secondary column. **FIGHTERS ARE COUNTED FROM EACH MEMBER'S OWN RETURN DATE** (f.created_at >= g.back_at) in one grouped join, not per row and not against a fixed date -- a fixed date would count Fighters built years before the invite and report a campaign that worked when nothing happened, and it would look entirely plausible. 'Came back again' reads last_login more than a day after the package, because last_login is written at most once a day. |
| admin only (no player-facing page) | The invite message, written from what the member actually played | lap_invite_line() and lap_invite_text() in admin-lapsed.php, surfaced in the package modal; lapsed-harness.php | **THIS TEXT IS PASTED INTO A REAL DM, so a claim it invents is a claim the owner makes.** The harness RUNS both functions rather than reading them, because the failure mode is a sentence that is perfectly grammatical and untrue. **GRADED BY VOLUME AND THE BOTTOM TIER SAYS NOTHING**: below 10 plays the personal line is dropped entirely, because telling somebody with three runs that they were big on a game reads as a mail merge, which is the one thing a personal invite cannot read as. **THE REALM LINE IS CONDITIONAL** -- it is the strongest sentence in the message and the easiest to disprove by logging in. **NOTHING THAT ADMITS TO A BATCH.** The no-history fallback opened "I'm reaching out to folks who haven't been around in a while", which tells the reader they are one of a list -- the opposite of what a personal invitation is for, and worse than saying nothing about their absence at all. It now just addresses them and gets to the point. Swept for ('folks', 'everyone who', 'members who', ...) because that sentence is the natural way to write a fallback. **NO INVENTED HISTORY**: these go to members the owner knows of but has never messaged, so the harness sweeps for 'miss seeing you', 'last time we' and similar; his own earlier drafts used those legitimately with people he actually talks to. **TWO LABELS ARE COLUMN HEADINGS, NOT PLACES** -- 'Fighters Built' and 'Drop Ship Games' produced "spending some time in Fighters Built", so they are rephrased ('building Fighters', 'Drop Ship') rather than skipped, and the verb is 'big on'/'into' rather than 'in' because only those take both a game name and a gerund. 'Daily Streaks' is excluded outright: it is a login counter. **A WAY BACK INTO DISCORD, BUT ONLY FOR SOMEBODY WHO LEFT.** Most people who left cannot be DMed at all, but the roster check only knows about the SKULLIANCE guild -- a member who left yet shares another server with the owner, or is a friend, still receives the message, and for them the invite is the difference between logging in and actually being back (Discord roles are granted in the guild, so a non-member gets none however much they stake). Shown to a current member it would read as a mail merge, so it is conditional and the harness drives both. It goes in BARE, with no sentence in front: it opened "Looks like you're not in the Discord server anymore", which reads as being told off for leaving, and a second link under the first needs no explaining. The link is discord.gg/JqqBZBrph2, verified against the Discord API as resolving to SKULLIANCE. The guessable vanity form does NOT exist -- Discord answers Unknown Invite -- and partner.php's only call to action pointed at it, sending every prospective partner to an error page; fixed to the same code the homepage uses. Worth re-running that check if a Discord link is ever added: a dead invite looks identical to a live one in a diff. Adding this message also added a 'Left the server' view, because those members are deliberately kept out of To write and otherwise the only way to reach the message written for them was to scroll Everyone. **THE GIFT IS DESCRIBED AS TRAITS**, not a built Fighter, because traits are what lands. |
| games-dhc-bosses.md | The daily shared-health boss crew | dhcbosses-lib.php, ajax/dhcbosses-action.php, the DHC Bosses panel and boss mode in dhcarena.php, dhcbosses.php (public), checkDHCBossesLeaderboard() in db.php, the dhcbosses and dhcbosses-day jobs in rewards.php, dhcbosses-schema.md; dhcbosses-harness.php | **IT IS AN OVERRIDE OF THE ARENA, NOT A SECOND GAME.** The board, the matching, the AI, the damage model, the Crew query, the Fighter art and the trait tables are all the Arena's and are CALLED, never copied -- dhca_turn() was extracted out of dhca_move() for exactly this, so one exchange of a player move for its replies serves both and a retuned blast radius lands in both the same day. The harness fails if the turn loop, the battle constructor, the roster or the award stop being the Arena's. **THE BOSSES ARE THE THREE FOES**, which is why no targeting or rank code needed changing: the engine already expects three opponents in three ranks. The only place a boss is not simply a Fighter is its picture -- it carries a flat `art` field and layerImgs() short-circuits on it. **EACH BOSS ENTERS AT ITS SHARED HEALTH, AS A SNAPSHOT**, and the damage is reconciled once at the end under a FOR UPDATE row lock: two players who both take 800 off a bar that had 1,000 cannot remove 1,600, and the clamped figure is what gets recorded, because the monthly board ranks on it and racing somebody must not pay. **LOSING IS THE NORMAL OUTCOME AND STILL PAYS** -- the day is decided by damage, not kills. **THE REWARD CURVE HAS NO FLAT SPOT**: it rises continuously with aggregate damage so stopping is never worth more than continuing, which matters because ten players who all talk to each other would find a threshold in a week. An earlier design escalated the table on days the crew SURVIVED and its optimal play was to stop at 99%. The full clear is a DISCRETE step above the gradient, so 99% is still one table short. **COOLDOWN IS ITS OWN TABLE** (dhc_boss_fighters): benching an Arena Crew would make the boss cannibalise the participation it is scaled against. **SCALED ON RUNS THAT CAN BE PLAYED, NOT ON ROSTER SIZE** -- a player with forty Fighters still cannot send more than the allowance, so the budget is min(players x allowance, fighters / crew) x damage-per-run x 0.6. **DHCB_POW_MULT 1.4 AND DHCB_DMG_PER_RUN 950 ARE MEASURED, NOT GUESSED**: twelve seeds at each setting gave 2.2 -> 8.2 turns, 1.4 -> 11.7, against an Arena battle's ~15, and the health budget is derived from the damage figure. The harness re-measures both every run and fails if either drifts, because nothing else would notice. **THE DAILY SETTLE IS SAFE LATE AND SAFE TWICE** (dhc_boss_payouts keyed day+user, written BEFORE the award), which makes it the only cadence in rewards.php that can be re-run. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (Coming back after a month away) | The one-of-every-category package a lapsed member gets on sign-in | lib/return-gift.php (return_gift_traits(), return_gift_url(), return_gift_due(), return_gift_grant()), the gift block in skulliance.php, return-gift-card.php, the gift column and package modal in admin-lapsed.php (Copy name / Copy message / Copy image, prev-next, arrow keys); return-gift-harness.php | **DERIVED, NOT STORED.** The package is a pure function of the Discord ID, so the preview in the admin and the grant on login are the same calculation and cannot disagree -- an invitation promising a picture is still true weeks later with nothing reserved, expired or reconciled, and no new table. **RENDEZVOUS HASHING, NOT MODULO**, which is the reason that holds: hash(id) % count(slugs) reshuffles EVERY player the moment a trait is added to a category, so Monday's preview is a different package by Friday. Each slug is scored hash(id|category|slug) and the lowest wins, so adding one moves roughly one player in N. Measured: removing one background of 19 moved 4.8% of 400 ids. **ONE OF EVERY CATEGORY (8), WRITTEN OUT RATHER THAN READ FROM THE RARITY TABLE**, so adding a ninth is a decision rather than a giveaway that quietly grows -- the harness diffs RETURN_GIFT_SLOTS against the table both ways and fails on either. It began as DHCF_REQUIRED's three and was widened deliberately: three gets somebody to a plain Fighter, eight gets them a finished one. Two knock-ons, both found by widening it: **DHCF_UNSEEN_MAX** had to go 9 -> 12, because the session reveal queue silently drops anything over the cap and a package of 8 can land on top of a 3-trait invite -- the trait is still in the ledger, the player just never sees it and nothing looks wrong; and the per-trait Discord post had to be suppressed, because eight embeds in a row reads as a bug. dhcf_commit_drop() took an **$announce** parameter defaulting to true for that, and the harness pins the default, since flipping it would silence every drop on the platform. One summary post goes out instead, carrying the assembled package as its image -- the same render the admin preview links to, so the DM and the announcement cannot show different Fighters. **TWO BUGS THE OWNER CAUGHT BY LOOKING AT THE PREVIEWS.** (1) The effects trait never drew. The package is keyed by CATEGORY, but dhcf_clean_traits() and dhcf_layers() both walk dhcf_slots() and ignore any key that is not a slot -- and there is no 'effects' slot, only effects1 and effects2. The trait was granted and written to the ledger correctly and then silently dropped by everything that draws, with nothing erroring. return_gift_layout() now maps category -> slot (a slot whose name IS the category wins, so weapon not weaponBack; otherwise the first slot of that category, so effects1), and the harness asserts every key is a real slot AND that removing the effects layer changes the rendered bytes -- the key check alone would pass on a slot nothing happens to draw. (2) Illegal pairings. The platform refuses three (dhcf_headgear_blocked, dhcf_companion_blocked, dhcf_arms_weapon_clash) and dhcf_layers() silently declines to draw the offending piece, so an unfiltered package hands somebody a trait that cannot be worn and does not appear -- measured at 113 of 400 ids, almost all plastic-blaster against arms that cover it. return_gift_clashes() is the single home for all three and filters the CANDIDATE POOL rather than dropping the slot, so the next legal piece wins and the package stays at eight; a category with no legal candidate left is simply omitted. Order matters: DHCF_REQUIRED (background, torso, head) comes first in RETURN_GIFT_SLOTS so the required pieces never yield, and headgear/companion/weapon give way to the head and arms already chosen. The rules are called, never restated -- a copy of those lists would be wrong the first time a head is added. **THE ANNOUNCEMENT DID NOT FIRE AT ALL AT FIRST**, and nothing said so. dhcfighters-notify.php is loaded by the very branch of dhcf_commit_drop() that the package skips with $announce false, so on the real login path neither skl_discordmsg() nor dhcf_author_block() existed, return_gift_announce() hit its own guards and returned, and the traits arrived in silence. return_gift_announce() now loads the notifier itself (buffered, and respecting DHCF_NO_NOTIFY). The harness missed it because the harness required the notifier at the top; it now probes in a SUBPROCESS loading exactly what skulliance.php loads, which is the only way to see what production sees. **THE GRANT IS A SIBLING OF THE REFERRAL BLOCK, NOT NESTED IN IT** -- one extra brace would make the whole feature fire only for people who arrived through an invite link, which is the opposite of the intent and looks normal in a diff, so the harness measures brace depth with token_get_all() rather than trusting indentation. **COMMON AND UNCOMMON ONLY** -- a present for signing in must not out-rank what people earned, and the harness checks the tiers against a hardcoded list rather than against RETURN_GIFT_TIERS, because the first version read the constant and widening it to 'rare' widened the check at the same time. **RETURN_GIFT_DAYS IS 30 AND IS WRITTEN TWICE**: skulliance.php cannot read the constant, because it lives in the file its cheap pre-check exists to avoid loading on every signed-in page view, so the harness reads both and fails on drift -- lower one without the other and the feature stops firing for everybody with nothing in any log. **NOT GATED ON BEING INVITED.** admin-lapsed.php is for deciding who to write to personally; the grant fires for anyone who comes back on their own, which is most of them. **A DIFFERENT FIGHTER EACH RETURN.** The hash is salted with how many packages the member has already had, so a second lapse is a new set rather than eight duplicates. **LAPSE 0 IS UNSALTED ON PURPOSE** -- 344 previews were already out in DMs, each a rendered file keyed on the traits, and salting unconditionally would have changed every one of them while the pictures already sent kept showing the old fighter, silently. The harness pins the first package against eight slugs TRANSCRIBED FROM THE LEDGER of a member who actually collected, so it is checked against what was really promised rather than against this code's own answer. The lapse count is COUNT(DISTINCT DATE(awarded_at)) and EXCLUDES the current window: counting rows breaks on a resumed grant (a package is eight rows), and counting the in-progress package would make a resume finish with traits from a different set. Every rule still holds on later packages -- the harness re-runs the tier and clash checks for lapses 1 to 4. **ONCE PER LAPSE, NOT ONCE EVER**, and the claim is the trait rows themselves -- dhc_trait_drops where source = 'returngift' and awarded_at is inside RETURN_GIFT_DAYS. The first version wrote action = 'claimed' into the `nudges` table, which has only ever held shown/clicked/snoozed/rested/none; an ENUM there would either kill the feature silently or, on a non-strict server, coerce the value to '' so the check never matched and the package re-granted on EVERY login. The ledger needs no schema change and is the evidence rather than a note about it. The window is what makes it repeatable: a second lapse is by definition 30 days of not signing in, so the previous package has aged out, while a package handed over seconds ago is still inside it and stops a double grant within one visit. The grant is also SELF-HEALING -- it hands over only the categories missing from that window, so a request that died halfway is finished on the next sign-in rather than skipped or repeated. Every read fails closed: a ledger that cannot be read counts as already claimed. **THE IMAGE IS COPIED, NOT LINKED.** The owner pastes a picture into Discord, so the modal writes the PNG itself to the clipboard with ClipboardItem; the link stays as a fallback. Three things make that work and all three are load-bearing: the PROMISE FORM of ClipboardItem is required, because awaiting the fetch first loses the user gesture in Safari and the write is refused; the blob's type must match the key exactly, so it is re-stamped with slice() in case the render is ever served as anything but image/png (verified live as image/png); and it needs a secure context, so navigator.clipboard is checked rather than assumed. It fetches the PUBLIC dhcrenders file rather than the admin endpoint -- same origin either way, but static, already in the browser cache from the thumbnail, and no redirect. VERIFIED IN A REAL BROWSER, not headless: headless Chrome never settles the write because the document is not focused, so the handler was driven through a real click and reported Copying -> Copied. **THE PREVIEW IS A REDIRECT, NOT A STREAM.** return-gift-card.php is admin-gated, so its own address shows the recipient "Not found."; it renders on demand and 302s to the public dhcrenders/ file, which is the address worth pasting. The admin column loads it lazily because compositing a stack of thousand-pixel layers per row would make a four-hundred-row list unopenable. return_gift_url() restates the renderer's filename scheme to print links without rendering, and the harness RUNS both and compares, because a drift there is a 404 in every DM with nothing to notice it. |
| realms.md (what a raid costs) | The honest stakes of raiding, and whether a casual player is safe | The loot sum near db.php:10855 (`round(($balance/100)*$difference)`, capped 500, doubled by Double Rewards), selectRandomProjectID() (`balance >= 100`), the range gate at db.php:9719 (`($offense-$defense) > 3` -> Out of Range), the Establishing Realm branch beside it, getRecentRaidedRealms(), rollRaidSoldierDeaths() and getCryptResurrectionHours() | **THE DISCLAIMER WAS SCARING PEOPLE OFF THE GAME.** A staker told the owner the description "almost sounded like I'd have to worry about losing currency if I wasn't active", and the old text earned that: it opened "you agree to being vulnerable", said "abandoned realms are a prime target for looting and continual damage", and named no number anywhere. **EVERY PROTECTION IT OMITTED IS REAL AND IN THE CODE.** Loot is 1% of ONE project's balance per level of gap, hard-capped at 500; only projects holding 100+ are eligible; a raider more than 3 offense levels above your defense literally cannot see you as a target; a realm with no defense yet reads Establishing Realm and cannot be raided at all; and a raider cannot re-hit the same realm until they have raided elsewhere, Portal level deciding how many in between. **THE LEVEL GATE INVERTS THE OLD WARNING:** an unattended realm stays low, and low realms are only visible to low raiders, whose small level gap is exactly what makes the loot small. Abandonment is self-limiting, not a death spiral. **DEATH IS A TIMEOUT** -- the Crypt resurrects on its own after (11 - crypt level) days, halved by Fast Forward, training intact; the gear is lost but gear wears out with use anyway. **THREE PASSES TO GET IT TRUE,** which is the lesson: the first draft said "your NFTs are never at stake" without checking and would have shipped a false reassurance (soldiers do die); the second said death returns them as untrained recruits, which the enlist path suggested and resurrectSoldiers() disproves (`trained = 1`); the owner supplied the timeout framing and the detail that gear is consumable regardless. Soften copy about mechanics only after reading the mechanics. |
| games-drop-ship.md (the run loop) | How many rounds a run lasts and when a Medkit is spent | The `while($dead == 'false')` loop and `extralife()` in dropship/dropship.php, `dropshipOddsFlags()` in dropship/dropship-odds.php. **THE LOOP IS NOT IN A FUNCTION, so dropship-run-harness.php LIFTS it and `extralife()` out of the page by brace matching and drives them with no database** (same trick as dhcgallery-perf-harness.php). Do not reason about this loop, run it: it is nested ifs over eight session flags with a counter four branches advance, and reading it produced a wrong answer twice on one report. ROUNDS 0, 1 AND 2 ARE FIXED INTRO SLIDES AND ROLL NOTHING, so round 3 is the FIRST roll and therefore the single likeliest round to fail on: 20% of runs at a Super Soldier Squad's 80%. That is a geometric distribution's mode, not a bug, and the harness pins the percentage so the answer is a measurement rather than an argument. FIXED HERE: `extralife()` tested `$extralife == 'true' || $_SESSION['medkit'] == 'true'` and then cleared BOTH, so a Medkit soldier plus a bought Medkit was ONE life. dropshipOddsFlags() has always counted one per Medkit in the roster plus the purchased one, the Squad pane quotes dropshipTypicalRound() off that count and the shop prices the item at +1 life, so the screen sold a second life the run could not give. The run now takes its COUNT from dropshipOddsFlags() (the same function the screen quotes, so they cannot drift) and spends one per failed roll. dropshipOddsFlags() also counts the PROMO life now: the no-armour branch grants a free Medkit, Melee and Demolition, and leaving it out understated a bare squad by a life. ALSO: the Extra Life caption was the one round not passed through evaluateText(), so a reskin announced Drop Ship's words mid-vocabulary. THE REPLAY NOW RECORDS THE NUMBERS: an HTML comment at the end of the markup carries the threshold, the lives carried and every roll (`<!-- run: survive<80 lives=1 rolls=... -->`). It is stored with the replay, invisible on the page, and ignored by dropship.js (which matches .round ELEMENTS). Without it, "the odds feel wrong" can only be answered by quoting a probability back at the player. ONE HAZARD: the loop has NO upper bound on $counter. Nothing in the real code can keep a run alive forever, but a mutation that stopped spending lives hung the request and had to be dropped from the mutation set. |
| games-drop-ship.md (Results in Discord) | The two posts every run makes: the death, then the standings | dropship/dashboard.php (the `$ds_just_ran || $ds_game_over` block at the foot of the page), checkLeaderboard($conn, "true") captured through dropshipCapture(), discordmsg() in dropship/webhooks.php which routes on $project_id. Pinned by dropship-themes-harness.php. **THE LADDER POST WAS GATED ON `project_id == 1 || project_id == 4`** with a comment calling Dread City and Filthy Mermaid a work in progress, so two live themes posted a death and no standings and nothing said why. That is the SECOND copy of that exact gate: dropship/db.php's payout carried it until Filthy Mermaid got a weekly cycle and paid nobody. Nothing in the block is theme-specific -- checkLeaderboard() already scopes to the session's game_id and project_id, getProjectName() names the theme, dropshipThemeImageUrl() resolves the theme's OWN picture and returns '' (which discordmsg() omits) rather than falling back to Drop Ship's -- so the real conditions are a game and a non-empty board, and those are what gate it now. The harness checks the LADDER BLOCK, not the file: dashboard.php compares project_id to a literal in eight other places and most are honest (the VIP door is Oculus Lounge's, the Grunts are Drop Ship's). It also forbids the list written as in_array(), and pins the empty-board guard and the themed image, because dropping either is how you get an empty embed on every run or Dread City posting Drop Ship's art. Verified live: images/dropship.jpg is 200 under all four theme roots. |
| (operator) | The Drop Ship loader | dropship/dropship.js (dsShowLoader, selectProject), the data-ds-prefix on <body> in dropship/header.php, #loading in dist/flexbox.css; dropship-loading-harness.php | **IT EXISTS ONLY FOR A THEME CHANGE, and only exists AT ALL at the moment of one.** It used to be a div on every page that any code could show, so it covered the screen on every navigation and every form post -- most of what Drop Ship does -- for work that was usually instant. Now nothing renders it and selectProject() builds it, so there is no element for anything else to show and it cannot spread again by accident, which is how it spread the first time. A theme change earns one: it is the request that re-reads the player's holdings and the only one that routinely takes a second or more. It goes up BEFORE the form submits or it never paints, and it is idempotent so a double click does not stack overlays. The ART IS THE THEME'S -- every theme ships its own images/loading.gif -- which is why <body> carries data-ds-prefix: the reskins nest under their slug and Drop Ship's own art is flat, and that is the one path JavaScript cannot work out for itself. **#loading AND #loading-image STAY IN dist/flexbox.css**, which both apps share, so the built element is styled for free and the platform's own usage is untouched. **THE LOADER WAS ON THE WRONG DOCUMENT.** dsShowLoader() builds the overlay and then submits the form, so it lived on the page being LEFT and died with the document swap -- well before the arriving page's art was there. Reported as "it flashes for a second, then the page displays without all the content rendered". The two halves are two documents: dropship.js still covers the request, and dropship/header.php now paints an overlay on the ARRIVING page, gated on $project_id_changed so it is a theme change and nothing else (an overlay on every navigation is what got the loader removed in the first place). It lifts on window.load, NOT DOMContentLoaded, because "full content" means the images -- and load fires even for subresources that failed, so a 404 in a half-uploaded theme cannot strand it. **PLAIN DOM AND A HARD CAP**, not dropship.js's own $(window).on('load'): jQuery comes from Google's CDN, Brave's Shields block that host for some stakers, and an overlay at z-index 99 that never lifts takes the whole site with it. It also lifts immediately when readyState is already 'complete', since waiting for an event that has been and gone is the other way to strand a page. The lift script is EXECUTED, not grepped -- dropship-loader-lift-harness.js runs the real thing out of the template against a DOM stub, and the no-element case is driven to the end rather than merely constructed, because an early return is invisible until a listener fires. **THE SPINNER IS A SLOT NOW TOO:** images/loading.gif was asked for by literal name in dropship.js and so was never in the manifest, which is why Drop Ship, Dread City and Filthy Mermaid all serve the same 5KB file (Oculus Lounge has its own). It stays a literal .gif rather than following the theme extension -- a still spinner says nothing -- and it sits in a named "Around the game" group with the empty-squad placeholder, not in the leftovers bin where nobody found the Discord images. 14 mutations, all caught. |
| (operator) | The instant replay | dropship/dropship.js (roundEl/roundCaption/roundImage/showRound), logScore() and getReplay() in dropship/db.php; dropship-replay-harness.php | **THE SKULLIANCE MIRROR IS FOUND BY NAME, NOT BY A LIST OF PROJECT IDS.** discordmsg() posts to the theme's own channel and, where one exists, mirrors into a Skulliance channel. That was `if project 1 ... elseif project 4`, so adding a mirror meant writing the credential AND editing that branch -- and the credential alone is what anybody would expect to be enough. The accessors already follow the theme slug in CamelCase (drop-ship -> getSkullianceDropShipWebhook, filthy-mermaid -> getSkullianceFilthyMermaidWebhook), so the name is derived and function_exists() decides: write the function, get the mirror. Still guarded, because those live in a credentials file outside the repo and an undefined call fatals every discordmsg() rather than just skipping the mirror. The theme check derives it the same way, so it reports what will actually happen. **A JAVASCRIPT FIX ONLY COUNTS IF THE BROWSER FETCHES IT.** dashboard.php asked for `dropship.js?var=` . rand(0, 999) and the server sends Cache-Control: max-age=604800 -- a thousand urls, each cached for a WEEK, so every load rolled a die and landed on a fresh copy or on one from before the last deploy. The same code appeared to work, then not, with nothing having changed. Every OTHER Drop Ship page asked for it bare and served a week-stale copy outright. Observed live: the dashboard running `?var=591` with none of the new functions in it while the server served the new file correctly. dropshipAsset() busts on filemtime() -- the url changes if and only if the file does. **THE SAME BUG WAS ALREADY FOUND AND FIXED FOR THE STYLESHEET** and its comment in header.php says the collision was live-tested; the javascript was simply never given the same treatment, which is worth remembering the next time a fix "does not work". **CHANGING THEME CLEARS THE OLD THEME'S SESSION STATE.** The switch set dropship_project_id and cleared nothing else, but current_score, score, game_id, battle_id, notification and run_logged are all scoped to a project in the database. current_score is the one that showed: the dashboard renders the Instant Replay button from it and resolves the row with checkMaxScoreResultID() filtered to the NEW project, so a score carried over from the old theme matched nothing, the button rendered with an EMPTY result_id, getReplay('') returned nothing, and the stepper walked ZERO rounds while the audio branches -- which compare round numbers and never touch the DOM -- carried on playing. Audio advancing over a frozen screen. It corrected itself on the next load because checkScore() runs at dashboard.php:610, AFTER the button renders at 596, which is why change-theme-then-refresh-then-replay worked and change-theme-then-replay did not. instantReplayButton() also refuses to render without a real id, because a control that cannot work should not be offered. **A ROUND IS FOUND BY POSITION, NEVER BY ITS BARE NUMERIC ID.** Round divs carry id='0', '1', '2'... and those ids are NOT unique on the dashboard -- the Armory, the soldier cards and the results tables all render elements whose id is a small database integer, and every one appears BEFORE #hidden-results, so getElementById() returns the wrong one. Land on an item card and it has no <h3> and no <img>: the step throws partway through, the caption and picture freeze while audio already started carries on, and WHICH ids collide depends on how many items and soldiers a theme renders -- which is why it looked different per theme and came and went. Scoped to #hidden-results, where position IS the round number (asserted: every round id comes from the same $counter). The reads also DEGRADE rather than throw, or the fix is the same crash wearing a different hat. **AN HTML ESCAPER IS NOT AN SQL ESCAPER:** the insert interpolated htmlspecialchars($replay) straight into the statement and the markup is full of single quotes, so it survived only because PHP 8.1 changed that function's DEFAULT to include ENT_QUOTES -- on 8.0 it is ENT_COMPAT, and this server picks its PHP version per domain from a dropdown. Escaped properly now, with the entity flags pinned on BOTH sides: encode with ENT_QUOTES and decode with ENT_COMPAT and every quote stays a literal &#039;, which is a replay full of broken attributes rather than a missing one. |
| (operator) | Koios calls from Drop Ship | lib/koios.php, used by dropship/dashboard.php (x2), dropshipSyncFromSkulliance() in dropship/db.php and dropship/dropship-nft-probe.php; dropship-themes-harness.php | **ONE COPY OF THE TOKEN, AND IT IS DROPPED ONCE EXPIRED.** Drop Ship carried a bearer that expired 2024-12-20 while the rest of the platform had moved to a current one, and nothing compared them. AN EXPIRED TOKEN IS WORSE THAN NONE: Koios answers an expired subscription with HTTP 403 and a sentence, where an anonymous call answers 200 -- and json_decode() of a sentence is null, which every caller's is_array() check drops without a word. Twenty-two months of silence, and it is why no reskin on the Koios path ever registered a soldier. Renewing is one edit in lib/koios.php. **NONE OF THE FOUR CALLS WAS BOUNDED**, which the broken token had been hiding: every request failed in milliseconds, so an unbounded one cost nothing until the token worked and a slow reply started holding the page render open to max_execution_time -- the UI locking up on a theme switch. koios_timeouts() sets connect, total AND low-speed limits, since a server that connects then dribbles bytes satisfies the first two; short on purpose, because every caller already treats a bad reply as "nothing to sync" and a page that renders beats one that does not. **CARDANO WALLETS ONLY:** Skulliance keeps Solana, EVM and XRPL addresses in the same wallets.stake_address column, and Koios answers the WHOLE batch with 500 ("Failed to decode Bech32 string") if ONE entry is not Cardano -- so a single Solana wallet blanked every Cardano wallet a player had. Filtered at the source on blockchain_id = 1, with koios_valid_stake_address() as a shape check at the call site for rows that are Cardano-labelled but malformed. Measured, not inferred: 10 valid -> 200, 9 valid + 1 malformed -> 500. |
| (operator) | Reaching the Skulliance database from Drop Ship | dropship/dropship-skulliance-db.php, called by dropshipConnectedStakeAddresses(), dropshipOculusLoungeLocalImages() and dropshipSyncOculusLounge() in dropship/db.php and by dropshipDropAward(); dropship-skulliance-db-harness.php | **BOTH CREDENTIALS FILES USE THE SAME FOUR VARIABLE NAMES** -- $servername, $username, $password, $dbname -- and Drop Ship's are what built $conn at db.php:60. Four places needed to cross to Skulliance's database and each did its own `include __DIR__ . '/../credentials/db_credentials.php'` followed by `new mysqli(...)`, every one of which OVERWRITES those four names. It was safe only by two accidents of placement: $conn is already a built mysqli object by then so overwriting its ingredients does not reach back into it, and every include happened to sit INSIDE a function so the overwrite was function-scoped. **NOTHING ENFORCED EITHER.** A fifth call site at file scope, or somebody moving one of these includes to the top of a page, repoints $servername and friends globally and the next connection built from them silently talks to the wrong database -- which presents as "Drop Ship is acting strangely", not as an error. The include now happens exactly once, inside dropshipSkullianceDb(), where the four names are function locals that die on return. **THE CALLER STILL OWNS AND CLOSES THE HANDLE**, deliberately: a cached shared connection would be fewer per request, but all four existing sites call close() when done and a shared handle closed by the first caller is a use-after-close for the second. That is a separate job from removing the trap. Fails quiet and returns null, because every caller is an optional enhancement to a page that must keep working when the platform database is unreachable; the harness drives that failure branch for real (a checkout has no credentials file) and also asserts the four names have NOT leaked into $GLOBALS. 5 mutations, all caught. |
| (operator) | Seeing and replacing a Drop Ship theme's art | dropship/dropship-art.php (manifest, grouping, pairing, upload, rename), dropship/admin-art.php; dropship-art-harness.php | **THE PAGE BACKGROUND IS FOUND, NOT HARDCODED, AND IS UPLOADABLE LIKE EVERYTHING ELSE.** It was markup repeated per project id -- header.php for project 4, a second copy in dashboard.php for 3 and 4 -- and the Filthy Mermaid one was missing the /staking/dropship prefix every other path carries, so it 404'd while the file sat on the server. Even fixed it only ran on the dashboard. One lookup now, in the header every page includes, by convention <slug>/<slug minus hyphens>background.<ext> with Drop Ship's flat like the rest of its art. **THE EXTENSION IS FOUND, NOT ASSUMED** (Oculus Lounge .png, Filthy Mermaid .jpg), and a theme with no file gets '' so the stylesheet's own background stands rather than a broken url(). **CAPPED AT 1920, NOT 1080:** it is background-size:cover on the body, scaled to the VIEWPORT rather than to a pane, so dropshipArtStore() takes an optional maximum. Replacing one removes the other extensions, or the glob picks between a .jpg and a .png by alphabet. The flat-vs-nested rule lives in ONE function -- it was in two, and a mutation breaking one left the other passing. **AN ICON IS MANAGED BESIDE THE ART OF THE SAME THING.** images/weapons/rocket-launcher3.gif and icons/rocket-launcher.png are two pictures of one object, and the only reason they sat on opposite halves of this page was that they live in different folders -- so replacing "the rocket launcher" meant five frames in one place and the icon below every other icon in the game. dropshipArtPlan() merges them, putting the icon after the LAST frame of its thing so a run of frames is closed, not split. Pairing is by the file stem, which IS the icon term, with one declared alias ('demo' -> 'demolition'); a prefix rule would also pair 'drop-box' with 'drop-ship'. The icons with no round art at all -- armour, the suits, the Medkit, the squad marks -- get sections named for what they are, taken from their vocabulary group, rather than a leftovers bin. Every icon must land somewhere and nothing may appear twice, both asserted. **A PICTURE ALREADY ON THE PAGE CAN BE DRAGGED INTO ANOTHER SLOT.** Several slots are deliberately the same image -- the two Super Soldier icons, a weapon whose frames barely move, a theme reusing one death -- and saying so meant finding the file on your own machine and uploading it a second time. The drag carries a PRIVATE MIME TYPE holding the slot name the form itself posts, not the image's url: a dragged <img> already offers text/uri-list, and reading that back would mean parsing a url into a server path, which is the kind of parsing that ends up accepting one it should not. text/plain goes alongside it because Firefox will not start a drag carrying only an unknown type. **THE SOURCE IS VALIDATED EXACTLY LIKE THE DESTINATION** -- it arrives off the form and becomes a path to READ, so an unchecked one is a file disclosure: the page would copy ../../credentials into a slot and then serve it. One $dsaSlot() answers for both, because two copies of "which slots does this theme have" is how a checked destination ends up beside an unchecked source. **COPIED TO A SCRATCH FILE FIRST**, because dropshipArtStore() MOVES its input when the file already fits and needs no conversion -- handing it the source slot would empty the slot being dragged FROM, and one picture in two places was the whole request; the harness drives the store to prove it still moves rather than trusting the comment. The destination's extension drives the conversion, so dragging a .jpg slot onto a .gif one writes a gif. The copy handler is guarded on !isset($_FILES['art']) and the client deletes the empty file input, because an untouched file field arrives as UPLOAD_ERR_NO_FILE and the upload handler runs on isset() alone -- without both ends the server answers "Upload failed (code 4)" and never looks at the copy. **AND THE IN-PLACE REPLY NOW RESOLVES THE SAME WAY THE WRITE DID**, which fixed a standing bug: it worked the path out a second time and knew only about icons, so the background slot -- whose rel already begins with the theme folder -- came back as <slug>/<slug>/..., and a file that saved correctly reported itself missing. 16 mutations, all caught. **AND THE COPIED FILE HAS TO BE READABLE BY WHATEVER SERVES IT.** The first copies stored perfectly, reported their real dimensions, and showed as broken images. dropshipArtStore() MOVES its input when the file already fits and needs no conversion, and a move preserves the SOURCE's permissions: an upload comes from PHP's own upload directory at the umask, but tempnam() creates at 0600, so every copied slot landed unreadable by anything but the PHP user -- and on this server static files are served by Apache, not PHP. dropshipArtReadable() chmods 0644 on EVERY one of the store's four success paths, not just that one, because which branch runs depends on the picture and on what the server has. The harness drives a 0600 scratch file through it and also COUNTS `array(true,` against `dropshipArtReadable(`, because this box has no Imagick (so that branch cannot be driven here at all) and its GD branch creates files at a 0644 umask (so dropping that chmod changes nothing here and everything on a tighter server). The in-place reply also stopped printing `&mdash;` at the admin: strip_tags() removes tags, not entities, and those messages are HTML for the non-JS path. **LEFT AND RIGHT WALK THE WHOLE PAGE.** Comparing seventy slots by closing and reopening each one is the thing the overlay exists to avoid, so the arrow keys and two on-screen buttons step through the pictures in the order they are laid out. It STOPS AT THE ENDS rather than wrapping: with that many slots, silently looping back to the first is disorienting. Each step REBUILDS the overlay rather than swapping the src, because the caption, the path and the dimensions all have to change with the picture and lightbox() is the one place that knows how -- which makes every step a close as well, so the listener bookkeeping has to hold; the harness steps to both ends and back and asserts no overlay or listener accumulates. The arrow buttons stop the click bubbling, since the backdrop closes on click and they sit on it. The list of pictures is re-read on every open, because an upload replaces a card's <img> in place and a cached list would step onto an element no longer in the document. **AND THE LIGHTBOX'S CSS WAS SCOPED TO A WRAPPER IT DOES NOT LIVE IN.** Every rule read `.dsart .lightbox` while the element is appended to <body> -- which is right for a fixed overlay, it must not sit under an ancestor with a transform or an overflow -- so not one rule matched. It was an unstyled div at the foot of the page: not centred, no backdrop, nothing to click outside of, and a fresh one on every click. A DOM stub cannot see that, so the harness now reads the STYLESHEET: the overlay's class must have an unscoped rule, and must not appear as a descendant of .dsart. It also has a close button, closes on the backdrop but NOT on the picture (pointing at the thing you came to look at must not throw it away), and replaces any overlay already open. **CLICK A PICTURE TO SEE IT FULL SIZE.** A 1080px frame shown at 210 says which image is in a slot and nothing about whether it is the right one, which is the question somebody is on this page to answer. Built on demand and removed on close, like dsShowLoader(), so no hidden overlay sits over the page. The close is what matters and is driven in dropship-art-lightbox-harness.js: the overlay is fixed at z-index 200 over everything, so a close that forgets the node leaves the admin staring at a covered page, and one that forgets its document-level Escape listener leaks one per open -- invisible until the fiftieth slot. The caption is textContent throughout, because a theme names its own icons and that name reaches the card. 10 further mutations, all caught. **A FILE ALREADY ON THE SERVER IS RENAMED, NOT RE-UPLOADED.** When the wording changes the picture is usually already there under the name it used to ask for, so the art page lists the shared folder and offers a rename. **THE SHARED FOLDER IS THE WHOLE RISK:** one icons/ serves every theme, a missing icon is a silent gap rather than an error, so a rename refuses to move a file any OTHER theme still asks for and refuses to overwrite one that exists. That guard is load-bearing on real data -- of the 9 files the derivation renamed, 8 were safe and radio.png was not, because Drop Ship itself uses it and Dread City merely calls it a Walkie Talkie; that one needs a copy. Both names are rebuilt with dropshipThemeSlug() and the target is checked against what THIS theme asks for, because they become a filesystem path. **THE MANIFEST HAS TO COVER WHAT THE RUN ASKS FOR, AND FOR THE BUNKER IT DID NOT.** live() picks a bunker frame from 1-6 while the round counter is under 16 and from 7-12 from 16 on -- the only stage with two bands -- while dropshipArtStages() declared six. Frames 7 to 12 were requested by the game and listed by nothing: no slot, no upload, no way to see they were absent. Found by a player, not by the page: a blank frame on a long Dread City run, inspected as bunker/11. Drop Ship, Filthy Mermaid and Oculus Lounge all have the full twelve on disk in the format they render; Dread City renders jpg and had jpgs for 1-6 only, which is why it is the one that broke. The two bands are labelled as such (1-6 "rounds 13 to 15", 7-12 "round 16 and beyond", numbered WITHIN the band) because a card reading "scene 11 of 6" says a frame is spare when it is the one a long run reaches. **THE HARNESS NOW SCRAPES THE RANGES OUT OF live() RATHER THAN RESTATING THEM** -- the real arguments to the real dropshipStageFrame() calls, paired with the path each branch writes -- and asserts the manifest matches EXACTLY in both directions, since declaring frames the run never asks for sends somebody off to draw art for nothing. A second hand-written list of the ranges would have been the same mistake twice. **AND A THEME WITHOUT THE SECOND BAND REPEATS THE FIRST.** Declaring frames 7-12 gives them a slot; it does not put art in them, so until somebody uploads six more a long run would still meet an empty pane. live() asks dropshipThemeHasFrames() before reaching for the band and falls back to 1-6 when it is not all there. ALL SIX OR NONE: Dread City's gif set has 7 and 8 and stops, so accepting a partial band would still blank the rounds landing on 9 to 12. Checked in the format the theme RENDERS, not whichever one happens to be lying about -- that distinction is the whole Dread City case. The manifest still declares twelve, because the fallback is a runtime kindness and not a reason to stop asking for the art: Theme Art keeps listing the six as missing. 12 mutations, 11 caught, the twelfth equivalent (dropping the `$ext === ''` guard changes no answer, since is_file('.../7.') is false anyway) and recorded as such in the harness. 10 mutations on the coverage invariant, all caught. One bug in the scraper itself worth remembering: an optional regex group that did not participate is UNSET, not '', so `$pm[2] !== ''` is true of null and every plain branch was filed under the exo key. **THE MANIFEST IS THE POINT.** A reskin is 70 images in a fixed tree and the only register of WHICH 70 was the run itself, spread across live(), kill(), extralife() and the weapon and equipment branches -- so "is this theme finished" had no answer and new themes stalled. Counts come from dropship.php's own initialisers ($beach_random = 7 means "last gif number for the beach") and were confirmed live: images/live/beach/7.gif is 200 and 8.gif is 404. **A STAGE IS TWO ROWS, because the exo frames are an alternate rotation and not more frames of the same thing.** A player wearing the suit sees those INSTEAD of the plain ones, so nine cards in one band read as a nine-frame beach when it is seven plus a secured frame and two alternates. The exo row is keyed on '@exo' rather than on the icon term: the row key doubles as an icon lookup, and a row called 'exo-suit' would pull the Exo Suit icon out of the Armor section into the beach -- and the hill and bunker would then get none either, because an icon is removed from the pool once a row claims it. It still carries the theme's word for the suit as its heading. **AND THE EXO ROTATION HAD LOST ITS SOUND.** The old chain played exo.mp3 at 300ms over those frames; their paths carry a variable frame number, so the scrape that built the exact audio table never saw them and they fell through to the surviving-round gunshot. Three prefix rows now, listed BEFORE 'live/' because prefixes are walked in order and the broader one would swallow them. The harness scrapes every folder the game addresses with a variable, not just the literal paths, and asserts each is covered by an exact row or a prefix -- with deaths named as the one deliberate silence. **THE GROUPS ARE DERIVED FROM THE MANIFEST**, with a catch-all, so a file added to the game becomes manageable without anybody adding it twice -- deleting a group is an equivalent mutant because the orphans fall into "Everything else" and stay uploadable. **THE UPLOAD SLOT COMES OUT OF THE MANIFEST, NEVER OFF THE FORM:** it becomes a filesystem path under the theme folder, so a posted one would write anywhere the web user can reach. Validated before use, and the page refuses to guess a folder for a project with no theme row. **A FILE IN THE WRONG FORMAT IS CONVERTED, NOT REFUSED.** The run asks for its art by filename, extension included, so whatever arrives has to end up in the slot's format -- refusing meant exporting again and coming back for a conversion the server can do. THE LOSSY CASES ARE NAMED in the result rather than done quietly: an animation flattened onto a still slot, and a photograph forced into gif's 256 colours. **ALPHA IS KEYED ON THE TARGET, NOT THE SOURCE** -- a png resized with its alpha channel intact and then handed to the jpeg encoder renders every transparent pixel BLACK -- and it is composited onto the page's own #07111d rather than white, which would ring every edge on a dark page. Imagick keeps frames only when the target is itself a gif; writing a multi-frame image to a .png produces png-0, png-1, ... one file per frame and no slot the run ever asks for. A file that already fits is only MOVED when it also needs no conversion. **AN ANIMATED GIF IS NEVER FLATTENED TO RESIZE IT.** GD reads the first frame and writes a still, so where Imagick is absent an animated upload is stored AT ITS ORIGINAL SIZE instead: an oversized animation costs bandwidth, a flattened one breaks the theme in a way nobody notices until a player says the animations stopped. With Imagick it is coalesced, resized frame by frame, then deconstructed -- skipping the coalesce corrupts the frames. Animation is detected from the BYTES, not the extension, which is only a claim. **SCALE TARGET IS DERIVED FROM THE STYLESHEET AND THE KEYFRAMES:** .container 1400 -> .main flex 67% = 938 -> minus 20px padding each side = 898 of content; #results pins min-height AND max-height to 898 so the box is SQUARE; #results-image is background-size: contain inside it; and @keyframes move scales the element 1.0 -> 1.2. So the widest art is ever PAINTED is 898 x 1.2 = 1078, rounded to 1080. **THE ZOOM IS WHY IT IS NOT 898** -- capping at the pane would look right on the first frame and soften over the next forty seconds, which a pan makes obvious because the viewer stares at one image throughout. **SQUARE** because contain fits by whichever side is limiting, so a portrait still needs the same headroom. A first version used 940x650, taking the 650 from `#results img { max-height: 650px }` -- that rule governs the replay's <img> frames, which never pan, and applying it to the Ken Burns background would have upscaled a tall still by about 1.7x at full zoom. Existing art at 1024 is 95% of the peak and is left alone, since nothing is ever enlarged. Nothing is enlarged and anything already smaller is stored as it arrived. The declared format must match the slot, because the run asks for the slot BY NAME and a png in a .gif slot is a 404 at play time. 6 mutations, 5 caught, the sixth equivalent as above; the pairing and rename work added 9 more, all caught. **A PRESENT FILE IS NOT A THEMED ONE, and the page could not tell.** Every reskin's folder was seeded by copying Drop Ship's, so a slot counted as done whether it held this theme's picture or the original game's, and "68 of 70 in place" read as finished when most of it was Drop Ship. Found from Discord: a Dread City run posted Drop Ship's animated death, and dread-city/images/die/1.gif is byte-for-byte dropship/images/die/1.gif -- as is its whole .gif set, beach frames included. Dread City's own art is the .jpg set beside it. dropshipArtIsDropShipCopy() marks those slots amber and counts them apart from missing ones, because they are different jobs: one is art nobody has made, the other is art nobody has replaced. SIZE FIRST, HASH ONLY ON A TIE -- Drop Ship's files run to 8MB and this is asked about seventy slots on one page load, so two pictures that merely weigh different amounts are never hashed (an equivalent mutant for the return value, asserted at the source for that reason). NOT @-SUPPRESSED: display_errors is on platform-wide, so the is_file() guard exists to keep a theme mid-upload from papering the page in filesize() warnings, and the harness asserts the function prints nothing rather than that it returns false, which it would either way. **THE GAME OVER SCREEN WAS NOT A SLOT EITHER.** The still that holds the results pane once your last soldier is gone was asked for as a hardcoded images/gameover.gif, so it was never in the manifest: no slot, no upload, no way to give a theme its own, and all four folders ended up holding the same picture (dread-city's is byte-for-byte Drop Ship's; filthy-mermaid and oculus-lounge do have their own). It was also a LITERAL .gif on a page that renders everything else through evaluateText("gif"), so Dread City and Oculus Lounge, both still themes, showed an animated game over with animation switched off. It is now images/gameover.$ext in the manifest, grouped with the Deaths it follows, and dashboard.php asks dropshipThemeArtFile() for it so the player's animation switch moves it with everything else. **THE GIF IS A FALLBACK HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE**, deliberately: for the death images the themed file already existed and a fallback could only regress to Drop Ship's copies, but NO theme has a non-gif game over at all, so asking strictly would have blanked the end-of-run screen everywhere the moment it shipped. It stays on the gif until a still is uploaded, and theme art lists that still as missing until then. **WHERE IT SITS IN THE MANIFEST IS LOAD-BEARING:** dropshipThemeGifProbes() takes the first and last extension-varying gif, so appending gameover.gif at the end would have made the Animation switch ask about the end screen instead of a round frame; it goes in with the deaths. An empty answer prints no rule at all rather than url(''), which would paint the pane with a broken background instead of leaving the stylesheet's. 13 mutations, all caught, two of which only failed once the harness stopped asserting '' against a path with nothing at either end -- the repo carries no art, so a guard against falling through to Drop Ship's flat folder passes vacuously unless the probe makes a file there to fall through TO. **THE TWO IMAGES DISCORD POSTS ARE SLOTS LIKE ANY OTHER, AND THEY HAVE THEIR OWN SECTION.** Reported as "I'm not seeing images attached and when I do, they are drop ship proper", which was two faults. images/vip.gif -- the thumbnail beside every embed's title -- was not in the manifest at all, so no theme could ever have its own and dread-city still has none; images/dropship.jpg WAS in it but sat in the trailing "Everything else / page furniture and placeholders" band with no row header, at the bottom of seventy slots, which is why the image on every post read as something that could not be themed. Both are asked for by LITERAL NAME in the code, not through the theme's extension, so they are listed literally in the manifest too -- and that is exactly why dropshipThemeGifProbes() had to stop taking every .gif it found: vip.gif is a .gif under a png theme as well, it is listed last, so it became the second probe and "does this theme have animated art" quietly turned into "does it have a Discord thumbnail". The animated set is the entries that CHANGE with the extension, which is a name in the gif manifest and not in the png one. |
| (operator) | Which art row a sound renders under | dropshipAudioPlanRows() in dropship/dropship-audio.php, the $snd_by_row / $snd_loose split in dropship/admin-art.php. Pinned by dropship-audio-harness.php. A sound is bucketed by the ART PATH of its frame, so even an unlabelled band of art gets its audio underneath. **FIVE EVENTS ARE REGISTERED ON A PREFIX, NOT A FRAME** -- 'live/beach/exo/', 'live/hill/exo/', 'live/bunker/exo/', '1up/' and 'live/' -- because each covers a rotation rather than one picture. A prefix never equals a frame key, so all five fell past the exact lookup into the general Sound section at the foot of the page: the three Exo Suit rows sat pages away from the exo art and the Extra Life sound away from the extra-life pictures. A prefix now attaches to a row **only when every frame under it is in that one row**, which is the whole rule: the exo prefixes and '1up/' each cover a single band and move; 'live/' covers the beach, the hill AND the bunker, so it has no single home and stays loose, which is truer than picking whichever band came first. The harness pins BOTH halves -- a mutation that drops the count-of-rows guard attaches 'live/' to the beach and is caught. It attaches at the position of the LAST frame under the prefix so the sound follows its pictures; that ordering is NOT covered, on purpose, because each of the four bands has exactly one sound and a one-item sort cannot be observed. It becomes checkable the day a band has both a framed sound and a catch-all. |
| (operator) | The image on a Drop Ship Discord post | dropshipThemeImageUrl()/dropshipThemeImagePath() in dropship/dropship-themes.php, dropshipDiscordEmbed()/discordmsg() in dropship/webhooks.php, the four call sites in dropship/dashboard.php and dropship/automate.php; dropship-themes-harness.php | **THE PATH COMES FROM THE PROJECT BEING ANNOUNCED, NOT FROM A GLOBAL.** Every announcement built its own url out of the global $prefix, and automate.php -- the weekly cron that posts Game Over and New Game -- does not include dropship.php, which is the only file that sets it. automate() has a $prefix of its own but it is a LOCAL, so discordmsg()'s `global $prefix` found nothing and the thumbnail went out as "https://skulliance.ioimages/vip.gif". **DISCORD FAILS SILENTLY ON ART IT CANNOT FETCH**, which is why this read as "images are not attached" rather than as an error anywhere. $project_id is the one argument every discordmsg() call already carries, so the slug is read off that project's theme row and the file is checked ON DISK before its url is handed over. '' means NO IMAGE KEY AT ALL, not an empty one: Discord rejects a whole embed over a url it cannot parse, so an empty string would have cost the announcement as well as the picture, and dropshipDiscordEmbed() is split out of the curl-and-credentials function purely so that rule can be executed. **AND '' RATHER THAN A FALLBACK ON PURPOSE** -- Drop Ship's own logo in Dread City's channel is the thing that was complained about, so a theme with no copy of a file gets a post with no art instead. The flat-vs-nested rule is dropshipThemeRoot()'s, same as the background and the art admin, and a project with no theme row falls back to Drop Ship's own flat art rather than putting an empty folder in the middle of the path. The death image also stopped hardcoding .gif, since the run itself asks for evaluateText("gif") and a png theme would have had Discord fetch art the game never shows; it rolls the frame ONCE and falls back to that frame's gif. **AND NO FALLBACK TO THE .gif SET WHEN THAT FILE IS MISSING.** The first version fell back on the same frame, reasoning that a theme can ship both sets -- but for Dread City the .gif set IS Drop Ship's copies, so the fallback reproduced the exact bug it sat under. No picture beats another game's. 13 mutations on the url work, all caught; 12 more on the borrowed-art detection, all caught. |
| (operator) | Which sound a Drop Ship round plays | dropship/dropship-audio.php (the event registry and the resolution), playRoundAudio()/loadFile() in dropship/dropship.js, the window.dsAudio emit in dropship/dashboard.php, the Sound rows in dropship/admin-art.php; dropship-audio-harness.php and dropship-audio-play-harness.js | **THE FILENAME IS THE WORD PLUS THE FRAME NUMBER, and the key is the FRAME, not the caption.** The art already numbers frames -- demo1 is the activation, demo2 the explosion -- so the sound follows: Drop Ship asks for demolition1.mp3 and demolition2.mp3, and a theme calling the thing a Paddle asks for paddle1.mp3 and paddle2.mp3; a single-frame thing takes no number, as melee.gif does. CAPTIONS CANNOT CARRY THIS: machine-gun2 and machine-gun3 share the caption "Machine Gun", as do rocket-launcher2 and 3, so which picture is on screen is a fact about the IMAGE. Keying on the art path also ends the ordering problem a caption chain has, where "Smoke Bomb" shadows "Pull Smoke Bomb Pin" unless the list is kept in a sequence it never declares -- an exact key cannot shadow another. A surviving round has no moment to name and draws one of seven beach frames, so those match on the START of the path, after the exact table has had its chance. **NO THEME IS EVER HANDED DROP SHIP'S AUDIO.** Reported twice -- "why are you playing drop ship sounds on filthy mermaid", then the gunshot that plays on EVERY surviving round turning up on themes that want nothing of the sort, which is the same leak where it is loudest. A theme plays its own file or nothing; Drop Ship alone falls back to the old sound-named files, because those ARE its own under a name the convention has not reached. The one exception is the opening, where crowd.mp3 was only ever Filthy Mermaid's and flyingcar.mp3 only ever Oculus Lounge's, so falling back borrows nothing. **THE FILENAME IS THE WORDING, exactly as it is for an icon.** Nothing about a theme's audio is stored: Oculus Lounge calling the Melee a "Vibrator" IS the instruction to supply sounds/vibrator.mp3. displayRound() used to carry THIRTY-FOUR if-branches matching caption text against filenames, and every reskin's own words sat in there beside Drop Ship's -- Vibrator=Melee, Dildo=Tactical Katana, Whip=Smoke Bomb, Spank=Explosion, Paddle=Demolition, Ball Gag=Radar, Butt Plug=Sniper Rifle, Anal Beads=Grenade. Every one is a word the wording table already held, so the chain was the wording table written a second time in a file no admin page could reach. 28 events is what is left. **THE project_id GUARDS WENT WITH THEM:** a dozen branches were wrapped in `project_id != 3 && project_id != 4`, which is "these two have no sound here" spelled as a hardcoded id check; a theme with no file is silent, which is the same answer arrived at honestly and is now fixable by uploading rather than by editing. **ORDER IS THE RULE AND ALWAYS WAS.** The match is a SUBSTRING of the caption, so "Smoke Bomb" also matches "Pull Smoke Bomb Pin" and the pin must be tested first or it never fires. The branch order carried that; the array order carries it now, and the harness DERIVES the invariant from the terms themselves (if one term contains another, the longer is tested first) rather than listing the pairs, so an event added later that shadows an existing one fails there instead of silently stealing its sound. **RESOLUTION IS THREE DEEP: the theme's own file, then Drop Ship's, then the OLD filename.** The third is what stops this being a silent game on the day it ships -- Drop Ship's sounds are named after the SOUND (grenadecontra, massexplosioncontra, enemyguncontra), not after its words, so almost nothing sits at the name the convention asks for yet; each rename takes over as it happens. **EVERY NUMBERED FRAME IS ITS OWN MOMENT AND GETS ITS OWN SLOT.** demo1 is the activation and demo2 the explosion: two frames, two captions ("Demolition" and "Explosion"), two sounds. That was already right, but five frames had no event at all -- Night Vision Goggles, Radio, Reinforcements, Jet Pack Takeoff, Drop Box -- which is not the same as a frame that should be silent, so they are listed with an empty default: silent exactly as today, and fillable. **AND ROUND 0/1/2 WERE OFF BY ONE.** The markup is explicit: round 0 is reset.gif captioned "Drop Ship Initiating" and never had a sound; round 1 is drop.gif captioned "Drop Ship" and is the one that played dropshipcontra; round 2 is team.gif captioned "Drop Ship Landed". The first pass hung round 1's sound on round 0's caption. "Drop Ship" is a SUBSTRING of the other two so it is tested after them, which the derived ordering check enforces. It also forced the opening sound off the bare theme slug and onto <slug>-opening.mp3, because Filthy Mermaid's word for the round 1 caption is "Filthy Mermaid" and two different sounds would have been fighting over filthy-mermaid.mp3 -- whichever was uploaded second silently becoming both. **THE HARNESS SCRAPES EVERY CAPTIONED FRAME OUT OF dropship.php** and asserts some event matches each one, so a frame added later with a caption nothing answers fails there rather than going quietly silent. **TEN FILES GENUINELY MOVE** and the harness lists them: door and stairs (Filthy Mermaid's opening rounds), ballgag, analbeadsinsertion, analbeadsremoval and spankme (Oculus Lounge's, named with the spaces squeezed out or after the sound). Until each is copied onto the name its wording asks for, that event falls back to Drop Ship's sound; the art page shows it as "playing <old file>" with a picker, which is the two-click fix. **THE OPENING IS ROUND 0, NOT A SLOT OF ITS OWN.** Asked why it was not associated with a slide; it should have been, because it plays on round 0 and round 0 IS a slide -- reset.gif, "the ship arriving", with a caption like every other frame. It was modelled twice: once as a frame with no sound and once as an "Opening" with no frame, which left it nothing to sit under and forced a <slug>-opening.mp3 name invented purely to dodge a collision with the round 1 frame (Filthy Mermaid's word for which is "Filthy Mermaid"). Folded into the reset/reset row, named by its own caption like everything else, and dsAudio.open is gone -- displayRound just calls playRoundAudio(0). **ITS OLD FILES ARE THE ONE PER-PROJECT FALLBACK**, via 'legacy_by': alarm, crowd and flyingcar were per-project from the start, so crowd is Filthy Mermaid's OWN and falling back to it borrows nothing, which is why this row is exempt from the Drop-Ship-only rule. The harness pins that exactly one row carries it, that Dread City (which never had an opening) does not acquire one, and that the fallback actually PLAYS rather than merely being recorded. **A SOUND SITS UNDER THE FRAME IT PLAYS AGAINST, bucketed by the art path.** The first pairing matched a sound's vocabulary FAMILY against the LABELLED art rows -- and the drop rounds, the deaths and the beach frames all render as bands with no label, so nothing could ever attach to them. Filthy Mermaid's crowd on round 0 and its door on round 1 were both in the section at the bottom instead of under the pictures they play against, which is the whole point: it is hard to tell where audio goes if it is not paired with the imagery. A sound belongs to a frame and the frame is in a row, so dropshipAudioPlanRows() finds the row by the art path -- exact, and it works for an unlabelled band. Only the two catch-alls (which span whole stages) and the opening (which has no frame) stay loose, and the harness pins that list so "loose" remains a decision. **IT LIVES IN dropship-audio.php, NOT IN THE PAGE**, because the same lookup written out twice -- once in the page, once in a harness asserting the page -- is a test that passes while the page is broken, which is exactly how the Set button shipped dead. **AND THE ROW ANSWERS WHERE IT WAS PRESSED.** "Set does nothing" was reported twice. The second time it was doing something: the handler wrote the file and said so at the TOP of a page seventy slots long, which from the row you are looking at is indistinguishable from nothing happening. The art slots have reported in place since they got drag and drop; the sound rows were posting the whole page and trusting you to scroll up. Same fetch, same handler, the answer lands on the row -- and the reply carries whether it was a REFUSAL, because a refusal is a question and the reload used to be what carried the answer back to the confirm field. Without JavaScript the form still posts normally. **A ROW IS IDENTIFIED BY ITS FRAME, in the form as well as in the run.** The Set button did nothing at all for a while: the form posted the FAMILY and the resolver compared it against $e['term'], a key events do not have -- they have 'family' -- so every press resolved to null and bailed. Even spelled right the family is ambiguous, since Flamethrower is four rows and Demolition is two. dropshipAudioSlotByArt() takes the art path, the form posts it, and the harness DRIVES that round trip for every row the page renders rather than grepping at it, which is exactly why a dead array key sat there unnoticed. The overwrite warning excludes the row being edited by ART too: by family it would have hidden demo1 from demo2's warning, the one most easily confused with it. **NOTHING IS OVERWRITTEN WITHOUT A WARNING FIRST, and the picker COPIES.** An icon with a new name is renamed, because the old name is then asked for by nobody; a sound chosen from the list is still being asked for under its own name by whatever led you to it, so it is copied and the original is left alone. An upload lands on the DERIVED name whatever the file was called. The first version warned only when some OTHER theme also wanted the file, so replacing a sound nothing else used happened silently -- a file gone by accident is still a file gone, so any overwrite now refuses once and says so, with the claims changing only the wording. Writing a name that does not exist yet goes straight through, because creating a file destroys nothing and asking there trains somebody to click past the question. **AND THE CONFIRM IS ARMED BY A REFUSAL, NOT BY HAVING POSTED:** keyed off the post alone it stayed armed after a SUCCESSFUL write too, so the next thing done to that row would have overwritten unasked. **THE WARNING IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE SHARED FOLDER.** sounds/ serves every theme, so replacing melee.mp3 replaces it for everyone whose word slugs to that name and every event that falls back to it. The icon rename can REFUSE because a rename has somewhere to leave the file; an upload has nowhere, so this refuses once, names exactly who else is listening, and goes ahead on a second press. **PLAY IS A PREVIEW, NOT A REPORT.** It was disabled whenever the run plays nothing, so a row could read "silent, Drop Ship uses demo.mp3" -- naming a file and then refusing to play it. Those are two different questions: what the RUN does is the status text, what you can LISTEN to is anything the row can point at. So it falls back to the candidate and marks itself dashed amber, because a button that plays something the game will not must not look like one that does. It also follows the picker: choose a file in the dropdown and the button plays THAT, cache-busted outright since the whole reason to press it is to hear what is there now -- being handed a list of sounds to choose between and not being able to hear them is the list not doing its job. **MP3 ONLY, SNIFFED FROM THE BYTES:** there is no ffmpeg here, so unlike a picture a sound cannot be converted into the format the slot needs and refusing beats storing something no browser will play. 15 mutations, all caught; one equivalent (the player's empty-caption guard) recorded in the harness, where the assertion worth having turned out to be the opposite case -- a row with EMPTY match text matches every caption, because indexOf('') is 0, and writing that test found the bug. |
| games-drop-ship.md (the other themes, and who can play them) | Which theme a player may enter, and where to buy into it | the Play gate in dropship/dashboard.php, dropshipCollectionUrl()/dropshipCollectionLink() in dropship/dropship-themes.php, the Where to Buy section in dropship/instructions.php, getProjects()/dropshipSoldierCount() in dropship/db.php, dropshipActiveProjectIds() in dropship/dropship-themes.php; dropship-vip-harness.php | **ONE QUALIFYING NFT IS THE GATE, bar Oculus Lounge's Discord role.** Filthy Mermaid was refused outright and the copy did not describe the code: the Patron role (5+ NFTs, Discord 984224392114495539) got "Cannot Play Until Fully Developed" unless the player was one of two hardcoded Discord ids, everybody else got "You Must Have 5+ NFTs to Play" -- a number that would not have admitted them either, since no branch reached the play button -- and a Patron saw BOTH messages, because the first block had no else before the chain after it. Per the owner the Shorty Verse NFTs are expensive and holding one is already the hard part, so the project-3 branches are gone and checkSquadCount() >= 1 is the whole test: a soldier exists only because a qualifying NFT was synced off the wallet, so a deployed squad IS proof of holding. $patron is now set and read by nothing. **OCULUS LOUNGE IS UNTOUCHED** and is a different question -- a Discord role the token does not grant by itself, see games-oculus-lounge.md. Which themes appear in the picker is already themes.active via dropshipActiveProjectIds(), and that list doubles as the allow-list dropship.php validates ?project_id against, so the two cannot diverge. **THE COLLECTION LINK IS THE THEME'S OWN, DERIVED FROM ITS POLICY ID.** instructions.php hardcoded Drop Ship's wayup.io collection for every project except Oculus Lounge, so a Dread City or Filthy Mermaid player following "where to buy" was sent to a different game's NFTs -- and that reader is by definition the one who does not know what the qualifying NFT is.
