One url prefix plus projects.policy_id, which every playable theme already has because Koios is queried with it, so a new theme needs nothing added. SHAPE-CHECKED at 56 hex characters (28 bytes) and lowercased, because it becomes the path of a url handed to a player; anything else renders nothing rather than a dead collection page. On the Play refusal it is gated on dropshipSoldierCount(), not checkSquadCount(): that refusal also catches somebody who owns a squad and has not deployed it, and sending them shopping is the wrong answer. 17 mutations, all caught. |
| games-drop-ship.md (soldier card) | The Rank line on a soldier card | getSoldiers() in dropship/db.php, DROPSHIP_DEFAULT_RANK + the 'Ranks' vocab group in dropship/dropship-themes.php and dropship-theme-vocab.php. Pinned by dropship-themes-harness.php. **EVERY THEME SHOWED "Neo Miami Citizen"**, Oculus Lounge's word. THREE causes and fixing any one would have left it wrong: (1) the card printed rank RAW while printing armor and gear through evaluateText() on the next line; (2) db.php's sync stored the literal for EVERY collection; (3) dashboard.php chose it from a PROJECT-ID LADDER -- 2 and 3 got "Henchmen", 4 got "Neo Miami Citizen", and anything else left $rank UNDEFINED, so a new theme got whatever the previous loop iteration held. FOURTH ladder of that shape pulled out this week. The canonical is DROPSHIP_DEFAULT_RANK ('Soldier'), the game's own noun, and Oculus Lounge overrides it -- which is the right way round: a theme-specific term as the base string makes every OTHER theme override a word belonging to a game it has nothing to do with. Lives in dropship-themes.php because it is a vocabulary default (it was in dropship-grunts.php first, which the theme harness does not load). Drop Ship's own soldiers carry real ranks from metadata and are untouched. EXISTING ROWS KEEP THE OLD STRING -- the sync only UPDATEs armor, gear and level -- so normalising them is a one-off UPDATE, in dropship-themes-schema.md. Harness note: a blanket search for "Henchmen" in dashboard.php catches the PLAYER's Discord-role rank, which is a different thing; the check targets `$rank = "..."` only. |
| (operator) | Adding and editing a Drop Ship theme | dropship/dropship-themes-schema.md (manual CREATE), dropship/dropship-themes.php, the admin at dropship/admin-themes.php, the hook in evaluateText() in dropship/db.php; dropship-themes-harness.php | **WHAT BLOCKED NEW THEMES WAS NEVER THE ART.** evaluateText() was a 202-line if/else chain on project_id holding 156 replacement lines, so every reskin was a code change and a deploy. The MECHANISM was always fine: an EXACT whole-string lookup, falling through to a SUBSTRING pass for descriptions only. Both now come out of theme_text, and the split is load-bearing -- collapsing them either breaks whole-word labels ("Soldiers of fortune" must NOT become "Citizens of fortune") or stops sentences rewriting. Exact wins outright or a label containing a description word gets rewritten twice. **ADDITIVE AND SILENT UNTIL USED:** every lookup returns null -- and evaluateText() falls through to the untouched chain -- when the tables are missing OR the theme has no rows. That is what makes deploying the schema a no-op; the chain MUST NOT be deleted, or an empty table blanks every label on the site. A theme goes live when somebody saves one and reverts by deleting its rows. **CACHED PER REQUEST, NOT OPTIONAL:** evaluateText() runs hundreds of times rendering one dashboard, so a query per call would be slower than the chain it replaces and the whole change a regression. **THE SLUG IS STORED, NEVER DERIVED:** dropship.php built the image folder from the project NAME, so a rename silently moved the entire art directory; dropshipThemeSlug() also strips anything that could leave the folder, since the slug is concatenated into a path. **THE ADMIN NEVER WRITES TO projects**, because the collection a theme draws from is ALREADY data there (projects.policy_id) and several themes legitimately share one collection -- Dread City and Filthy Mermaid both sit on Shorty Verse, Oculus Lounge on Disco Solaris, and only Drop Ship owns its own. Presentation here, ownership there. Text pairs are REPLACED WHOLESALE on save, because the form posts the complete set and merging would make deleting a row impossible; a partial write says so rather than reporting a clean save. **THE ADMIN IS A CHECKLIST, NOT A BLANK BOX.** dropship-theme-vocab.php holds the UNION OF WHAT THE THREE EXISTING THEMES ACTUALLY OVERRODE, generated by RUNNING the chain rather than trawled from source, and the form prefills every one. Without it, reworking a theme meant guessing which words mattered or reading the game until you found them all. 'themes' records how many of the three bothered with a term, a usable proxy for how load-bearing it is. **ONE BOX PER THING, NOT PER STRING.** The game names one thing up to four ways -- the bare tier on a soldier card ('Heavy'), the item in the shop ('Heavy Armor'), the word mid-sentence in a results line, and the icon file icons/heavy-armor.png -- and all four ALWAYS take the same answer: 46 of 46 exact/description pairs across the live themes are byte-identical and 12 of 12 X / 'X Armor' pairs agree. So 111 terms are grouped into 58 families and one box fills all of them; four boxes that must agree are four boxes that can drift. **THERE IS NO DESCRIPTIONS GROUP** -- exact and description are match TYPES, not categories, every term in that group was the same thing as one listed above it, and grouping by how a term is matched told you nothing about what you were renaming. **AN ICON NAME IS DERIVED AND STORED NOWHERE.** It is the theme's own word made into a filename, worked out in dropshipThemeText() on the way out, so there is no icon row and no icon field. It was briefly both, which is the same fact in two places free to disagree -- and the live data had already drifted: 13 of 64 stored icon names no longer matched the word beside them, so renaming the Medkit to "Sexy Nurse" left the shop asking for nurse.png with nothing on screen saying why the picture was wrong. **THE LEGACY CHAIN DERIVES THE SAME WAY**, which removed 39 more lines that could disagree with the word directly above them. One rule, both paths, so a renamed file is right either side of a migration. **THE FILE IS MANAGED ON THE ART SCREEN**, beside the art for the same thing. Two icon facts came out of MEASUREMENT and a reasonable guess gets both wrong: 'dropship' is Super Soldier's icon, not Drop Ship's (both themes send it to 'vip', as they do 'supersoldier'), and the armor icon is 'heavy-armor', never 'heavy' -- which had Armor reporting zero icons. **NO FAMILY LABEL MAY ALSO BE AN ICON TERM** or the derivation recurses; asserted over the whole vocabulary. **DROP SHIP HAS NO ICON:** the chain carried a 'drop-ship' entry but nothing has ever rendered icons/drop-ship.png -- every other use of that string is the art FOLDER slug -- so project 1 had been asking for a 404 since long before any of this, and claiming it put a permanently missing slot on the art page demanding three files nobody uses. Found by curl-checking all 65 names the four projects ask for, not by reading. All 62 that remain are 200. **THERE IS NO KEEP COLUMN.** It stored a decision as a row whose replacement equalled its find, to tell "not looked at yet" from "uses the Drop Ship word on purpose" -- but evaluateText() already falls through to the original when it finds no row, so that was a no-op row and a checkbox on every line of the form, bought with nothing. Blank means the Drop Ship word. **NO LABEL COLUMNS EITHER.** currency, squad_label and character_label held the same three words the wording owns (SCRIP, Super Soldier, Troops); dashboard.php read the COLUMN, so renaming SCRIP in the wording changed nothing there and the admin never said why. They are rows now and dashboard.php reads them through evaluateText() like every other label. **trait_source IS NOT ONE OF THEM, and removing it was a mistake worth remembering: TWO DIFFERENT THINGS WERE CALLED THE TRAIT SOURCE.** The column picks between two real NFT-loading pipelines -- 'koios' reads the player's connected wallets live through account_utxos, 'skulliance' skips that round trip and reads Skulliance's already-synced nfts table. $source in dropship-traitdrop.php is the other one: the key naming which GAME a Discord trait drop came from, which genuinely IS the slug with the hyphens out ('oculus-lounge' -> 'oculuslounge'), derived by dropshipDropAward() so a new theme is credited by being registered in dhcfighters-config.php rather than by editing that file, falling back to Drop Ship when unregistered (checked AFTER dhcfighters-lib.php loads, or it always falls back). Deleting the column on the second one's reasoning left dashboard.php choosing the pipeline on a hardcoded project_id == 4 -- exactly what the column existed to replace. dropshipThemeTraitSource() reads it, anything unrecognised is 'koios' (an unknown pipeline must not silently become the one that reads a different collection), and with no theme row project 4 still gets 'skulliance' so an unseeded site is unchanged. **THE POLICY HAD TO MOVE TOO:** dropshipSyncOculusLounge() hardcoded Disco Solaris' policy id, so the setting would have been a lie for any other theme -- quietly syncing somebody else's collection. It takes the policy from projects.policy_id via the caller now and is called dropshipSyncFromSkulliance(), because a function named after one theme is what stopped a second theme choosing it. 'gif' is deliberately absent from the vocabulary: the extension is a column. **THE THEME CAN OVERRIDE THE PAN.** themes.ken_burns is 'auto' (the format decides, which is the original rule and where every theme starts), 'on' or 'off'. The format is a good guess and not an answer: a still whose art is already a composed scene does not want drifting, and a gif whose frames barely move might. Only the two literals override; anything else -- a blank, a value from a newer admin -- falls through to the format rule, and normalising before that comparison was dead code that could not change the outcome. **MEDIA EXTENSION DECIDES THE MOTION.** themes.extension is what a theme's round art is stored as, and it picks the treatment: a gif animates itself and gets nothing laid over it, a still (png) gets the Ken Burns pan -- .image-wrap in dist/flexbox.css, a 40s drift. That pan used to be switched on for project 4 and nothing else, which was correct only BY COINCIDENCE: Oculus Lounge is simply the theme that happens to use stills. A new png theme would have rendered a dead frame and a gif theme carrying project 4's id would have had a pan dragged across its own animation. dropshipMediaIsStill() asks through evaluateText('gif'), which is the one accessor that answers from the theme row when there is one and from the built-in chain when there is not, so it works either side of a migration. Called "media" and not "animation" because half the point is that one of the two formats does not animate. **STAGE ART RUNS SHUFFLED OR IN ORDER, PER THEME.** verifyRandom() picks a frame from the scene's set and never repeats the last one, which suits frames that are interchangeable takes on "you survived the beach" and reads as the story jumping about when they are a sequence. themes.stage_order defaults to 'random', so nothing changes for a theme that has not chosen. **LINEAR WRAPS AND CLAMPS:** every scene seeds "previous" with its LAST frame, so the first step wraps round to 1, and the bunker shares one counter across two ranges (1-6 early, 7-12 late) so stepping out of the first would land on a frame the second does not own -- anything outside the range restarts at its first. Read ONCE per round and passed to all SEVEN stage branches, which is one more than it looks: the bunker has two. **THE ART PANEL MANAGES BOTH SETS.** A still theme ships two -- the one its extension names and the gif set the Animation switch turns on -- and this page could only ever see the first, so the second had to be FTPed by hand, which is the gap the panel exists to close. The tabs show each set's count so the switch says what it leads to. **IT IS NOT THE PLAYER'S SWITCH:** that one is gated on the files existing, this one must work when the set is EMPTY or there is no way to create it. The chosen format rides every form, or an upload lands in the set you were not looking at, and it is never taken off the request as-is because it builds the manifest and the manifest becomes filesystem paths. **THE GIF PROBES COME FROM THE MANIFEST, NOT A GUESS:** the first cut hardcoded images/drop/1.gif, a path that has never existed -- that folder holds drop.gif and team.gif, not numbered frames -- so the check was false for every theme and the switch never appeared at all. The harness had guessed the same way, so it agreed with the bug. First and last gif in the manifest, so a half-uploaded set does not read as finished, and .gif only because the manifest carries a .jpg intro frame in every format. **ANIMATION IS A PLAYER TOGGLE, WHICH IS WHAT 3D ACTUALLY WAS** -- and it keeps 3D's own icon, because it is the same control. **TWO EXTENSIONS, NOT ONE:** dropshipThemeMediaExtension() is what the THEME is, dropshipMediaExtension() is what is rendering RIGHT NOW. Conflating them made the switch hide itself on first use -- it was shown only when the extension was not 'gif', and turning it on makes the resolved extension 'gif' -- leaving no way back to the stills. "Can this be animated" is a property of the theme; "is it animated" is a property of the session. themes.extension records what a theme is SET to, not what it HAS: Oculus Lounge ships both sets, and retiring 3D removed the only way to see the animated one. The switch is offered ONLY when it would do something -- the theme resolves to a still AND dropshipThemeHasGifArt() finds the files -- so it can never point a theme at art it does not have, and a leftover session flag cannot either, because the preference is re-gated on the files every time it is read. TWO PROBES, not one, so a single stray gif in an otherwise still set does not advertise the theme as animated. Only the literal string 'true' counts; the value decides which FILES the run asks for, and the neighbouring toggle-audio.php writes $_GET straight into the session, which this deliberately does not copy. THE CLIENT RELOADS, unlike the audio toggle: audio is a property of elements already on the page, this decides what the SERVER renders -- and it waits for the write to land first or the reload races it and comes back unchanged. The Ken Burns pan follows automatically, since dropshipMediaIsStill() asks the same resolver. **3D IS RETIRED** -- the session flag, the ajax endpoint, the checkbox, the conditional 'gif' => 'png', and two blanket str_replace passes that rewrote the word anywhere in a stored replay, not just in a file extension. **THE SEED (dropship-themes-seed.sql) WAS GENERATED BY RUNNING THE CHAIN, NOT READING IT**, and verified key by key: 261 keys across projects 2, 3 and 4 produce identical output through the chain and through the seeded dictionaries. Project 1 is the base and is deliberately not seeded. Re-verify by comparison, never by eye, if the chain is edited. **TWO HARNESS BUGS WORTH REMEMBERING:** a vocabulary lookup keyed on find alone let the description 'Medkit' overwrite the exact one so the icon-flag check tested the wrong row; and earlier, two python edits in one block where the second wrote from the unmodified string silently discarded the first, so a whole section of assertions was never running. Both were found by mutation, not by reading. A third: `perl -0pi -e 's/\Q...\E/.../'` STILL INTERPOLATES $variables inside \Q, so every mutation pattern containing a PHP variable silently became an empty pattern and eight mutations reported as caught when none had been applied. Mutate with a literal string replace that asserts it matched exactly once. **active IS WHAT PUTS A THEME IN FRONT OF PLAYERS.** It was written by the admin and read by nothing -- which projects a player saw was two names hardcoded in getProjects() -- so launching a theme meant a code edit and the tickbox was decoration. dropshipActiveProjectIds() makes it real. **ONE LIST, TWO DOORS:** getProjects() builds the dashboard dropdown AND is what dropship.php validates ?project_id against before that value reaches SQL, so the flag has to drive both or a hidden theme stays reachable by URL. It returns NULL -- and the old exclusion stands -- for no table, no themes, or none active; the first two keep it invisible until a theme is saved, the third is a judgement that an empty picker reads as an outage rather than a choice. Not cached, because a static would hand admin-themes.php the list from before the save it just did. **THE SEED REPRODUCES TODAY'S BEHAVIOUR EXACTLY** -- active on 1 and 4, which is precisely what the name exclusion already allowed -- and that equivalence is asserted, because re-seeding otherwise silently opens a work-in-progress theme or shuts a live one. **A BUFFERED CAPTURE IS A PUBLIC CHANNEL FOR PHP WARNINGS.** checkLeaderboard() and checkPlayerItems() PRINT, so putting their output in a Discord embed means buffering them -- and display_errors is on platform-wide, so anything raised while that buffer is open is posted with the board. A real Game Over post went out carrying `Undefined array key "game_id" in /home/jeremiah/.../db.php on line 2220`, absolute server path and all, into a channel players read. dropshipCapture() turns display_errors off for the duration AND scrubs anything shaped like a PHP diagnostic from the result, including the html_errors form a line-based pass cannot see -- two layers, because an error raised before the helper is reached would otherwise sail through. Errors still reach the error log; they are kept out of the EMBED, not silenced. Every Discord capture goes through it; a bare ob_get_contents() feeding an embed is the bug returning. **THE THEME CHECK EXISTS BECAUSE EVERY ONE OF THESE FAILS AS SILENCE.** No NFTs, no game, no Discord post -- all three look identical from outside, a theme that simply does not work, and Filthy Mermaid hit three in a row, each found only by reading source. dropshipNftProbe() reports every step the dashboard takes and the first zero is the answer; it is read-only, behind a GET, checks BOTH NFT sources whichever the theme is set to, and never prints the webhook URL -- only whether there is one. It asks the games table DIRECTLY rather than through checkGame(), which WRITES the session game id as a side effect. getWebhook() is called behind function_exists() because it lives in a credentials file that is not in the repo. **WHICH GAME PAYS WHICH CURRENCY IS ONE MAP, ON THE SKULLIANCE SIDE.** dropshipRewardProjects() in the root db.php keys Drop Ship's project id to the Skulliance project whose points the weekly prizes credit (1, 2, 3 -> DREAD; 4 -> MOON). Drop Ship posts for EVERY game and that map decides -- the gate was `== 1 || == 4`, so activating a theme meant editing a second place, and Filthy Mermaid was given a weekly cycle without one and so ran every week paying nobody. An unmapped game pays nothing rather than falling through with $project_id undefined and crediting project 0. **THE TARGET IS CHOSEN THERE, NEVER POSTED, AND THE REASON MATTERS: THAT ENDPOINT HAS NO AUTHENTICATION.** It runs at db.php include time and db.php is on every page, so an unsigned POST of discord_id/rank/project_id to any url credits a balance, repeatably. It predates this work and is written down rather than left to be rediscovered; the fix is a shared secret between the two apps, which is a deploy change. Until then the map keeps the blast radius at two currencies instead of every project on the platform. **A THEME WITH NO GAME CANNOT BE PLAYED, AND ONLY THE CRON MAKES ONE.** automate.php ends the week's game, posts the final board and opens the next; it listed projects 1 and 4, so a newly activated theme had no cycle at all and simply no active game. It takes `project_id=N` now -- giving one theme a game mid-week used to mean running the whole cron, which ENDS whatever the other themes were in the middle of. It also skips the end-of-game half when checkGame() finds none: that half assumed a game always existed, so deactivateGame() would UPDATE ... WHERE id='' after pointlessly logging balances and resetting soldiers, and Discord would get a Game Over post with an empty board for a game that never ran -- precisely the state a theme is in the first time it is switched on. **A COLLECTION WITH NO TRAIT MAPPING GETS A STABLE SPREAD, NOT Base/None.** discosolarisArmorTier()/discosolarisGear() read Disco Solaris trait NAMES, so over any other collection every lookup misses and every soldier comes out identical -- Filthy Mermaid synced 55 soldiers all "Boxer Briefs / None", which reads as the stat filter being broken when the data is simply uniform. dropshipStatsFromAssetName() fills the gap, chosen per NFT by dropshipHasDiscosolarisTraits() so a real mapping still wins wherever the traits exist. **crc32, NOT rand():** this path UPDATEs armor and gear on every sync, so a random pick would reshuffle everybody's squad each time a page triggered one -- the Koios path gets away with rand() only because it runs for soldiers that do not exist yet. Gear uses a SALTED second hash or every Heavy would carry a Medkit. It is a placeholder and distributes evenly rather than meaning anything; a theme that wants stats from its own traits needs its own mapping. **THREE FUNCTIONS WERE WRITTEN FOR ONE THEME AND CARRIED ITS POLICY AS A LITERAL** -- the Skulliance sync, the local-art lookup and the bulk pre-cache. Each therefore served Oculus Lounge and silently returned nothing for anybody else, which is why Filthy Mermaid loaded soldiers with no pictures while the art sat cached on Skulliance the whole time (project 2, collection 9, HTTP 200). All three take the policy now, from projects.policy_id via the caller, and the local-art lookup REFUSES rather than guesses when it has none -- falling back to a literal is how this failed quietly in the first place. The render gate was `project_id == 4`; it is `!= 1` now, because Drop Ship's own art is genuinely local to it and everything else is a Skulliance cache. The pre-cache takes a policy on the command line, so caching a new theme's art is a command rather than an edit and a deploy. **THE ADMIN SHOWS projects.policy_id, because every NFT path runs through it.** The Koios path filters the wallet's tokens by it and the Skulliance path queries collections.policy with it, so a project with none set loads nothing on EITHER setting -- silently, with no error, just an empty barracks and a theme that looks broken for no visible reason. Filthy Mermaid hit exactly that, and BOTH PATHS FAILING TOGETHER IS THE TELL: they share only this one input. dropshipProjectPolicy() takes the id rather than reading the session like getProjectPolicyId(), because the admin is looking at a project it is not playing. **EVERY SEEDED THEME MUST BE IN DHCF_GAMES, and the harness derives that from the seed rather than listing it.** An unregistered key falls back to 'dropship', so a Dread City drop posted "Dropped from Drop Ship" -- silently and plausibly, which is the worst way for it to fail. I registered filthy-mermaid by hand and forgot dread-city; a list in the harness would have had the same gap as the registry, so it walks the seed's own theme rows and checks each slug-minus-hyphens resolves and has a floor of 0. **THE BUILD GUIDE SPEAKS THE THEME'S LANGUAGE:** dropshipSquadAdvice() was the one place still printing Drop Ship nouns, so Filthy Mermaid read "Next: a soldier in Base armor" under a roster of Perverts in Boxer Briefs. Tier names, gear names, 'Super Soldier' and the singular 'soldier' all go through dropshipWord(), which is evaluateText() behind a function_exists guard because this file is also loaded by harnesses with no database. 'soldier' is a vocabulary term in its own right -- lowercase, because it only appears mid-sentence, and distinct from the plural 'Troops' the dashboard shows. **THE ADMIN'S PROJECT PICKER IS NOT getProjects().** That is the PLAYER-facing list and it excludes Filthy Mermaid and Dread City BY NAME, so the theme admin could not select the two themes most in need of editing. It cannot simply be widened: dropship.php validates ?project_id against it before that value is interpolated into SQL by getProjectName() and getProjectPolicyId(), so loosening it would un-hide work in progress AND weaken an injection guard. dropshipAdminProjects() answers the other question and always returns an array -- getProjects() returns NULL on an empty table, which the foreach after it would warn on with display_errors on. The harness stub HONOURS THE WHERE CLAUSE, because a stub that returned every row regardless passed happily with the exclusion copied back into the admin query. **THE ADMIN GATE IS DROP SHIP USER 1, plus the Discord ids dashboard.php already gates its game controls on.** user_id is sound as a key here even though it looks like it should not be: the Drop Ship users table carries NO project_id, checkUser() matches on discord_id alone and there is one row per person for the whole app, so an id is a stable identity rather than a per-project counter meaning somebody different on each theme. ctype_digit runs BEFORE the cast because PHP reads (int)'1x' as 1, which would admit anything starting with the digit -- found by mutation testing, not by reading. **INHERITED EXPOSURE, NOT INTRODUCED:** $_SESSION is restored from the client-held SessionCookie JSON, which carries no signature, so any session-derived admin check on either app is forgeable by editing that cookie. The pre-existing discord_id gate has the identical property, so this adds no new surface -- but it is the reason not to put anything destructive behind this gate until SessionCookie is signed. 8 mutations, 7 caught; the str_contains() guard before the description pass is a performance guard only and is an equivalent mutant. The family rework added 26 more and the derived-icon rework 5 more, all caught. |
| (operator) | Creating a project for a brand new game | dropshipProjectCreate() in dropship/dropship-themes.php, the New project form in dropship/admin-themes.php. Pinned by dropship-themes-harness.php. A theme hangs off a `projects` row and a new game has none, which was the last thing that still needed a database client. admin-themes.php's header says it never edits `projects` -- still true of EDITING, since several themes share one collection and a theme must not own it; CREATING is the case that boundary did not cover. **A PROJECT ON ITS OWN REACHES NOBODY:** getProjects() is built from dropshipActiveProjectIds(), which lists only projects with an ACTIVE theme, and that is also the allow-list ?project_id= is validated against -- so a new project is invisible to players, the picker and the URL until a theme is saved and ticked. The success message SAYS so, and the harness pins that, because an admin who does not know it has just made something they think is public. **THE COLUMNS ARE INSPECTED, NOT ASSUMED** -- `projects` predates all of this, has no schema written down, and nobody writing the form could see the live table, so it runs SHOW COLUMNS, fills name and policy_id, and refuses by NAMING any other NOT NULL column with no default instead of dying on a MySQL error. A failed insert reports $conn->error rather than shrugging. policy_id may be blank (collection not minted yet) but is otherwise 56 hex characters, since it is interpolated into every NFT lookup. Its own POST, handled BEFORE the theme save and excluded from it (the theme form was never filled in), then redirected so a refresh cannot make two. **AND IT RUNS BEFORE `include 'header.php'`** -- it shipped below it, so the Location came out as "Cannot modify header information" and the redirect never happened: the project WAS created and the admin was left on a page that said nothing about it. header.php is the first include that writes, everything above emits nothing, and the harness pins the positions. The page's own dropshipThemeAdmin() gate renders a styled refusal so it runs AFTER header.php, too late to stop a write -- the write carries its own gate. |
| games-drop-ship.md (the other themes) | Choosing which of the four games to play | The Select Game button and #dsgame picker in dropship/dashboard.php, built from getProjects($conn) in dropship/db.php, landing on ?project_id= which dropship/dropship.php validates against that SAME list. **WAS A <select> LABELLED "Select Project"** -- two clicks to a list of four words, and a word is the worst way to tell somebody Dread City is not the game they just finished. "Game" not "Theme" on the button, deliberately: they share an engine but a control called Change Theme invites the assumption that it is a reskin of the one you have played. THREE THINGS THAT WILL BITE: (1) the picker is a child of <body>, NOT of the stats panel its button sits in -- .side would clip a fixed overlay -- and its CSS is therefore unscoped #dsgame ids, because a selector naming an ancestor the element does not have is a stylesheet that silently does nothing (the Theme Art lightbox shipped exactly that). (2) flexbox.css sets `a {text-align:center; color:#00c8a0}` GLOBALLY, so .dsgame-tile restates both or the captions centre themselves and go teal. (3) The hero jpgs are 85KB to 940KB each and there are four, so they are data-src and swapped on first open -- loading="lazy" does not help, a hidden image is fetched at the browser's discretion. Tiles are plain anchors, so no JS is needed to switch and a tile can be opened in a new tab; selectProject() and its hidden form are gone, and so is the line that pre-set the dropdown's value, which would have thrown on a null the moment the <select> went and killed the scripts after it. Measured 2x2 at 400 and 360px, one column under 360px, no sideways scroll. |
| (operator) | Discord webhook credentials, and why a wrong name is invisible | webhooks.php (platform channels), dropship/webhooks.php (per-project), credentials/webhooks_credentials.php in BOTH apps (not in the repo). Pinned by webhook-names-harness.php. **EVERY BRANCH GUARDS WITH function_exists(), SO A MISNAMED CREDENTIAL IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM AN ABSENT ONE** -- nothing posts, nothing errors, a channel just goes quiet. That guard is correct (a player earning a trait must not see a fatal because a webhook is unwired) and it is exactly why the names need pinning. The harness runs each case in its OWN PROCESS, because the question is which functions EXIST and one process cannot both declare and not declare them. It also BASELINES the nineteen historically unguarded calls: that list may shrink, never grow -- asserting none exist would claim a contract that was never true. **dropshipGetWebhook($project_id) HAS NOW LANDED** in Drop Ship's credentials, which closes the getWebhook() collision at its source (see dropship/dropship-traitdrop.php) and means Drop Ship trait drops announce for the first time. The DHCF_NO_NOTIFY gate STAYS: credentials are per-server, so a fresh deploy or a restored backup can arrive without it and the answer then must be a quiet drop, not a dead play page. A Skulliance-prefixed alias for the dhcfighters channel was supported briefly and removed once that landed -- a second accepted spelling with nothing behind it is a second thing to keep right. NOTE the platform's 'dhcfighters' channel is a SEPARATE credential (getDHCFightersWebhook) from Drop Ship's per-project one; having one does not configure the other. |
| (operator) | Turning a new Drop Ship theme on end to end | THREE lists, down from four, and the harnesses pin that they agree because every one of them has been the thing somebody forgot: dropshipRewardTokens() in dropship/dropship-themes.php (drop-ship project -> the CURRENCY SYMBOL, 5 => MAXI), dropshipRewardProjects() in db.php (drop-ship project -> the SKULLIANCE project whose balance is credited, 5 => 9 for Maxingo), and DHCF_GAMES in dhcfighters-config.php (trait drops). **TWO OF THE FOUR WERE COLLAPSED INTO THE FIRST.** $all in dropship/automate.php is now array_keys(dropshipRewardTokens()) and $ds_pays in dropship/instructions.php is gone, both reading the one map -- they were separate copies of 'which themes exist' and both had drifted: automate.php had no branch for theme 5, so a DHC2 Fighters game would have been created called "1K, 500, 250 DREAD" and announced that way to players credited MAXI, and instructions.php had the right map behind a gate reading project_id == 1 || == 4, so Dread City, Filthy Mermaid and DHC2 Fighters all told their players the top three win SCRIP. **THE TWO REMAINING MAPS CANNOT BE ONE:** they live in different apps with separate databases and no shared include, and hold different facts. dropship-themes-harness.php asserts they cover the same theme ids, that themes paying one Skulliance project name one currency, and that the 1,000/500/250 the page advertises are the amounts the handler in db.php actually credits -- read back out of that file, not restated. Filthy Mermaid once shipped a weekly cycle that paid nobody because only one list was updated, which is why they are cross-checked rather than listed. **THE PRIZE NAME IS BUILT, NOT TYPED:** dropshipRewardPrizeName() composes "1K, 500, 250 <TOKEN>" and automate() REFUSES to open a game for a theme with no currency rather than naming it after the old DREAD default. **THE TRAIT-DROP KEY IS THE SLUG WITH HYPHENS REMOVED** ('dhc2-fighters' -> 'dhc2fighters'), derived by dropshipDropAward(), and getting it wrong FAILS SILENTLY -- an unregistered key falls back to 'dropship', so the drop still lands and simply credits the wrong game. Confirm the slug, never guess it. The harness derives the key through the real dropshipThemeSlug() rather than restating it. x_tag is taken from the account itself (x.com/MMAXI404), not inferred from a name: an @ in a shared post tags whoever actually holds the handle. The harness checks handle SHAPE only -- nothing offline can tell whether an account exists. |
| games-drop-ship.md (saved squads) | Saving a squad, fielding it in one press, breaking it up | dropship/dropship-squads.php, dropship/dropship-squads-schema.md (two tables, run by hand), the panel + POST handlers in dropship/dashboard.php, the NOT IN clause in getSoldiers(). Pinned by dropship-squads-harness.php. **A SQUAD LIST IS READ FROM `squads` ALONE.** Joining it against the soldiers you own is the obvious way to build the screen and it is the bug: a squad whose members are all sold would vanish while its rows sat in the table, so the player could neither see it nor delete it. Members resolve in a second pass to ready/fielded/fallen/sold/gone. Keyed on soldiers.id because updateSoldiers() does NOT delete a sold soldier -- it sets user_id = 0 and re-associates what you still hold -- so the row survives and loss is detectable rather than dangling. **FALLEN IS NOT DAMAGE, IT IS TUESDAY.** killSquad() kills the field after every run and the only revival is resetSoldiers(), project-wide, on game deactivation (resurrectSquad() exists and is called by NOTHING), so a squad fields once per GAME and reads as fallen for most of the week. The harness REFUSES the words missing, no longer, error, problem and broken in that sentence. **NO NAMES:** a squad is identified by dropshipSquadPower() from the same dropshipThreshold() the run rolls against, and ordered strongest first. It is the squad's ARMOUR only -- suits are session items and OVERRIDE armour in that chain, so folding one in reports the same number for every squad, which is exactly when ordering by strength has to work. Hence the wording "from armor". dropshipSquadSave() takes ONLY $conn: the members are whoever is on the field, read from the database, never a list the browser sends. Also fixed here: deploySoldier() never checked ownership (its own comment said so), so a guessed soldier_id moved another player's soldier; scoped in the statement, not in a SELECT above it. **A SOLDIER BELONGS TO ONE SQUAD**, enforced in dropshipSquadSave(). Found by dropship-squads-walk-harness.php, which drives the real save/deploy/disperse through 6000 random sequences of 11 actions and checks the invariants after EVERY step: deploy a squad, withdraw one member, field somebody else in the gap and save, and three soldiers were in two squads with neither deployable while the other was out and nothing saying why. The FIRST fix had a second hole the walk found in eight steps -- `count($clash) !== count($ids)` only fired when SOME of the field was spoken for, so save {D,E}, withdraw D, save again slipped {E} through as not-a-duplicate. A SUBSET IS NOT A DUPLICATE, and the clash check must run AFTER the duplicate check (so re-saving the same four says "already saved") but must fire on ANY clash. dropshipSquadShared() stays as the net for a row this code did not create, and the walk asserts both halves. MEMBERS RENDER HEAVIEST ARMOUR FIRST (dropshipSquadOrderMembers), the order dropshipThreshold() builds the number in; display only, slot keeps the saved order, and the walk checks the ORDER OF WHAT dropshipSquadsAll() RETURNS rather than the sorter in isolation -- removing the call left the sorter passing its own tests while every squad on the page was unsorted. One equivalent mutant is documented and not chased: the tie-break returning 0, since PHP's sort has been stable since 8.0. **THE DEPLOYED SQUAD'S ORDER** is dropshipFieldOrder(): what the player added themselves first in the order they added it, then anything deployed as a saved squad in armour order. Needs `soldiers.active_at` (one more ALTER, in the schema doc), optional at runtime the same way `ko` is in the Arena -- deploySoldier() and dropshipSquadDeploy() both RETRY without it, because naming a column that does not exist fails a statement rather than degrading it and losing the ordering beats losing the deployment. Cleared on withdraw, so a soldier redeployed later joins the end of the row. Which group a soldier is in is DERIVED, not stored: a soldier in a saved squad has no individual Deploy button, so if it is deployed it got there through Deploy squad. **WITHDRAW ALL** (dropshipWithdrawAll) exists for batch building -- build, save, clear, build the next -- because four individual Withdraws per squad is sixteen presses to save four. It empties the deployed squad and touches no saved squad. **VOCABULARY: THE DEPLOYED SET IS "YOUR SQUAD", NEVER "THE FIELD".** deploySoldier() has always said "Your squad is full."; "the field" was a second name for a thing already named, and the harness scans the comment-stripped source for the phrase. Two harness bugs of my own in that one check: pairing quotes to extract string literals mis-pairs on the first apostrophe inside a double-quoted string (307 literals found, not one of them the offender), and the scan read $src before it was assigned, so it ran on an empty string and passed on anything -- the same shape as the dhcarena check that referenced $dbsrc6 early. Also: no arithmetic sentences. "There is only room for 0 more" was the giveaway; zero gets its own wording. AVERAGE DEPTH PER SQUAD is deferred, not forgotten -- see the schema doc for the join and the one trap in it. |
| (chrome) | Drop Ship's visual language, and why it now matches DHC | dropship/dist/flexbox.css (--ds-* tokens at the top), renderDeployButton() and renderBuyButton() in dropship/db.php. **dropship/dist/flexbox.css IS NOT THE PLATFORM'S** -- dist/flexbox.css at the repo root is a different 94KB file, and only dropship/* links this 26KB one, so the blast radius is Drop Ship alone. Checked before editing. THE COLOURS WERE ALREADY RIGHT: #0a1929/#00c8a0/#e8eaed are exactly dhcarena.php's --panel/--ochre/--bone, so what read as amateurish was never the palette, it was SHAPE. Three things, all of them repeated: radii from 0.5rem to 2.5rem (DHC uses 3px), `box-shadow: 0 Npx #007a62` plus a translateY on :active under every button (the skeuomorphic pressed-key trick), and ONE `drop-shadow(5px 5px 5px #202225)` pasted under twelve different elements. Now: --ds-r is 3px and used by all 14 structural radii; the four 3D shadows are flat fills that darken on hover; all 24 drop-shadow lines are gone and panels carry a 1px --ds-line hairline instead. ROUND ON PURPOSE AND LEFT ALONE: .rounded (the toggle pill, 25px), .rounded-full, .slider.rounded + :before, .avatar -- a pill at 3px is not a pill, so the radius pass is block-aware rather than a blanket swap. .small-button went from 3px/10px at 12px type (a 22px tap target) to 34px tall, and on a card it is a full-width bar at the foot via `.nft form{margin-top:auto}` with .nft-data a flex column so bars line up across cards of different heights. WITHDRAW IS .secondary (outline), and `.nft-data:has(.secondary)` marks the deployed card -- :has() rather than a class because the card and the button are rendered by different functions; a browser without it loses the highlight and nothing else. ONE FRAME ONLY: .nft is the 25%-wide layout cell and .nft-data is the visible box inset by its own 10px margin, so a border on both draws two rectangles 10px apart, which is what putting it on .nft first produced. Also added the first focus-visible style in the app. Compared before/after against the real stylesheet, not a stub. |
| (chrome) | Drop Ship's tab icon | lib/favicon-head.php, included by dropship/header.php as `../lib/`. Pinned by favicon-harness.php, whose $pages list now carries dropship/header.php. Drop Ship had NO icon at all -- it is a separate app with its own header.php, which is the exact case lib/favicon-head.php's own header names ("every page that skips header.php had no icon at all"), and every check in favicon-harness keyed on the root-relative `__DIR__ . '/lib/...'` spelling so it could not see a page one directory down. The list is a map now, page => include prefix. Paths stay root-relative (/staking/pwa/...) so they resolve from /staking/dropship/ and do not pin one of the two hosts the login cookie cannot share. Theme-agnostic on purpose: a per-theme favicon is five more files to FTP and one more thing to be missing. |
| (chrome) | The barracks grid, the ledger rows, and the picker's art | dropship/dist/flexbox.css (.nfts, .credit/.debit/.winner), the #dsgame tile rules in dropship/dashboard.php. Pinned by dropship-themes-harness.php. **THE BARRACKS WAS `float:left; width:25%`** -- a floated box catches on the tallest thing to its left, so a collection whose cards differ in height (a deceased soldier has a Status line and no Deploy bar) left holes in the second row and pushed cards out of column. It is a grid now: 4 columns, 2 under 1200px, 1 under 700px, the same breakpoints the float widths used. That also makes the Deploy bars line up, because a stretched grid item gives .nft-data a height to fill and margin-top:auto on the form something to push against -- and .nft-data uses flex:1, NOT height:100%, because the box carries a 10px margin and 100%+margin overflows the cell. **THE LEDGER ROWS WERE THREE SATURATED FILLS** (#256839, #243e66, #502466) painted edge to edge, one of them a purple the platform uses nowhere. Now a 10% wash plus a 3px left stripe, spending uncoloured because it is most of the rows and the unremarkable case. Amber #f5a623 for a win is borrowed from dhcarena.php's battle scope; teal would read as another credit since teal and the accent are the same family here. **THE STRIPE RULES MUST BE QUALIFIED WITH `#transactions`**: `#transactions th, #transactions td` sets `border-color: ... !important`, and an id beats two classes even when both are !important, so the unqualified version painted no stripe at all. **THE PICKER TILES ARE SQUARE WITH object-fit:contain.** Measured, because the four hero images are nothing alike: 745x850, 1038x1080, 480x356 and 891x891 -- three portrait and one landscape -- so the original 16/9 cover crop took the middle band out of three of them, on a picker whose only job is telling four games apart by looking at them. Square because three are within 13% of it; contain because nothing else shows every one whole; the box is the tile's own ink so a letterboxed edge reads as the tile. Max-width dropped 720->560 so two rows of square tiles still clear a laptop without scrolling. HARNESS NOTE: the pill-radius check needs `\s*+` POSSESSIVE. With a plain `\s*` the engine matches zero whitespace, lands on the space after the colon, and `(?!var)` is satisfied by " va" -- so it passed on the exact value it exists to reject, and the mutation survived twice before that was spotted. It also needs `(^|[\s,}])` in front or `.rounded` matches inside `.slider.rounded` and checks the neighbour's radius. |
| (operator) | Pinned loadouts for named NFTs | dropshipNftLoadouts() + dropshipGruntLoadout() in dropship/dropship-grunts.php, filter half in dropshipGruntFilterSql(). Pinned by dropship-grunts-harness.php. Drop Ship reads only `armor` and `gear` off a soldier, and for a collection whose metadata it cannot map BOTH come from dropshipStatsFromAssetName() -- a derivation from the asset name, not a fact about the art. On DHC2:F that gave two Base tiers, no Heavy and no Medkit, so the collection could not make a Super Soldier Squad at all. ALL FOUR ARE PINNED, not just the one that changes: the NFT sync UPDATEs armor/gear/level on every wallet refresh from that same derivation, so an unpinned soldier drifts the next time the mapping is touched. KEYED ON asset_name ALONE (falling back to the display name) -- these names exist in one collection, and a project gate would add the thing most easily got wrong, since a theme id and a project id are both small integers. Checked BEFORE the Grunt gate, which would otherwise return a real NFT untouched. Armour and gear only: level and rank stay the collection's, exactly as for a grunt, so a card shows the pinned armour beside the original level. **THE BARRACKS FILTER NEEDED BOTH HALVES** -- the stored column still holds the derived value, so Heavy hid the card that says Heavy AND Base showed it. Adding to the right filter without removing from the wrong one fixes half and looks finished. The exclusion is emitted FIRST and relies on AND binding tighter than OR inside the caller's `AND (armor = 'X' <fragment>)`; the harness pins the caller's shape for that reason, and only emits at all for a value the kit tables actually use. |
| games-drop-ship.md (the four free Grunts) | What Blood Bag, Delay, Hagar and Sektr carry | dropship/dropship-grunts.php, applied in setSquadAttributes() and getSoldiers() in dropship/db.php and in dropshipOddsFlags(); dropship-grunts-harness.php | **THE OLD BEHAVIOUR WAS BACKWARDS AND THE DOCS TAUGHT IT.** Grunts are stored armor 'None', gear 'None', so the run read them as no armour and fell to the bottom of the threshold chain -- the Skulliance promo branch, worth 79 PLUS free Medkit, Melee and Demolition. So one grunt scored 79, four grunts also scored 79, and a real NFT in Heavy armour scored 74: deploying a single grunt was the strongest opening in the game, the fourth slot was worthless, and buying armour made you worse. instructions.php said so out loud ("send them in one at a time for full effect"). **THE KIT IS A CODE OVERRIDE, NOT A MIGRATION:** the rows stay None/None/level 0 so nothing in the store changes, and dropshipGruntLoadout() rewrites armor and gear at read time for the four asset_names GRUNT001-4. **LEVEL AND RANK ARE NEVER TOUCHED** -- they feed the rank tiers, and a loaner kit must not promote anybody. **SEKTR CARRIES NO GEAR ON PURPOSE:** three gear types, four grunts, and duplicating one would hand a two-grunt squad a second Medkit the run cannot use; the gap is the price of the fourth armour tier. Together they are Heavy+Medium+Light+Base = 74+3+2+1 = **80**, exactly a Super Soldier Squad, and no partial squad reaches it (asserted over all 15 subsets) so the intuitive move is now the right one. **THREE READERS MUST AGREE** -- the run, the Barracks listing and the odds readout -- or the screen quotes a squad the run does not field; all three call the one function and the harness pins each call site. Grunts are DROP SHIP ONLY (addDefaultSoldiers() is gated on project 1), and the Play for Free block in instructions.php is now gated the same way: it was promising Oculus Lounge four free soldiers it never receives. **THE BARRACKS FILTER RUNS IN SQL AGAINST COLUMNS THE KIT IS NOT IN** -- a grunt's row still reads None/None and only dropshipGruntLoadout() fills it in, so filtering by Heavy hid Hagar while his own card showed Heavy. dropshipGruntFilterSql() adds the matching grunts by asset_name, built from the SAME kit table the cards are drawn from so the filter cannot disagree with what it filters, guarded on rank so a colliding NFT asset_name is never swept in, and BRACKETED into the clause -- an unbracketed OR would widen the whole WHERE and return every soldier on the platform. It refuses any field outside armor/gear BEFORE the lookup rather than just returning empty, because display_errors is on and an undefined-key warning prints into the Barracks; a check on the return value alone passes either way and let exactly that mutation through. db.php also require_once's dropship-odds.php now: loadItems() calls it, and it was only reaching those functions because dashboard.php happens to include dropship.php afterwards. 11 mutations, all caught. |
| games-drop-ship.md / games-oculus-lounge.md (trait drops) | When these two pay a DHC Fighter trait | dropship/dropship-traitdrop.php (the rule), the call sites in logScore() in dropship/db.php, the registry entries in dhcfighters-config.php, the DHCF_NO_NOTIFY guard in dhcf_award(); dropship-traitdrop-harness.php | **THESE TWO INVERT THE PLATFORM RULE, DELIBERATELY.** Everywhere else a genuine attempt qualifies and placement only drives QUALITY -- the note above DHCF_TIERS is explicit that letting placement drive the RATE lets strong players compound away. Here nothing drops unless the run TAKES OR TIES THE LEAD of the current game, then it pays placement_1. The owner's call, reasoned for these two specifically: niche boards with a handful of regulars where the problem is silence, not runaway winners, so the reward is attached to the EVENT (the lead changing) rather than the STATE (being good). **TWO GATES, AND THE SECOND IS NOT OPTIONAL:** logScore() REPLACES the player's row rather than keeping their best, so lead-alone is farmable (score 1000, then 10 -- you still top a board holding only your own 10 -- then 1000 again "takes the lead" from yourself). Requiring the run to also beat your own previous score in that game closes it. A player who goes well/badly/well still collects twice; explicitly accepted, since the bad run risks a real position. **A STANDING LEADER WHO IMPROVES ALSO PAYS** (they beat the board and themselves), so three rising runs pay three times and the per-source daily cap is what bounds that, not the rule. **THE WINDOW IS game_id, NOT A DATE.** The "Weekly" board is checkDropShipLeaderboard(..., 'weekly'), which selects results for games.active = 1 -- there is no date arithmetic anywhere and `games` has no date column. dropship/automate.php closes one game and opens the next on cron, so the cadence is the cron's, and a missed or doubled run silently changes the window. **THE DROP ANNOUNCES IN DHC FIGHTERS, AND GETTING THERE NEEDED A PLATFORM CHANGE.** dashboard.php includes db.php -> dropship.php (where the score is written, so where the award fires), THEN its own webhooks.php, and NEITHER webhooks.php guarded its discordmsg() declaration (platform arity 12, Drop Ship's 5, with a project id where ours takes a link). Announcing means loading the platform's webhooks.php from inside a Drop Ship request, which declared discordmsg() first and made Drop Ship's next include a fatal "cannot redeclare" -- the whole dashboard, not just the drop. GUARDING BOTH DECLARATIONS WOULD HAVE BEEN WORSE: Drop Ship's own calls would then silently land on the platform function and post a project id into the title-link slot. So webhooks.php defines **skl_discordmsg()** and only aliases the bare name when SKL_WEBHOOKS_NO_ALIAS is unset; Drop Ship defines that constant before loading, and dhcfighters-notify.php asks for the prefixed name. discordmsg IS THE ONLY COLLIDING NAME between the two apps -- checked across webhooks.php, activity-lib.php, avatar-lib.php and dhcfighters-notify.php against every file in dropship/ -- which is what makes a one-name fix sufficient. The alias is variadic so all 150-odd existing call sites forward unchanged (no caller uses named arguments; checked). The harness runs the redeclare scenario in a SUBPROCESS, because no source check can prove the absence of a fatal. **THE AWARD IS FULLY BUFFERED** because it runs mid-render inside dropship.php with display_errors on platform-wide; a missing credentials file printed two lines of warning into the game page before ob_start() went in. NOTE the harness cannot prove the buffering: with no credentials in a checkout the is_file() guard returns before the only reproducible warning, so removing ob_start() entirely still passes -- one branch leaking a buffer IS caught. Never make it testable by writing a credentials file from a harness; on the live server that overwrites the real one. **logScore()'s replace-on-any-score IS DELIBERATE and must not be "fixed"** -- risking your position is the spam deterrent. **EVERY RUN NOW ANNOUNCES, NOT JUST THE PATIENT ONES:** the death and leaderboard posts sat inside dashboard.php's game-over panel, which only renders once `current_score` is set -- and checkScore() sets that at the BOTTOM of the page, so on the request that logs the run it still holds the previous value. The post therefore waited for a LATER request, and the thing that reliably produced one was a button at the end of the replay labelled "Send Results to Discord". Close the tab early and the channel never heard about that run. logScore() sets run_logged; dashboard.php consumes it BEFORE the send (so an early return cannot leave it set and re-post), the 'notification' guard still stops a reload repeating it, logScore() clears that guard so each new run can post, and the button now just says Refresh. The send stays in dashboard.php rather than logScore() because Drop Ship's discordmsg() loads AFTER dropship.php -- guarded with function_exists rather than assumed. 19 mutations, 18 caught. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (proven ranks) | The Collection's Proven sorts, Record filters, and the claim-vs-evidence strip | dhcarena-lib.php (dhca_performance / dhca_performance_score / dhca_performance_pool / dhca_proven_axes, DHCA_EDGE_PCT=15, DHCA_PROFILE_MIN=5), dhcgallery.php ($dhcg_perf, $dhcg_ppool, $dhcg_cpool, $dhcg_paxis, $dhcg_pcounts, ?sort=preal|plive|pdmg, ?perf=proven|attack|defend), dhc-fighter-modal.php (#dhcg-proven, PAXES). Harnesses: dhcarena-perf-harness.php (arithmetic, no DB), dhcgallery-perf-harness.php (lifts the page's own filter/sort/re-rank blocks and evals them), dhcgallery-strip-harness.js (runs the shipped strip JS on fixtures). THREE THINGS THAT WILL BITE: (1) round() returns a FLOAT, so winpct/live/dmg are cast to int or they ship as 63.0 and match nothing with ===. (2) The claimed axes are RE-RANKED over the proven population only ($dhcg_cpool) -- comparing a proven rank out of 31 against a claimed rank out of 108 manufactures disagreement from arithmetic alone. (3) Unproven Fighters are ABSENT from the pools, not zero in them, so prankOf is the measured population and a proven sort parks them at the back as a block. The whole Proven/Record group is hidden while $dhcg_pcounts['proven'] is 0, which is also the empty-ledger state. dhca_proven_axes() is the single place an axis is paired with the claim it tests; the sort button, the title text and the strip all read it. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (arms/weapon) | Which arms an exclusive weapon can wear | dhcf_arms_weapon_clash() in dhcfighters-config.php, both save paths in dhcfighters-lib.php, armsWeaponClash() + 6 call sites in dhc-assembler.php, covered by dhc-assembler-harness.php. DHCF_ARMS_EXCLUSIVE (plastic-blaster, dh-raider-equipment, electric-morning-star) blocked ALL arms, and the real question is not "are there arms?" but "is the arm the weapon rests on still visible?". Only BEHIND_TORSO arms pass. **ESTABLISHED FROM RENDERS, NOT FROM THE RULES.** perforator-arm-replacement sits behind the shoulder and leaves the blaster untouched -- blocked for nothing, 3 combinations. infested-robo-limb is the trap: dhcf_armless_mode() calls it 'none' (it keeps the torso's native arms), so the obvious condition `armless_mode !== 'none'` looks right and IS WRONG -- arms draw after weapon in dhcf_slots(), so the limb covers the blaster's grip and trigger guard and the gun reads as embedded rather than held. Its own config comment predicts it: it "covers what is under it", which here includes the arm doing the holding. Compose the pair and look before widening this rule. **THE RULE HAD NO SERVER-SIDE CHECK AT ALL.** DHCF_ARMS_EXCLUSIVE appeared exactly once outside its definition -- a JS constant in dhc-assembler.php -- while the sibling head/headgear rule beside it IS enforced in dhcf_save_fighter() with the comment "Enforced here, not only in the browser: the page is a suggestion." A stale tab or hand-edited link could commit a Fighter the Collection, Arena and Discord embed all draw wrong. Now checked on BOTH the save and edit paths. The browser must take its exempt list FROM DHCF_ARMS_BEHIND_TORSO, not a copy: stubbing it to [] leaves the name in place, silently restores the blanket block in the UI while the server still allows the pair, and the two disagree -- which is the single outcome this change exists to prevent. The harness asserts the json_encode line itself for that reason. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (staff share) | Sharing a Fighter you don't own | dhc-fighter-modal.php (canUse/staff, shareText's `showcase` arg, X_SHOWCASE), dhc-download.php (the `$own` clause), covered by dhc-share-harness.js. USER 1 -- the account that posts the Collection to X -- MAY SHARE AND DOWNLOAD ANY FIGHTER. Owner-only was right and stays right for everyone else (a flattened ready-to-post picture of somebody's assembly is theirs to hand out), but it left the brand account able to showcase only its own Fighters, which is the least interesting slice of the Collection. TWO GATES, AND THE SERVER ONE IS THE GATE: the modal's canUse only draws buttons; dhc-download.php drops the `AND user_id = %d` half of its WHERE for `$me === 1` and keeps `disassembled_at IS NULL`, because anyone can type the URL. The **?build= branch is NOT exempt** -- its gate stops the trait art being walked out one clean 1000px layer at a time, which does not change for user 1. THE SHOWCASE VOICE (shareText's second arg) differs in exactly two ways and both are load-bearing: the builder is NAMED AND LEADS the sentence (the point of the post is that a player made this), and the tail is X_SHOWCASE rather than X_HANDLE because "Join @skulliance" is the account TAGGING ITSELF. The builder is named in PLAIN TEXT, never with an @: f.owner is a Discord username and the platform stores no X handle for anyone, so an @ would tag whichever stranger holds that name on X. The stat tail also drops its own "assembled from" when the builder line already said "assembled". The card warm follows canUse, not mine -- a staff share of somebody else's Fighter is now the likeliest share on the platform and the crawler caches a miss. HARNESS TRAP HIT HERE: dhc-share-harness.js lifted the function by matching the literal `'  function shareText(f) {'`, so adding a second parameter silently returned an empty slice and the harness died on the first call rather than reporting anything. It anchors on the NAME now. The three old `mine` assertions were REWRITTEN, not deleted -- what they guard is that an ordinary viewer still cannot share or download someone else's, and canUse is pinned to its exact `mine || ME === 1` text because `mine || ME > 0` would satisfy a loose check while opening it to everybody. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (trait status) | The mobile shortcut to "Where traits drop" | dhc-nav.php ($dhcnav_items, the 5th field), dhcfighters.php (`id="drops"`). "What can I still pull today" is the single most-repeated question a trait hunter has -- several times a day -- and on a phone dhcfighters.php stacks, so the list sits below the assembler, the canvas, the draw order and twenty roster cards. IT IS AN ANCHOR, NOT A PAGE. A dedicated dhcstatus.php was built and thrown away: a second URL, a second menu entry in Play and a second copy of the panel's markup, all to show a list that is already rendered, is a worse trade than a hash. $dhcnav_items rows are now **[href, label, hint, gated, mobile_only]** -- a 4-field row is an undefined offset on EVERY DHC page at once, not just the one that added it, so the harness counts the fields. Hidden on desktop with `.dhcnav a.only-mobile{display:none}` rather than skipped in PHP: one menu, one markup, no server-side device guess. Under 700px the strip WRAPS to two rows instead of scrolling sideways -- five chips do not fit a phone and a horizontal scroller hides the overflow behind a gesture nobody is told about, the same trap the trait grid had. It is in normal flow below the navbar, so it cannot reach the burger. Label is the noun and hint is the verb, matching Fighters/build, Collection/browse, Arena/fight: hence **Games/review**. Measured at 360/400: five chips, two rows, 83px, nothing clipped, no sideways scroll; at 1000: four chips, one row. Guests get it as a locked "sign in" chip, same as Fighters. **A FRAGMENT AIMED AT THE CURRENT PAGE IS COLLAPSED TO JUST THE HASH**, and that is not cosmetic: "page.php#frag" only jumps in place when everything before the "#" matches the current URL EXACTLY -- it does not while editing (?edit=12) -- and any mismatch makes it a NAVIGATION that reloads the assembler, the trait index, the roster art and two leaderboards to reach a list already further down the same document. In the installed PWA that reload was reported as never finishing. The collapse is derived from $dhcnav_items[$dhcnav_at][0], not special-cased on 'status', so a future fragment item needs no new code. (The service worker is a deliberate no-op, so it was never caching anything -- the reload really was a reload.) THE INTRO FOLDS ON A PHONE, `<details class="dhcf-note" open>` with the summary hidden on desktop, so a desktop renders exactly the paragraph it replaced (74px) and a phone opens at 20px instead of 245px. **`open` is in the MARKUP and removed by script**, so with JavaScript off a phone gets the full text rather than a notice it cannot reach -- and it is a fold, not a deletion, because the second half is the RIGHTS NOTICE (not an NFT, no ownership of the artwork, Maxingo's art). The harness asserts those sentences still exist. A matchMedia listener handles rotation, and a `toggle` listener stops a resize overriding a choice the reader made. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (where traits drop) | The list of games on the DHC Fighters page | DHCF_GAMES in dhcfighters-config.php, rendered by the foreach in dhcfighters.php that skips any row carrying `'unlisted' => true`. Pinned by dropship-traitdrop-harness.php. **THE FOUR DROP SHIP THEMES ARE ONE ROW NOW.** Oculus Lounge, Filthy Mermaid and Dread City are Drop Ship themes switched between from inside the game, and all four rows are 'wildcard', so they rendered IDENTICALLY: same "any trait", same count out of the whole collection, same trigger, same limit note, four times in a sixteen-row list. The three reskins carry 'unlisted' now and Drop Ship's trigger reads "take or tie the lead in any theme", which is the ONLY place a player is told the other three pay it. 13 rows listed. UNLISTED IS A DISPLAY FLAG AND NOTHING ELSE -- the rows stay in DHCF_GAMES because dropshipDropAward() derives these KEYS from the theme slug ('filthy-mermaid' -> 'filthymermaid') and dhcf_award() resolves the label, the X tag and the link from them, so deleting one would post that theme's drops as Drop Ship or as nothing. Same flag as dailystreak, referral and digestcache, different cause: those are not games, these are the same game. The harness pins BOTH halves -- the three unlisted AND Drop Ship not -- because unlisting all four removes the only row that leads anywhere, and it checks each unlisted row keeps its url and x_tag. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (editing)    | What clicking a saved Fighter does | dhcfighters.php (.dhcf-card .art, $dhcf_edit, EDITING). **CLICKING A FIGHTER EDITS IT. There is no Edit link any more.** It used to only LOAD the build onto the canvas, which looks identical to editing and is not: the traits stay committed to that Fighter, so the picker greys them out and Save refuses -- and you then had to find a separate Edit link to do the thing you had already asked for. Worse, the two states could DISAGREE: editing A and then clicking B swapped the canvas to B's build while the page was still editing A, so Save would have turned A into a copy of B and then failed on the duplicate trait set, reporting something the player never asked to do. One meaning per click fixes both at once. It is a NAVIGATION (`?edit=`), not a canvas swap, because only the server can free that Fighter's own traits back to it -- dhcf_available()'s $ignore_id -- and that is the entire difference between looking at a build and being able to save one. Clicking the Fighter already open is a no-op, or the reload would discard the canvas. A fresh load starts above the header, the stats strip and the save bar, so `if (EDITING)` scrolls .shell into view on arrival; the old in-page scroll went with the old handler and the complaint it fixed would otherwise have come straight back. THE "WHY ARE THESE GREY" BANNER REPORTS A COUNT, NOT ONE TILE'S REASON. It used to quote the FIRST blocked tile verbatim, so one committed torso among forty free ones announced "Your only copy is in a saved Fighter" on a fresh page with a random build -- read as a claim about the trait you are wearing. Rule now: ALL blocked -> quote the reason (true of the whole tab, and usually a conflict rather than ownership); SOME blocked -> a count with NO cause, because blockedReason() returns ownership, head/headgear conflicts AND arms/weapon exclusivity, so naming one would be wrong on the Headgear tab. Per-tile reasons stay in each greyed tile's tooltip. THE SAVE NOTE AND THE SAVE RESULT SHARE ONE LINE, and they used to arbitrate by SNIFFING style.opacity -- syncSave only cleared its own note if the opacity still read ".6". That is not state, it is a guess about who wrote last, and it is wrong the moment anything else touches the element: the note then sits under an ENABLED Save button insisting a background is missing while one is on the canvas. A $sayIsNote flag owns it now, released by msg(). syncSave also re-runs on keyup/change/dragend and once on load, not just click -- a click covers picking a trait but not a build arriving from ?build=, an edit preload, a keyboard pick or the layer drag, and the first wrong state used to stick until something else was clicked. LEAVING EDIT MODE IS A HOLLOW BUTTON, not a quiet link: edit is a state the whole page is in, and the way out of it was an 11px grey word beside a filled button. Same box as Update Fighter (both 35px, top-aligned) -- filled is what you came to do, outlined is the way back -- rather than a second filled button competing with it. The card being edited is marked in the roster (border + an EDITING strip) because the save bar that names it is at the top of the page and the roster is beside the picker -- on a tall page they are nowhere near each other. LOST DELIBERATELY: loading a build you cannot save, as a starting point for a different one. It was never really usable (every piece is committed, so you had to swap enough of them to free a saveable set) and it was the source of the confusion. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (typography) | One typeface across the DHC tabs | dhc-type.php (dhc_type_styles()), called by dhc-assembler.php, dhcgallery.php and dhcarena.php. Each page used to declare fonts against its OWN wrapper, so anything that was not one of those wrappers got the platform Arial. **THE FIGHTER PANEL IS position:fixed AND SITS OUTSIDE .dhcg-wrap ON THE COLLECTION BUT INSIDE .dhcf-wrap ON THE ASSEMBLER** -- same panel, two fonts. Measured: Arial/Arial/Arial for body, title and close button before; JetBrains Mono / Archivo Black / JetBrains Mono after, with non-DHC content still Arial. **AND dhc-assembler.php DECLARED h1,h2,h3 UNSCOPED**, restyling the host page document-wide -- which is why the panel looked right on the assembler and wrong on the Collection: a rule leaking was propping up a rule missing. dhc-nav.php declares nothing and travels across all four pages, so it is in the list too. Form controls are named explicitly (they do not inherit font). NOT LOADED, EITHER FAMILY: no @font-face and no Google Fonts link anywhere, so JetBrains Mono resolves to ui-monospace/Menlo and Archivo Black to Impact -- the fix is about reaching every element with the same STACK, not about shipping fonts. SIZES LEFT ALONE: the Arena runs 13px to everyone else is 14px and its board is laid out around that. The partial is INERT ON INCLUDE (a function, not output) because every DHC page requires its libraries before header.php has emitted anything. | games-dhc-fighters.md (the credit) | Crediting the artist | dhcfighters-config.php (DHCF_ARTIST, DHCF_ARTIST_X, dhcf_artist_link()). Every DHC intro links Maxingo to x.com/MMAXI404 -- the Collection, the assembler, the sandbox, the marketing page and the Skull Paper pages. ONE DEFINITION: a credit that is a link on one page and plain text on the next is a credit nobody follows, and dhcgame.php had its own hardcoded copy of the URL before this. DHCF_POLICY / DHCF_COLLECTION_URL / dhcf_collection_link() do the same for the MINTED COLLECTION: the rights notice ends "the genuine, ownable Fighters are from the official NFT collection", which without a link names a destination and withholds it. dhcgame.php read its own hardcoded copy of that URL too and now reads the shared one. **homepage.php LINKS A DIFFERENT HANDLE** (x.com/madmaxi__) in its partner row; left alone deliberately, since which account is current for a partner listing is not something the DHC config should guess at -- raised with the owner instead. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (the panel)  | The Fighter detail panel, shared by two pages | dhc-fighter-modal.php, ajax/dhc-fighter.php, plus dhc-share-harness.js. THE BUILDER LINK NAMES A COUNT -- "see their 27 Fighters" is worth a click where "see their Fighters" is not. Its own grouped query in dhcgallery.php and its own COUNT in ajax/dhc-fighter.php, NOT a tally of the rows on screen: those are whatever the page is filtered and paged to, so counting them would say "see their 3 Fighters" to somebody on a tier filter whose builder has thirty. Pluralised, and a payload with no count falls back to the bare wording rather than rendering "see their 0 Fighters". **THE CLOSE CONTROL MUST BE ANCHORED TO THE PANEL, AND PINNED ON A PHONE.** It was position:absolute with nothing positioned between it and #dhcg-veil, so top:12px meant twelve pixels from the top of the VIEWPORT -- and the veil padding reserves env(safe-area-inset-top) while an absolutely positioned child ignores padding. On a notched phone in the installed app the only close control sat under the status bar; the reported symptom was having to tap the very edge of the screen, which is the veil click-to-close, all that was left. #dhcg-panel is position:relative now, and under 760px the control is position:fixed at env(safe-area-inset-top)+10 with a 44px minimum, because the panel is nearly the whole screen there and the panel scrolls INTERNALLY (an absolute child scrolls away with the art). Measured: 390px -> fixed, 65x44, unmoved after scrolling the panel to its end, elementFromPoint returns the button; 1200px -> absolute, anchored to #dhcg-panel. Escape and the veil tap stay as second ways out. ONE COPY, TWO CALLERS: the Collection opens it on a card click, dhcfighters.php opens it the moment a save lands so the Fighter just built can be reviewed, downloaded and posted without going to find it. Host sets `$dhcm_user`, `$dhcm_base`, `$dhcm_deeplink` and then drives `window.DHC_MODAL.open(f)`. `DHCM_RENDERED` guards against being emitted twice, which would duplicate every id in it. **EVERY RULE FOR THE PANEL MUST LIVE IN THE PARTIAL.** `#dhcg-close` was left behind in dhcgallery.php when the panel was extracted -- the lift started at the `#dhcg-veil` rule and that one sat above it -- so the Collection kept its styling while the assembler rendered a bare browser button: white, rounded, system font, in the middle of a dark panel. Nothing warns about a rule only one of two callers has, and neither page is wrong on its own. The harness scans every `<style>` in dhcgallery.php, dhcfighters.php and dhc-assembler.php for selectors naming anything the panel emits. Scoped to the names it ACTUALLY emits, read from its own markup -- matching the bare `dhcg-` prefix flagged the Collection page's own `.dhcg-wrap`, `.dhcg-head` and `.dhcg-grid`, which are nothing to do with it. **ONLY THE COLLECTION OWNS ?fighter=** -- `$dhcm_deeplink` is true there and false on the assembler, where rewriting the address to a different page would send a refresh somewhere else. SAVE NO LONGER RELOADS ON A TIMER: it fetches ajax/dhc-fighter.php and opens the panel; the reload happens on CLOSE (onClose, one-shot), because closing is the natural end of "I made this" and a 900ms timer the player cannot see is a worse place for a page change. Every failure path -- no serial, no panel, bad fetch -- falls back to exactly the old reload. ajax/dhc-fighter.php returns the same object the Collection puts in `data-f`; widening the SAVE endpoint to carry layers, parts, stats and ranks would put presentation inside a write endpoint, and a write that fails because a rank pool could not be read is a Fighter that saved and said it did not. THE ASSEMBLER'S OWN X BUTTON IS GONE: x.com/intent/post shows the image the SHARED URL declares, and an unsaved build on a login-gated page has no URL and no card -- that button could only ever post text. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (sharing)    | Share on X, and the ranks on the Collection modal | dhc-assembler.php (#sharex), dhcgallery.php (#dhcg-share, #dhcg-rank, ?fighter=), dhc-ranks.php, plus dhc-share-harness.js. RANKS, NOT SCORES, ARE THE SHAREABLE CLAIM: "#4 deadliest of 108" is something a stranger can weigh; "6,846" is a number with no scale. The modal shows all four axes from dhcf_rank_axes(); a post names the BEST TWO (four reads as a stat dump and X truncates anyway). Ranked against the WHOLE collection, never the page's current filter -- "#3 of 9 legendary" is a different and much weaker claim than "#3 of 108". dhcf_rank_pool() is read ONCE per page, outside the row loop (it is disk-cached, but re-reading it per row is still a file read and a json_decode each time); the harness counts the call sites. THE TAIL INVITES THE READER AND THEN CREDITS THE ARTIST, the credit in its own PARAGRAPH under the invitation (blank line between them, so it reads as a separate statement rather than a second sentence of the pitch): "Join @skulliance to assemble your own Fighter and battle other players in the Arena!\nArt by @MMAXI404" -- the post is an invitation first, and the credit is a fact it carries rather than its opening line -- none of this exists without Maxingo's art, so a Fighter going out uncredited is the wrong default and the credit is part of the tail rather than something a player has to remember -- a bare handle tells a stranger who made the thing and nothing about why to join, and the next player is the point of the post. It costs ~104 characters, which is why the stat sentence is conditional: the credit and the invitation both outrank the health number. One string, in dhc-fighter-modal.php, now that the panel is the only place a share is offered. **A THIRD BUTTON, ON THE TRAIT DROP** (dhc-dropmodal.php, X_TAIL/shareText/shareHref): same two traps, its own copy, because that file is deliberately standalone -- no framework, no platform CSS, no platform PHP, since it sits on top of nine game interfaces. It posts the trait, its tier and how many of the 226 originals wear it, and links to dhcgallery.php?trait=cat:slug so X has a card to fetch. CATEGORY AND SLUG BOTH: nine slugs exist in two categories at once and are different traits with different art. The url is NOT encodeURIComponent'd before being encoded again as url= -- double encoding still resolves but puts %253A in the posted link. Hidden on .miss (a near miss and a spent daily cap both set it) because there is no trait to show, and the href is rebuilt inside show() rather than once at load, or every drop after the first would post the first one. Its own row in the actions bar via order:-1 rather than markup order, so Close stays first for anyone tabbing: three controls on one row in a 348px box leaves 100px each and "Open assembler" alone is wider than that. Black on white, X's own look, so it reads as neither the mint primary nor the outlined secondary. THE LONG-NAME FIXTURE HAD TO HAVE SPACES IN IT: 'A'.repeat(300) is one unbroken token, the trim's word-boundary rule threw the whole thing away and left "Earned …" at 100 characters, and the mutation that drops the 24-character url cost passed against it. **X COUNTS ANY URL AS 23 CHARACTERS** and cuts the overflow off the END, which is where the invitation lives -- a post that loses its handle looks fine to the poster and reaches nobody. Both buttons budget 280 - 24 - handle, the same arithmetic shareOnXUrl() does server-side. The name column caps at 48 so real data cannot reach the limit, which is exactly why the harness drives the truncation path with an oversized name instead of hoping a fixture trips it: a mutation deleting the budget survived the realistic fixture. The harness READS X_HANDLE OUT OF dhc-fighter-modal.php rather than declaring its own, and UN-ESCAPES it: the capture is source text, so a \\n in the literal arrives as two characters where the browser sees one, which both misreads the post and overstates the tail length in the budget -- it had stubbed '@skulliance' while the shipping tail had grown to 84 characters, so every budget assertion was measuring a string 73 characters shorter than the real one. A real post measures 228 of 280 including the URL. SHARE IS OWNER-ONLY in the Collection, the same rule the download follows -- posting somebody else's assembly, prefilled in your voice, is not a share. The assembler's button is the X mark alone (five labels would wrap) and starts **disabled** until the ranks strip answers, because the post is built from those placements; a failed rank fetch disables it again.
