Not offered in the sandbox, where every trait is unlocked and a rank would be a claim about a build nobody earned. `?fighter=SERIAL` opens that card on arrival (replaceState, not pushState) and is cleared on close, AND makes the page's og/twitter tags about that one Fighter -- its own indexed query, not a scan of the page's rows, or a link to a Fighter outside the current filter would not unfurl. **THE POST CARRIES THE FIGHTER, via dhc-card.php** (see its own row). x.com/intent/post cannot attach a file; the only image a post shows is the one the shared URL declares. NOT A LOOSENING OF dhc-download.php: that still gates the named, full-size, ready-to-post file. The card draws from dhcf_render_fighter(), whose output already lives in dhcrenders/ and is already fetched by Discord's servers for every announcement. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (share card) | The 1200x630 image X renders | dhc-card.php, plus dhc-card-harness.php. **1200x630 AND NOT THE SQUARE RENDER**: summary_large_image is 2:1 and X centre-crops anything else, so handing it the 1000px square beheads the Fighter and cuts its feet off. The art is fitted WHOLE inside the frame (height minus 36px of air) and the sides are filled with a soft, dark wash of the same art, so the card reads as one picture rather than a portrait on a slab. **THE BLUR IS A DOWNSCALE-THEN-STAGED-UPSCALE, NOT IMG_FILTER_GAUSSIAN_BLUR.** That filter is a fixed 3x3 kernel: on a 1200px canvas even several passes leave the art essentially sharp, and the first card shipped with crisp spikes down both sides competing with the Fighter. Resample to DHCC_BLUR (28px), then back up through 120 and 400 with gaussian passes at each -- NOT in one jump, because GD stops interpolating usefully at a 40x ratio and renders hard squares, a mosaic that reads as a rendering fault. Then DHCC_DIM (-110) and a colorize towards the platform ground; at -72 the sides still read as art. Both of those were only visible by rendering a real card and LOOKING at it. The harness measures them now, and measuring the mosaic took three attempts: neighbour-difference does not separate the two cases because the dim clamps most of the backdrop towards black, so what is asserted is the LONGEST RUN OF IDENTICAL PIXELS (86 staged vs 341 one-jump on its fixture, threshold 200). Its fixture is a fine CHECKERBOARD for the same reason -- against the flat red square it used first, both the mosaic and the undimmed-backdrop mutations passed. NO TEXT ON THE IMAGE: there is no TTF in this repo and GD's bitmap fonts at 1200px look like a mistake -- and the post itself already carries the name and placements, which X renders directly above the card. Draws through dhcf_render_fighter(), never its own compositor (the armless-torso swap and single-arm clip are the bug class that cost the Arena its effects twice). SAVED Fighters only, keyed by serial, `disassembled_at IS NULL`; there is deliberately NO ?build= form, which would be a way to walk the trait art out without owning any of it. Cache key is the TRAITS HASH, not the serial -- an edit keeps the serial, so a serial-keyed card would serve the old picture for a week. Atomic rename, nonzero check, `ob_start()` around db.php (display_errors is ON and a notice before the header is a corrupt card that X then caches), and **no imagedestroy()** for the same reason it was removed from dhcfighters-notify.php. The harness LIFTS THE REAL COMPOSITING BLOCK OUT AND RUNS IT against a source whose top and bottom rows are colour-marked: if either marker is missing from the output the art is being cropped. **THE TRAIT CARD, dhc-card.php?trait=cat:slug**, shares everything below the source: same 2:1 frame, same backdrop, same fit, same atomic cache. It exists because players were SCREENSHOTTING the drop modal and posting the photo -- phone status bar and Android nav buttons included -- which is the platform telling you a share button is missing. Validated by dhcf_trait_info() BEFORE the two halves become a path, with a [a-z0-9-] character class as a second gate. **A TRAIT IS CROPPED TO ITS OWN INK AND THE FIT IS WIDTH-BOUNDED**, neither of which a Fighter needs: a trait PNG is drawn on the full 1000x1000 BODY canvas at the position it occupies on a Fighter, so a belt is a band across the lower third of an empty square -- fitted to the card height it arrived small, low, and sitting on a near-black wash. Cropped, a belt is 503x183 and would be scaled 3.3x to the height, so min() against (DHCC_W - 120) is what keeps it on the card. **THE CROP IS IMG_CROP_SIDES, NOT IMG_CROP_TRANSPARENT**, which is what it shipped with and what silently did nothing to 22 of the 235 trait PNGs: GD's transparent mode compares every pixel against imagecolortransparent(), ONE palette index, and 18 files are truecolor with an alpha channel (no index at all) while four more are palette files whose index is not their border colour -- axe, scythe, skull-krusher, tank-helmet, all sparse art, all rendering as a speck. Sides decides from the corner colours instead; measured over the whole set it crops those 22, is never smaller than transparent on the 213 where both work, and never degenerates. Transparent stays behind it as a fallback. The crop is gated on the trait branch: a Fighter render fills its canvas, and sides on a Fighter with a flat background would start trimming the background off cards that already work. The harness runs the real crop over EVERY trait PNG in the repo and names the sparse ones that must shrink -- a count would pass while the wrong 22 cropped. og:image, title, description and canonical for ?trait= are set in dhcgallery.php between the general case and the single-Fighter case, in that order; without the title the post unfurled as "The Fighter Collection" over a picture of one trait. |
| platform-profile.md (avatars) | Broken Discord avatars heal themselves | avatar-lib.php, ajax/avatar-refresh.php, the capture-phase listener in header.php, avatar-harness.php. **THE BUG:** users.avatar holds a Discord avatar HASH and is written ONLY at login (db.php:2933 insert, :2982 update). Change your avatar on Discord and the stored hash is dead -- the URL is still well-formed, the CDN 404s, broken image. Long-session players never log in again, so it is the ACTIVE users who stay broken while a dormant one at least reads as dormant. **WHY THE EXISTING FALLBACKS NEVER FIRED:** 101 hand-built copies of this URL across 20 files, every one a ternary falling back to icons/skull.png when there is NO hash stored. A stale hash is stored and wrong, which produces a valid URL -- the fallback is testing the wrong condition, so no number of extra fallbacks would have fixed it. **THE CASCADE (owner's design):** render the stored hash (free, right for almost everyone) -> on 404 ask Discord once via GET /users/{id} with the existing $bot_token and WRITE IT BACK -> placeholder only if that fails too. Cost lands only where there is something to fix, and it is permanent: a success writes a working hash so that user never 404s again, and a deleted account writes '' so the URL becomes the placeholder and stops 404ing too. The feature heals itself out of existence. Rejected a nightly cron for this: drift is caused by a user changing their avatar, not by time, so a sweep re-checks thousands of working avatars to catch a handful and still leaves a day's window broken. **ONE LISTENER, NOT 101 EDITS:** error events do NOT bubble but they DO capture, so a single capture-phase listener on document in header.php sees every image that fails anywhere, including ones injected later by ajax (which is most of them). Registering it without capture:true makes the whole feature silently dead and the page looks exactly as it does today -- the harness checks for `}, true)`. **SECURITY, and it is the reason the endpoint can be unauthenticated:** the id must already be in users, or this is an open proxy resolving any Discord account on earth for anyone who can reach the URL, on our bot's credentials and rate limit. An unknown id returns the SAME answer as a malformed one, deliberately, or it answers 'does this account play here?'. Unauthenticated on purpose because the Collection and leaderboards are public and show avatars. A null fetch (rate limit, network) stamps avatar_checked_at but must NEVER write the avatar column -- blanking a good-but-stale hash because Discord was busy would make a rate limit permanent. **FOUND ON THE WAY:** the 101 copies hardcode .png or .jpg, but Discord serves an ANIMATED avatar (hash prefixed a_) as .gif ONLY, so those users are broken on some pages and fine on others depending which file drew them; avatar_url() picks the extension from the hash. ONE-TIME ALTER: users ADD COLUMN avatar_checked_at DATETIME DEFAULT NULL AFTER avatar -- until it runs every avatar falls back to the placeholder and the endpoint logs why, which is degraded rather than broken but looks identical to the feature not working. **AND THE FIRST VERSION COULD NOT REACH DISCORD AT ALL.** $bot_token is defined in credentials/webhooks_credentials.php, included by webhooks.php and by NOTHING ELSE -- so an endpoint pulling in db.php and avatar-lib.php had no token, avatar_fetch_hash() returned null on its first line, the endpoint stamped avatar_checked_at and answered 'unavailable', and every avatar fell to the placeholder. From the outside that is indistinguishable from 'those users really did delete their accounts', which is how it was reported. avatar-lib.php now include_once's the credentials itself (guarded: the directory is server-only). A MISSING TOKEN IS NOW DISTINCT FROM A FAILED CALL and is NOT stamped -- stamping starts an hour's backoff against a fault that will still be there in an hour, so fixing the config would have left every user backed off. The harness had tested the endpoint's safety properties thoroughly and never asked whether it could do its JOB; it does now. **DIAGNOSIS METHOD WORTH REUSING:** curl the public Collection, extract every avatar URL, curl each against the CDN, then re-test the 404s with every extension. That separated two causes immediately -- one hash 404s on png/jpg/webp/gif alike (genuinely stale, needs the API) and another 404s only as .jpg while .png returns 200 (hash fine, the hand-built URL asked for the wrong file type). The second is not staleness at all and no amount of API refreshing would have explained it. **AND .jpg IS THE OTHER HALF OF IT, which is not staleness at all.** Measured against the live CDN: for the SAME hash, .png returns 200 wherever anything does, while .jpg 404s on some avatars. Of the ~19 literal URLs on the platform, 10 asked for .jpg. That is a plain bug no amount of refreshing from Discord would have explained, and it is why the breakage looked arbitrary -- the same user was fine on a .png page and broken on a .jpg one. Converted the three the owner reported: db.php's two realms-map renderers (rtc-avatar, rc-avatar), dhcarena.php's rival list, leaderboards.php's ladder. avatar-lib.php is required from db.php so avatar_url() is reachable platform-wide. **44 HAND-BUILT URLS REMAIN IN db.php**, nearly all .jpg, and most of them feed DISCORD WEBHOOK EMBEDS where the browser listener cannot help at all -- a 404 there is just a missing picture in a Discord post. Worth converting. **AN INLINE onerror ON AN AVATAR TAKES THE REFRESH'S TURN AWAY:** the realms map had onerror='this.src="icons/skull.png"', so a dead hash jumped straight to the placeholder. The capture listener still fires first, but the inline handler then blanks the src underneath it, and the result is a skull either way -- on the one page that kept getting reported. Removed there; others remain. **HARNESS LESSON, twice over:** a per-FILE check ('does this file mention avatar_url') passes while one of its two renderers regresses, and a BYTE WINDOW around a marker reaches into the forty neighbouring renderers in db.php and reports their faults against this one. Both were written loosely, both were caught by mutation testing, and the fix is line-scoped checks anchored on the CSS class each site renders. **TWO MORE HIDING PLACES, both reported as 'that page still does not work'.** (1) THE REALMS MAP BUILT ITS URL INSIDE SQL -- concat("https://cdn.discordapp.com/avatars/",users.discord_id,...,".jpg") AS user_image, consumed by map.js. A URL assembled in a query is invisible to every grep for the places that build avatar URLs, which is exactly why it survived the pass that converted the rest of the same file. The query selects discord_id and avatar now and PHP calls avatar_url(); the harness forbids the concat pattern returning. (2) THE ARENA RIVAL LIST had been converted and STILL looked broken, because its img carried onerror="this.style.visibility='hidden'" -- the refresh lands correctly on an element nobody can see. An onerror that HIDES is as damaging as one that swaps in the skull, and harder to spot because the symptom is an absence. **THE GENERAL LESSON: converting the URL builder is only half a site.** A page needs BOTH its builder calling avatar_url() AND its img tag free of inline onerror, and those are rarely the same line -- the URL goes into a variable a few lines above the tag. The harness checks them as two separate lists for that reason; a single list demanded avatar_url() on an <img> line that only references $av and failed on correct code. **THEN THE WHACK-A-MOLE ENDED WITH A SWEEP.** Fixing pages one report at a time kept revealing another hiding place, so every hand-built avatar URL on the platform is now gone: 82 conversions across ~30 files, in four shapes the originals came in -- "...$a/$b.png" interpolation, ".".$a."/".$b.".png" concatenation, "{$a}/{$b}.png" curly interpolation, and <?php echo $a; ?>/<?php echo $b; ?> inline in an HTML attribute. THREE DOCUMENTED EXEMPTIONS remain and the harness names them: header.php (the listener's own CDN prefix -- how it RECOGNISES a failed avatar, it builds nothing), digest-harness.php (fixtures), and gallery.php (built in JavaScript from a client-side payload, so no PHP function reaches it; it already asks for .png and a failure still goes through the listener, so only an animated avatar is wrong there). The harness now asserts NO file hand-builds one, and that every file calling avatar_url() can actually reach it -- seven libraries were relying on their caller having loaded db.php and would have fataled the moment something included them alone. **THE SWEEP BROKE FOUR HARNESSES AND THAT WAS THE USEFUL PART:** daily-reward, dhc-rewards and obscura-announce brace-extract db.php functions and eval() them in isolation, so the new call was out of scope -- each needed avatar-lib.php required. digest-harness failed differently and more interestingly: its fixture used discord_id '77' and avatar 'abc', which avatar_url() rightly refuses (an id is 5-25 digits, a hash 8-64 chars), so the fixture had been asserting a URL the platform would never build. A fixture that cannot pass validation is testing the wrong thing; it uses real-shaped values now. **THE LAST STUCK ACCOUNT WAS SELF-INFLICTED.** One user stayed broken for days while others healed. The row told the story -- avatar_checked_at stamped, hash UNCHANGED, which is uniquely the failure path (a deleted account writes '', a success writes the new hash). Probing five hours after the recorded stamp still answered 'cached', because every failure re-stamps the hour: the backoff renews itself and the window never stays open long enough to reproduce. CAUSE: the client deduped refreshes by id but still fired every DIFFERENT broken avatar at once, so a page with five of them made five simultaneous Discord calls, got rate-limited, and recorded five hours of backoff -- then the next hour's single attempt landed in the next burst and lost again. We were causing the failure we were backing off from. The refreshes are chained now with a 250ms gap, and the endpoint reports the upstream status in its reply and its log so 'rate limited' and 'account deleted' are no longer indistinguishable. Clearing avatar_checked_at and re-calling healed the account immediately: new hash 200, old hash 404. **AND TWO OF THE THREE REMAINING 'BROKEN' AVATARS WERE NOT BROKEN.** map.js line 547/636 draws icons/skull.png for any realm NOT in activeRaidRealms (built from this month's raidPairs) -- the skull on a map node means 'has not raided this month', not 'avatar failed'. The popup passes user_image unconditionally, which is why those players appear there and not on the node, exactly as reported. Worth knowing before fixing a map avatar that is working as designed. **THE MAP SHOWS EVERY AVATAR NOW, by the owner's call.** The skull-for-non-raiders was deliberate but wrong in practice: a realm is only on the map because it was raided recently enough to qualify, so a field of skulls reads as broken images rather than as a signal, and the raid state it doubled up on is already carried by the glow and the connecting lines. activeRaidRealms had no readers left afterwards and was deleted -- a set still built and never read gets 'restored' later by someone assuming it mattered. **AND THE LISTENER NOW HANDLES SVG <image>.** The map draws its markers as SVG, whose tagName is the lowercase 'image' and whose source is href, not src -- so a listener testing for 'IMG' and reading src skipped the one page where every face on screen is an avatar. It healed everywhere except there, which would have been invisible until a map-only user went stale. avatarAttr() picks the attribute, and the repair pass queries both kinds. The harness asserts the HANDLER derives the attribute rather than that avatarAttr() merely exists: hardcoding var attr = 'src' leaves the helper defined and unused and writes a src onto SVG elements that nothing reads -- which the looser check missed. 28 mutations, all caught. |
| platform-profile.md (avatar wall) | Admin: every account as one poster | admin-collage.php, registered in admin_tabs(), covered by admin-harness.php. A thank-you image: one circular tile per account, 399 of them in a 20x20 grid at 2686px square, downloaded as a PNG. Nothing stored, no POST -- same as admin-digest.php and admin-flyers.php, which is why the write-path checks in admin-harness do not apply. DRAWN IN THE BROWSER for the digest's reasons (no TTF in this repo, so GD cannot letter a username) plus one of its own: 399 avatars fetched server-side is 399 sequential curls before the first byte, where the browser fetches them in parallel and the grid fills in. **THE CANVAS STAYS CLEAN ONLY BECAUSE DISCORD SENDS CORS** -- cdn.discordapp.com answers access-control-allow-origin: *, the same fact activity_digest_src() relies on when it lets that one host through and drops every other remote URL. crossOrigin MUST be set BEFORE src: set after, the attribute is ignored, the images still draw, and the only symptom is toDataURL() throwing once all 399 have loaded. The harness asserts the ORDER of those two lines, not their presence, because both present is exactly the broken version. VISIBILITY IS A TOGGLE, NOT A FILTER: usernames and avatars already appear on the leaderboards, the Collection and the realms map whatever visibility says (it gates HOLDINGS -- see gallery.php:33), so the default is everyone; the option exists because posting to an external platform is a different context from a page somebody chose to open, and that is the owner's call. ALSO ADDED: the harness now checks every admin_tabs() entry points at a file that EXISTS. $PAGES is only the four pages with write paths, so the read-only builders' filenames were never checked at all and a typo in the registry would have been a menu item that 404s silently. 5 mutations, all caught; two initially missed because 'catch' survived elsewhere in the file and because // comments were not stripped, so a commented-out admin_require() still satisfied a strpos for it. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (roster)     | DHC Fighters: sorting the roster, and the page's three columns | dhcfighters.php, plus dhcfighters-roster-harness.php. SORTS: newest/oldest/rarest/least-rare/deadliest/toughest/name. "Least rare" is the one that answers the actual question - which Fighter has the most to gain from an edit. POW AND HP COME FROM dhca_build_fighter(), the Arena's own engine, NOT a second formula computed here: a roster that called a Fighter deadly while the Arena disagreed would only be found out after a loss. dhcarena-engine.php is safe to require from this page (no $conn, no $_SESSION, no echo) but it pulls only dhcarena-roles.php - dhcf_rarity() comes from dhcfighters-lib.php, which the page already has and a harness loading the engine alone does not. The sort is CLIENT-SIDE: the cards are all rendered and paged in the browser already, each carrying its own wiring and 250px layer stack, so it reorders nodes with appendChild rather than re-rendering. Every comparator ends in the same two tiebreaks so equal Fighters never shuffle. Sorting resets to page 1 - landing mid-list shows you the cards you were already looking at. Stored in localStorage inside try/catch on BOTH read and write, and the stored key is validated against ORDERS before use. THE ROSTER LIVES IN THE PICKER COLUMN. Embedded, .picker is position:absolute filling the shell's height (canvas + buttons + ranks + the whole draw-order list), and one slot's worth of thumbnails almost never reaches the bottom of that -- on Torso it left most of a screen empty. dhc-assembler.php renders an optional `$dhca_aside` STRING after .grid; dhcfighters.php buffers the roster panel into it with ob_start()/ob_get_clean() BEFORE the include. **BEFORE THE INCLUDE MEANS BEFORE ANYTHING THE ASSEMBLER DEFINES EXISTS.** `$dhc_base` was set at the top of dhc-assembler.php and read by the card loop, so every buffered card shipped with an empty src and `Undefined variable $dhc_base` printed into its alt text. It is `dhcf_art_base()` in dhcfighters-config.php now -- the one file both the assembler and the page already require -- and both call it. (Not to be confused with dhcf_trait_name()'s own scan of the same four candidate directories for trait-index.json; different file, separate loop.)  A string, not an include, because the assembler has to stay something the public dhcsandbox.php can use with no roster, no session and no database -- hence the !empty() guard, and the harness checks the sandbox never sets it. The aside is `flex:0 1 auto; max-height:50%; min-height:0; margin-top:auto`. max-height stops a tall roster pushing the picker past the shell it is positioned inside; **margin-top:auto is what PINS it to the bottom** -- .grid is flex:1 and absorbs the slack, so it ends up there anyway today, but by side effect, and the roster must not move when you click from a 42-thumbnail slot to a 6-thumbnail one. Verified at 60, 6 and 0 thumbnails: top stayed at 590px in all three. SAVE FIGHTER LEADS THE ASSEMBLER rather than trailing it -- below the shell it sat under the draw-order list on one side and twenty roster cards on the other, which is the busiest place on the page for the one control the page exists to reach. CLICKING A CARD SCROLLS TO .shell WITH block:'start', not the canvas with block:'center': the canvas is 66vh, so centring it put its midpoint at the viewport's midpoint, i.e. almost the top of the PAGE, and you scrolled back down to see what you just clicked. `.shell{scroll-margin-top:12px}` keeps it off the edge; the navbar is position:relative so nothing covers it. NO DOWNLOAD ARROW on the roster cards -- right for a 150px card in a full-width panel, twenty arrows of noise at half that size and seven to a row; selecting a Fighter puts Download 1000px PNG on the canvas beside it and dhc-download.php is untouched. Cards shrink to a 96px floor in that column (it is a character select, not the gallery) and PER_PAGE is 20, not 12 -- at seven-plus to a row a dozen was two rows and a pager. **BELOW 900px NOTHING SCROLLS INSIDE ANYTHING.** Desktop keeps its inner scrollers and should: the picker is a real column beside a canvas, the roster is capped at half of it, the all-time ladder fills what monthly leaves. Stacked on a phone none of them has a column height to fill, so the same declarations only produce short windows onto long lists -- scroll the page to the traits, then scroll again inside them, with Fighters past the second row clipped away entirely. Mobile lifts `.picker{max-height}`, `.dhcf-aside{max-height,margin-top}`, `.grid{flex,overflow}` and `.lb-ath{overflow}`. TWO CASCADE TRAPS, both of which defeated a first attempt and both now pinned by the harness: (1) the assembler's `<style>` is emitted AT THE INCLUDE POINT, i.e. AFTER the host page's own, so an equal-specificity mobile override written in dhcfighters.php loses -- the picker and aside rules have to live in dhc-assembler.php; (2) within the assembler, the picker section redeclares `.grid` with its own `overflow:auto` further down the file, so the mobile `.grid` rule must sit AFTER that declaration, not with the other mobile rules higher up. Computed overflow-y at 400px was still "auto" until it was moved. The ladder's breakpoint is 900, not 760, or between the two it is stacked and still scrolling inside itself. Measured at 400/900: zero inner scrollers, zero clipped elements; at 1400: grid auto, aside 50%, ladder auto. THE COLUMNS BELOW SPLIT WHERE THE ASSEMBLER SPLITS. .dhcf-panels tracks `minmax(0,calc(var(--stage) + 2px))`, not an auto-fit 50/50, or the page runs two vertical rules a couple of hundred pixels apart one below the other. `--stage` is declared on `.dhcf-wrap` as well as `.shell` (same value, so .shell is fine inheriting it or declaring its own -- dhcsandbox.php has no .dhcf-wrap). **NO COLUMN GAP, AND THE EDGE THAT MATTERS IS THE RIGHT PANEL'S LEFT ONE.** The ladder's left edge and the picker's left edge are the same column of the page and the eye reads them as one line. A gutter makes alignment impossible -- it puts the right panel 14px past wherever the left one ends, so only ONE of the two edges can meet the picker's border. A first pass aligned the LEFT panel's right edge and it still read as crooked, because the misaligned edge was the one being looked at. .shell has always solved this with `gap:0` and a shared border; the panels row mirrors it: `column-gap:0; row-gap:14px`, first track `calc(var(--stage) + 1px)` (the +1 is .shell's own border -- measured shell 14, stage 15, panel 14), and `.dhcf-games{border-right:0}` so the seam is ONE rule, the ladder's left border, not two touching. Measured: picker and ladder rules both [653,654) at 1200/1400/2000. Below 900px they stack and .dhcf-games gets its right border back, or it renders as an open box. DISASSEMBLE IS A TRASH ICON, not a word: it was the longest label in a three-control row on a ~96px card and rendered as "DISA", and a clipped word reads as a layout fault while saying nothing. Inline SVG, not the emoji (different shape, weight and colour per platform, and it would not take currentColor on hover); `flex:0 0 auto` so it does not take a 1fr share from Edit and Rename; name kept in title + aria-label; the confirm() stays, because an icon carries less warning than the word did. Measured: Edit 23px + Rename 51px + trash 20px in a 111px row, nothing clipped on any card. TWO COLUMNS BELOW, NOT THREE:  all-time and monthly were separate panels and "Where traits drop" was a full-width block beneath them, so a desktop player scrolled past two half-empty ladders to reach the one thing answering "what should I play next". One ladder panel (monthly first - it is the short, live list) and the trait-drop list, in that order: drops before ladder, because the ladder is the only one of them about other people. The harness pins that order, the panel COUNT (two, not three -- and the count pattern must exclude the wrapper's own class="dhcf-panels", which carries the same prefix) and, now, that the two are SIBLINGS. Counting could not catch what shipped: a misplaced `</div>` left .dhcf-ladder nested inside .dhcf-games, so the count was still 2, PHP still linted clean, and the grid had one child -- both panels rendered full width and stacked, which is the exact layout the change existed to replace. The harness walks the divs and checks each panel's parent. The ladder fills its column because grid items stretch: .dhcf-ladder is a flex column, .lb-ath is flex:1 with overflow-y:auto. BOTH it and .body need **min-height:0** - a flex child's default min-height is auto, so without it the table refuses to shrink and overflows the panel instead of scrolling in it. Under 760px the nested scroller is turned off; a scroller inside a page that already scrolls is a trap. MEASURED, not assumed: headless Chrome against the real stylesheet gives 3 columns at 1400px, 2 at 1000, 1 at 400, no sideways scroll at any, and every sort verified by driving the select. Note Chrome's --window-size cannot go below ~500px, so a "400px" test silently lays out at 500 - use Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride AND give the fixture a viewport meta, or it lays out at 980. | DHC Fighters: earn traits, assemble Fighters, compete on rarity | dhcfighters.php (assembler/workshop), dhcgallery.php (the collection browser -- also requires dhcarena-engine.php, PURELY for numbers: dhca_build_fighter() is side-effect free, so health, power, crit and fighting style are derived per row and shown on every card, sortable by Deadliest/Toughest/Hardest hitting and filterable by kit. Rarity score answers what a Fighter is WORTH and these answer what it can DO; the two are near-uncorrelated by design and the gallery used to show only the first -- PUBLIC since it is the shop window, shows owners regardless of profile visibility since Fighters are not holdings), dhc-assembler.php (layering rules, shared with dhcsandbox.php -- both are platform pages now, and $dhca_mode vs $dhca_standalone is what keeps the sandbox's powers separate from its old skin), dhcfighters-config.php (game->category map, tier tables, score floors, DHC2F numbering from 421; DHCF_DAILY_CAP = 3 per source per day, overridable per source via 'cap'). Not every source is a game: maxingo, dailystreak and raids award from db.php directly rather than through the claim endpoint, which is why the cap is enforced inside dhcf_award() rather than only in ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php. DHCF_GAMES['dailystreak'] carries 'unlisted' => true: it stays in the array because everything functional reads it (dhcf_award, the cap, the claim endpoint, the notifier) and removing it would stop the streak paying, but dhcfighters.php skips it when rendering "where traits drop", which is a list of games to go and play. That also leaves exactly 12 rows, which is 2 clean rows of 6 at the repeat(6,...) breakpoint, dhcfighters-lib.php (scoring, consumable inventory, save/disassemble, boards), dhc-dropmodal.php (shared drop modal, DHC_DROP()), ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php (the trust boundary -- floors, daily cap and tier tables live server-side), ajax/dhc-save-fighter.php, ajax/dhc-update-fighter.php (edit in place -- keeps serial/name/created_at, recomputes traits+score+hash; dhcfighters.php?edit=<id> reloads with dhcf_available()'s \$ignore_id so the Fighter's own traits are selectable again), ajax/dhc-rename-fighter.php, ajax/dhc-delete-fighter.php. Tables dhc_trait_drops (append-only ledger; also the source of newest_trait_at, which is why disassembly can hard-delete a Fighter without opening a monthly-board replay) and dhc_fighters (holds LIVE Fighters only -- disassembly deletes the row and frees its serial, and dhcf_next_serial() hands out the lowest unused number from DHCF_SERIAL_START); see dhcfighters-schema.md. Rarity tiers derive from on-chain frequency across the 226 minted DHC2 Fighters (policy b31a34ca2b08bfc905d2b630c9317d148554303fa7f0d605fd651cb5) via dhc/gen-rarity.py -> dhcrarity.php. Layering exceptions and assembly offsets are recorded in dhc/LAYER-MANIFEST.json (gitignored with the art). A TRAIT SET CAN ONLY EXIST ONCE: dhcf_hash_taken()/dhcf_hash_holder() are checked INSIDE the save and update transactions (outside is a race both writers win), and disassembly frees the combination. This REVERSES an earlier documented decision that nobody should ever be blocked -- the old reasoning was that a player holding one background, one torso and one head has exactly one possible character. That risk is real but small: a dressed Fighter is 1 of ~89 billion, the bare minimum build 1 of 36,750, so the refusal message names the holder and says to change any one piece. dhcf_is_first_build() is gone and the FIRST badge with it (gallery, roster, save/edit messages); DHCF_ORIGINALITY_BONUS is deliberately KEPT and now applies to every Fighter, because removing it would make every stored rarity_score stale until dhcf-rescore.php ran over the lot. A SLUG IS ONLY UNIQUE WITHIN A CATEGORY: nine of them are both a head and a torso (dh-alien-punk, golden-cyborg, silver-cyborg, mk100, mk200, c73, dh-cop-2, inferno-wasp-guardian, planet-8tz-specter) and are different traits with different art, different rarity and separate ledger rows. dhcf_committed() and dhcf_shortfall() therefore key on "category|slug", and blockedReason() in dhc-assembler.php compares s.dir (the category) and not the slot key -- weapon/weaponBack and effects1/effects2 share a dir, which is exactly when a second copy SHOULD be counted. Keying by slug alone made the alien punk head spend the alien punk torso. dhcf_category_of() is the same mistake preserved for callers that have nothing but a slug; prefer the category you already have. Tethered weapon halves (scythe/sash, krusher/sash) live in DHCF_TETHERED + dhcf_tethered_partner(); dhcf_award() awards BOTH halves via dhcf_commit_drop(), marking the second with DHCF_PAIRED_MARK in source_detail. THREE queries count the daily cap and all must exclude that mark or they disagree: dhcf_award()'s own check, dhcf_drops_today() (drives the "N of N left today" line), and ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php. The assembler's COUPLE table generates its mutual pairs from DHCF_TETHERED; the axe -> morning star link stays hand-written there because it is one-way UI convenience, not a tether. Head/headgear incompatibilities live in DHCF_HEADGEAR_EXCLUDED_BY_HEAD + dhcf_headgear_blocked() in dhcfighters-config.php and are enforced in FOUR places, all of which must stay in step: dhcf_layers() (every PHP renderer), paint() in dhc-assembler.php (the canvas keeps its own JS copy), blockedReason() (the picker, both directions), and dhcf_save_fighter()/dhcf_update_fighter() (the trust boundary -- a Fighter saved before a rule landed still holds the pairing, so renderers drop the layer rather than assume it cannot occur). FULL-SIZE DOWNLOAD (dhc-download.php, dhc-download-harness.php). dhcf_render_fighter() now takes a $size; the endpoint asks it for 1000, which is the size of every file under dhc/web/1000, and streams the PNG as an attachment. No second composer -- the reason is spelled out at length in dhc-compose.php's header and applies verbatim here. THE 500 KEEPS THE UNSUFFIXED CACHE NAME (f<serial>-<hash>.png; other sizes get '@<size>'): putting the size in every filename orphans every render already in dhcrenders/ and makes Discord, the Arena and the landing thumbnails each recompose a thousand-pixel stack. $size is clamped to 100..1000 -- above 1000 is upscaled blur and the memory to match. MEASURED: 1000px is 673 KB and 124 ms cold, 2 MB peak, then cached forever by traits hash. The only size-dependent arithmetic is DHCF_NUDGE * $size / 1000, and the harness catches a regression there by rendering both sizes and comparing the 1000 scaled down against the native 500 (correct: mean channel difference 0.07-0.68 of 255; nudge hardcoded back to 500: 2.46; threshold 1.5). THE ARM CLIP IS NOT POLICED BY THAT and the harness says so: DHCF_ONE_ARM_SPLIT has a deliberate ~200px tolerance window, so a wrong split still lands on plain torso (0.68 -> 0.75, inside noise). The filename rule lives in dhcf_download_filename() in the LIBRARY, not the endpoint, so it is testable without a web request -- an unnamed Fighter's display name IS its serial, so the naive form gives DHC2F0123-DHC2F0123.png. OWNER ONLY -- and it shipped PUBLIC first, which was wrong. The argument was that the Collection is public and the 1000px layers are already served to anyone who opens a Fighter; a flattened, named, ready-to-post picture of somebody's assembly is still theirs to hand out and not a stranger's to take. Signed-out requests get 403; another player's serial gets 404, NOT 403, because 403 confirms the serial exists. Ownership is the WHERE clause (`AND user_id = %d`), the same idiom as ajax/dhc-rename-fighter.php -- not a read then a compare. TWO FORMS: `?serial=` for a saved Fighter you own, and `?build=<json>` for the arrangement on the assembler canvas, which usually has no saved row at all. The build form is gated on dhcf_owned() -- NOT dhcf_available(), because a trait committed to another saved Fighter is still a trait you own and still something you are entitled to a picture of. **USER 1 IS NOW EXEMPT FROM THAT OWNERSHIP CHECK, WHICH REVERSES THE COMMENT THAT USED TO SIT ON IT.** The old reasoning was that the build gate exists to stop the art being walked out a layer at a time, so it should not be lifted for anyone -- but that reasoning was attached to the wrong check. The thing holding that line is dhcf_missing_required(), which refuses anything that is not a whole Fighter and STILL APPLIES TO USER 1; the ownership check only ever answered 'are these pieces yours'. dhcsandbox.php unlocks every trait on purpose, so without the exemption the account that posts the Collection to X cannot photograph anything it arranges there. SANDBOX-ONLY IS A CONSEQUENCE, NOT ENFORCED: an endpoint cannot tell which page a build came from (Referer is absent and forgeable), so the exemption is per-ACCOUNT and simply has no effect elsewhere, because every other page only puts owned traits on the canvas. dhcsandbox.php sets $dhca_can_download for user 1 alone and the endpoint re-decides from the session, since a hidden button is not a permission. HARNESS: the stub skulliance.php used to hardcode user 7, which made a one-account rule untestable -- it now reads user_id out of the SessionCookie the test sends, and $get() takes an $as argument. Four mutations: exemption widened to everyone / removed entirely / completeness gate dropped for user 1 all caught; `$me <= 0` weakened to `$me < 0` is an EQUIVALENT MUTANT (a signed-out visitor is $me = 0, so they still cannot reach the $me === 1 branch and are still refused, just one line later) -- the assertion that matters there is 'a signed-out request claiming user 1 must be refused', which is covered. THE BUILD FORM ALSO REQUIRES A WHOLE FIGHTER -- dhcf_missing_required(), the same DHCF_REQUIRED trio a save insists on. Without it ?build={"head":"x"} renders that head alone on a transparent 1000px canvas and the endpoint becomes a way to walk Maxingo's art out one clean layer at a time; reported by the user, not caught in review. The ownership check does NOT cover this -- every extraction build uses traits the player genuinely owns. dhc-assembler.php mirrors DHCF_REQUIRED so the button says what is missing instead of navigating into a refusal, exactly as dhcfighters.php's save button does; the server is the authority. The ?serial= form needs no such gate: those traits come from a row dhcf_save_fighter() already validated. An unsaved build is named DHC-Fighter-build-<6 hex of the traits>.png so repeats reuse the filename instead of piling up as '(1)', '(2)'. The endpoint reads $_SESSION with an isset() guard the ordinary pages skip: they can afford a warning, this one is streaming binary and one notice in front of the PNG is a corrupt download -- that exact warning is how the harness caught the missing session_start() in its own stub. PLACEMENT: the assembler bar, next to 'Copy link to this build', because both act on the canvas; $dhca_can_download is a caller-set flag so the SANDBOX does not get one (it unlocks every trait and the endpoint would refuse them). Plus a corner arrow on your own roster cards -- that row (Edit/Rename/Disassemble) already fills a 150px card exactly, measured, and a fourth control pushed it 7px over with Disassemble at min-content, so the arrow is an absolutely-positioned SIBLING of .art (.art is a button and buttons do not nest). NOT in dhcgallery.php; there is a comment there saying why, because it is the obvious place to put one back. THE ENDPOINT IS TESTED OVER REAL HTTP, because what breaks it is not reachable from a function call: the response headers, and whether any stray output got in front of the PNG. db.php runs with display_errors on and prints nothing today -- one echo or one notice turns every download into a corrupt file with no error anywhere -- so dhc-download.php buffers the include and the harness's stub db.php deliberately prints while loading. The DATABASE IS STUBBED BY SHADOWING db.php ON include_path: the endpoint says `include 'db.php'` with no leading dot, so a db.php in the temp dir wins; everything else is the real library, renderer, art and headers. FOUR TRAPS, all hit writing it: (1) shell_exec() BLOCKS on a backgrounded `php -S`, redirects and all -- it reads the pipe until EOF and the server never closes it; use proc_open with the descriptors on /dev/null. (2) $http_response_header is deprecated in 8.5 and prints a notice per request, so the request goes over a raw socket. (3) THE SERVER MUST BE STARTED WITH -d output_buffering=0. With the ini's buffering on, PHP buffers db.php's echo for free and deleting the endpoint's own ob_start() still passed -- measured, a false green. (4) PHP's + on arrays keeps the LEFT value, so `$plain + array('arms' => ...)` silently tested the plain arms; use array_merge. Reverts confirmed to fail it: ob_start() removed (11 checks), Content-Disposition dropped, the 404 guard removed, Content-Length wrong. COMIC COVERS AND DEMOTED COMPANIONS (DHCF_COVER_EFFECTS, DHCF_COMPANION_OVER_COVER; dhc-layerorder-harness.php). DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER moves a companion to just before Arms -- but the slot order is arms, effects1, effects2, head, headgear, so 'below Arms' unavoidably means below Effects too, and the comment only ever claimed the first. A comic cover therefore drew OVER Code Sea Predator while drawing UNDER every ordinary companion: the same frame, two opposite answers depending on which pet the Fighter carried. NO POSITION IN A FLAT STACK IS BELOW ARMS AND ABOVE EFFECTS, so the cover moves down to just before the companion rather than the companion moving up. Only when the cover is currently ABOVE the companion -- an undemoted companion is already last, and moving the cover to meet it would put the frame over the head. Consequence worth knowing: arms now draw over the frame in this pairing (they already did for head, headgear and weapon). DH VISION SHOULDER CAM CAME OFF DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER ENTIRELY, reported the same day: it was demoted because a shoulder mount drawn in front looks stuck to the outside of the character, but below Arms is also below Effects, head and headgear, and headgear drawn over a shoulder cam looks worse than the problem it solved. No stack position is under the arms and over the head, so it went back to the default (last). DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER now holds one entry, which makes two rows of dhc-layerorder-harness.php's matrix coincide -- it says so rather than letting them read as two cases. THEN THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME REPORT: the cam must be above head/headgear AND under the arms, and no single stack position is both -- so DHCF_ARMS_OVER_COMPANION moves the ARMS to the very front when the cam is on, via dhcf_move_last(). It runs before the behind-torso rule (which would undo it) and skips arms in DHCF_ARMS_BEHIND_TORSO (accents flanking the body; hoisting them to the front is the opposite of the point). MEASURED: 16 of the 17 arm traits render differently with the rule on, so it is doing real work and not a no-op. WHAT IT COSTS: Arms then draws over head and headgear too; checked against the art (the arm traits hang at the sides and do not reach the face), and if a future arm raises a hand above the shoulder the fix is to take the cam off the list, not to stack a per-arm exception on a per-companion one. The harness now also asserts that every ALL-CAPS constant the extracted JS reads is defined in its prelude -- adding this rule made node die with a bare ReferenceError that said nothing about what was missing. RULE ORDER IN dhcf_layer_order() IS LOAD-BEARING: companion demotion, then arms-behind-torso, then this, then effects-behind-torso -- this one must run after the companion has moved (it targets where the companion landed) and before the behind-torso rule (which is more specific and must still win). MIRRORED IN THE CANVAS: layerOrder() in dhc-assembler.php is a second implementation that has drifted from PHP before (armsBehindTorso() once had an armless-variant escape hatch PHP did not), so dhc-layerorder-harness.php now brace-extracts the REAL layerOrder/armsBehindTorso/effectBehindTorso out of the page, feeds them the same json_encode'd constants the browser gets, and diffs them against dhcf_layer_order() over 14 trait sets; it also asserts the two base slot lists ($dhc_slots vs dhcf_slots()) are identical, that every DHCF_COMPANION_OVER_COVER entry is also in DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER, and that DHCF_COVER_EFFECTS matches the *comic-cover*.png files on disk. Needs node. TEST TRAP: PHP's + on arrays KEEPS the left value, so `$plain + array('arms' => ...)` silently tested the plain arms -- use array_merge.  **THE RARITY CURVE WAS PER-TIER AND SHOULD HAVE BEEN PER-TRAIT (gen-rarity.py WEIGHT, was SHARE).** 44/28/17/9/2 was a tier's SHARE of a category, split evenly among its members. That is only the same as a per-trait weight when the tiers hold similar numbers, and it inverts badly when one does not: a tier with ONE member hands that member the whole share. DH Samurai Suit was the only legendary torso, so it took all 9% and dropped more often than any individual common torso at 5.50% -- a legendary commoner than a common. Headgear was the mirror: one common taking all 44%, by far the most frequent single trait in the game. **8 of 65 tier comparisons across the collection were inverted**, worst an uncommon weapon at 28.57% against a common at 14.97%. Reported by the owner as 'your method is flawed', which it was -- this is a design error, not tuning. Read as per-trait WEIGHTS the same numbers are right and the property holds BY CONSTRUCTION: 44 > 28 > 17 > 9 > 2 whatever the membership. A tier's total is then whatever its members add up to, which is the honest answer -- one common headgear SHOULD mean commons are seldom what you get there. The absent-tier redistribution is gone with it: a tier with no members simply contributes no weight. **NOTHING CHECKED THE ONE PROPERTY THE LABELS PROMISE**, which is why this survived from the first rarity commit. dhc-assembler-harness.php now asserts, per category, that each tier drops less often than the one above it, AND that every member of a tier shares one rate (a tier whose members differ means the weight was not applied uniformly) -- with a 0.011 tolerance so rounding is not a failure. 3 mutations, all caught; a fourth landed on a single-member tier and was correctly ignored, which is worth knowing when writing mutations against this file. **AND neckfill HAD BECOME A TRAIT.** gen-rarity.py globs the art directories and skipped armless/noarms/-front but not the new neckfill/, so the shared black block was scored as a one-trait category, MYTHIC at 0.24% -- a drop nobody can receive for a slot nobody can fill. Third instance of that exact trap; the skip list and the harness both name all three now. **SCORES MOVE: about -12.6% on a median build**, because dhcf_trait_points() is derived from rate. Run dhcf-rescore.php (dry run by default, --confirm writes) or the leaderboard ranks people on stale numbers. Ranks can shift, since the traits that were most over-rated move most. **TRIED AND REJECTED: DRAWING THE HEAD UNDER THE TORSO** (shipped as 289b8631, reverted as 477a6559; the Beheaded Cyborg exception it needed, 25d95de8, reverted as 1b5fb314). The idea is sound and will occur to somebody again: a head's neck is drawn down past the jaw, and over the torso it lies ON the neckline instead of going into it. Under the torso that one junction reads better, and on the LEFT side of most torsos it clearly does. IT FAILS ON THE RIGHT. Any torso whose shoulder or pauldron rises past the jawline then clips the face, and that is a large share of them -- the owner's test shot has a skull with its jaw cut off by a shoulder. Fixing it per-torso would need an exception list the size of the torso list, which is the point at which a rule is not a rule. The residual oddity it would have fixed is only visible to someone studying the neckline deliberately; the breakage it causes is not. **AND IT DOES NOT REDUCE BACKGROUND BLEED AT ALL** -- the obvious thing to assume, and wrong: background shows wherever NEITHER layer covers, which no ordering can change. Measured both ways, identical to the pixel. The neck fill is what closes that; the two are unrelated halves of the same junction. THREE THINGS HAD TO MOVE WITH THE HEAD and are the reason this is more than a one-line experiment, worth knowing before trying it again: the neck fill (anchored under the head, had to ride whichever of head/torso came first), the Demon's claws (anchored on the head, which put them behind the chest), and the arms that cover those claws (which landed before the torso and vanished behind the body). Each was anchored to the head back when 'after the head' also meant 'over the body'. Two harness assertions changed MEANING rather than value for the same reason -- they expressed 'over the body' as 'after the head', which stopped being the same thing, and a green test that no longer checks what it claims is worse than a red one. THE NECK FILL (dhc/web/{1000,250}/neckfill/neckfill.png, emitted by dhcf_layers() and by the canvas as L-neckfill; dhc-neckfill.php resamples the small one). A head that does not reach the edge of the torso's socket leaves a gap, and because it is a hole in the character's OUTLINE the background shows through and the head reads as detached. ONE BLACK BLOCK, AUTHORED BY HAND, drawn immediately under the torso. Under is what makes one block work: everything it spills sideways or down is hidden by the torso, so it only has to be roughly right. Only drawn with a torso AND a head -- with no head the socket is open sky rather than a hole and the block is just a black plate. **THE INSTRUCTIVE PART IS WHAT THIS REPLACED.** It was a GENERATOR: the block computed as the region provably never open sky for any torso/arms/head combination, 17,290 flood fills, ~14 minutes, a shape covering 21.7% of the frame, plus a fingerprint of every input so staleness was detectable. All correct, all verified, and all wasted -- the thing is hidden under a torso and only has to be approximately right, and the authored block does the same job at 1.38% of the frame. Two specific errors worth naming: (1) proving an exact edge for something nothing can see; (2) generalising 'the gap' to 'any enclosed hole', which dragged in skeletal-robot's RIB gaps -- deliberate art, not a neckline, and they are what made the measured 'bleed' look like 90% of the frame. When the thing is hidden, approximate IS the requirement. What survives is the one step that must not be done by hand: the 250px copy is RESAMPLED from the 1000px one, because every other 250px trait is a resample of its 1000px art, so an eroded or redrawn copy would drift from the torso and head it sits between. The harness no longer checks a fingerprint (an authored block is derived from nothing, so there is nothing to go stale against) -- it checks the two sizes cover the same fraction of the frame in the same place, and that the block stays under 5% of the frame so a body-sized shape cannot come back. **AND dhc/ IS NO LONGER WHOLLY GITIGNORED.** It was, on the reasoning that it is the artist's unreleased source art. True of the art and false of the files WE author: trait-index.json and the neckfill PNGs are ours, and a file that only reaches the live site when somebody remembers to FTP it goes stale -- trait-index.json sat 12 entries and 8 renames behind for exactly that reason, so ten new traits were live reading 'Dh Samurai Helmet'. .gitignore now excludes dhc/ with a ladder of directory re-inclusions (git cannot re-include a path whose parent is excluded, hence the ladder) for those three paths only; every piece of trait art, _originals and rarity.json stay ignored, asserted by eye against git check-ignore. These deploy by git pull now, not FTP. DHCF_HEADGEAR_OVER_COMPANION / dhcf_headgear_over_companion() DHCF_HEADGEAR_OVER_COMPANION / dhcf_headgear_over_companion(): saber-tech-headgear draws in FRONT of dh-vision-shoulder-cam. A companion is last in dhcf_slots() so it normally covers the headgear, which is right for a pet that hovers; this helmet's pipe runs down past a cam BOLTED to the shoulder, and behind the mount it reads as the pipe passing through the character. Keyed by pair -- the cam still covers every other headgear and the helmet still goes under every other companion, both asserted. RUNS AFTER the DHCF_ARMS_OVER_COMPANION hoist: that rule moves the ARMS to the very end for the cam, and this one moves only the HEADGEAR past the companion, so the arms stay in front of both. Running it first also works today, but only because move_last() happens to follow -- written down rather than left accidental, and that mutation is the one equivalent of the seven (the final order is identical either way). THIS IS THE THIRD PAIRWISE EXCEPTION of this shape alongside DHCF_ARMS_OVER_HEADGEAR (samurai helmet under samurai arms) and DHCF_ARMS_UNDER_COMPANION (turret limbs under the sea predator). They are deliberately three constants and not one table keyed by slot pair: each is read by a DIFFERENT step of dhcf_layer_order() and fires at a different point in the sequence, so a merged table's rows would mean different things depending on which two slots they named. If a fourth lands, revisit that -- the sequence, not the data, is what resists merging. 7 mutations, 6 caught. ARENA DASHBOARD, THREE REPORTS IN ONE PASS. (1) ALL-TIME LADDER beside the monthly one: dhca_ladder() takes DHCA_LADDER_ALL_TIME ('*') and drops the season clause -- a sentinel, not '' or null, because both of those already mean 'this month' and a typo must not publish all-time standings under a monthly heading. ONE function, one predicate swapped, because a second function drifts about what a win is; dhcarena-harness.php EXECUTES both against a stub connection and asserts the two SQL strings are identical once the season clause is removed. `outcome IN (1,2)` replaces `<> 0`: same result, and indexable, which all-time needs since it has no season to narrow on -- dhcarena-schema.md carries the matching ALTER (idx_outcome). Markup went into dhca_ladder_html() so the two boards cannot differ by a column. (2) THE RIVAL LIST'S 330px WINDOW became max-height:calc(100vh - 340px). It scrolled nine rivals (445px of content) while the column had hundreds of pixels spare. The cap is NOT pointless -- Enter the Arena sits below the list and dhca_opponents() returns up to 24 -- so removing it outright buries the primary action; sizing it to the viewport keeps both. MEASURED at the reporter's own 1454x1046: 9 rivals no scroll with the button at y=650, 24 rivals scroll with the button still at y=911. (3) SELECTING THE ARCH NEMESIS used a different colour from selecting any other rival -- .a-foe.nem is declared AFTER .a-foe.sel with equal specificity, so it won on background and border and selection showed only as a faint inset ring. Cascade-order accident, not a decision. .a-foe.nem.sel added, and hover moved to the accent too: fixing only the click would give that one row a crimson hover snapping to ochre on selection, a bigger jump than the bug. Crimson stays on the resting border and the ARCH NEMESIS badge, which is where identity belongs -- feedback about what you DID has to read the same on every row. **AND THE HARNESS COULD NOT FAIL.** dhcarena-harness.php's whole arena-layout section counted failures into $ar_fail, printed them, and returned 0 -- so every assertion in it was advisory, and a mutation reinstating the 330px window printed FAIL while the run 'passed'. Found only by mutation-testing; a harness that cannot fail looks exactly like one that is passing. It exits(1) now. 6 mutations, all caught. CODE SEA PREDATOR CAME OFF DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER, which is now EMPTY -- and so is DHCF_COMPANION_OVER_COVER. Reported from the Collection with five Fighters showing it: demoted below Arms is also below the head and the HEADGEAR, so Alien Predator and Alien Skull buried the creature's own head. 'Wraps the body and still reads correctly underneath' was true of the torso and false of the head. It takes the shoulder cam's fix -- stay last, move the ARMS forward (DHCF_ARMS_OVER_COMPANION) -- which is the second time that trade has been made and the second member of DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER to leave it for the same reason. BOTH LISTS ARE KEPT, EMPTY: nothing about either mechanism was wrong, both members just wanted the head more, and deleting the rules means rediscovering them. DHCF_COMPANION_OVER_COVER is now dead by construction -- it only ever mattered for a DEMOTED companion, and an undemoted one is already above both effects slots. NEW: DHCF_ARMS_UNDER_COMPANION + dhcf_arms_under_companion(), arms that stay BEHIND a companion that otherwise hoists them -- 'code-sea-predator' => turret-blue-cyborg-limbs. The note on DHCF_ARMS_OVER_COMPANION advises against per-arm exceptions, and this is the opposite case to the one it warns about: that warning is about an arm that COVERS the companion (answer: take the companion off the hoist list, the arm is doing its job), this is an arm bulky enough to SWALLOW it. Those limbs are independently on DHCF_TWOPART_OVER_ARMS, which puts the whole Demon in front of them for the same reason -- two separate readings of the art agreeing is why it is a per-arm exception rather than a second opinion. Keyed by companion so it cannot become blanket: a mutation widening it to any companion is caught by the shoulder cam, which still wants the turret limbs in FRONT. **AND THE HARNESS WENT VACUOUS WHEN THE LISTS EMPTIED.** $under = DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER[0] became null, so five matrix rows silently turned into 'a Fighter with no companion', passed, and proved nothing -- the same failure mode as a trimmed list, and the reason the fighter COUNT is pinned in dhc-mint-harness.php. Those rows are now built only when a member exists, the gap is PRINTED, and what is asserted instead is that both code paths still exist in PHP and in the canvas. Four other rows that merely used a demoted companion as SCENERY (cover-in-slot-2, behind-torso fx, behind-torso arm, one-sided arm) were switched to an ordinary companion -- they never cared which one, and pinning them to a demoted one made four unrelated rules hostage to a list that is now empty. FIXTURE TRAP WORTH REMEMBERING: the first version of these assertions used arms 'robo-arms', which DOES NOT EXIST. dhcf_layer_order() reads the trait array and not the disk, so every assertion passed -- it was only caught because the render built from the same fixture drew no arms at all. The harness now PICKS an ordinary arm off disk (not behind-torso, not the turret, art present) instead of naming one. 7 mutations, all caught. CLICK A TRAIT IN THE FIGHTER PANEL TO FILTER THE COLLECTION TO IT (?trait=<category>:<slug>; dhcf_trait_filter() + dhcf_traits_include() in dhcfighters-lib.php, the chip in dhcgallery.php, the links in dhc-fighter-modal.php, covered by dhc-gallery-harness.php). **THE KEY IS CATEGORY:SLUG AND THAT IS THE ENTIRE RISK.** Nine slugs exist in two categories at once -- dh-alien-punk, golden-cyborg, silver-cyborg, mk100, mk200, c73, dh-cop-2, inferno-wasp-guardian, planet-8tz-specter -- different art, different rarity, separate ledger rows (dhcf_committed() keys on 'category|slug' for the same reason). A slug-only filter looks correct on every other trait in the game and silently pools these two, reporting a count belonging to neither; the harness asserts the head mk100 does NOT match a Fighter wearing the torso mk100, and asserts the nine still exist so the check cannot quietly become vacuous. MATCHED ON CATEGORY, NOT SLOT: weaponBack and effects1/effects2 share a category, which is wanted -- a Fighter carrying the trait in either slot IS wearing it, and a slot-keyed match makes half the collection invisible to an effects filter (caught as a mutation). VALIDATED AGAINST dhcf_rarity(), not merely sanitised: a well-formed but unknown pair would render an empty grid under a chip naming a trait that does not exist, which reads as 'nobody has this' rather than 'there is no such thing'. THE RULES LIVE IN THE LIBRARY so they can be EXECUTED by a harness with no database, rather than pattern-matched out of a page that includes db.php -- the page is now two function calls. The link is a PLAIN <a>, not a click handler: the panel is shared with dhcfighters.php where there is no grid to rewrite, the Collection's other filters are already URLs, and the result is linkable. It carries NO other parameter -- arriving with somebody else's tier filter still applied would hide most of the answer. CSS NOTE: the trait name is its own <span class=nm> because a bare text node cannot be selected and text-decoration on the <a> propagates to children with no way to drop it; the link inherits body colour rather than the ochre used elsewhere in the panel, because forty ochre rows would bury the tier chips that are the thing being compared. A/B'd at 340px against the previous markup: layout identical. **THAT A/B WAS STILL WRONG, AND SHIPPED A BUG.** It rendered the panel's own <style> only, so it missed dist/flexbox.css's unscoped `a{color:#00c8a0;text-align:center}` -- the cell was a <span> until it became a link, and .tn re-stated color and text-decoration but not text-align, so the whole trait list shipped CENTRED. Reported by the owner. Any anchor added inside a scoped component has to re-state BOTH, and a preview that does not link flexbox.css cannot see it (the standing note about previews needing the real stylesheet is about exactly this and was not applied). dhc-gallery-harness.php now checks the global `a{}` block FIRST and only then demands the counter-rules, so if flexbox.css ever drops them the guard is dropped too rather than pinned forever as a workaround to a problem that no longer exists -- verified by mutating the global away and confirming the harness stops requiring the counter. 9 mutations, all caught. ALSO FIXED WHILE VISIBLE HERE: eight trait-index.json names broke the collection's own MK/DH convention (Mk100, Dh Cop #03) and are now MK100 / DH Cop #03 -- safe because gen-rarity.py matches on norm(), which lowercases, so no trait's on-chain count or tier moves; the rename script asserts norm(old) === norm(new) for every entry rather than assuming it. **THE MINTED COLLECTION OUTRANKS EVERY REFUSAL RULE, AND NOTHING CHECKED THAT (dhc-mint-harness.php, dhc-mint-traits.json).** The platform refuses pairings in three places -- DHCF_HEADGEAR_EXCLUDED_BY_HEAD, DHCF_COMPANION_EXCLUDED_BY_HEAD and dhcf_arms_weapon_clash() -- all judged by eye from the art, which is the right way to FIND them and no authority at all against the 226 minted DHC2 Fighters. A rule forbidding a Fighter that demonstrably exists is wrong by definition. Owner reported one: beheaded-cyborg + trojan-detection-mask is DHC2F043. Confirmed by pulling the CIP-25 metadata under the DHC2 policy from Koios (the token and endpoint db.php already uses) and checking ALL 14 head/headgear exclusions, not just the reported one -- only two minted Beheaded Cyborgs wear headgear at all (DHC2F038 Cyber Purr Helmet, never excluded, and DHC2F043), so the other 13 have no mint precedent and stand. The arms/weapon rule is clean against the mint too. THE ART AGREES ONCE YOU RENDER IT: the Trojan mask is a full faceplate enclosing the neck stump, so unlike a helmet it needs no skull beneath it -- the exclusion rule is 'needs a skull to rest on', not 'beheaded cyborg wears nothing'. dhc-mint-traits.json is a COMMITTED snapshot (226 fighters, slugs) resolved with gen-rarity.py's own ALIAS/MERGE/norm/variants logic -- reimplementing that matching loosely left 40+ names unresolved, and reusing it exactly leaves 26, all genuinely 1/1 pieces and variants with no platform art, which gen-rarity.py's own comments already document. It is derived from PUBLIC CHAIN DATA, not from the art, so it is tracked even though dhc/ is not -- which is the whole point, since a harness that needs an FTP'd art folder cannot run anywhere else. The harness pins the fighter COUNT at 226 first, because a truncated snapshot passes every exclusion check for the wrong reason (it contains no counter-examples) -- and that guard is what catches someone quietly deleting DHC2F043 instead of fixing the rule. 5 mutations, all caught. REGENERATE from chain if the policy ever mints again; never hand-edit. NEW ART GOES LIVE IN THREE FILES AND NOTHING USED TO CHECK THEY AGREED: the PNGs under dhc/web/1000 (FTP, gitignored), dhcrarity.php (regenerated by gen-rarity.py) and dhc/web/trait-index.json (display names, written at import). dhcf_draw() pools from the RARITY table, so art with no rarity row renders and can never be earned; a rarity row with no art drops and draws nothing; and a missing NAME is the quiet one -- dhcf_trait_name() falls back to ucwords(str_replace('-',' ')), which is why ten traits went live reading 'Dh Samurai Helmet' and 'Dhc2 Comic Cover 4' in the picker, the modal, the Discord post and the share text. dhc-assembler-harness.php now asserts all three describe the same set (207 traits), SKIPPING entirely when dhc/web is absent so a checkout without the art stays runnable, and excluding torso-noarms + companion-front, which are derived art keyed by another trait's slug and must never have rows. It immediately found two pre-existing gaps: weapon/scythe-sash and weapon/skull-krusher-sash, the tethered BACK halves, had art and rarity but no name (their `source` now names the piece they were cut out of, since they were never delivered as their own file). ARENA EFFECTS ARE INFERRED FROM THE SLUG and that had NO test at all until dhc-roles-harness.php. dhcarena-roles.php decides every trait's role from ~60 keywords tried IN ORDER, so a rule inserted in the wrong place silently reclassifies traits. Of the ten new traits: the four helmets all read 'helmet' -> ARMOUR (tank-helmet included, which is the toughness effect it should have); dh-samurai-augmented-arms -> WEAPON; dhc2-comic-cover-4 and earth-recovery-system-interface -> PLAIN, which is CORRECT and not a miss -- covers 1-3 and the other decorative effects are all plain, and plain pays +3% health, which happens to suit a 'recovery system'. **TORSO ROLE IS DEAD DATA**: dhca_build_fighter() loops only head/headgear/arms/effects1/effects2/companion, and a torso's health comes straight from its rarity multiplier -- so dh-samurai-suit landing on PLAIN costs nothing, and 22 of 26 torsos are plain anyway. The one real miss was demon-companion -> ENERGY via the 'demon-' keyword (added for occult EFFECTS like digital-666-smoke); it is a creature standing behind the Fighter, every other living companion is BEAST, and it is now in dhca_role_overrides() beside dh-destroyer -- the same class of error, a keyword matching what a piece is CALLED rather than what it does. The harness also PRINTS every trait that falls to PLAIN in a slot whose role is read; that list is the thing to review when new art lands. 10 mutations, 6 caught, 4 genuine equivalents (detect and mask both resolve to optic; 'vision' catches the shoulder cam before '-cam' is reached) -- every mutation that changes a trait's actual role is caught. A TIER WITH ONE MEMBER TAKES THE WHOLE TIER SHARE, which is a structural quirk of the designed 44/28/17/9/2 curve and is worth knowing before assigning rarity by hand: dh-samurai-suit is the ONLY legendary torso, so it holds all 9% and is therefore the MOST COMMON torso in the game, ahead of the eight commons at 5.50% each. The same quirk already existed unflagged in headgear, where cyber-purr-helmet is the only common and holds all 44%. Moving the suit to epic would put it at 4.25% and drag the three existing epics down from 5.67%; left to the owner, who assigns these by hand. DHCF_ARMS_OVER_HEADGEAR / dhcf_arms_over_headgear(): ONE PAIR where arms draw in front of headgear (dh-samurai-helmet + dh-samurai-augmented-arms). The helmet's neck flare and the suit's pauldrons share a band and the flare belongs behind the plate. KEYED BY PAIR FROM MEASUREMENT, not caution: masks were compared and both generalisations rendered and rejected -- 'helmet under every arm' changes 17 combinations and skeletal-stabber (28.9% of the helmet) swallows it; 'samurai arms over every headgear' changes 22 and dh-manta-ray (12.9%) and tank-helmet (10.5%) are broad flat pieces that end up behind the shoulder spikes. MEASUREMENT TRAP: most trait PNGs are PALETTE images, so imagecolorat() returns an INDEX and the alpha bits are meaningless -- the first overlap run reported an identical figure for all 17 arms, which is the tell. imagepalettetotruecolor() first. THE MOVE IS FORWARD-ONLY, and that guard is the whole subtlety: DHCF_ARMS_OVER_COMPANION has already pushed the arms to the very END for the shoulder cam, and moving them to just-after-headgear FROM there pulls them back BEHIND the companion and silently undoes it. Shipped broken in the first cut and caught by the harness case 'samurai pair + cam', which is a real buildable combination. So it moves only when arms currently sit BEFORE headgear. RUNS LAST OF THE ARM MOVES, which is also deliberate: with the demon, dh-samurai-helmet is on DHCF_TWOPART_HANDS_OVER_HEADGEAR (claws over the helmet) and dh-samurai-augmented-arms is on DHCF_TWOPART_HANDS_UNDER_ARMS (arms over the claws), and headgear < claws < arms is the ONLY order satisfying both -- which requires arms after headgear. Without this rule those two demon rules contradict each other on this pairing and the claws land on top of the arm meant to cover them, so this fixes that combination as well as the plain one. The canvas mirror hoists layerOrder()'s mv() helper out of the two-part block rather than writing the splice arithmetic twice. 7 mutations, all caught; the harness asserts the pair moves, that NEITHER generalisation holds, that the cam rule survives, and (through dhcf_layers(), since the claws are not a slot) headgear < claws < arms. THE DEMON: A COMPANION THAT IS ATTACHED, NOT FLOATING (DHCF_COMPANION_TWO_PART, dhcf_companion_two_part(); art in dhc/web/{1000,250}/companion/ AND companion-front/ under the SAME slug, the pairing torso-noarms already uses). One trait, two layers: the body goes behind the TORSO (not merely behind the arms, which is what DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER does) and the claws come back over the front. companion-front is NOT a slot and must never become one -- dhcf_slots() is the ordering authority and a tenth entry there would make it separately equippable, separately spendable and separately scored; gen-rarity.py also had to learn to skip any directory ending '-front' or the Demon would be counted twice. FIVE EXCEPTIONS, ALL ESTABLISHED IN THE SANDBOX BY EYE, none derivable from the rules: DHCF_TWOPART_BODY_OVER_TORSO (inferno-wasp-guardian, red-cyborg -- wings/plating wide enough to swallow the demon, so it comes FORWARD of the torso instead of behind it), DHCF_TWOPART_HANDS_UNDER_ARMS (skeletal-stabber, cyber-666-knight-armor, connected-protector-limbs, deflektor, dh-samurai-augmented-arms -- the ARMS move after the head so they cover the claws), DHCF_TWOPART_OVER_ARMS (turret-blue-cyborg-limbs, robo-crusher-arms -- the WHOLE demon moves in front), DHCF_TWOPART_HANDS_OVER_HEADGEAR (merged, ww2-helmet, cyber-insekt-helmet, xlon-implant, dh-samurai-helmet -- read via dhcf_twopart_hands_after(), which returns the SLOT the claws are emitted after, 'headgear' for these five and 'head' for everything else; hardcoding 'head' is the regression and the harness mutation-tests it), and DHCF_COMPANION_EXCLUDED_BY_HEAD + dhcf_companion_blocked() (beheaded-cyborg, cyborg-stryker-02 -- nothing to grip, so BOTH halves drop, never one). NOTE 'cyber insekt helmet' in the owner's list was read as the EXISTING cyber-insekt-helmet, not Maxi's new dh-insekto-helmet; confirm if the Demon ever looks wrong under the latter. dhcf_move_after() is the new mirror of dhcf_move_before(). Enforced in the same four places the headgear rule is, and the picker blocks BOTH directions (whichever slot is filled first rules the other out, with None always the way back). dhcarena-practice.php draws the companion last and re-rolls it, exactly as it already did for headgear. **AND THE GD RENDERER STOPPED BEING A THIRD IMPLEMENTATION.** dhcf_render_fighter() walked dhcf_layer_order() and re-derived the armless swap, the one-arm clip, the nudge and the claws alongside dhcf_layers() doing the same -- so every rule had to be written twice and the Demon's exceptions landed in one copy before the other. It now PAINTS dhcf_layers() and translates nothing but the nudge unit (that function reports a percentage of the canvas because the still renderer positions with CSS; GD wants pixels). THIS FIXED A LIVE BUG NOBODY HAD REPORTED: the old loop never called dhcf_headgear_blocked() at all, so a Fighter holding a refused pairing was drawn WITH the headgear in Discord, the Arena and the landing thumbnails while the Collection dropped it -- verified by rendering beheaded-cyborg + merged under both versions. VERIFICATION METHOD WORTH REUSING: render the same twelve builds with the pre-refactor file (git show HEAD:) and the new one and diff the md5s -- the seven builds no exception touches came out BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is what proves the collapse changed nothing it should not, and the five exception builds differed, which is the point. dhc-layerorder-harness.php now also asserts the GD renderer still contains no dhcf_layer_order() call, pins the MEMBERSHIP of all five lists by name (a loop over a list cannot notice the list being trimmed -- two mutations slipped through until this was added), and compares twoPartHandsAfter() canvas-vs-PHP for both kinds of headgear. 23 mutations, all caught. **THE MOBILE GAMES CHIP (dhcfighters.php#drops) LANDED AT THE TOP OF THE PAGE, AND IT IS AN ORDERING BUG.** Two fixes were reasoned from the source and shipped wrong before this one; the first was honest that it was a mitigation, not a diagnosis. The server renders EVERY roster card and rpaint() then hides all but one page of 20 (.dhcf-card.off{display:none}) -- and the landing code ran BEFORE rpaint(), so it scrolled to where #drops sat in a document containing the whole roster, rpaint() removed up to 40 cards of height above it, and the browser clamped scrollTop to the new maximum. With 20 Fighters or fewer nothing is hidden, nothing shrinks and the anchor always worked, which is exactly why it reproduced for a player with a deep roster and never for me. The landing now runs AFTER rpaint() and keeps a bounded rAF watch (2s, extended 400ms past load) that re-lands whenever the target has drifted more than 2px. NO EARLY EXIT when it 'looks right': that is precisely when the previous version stopped. IT YIELDS TO THE READER on wheel/touchstart/keydown/scroll -- a retry loop that fights the person reading the page is worse than the bug -- and its own scrollIntoView is flagged so it does not mistake itself for the reader. The watch does NOT depend on the load event, which was the old version's only correction. **?landdebug=1** draws an on-page readout (hash, whether the target matched, scrollY, panel top, doc height, cards total vs hidden, watch state) because this only ever reproduced on somebody else's phone, where a console is least reachable, and a hash with no matching element is a completely different bug from a scroll that got undone. dhcfighters-anchor-harness.js RUNS the real block against a fake DOM that actually shrinks, rather than reading it -- source-reading is what produced the two wrong fixes. Three traps it caught in itself: the first 'the old order fails' guard passed because the new code self-corrects in either order, so the discriminator is now the old two-liner run through the same world with no load event (its only correction) rather than an invented failure; a fixture modelling roster art growing after load was DELETED because .dhcf-card .art is aspect-ratio:1 with the image absolutely positioned, so those cards cannot change height and I would have been fixing an imaginary problem; and the yield test dispatched the event without moving the page, so the loop re-scrolled to where it already was and deleting the wheel handler passed -- the reader now moves away first. Plus the usual: a bare /settled/ matched the block's own COMMENT explaining why there is no early exit, so comments are stripped before that assertion, and the block lift closed on '  })();' which matched the nested debug IIFE's deeper indentation and lifted something that would not parse. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (rank preview) | Live rank while assembling | dhc-ranks.php (dhcf_rank_values / dhcf_rank_pool / dhcf_rank_of / dhcf_rank_build), dhcf_rank_axes() in dhcfighters-config.php, ajax/dhc-build-stats.php, the #ranks strip in dhc-assembler.php, dhc-ranks-harness.php. WHAT IT ANSWERS: saving SPENDS the traits, so "what did that piece actually do" was a question you could only ask afterwards. The strip shows where the build on the canvas would place on the four axes the Collection sorts by. ONE DEFINITION OF EACH AXIS: dhcgallery.php named Rarest/Deadliest/Toughest/Hardest hitting first and now READS them from dhcf_rank_axes(), and takes hp/pow/might from dhcf_rank_values() -- passing in the dhca_build_fighter() it already has, so it stays one pass per row. Deadliest = health x power, the Collection's own shorthand, not an engine stat. dhcf_rank_axes() lives in dhcfighters-config.php and NOT in dhc-ranks.php: the assembler needs the labels and already requires config, while dhc-ranks.php drags in the library and the Arena engine -- a caller that stubs dhcf_rarity() (the assembler's own harness does) then gets a fatal redeclare, which is how that was found. THE POOL IS EVERY SAVED FIGHTER read in full, because hp and pow are derived from traits at read time and no ORDER BY can answer 'how many are tougher' -- the same O(n) pass dhcgallery.php already makes per page view, but the canvas asks per keystroke, so it is cached as JSON under dhcrenders/ keyed on COUNT(*)+MAX(updated_at) (temp-then-rename; old pools swept after an hour). Ranking is COMPETITION ranking over a DESCENDING list -- ties take the best place -- by binary search, property-tested against a naive count over 6,000 random lookups with a deliberately small value range so ties are common. THE POOL USES STORED rarity_scores and the BUILD a freshly computed one, deliberately: the preview answers 'if I saved this now', and the fresh number is what would be saved. EDITING passes the Fighter's id so its own row is dropped from the pool, or it competes with itself.
