| ui-selectors.php + my-nfts + store  | Platform redesign, pages 1-2 | THE SAFETY NET FIRST. `php ui-selectors.php --snapshot <page> > before.json`, edit, then `--check before.json`: it reports any id or class the page stopped emitting that JavaScript anywhere in the repo still reaches for, INCLUDING the ~140 built by concatenation (getElementById('p' + n + '-image')), which a grep cannot see. Diff-based against the page's own before-state, so there are no false positives from the 771 global selectors. It is static -- rendering needs the database -- so it proves a hook still EXISTS, not that the page still works; drive the page for that. Verified in both directions: silent on an unreferenced id, FAIL+exit 1 on a referenced one.

MY-NFTS.PHP IS THE FIRST PAGE MIGRATED. The pattern for the other fourteen: (1) snapshot, (2) add a data-returning sibling in db.php rather than editing the renderer -- getNFTsData() has the same filters, columns and ORDER BY and NO output, while getNFTs() is untouched because showcase.php and diamond-skulls.php still call it, (3) the page owns its markup and a scoped `<style>`, (4) --check, (5) keep every JS hook. Result here: 9 ids and 17 classes kept, 0 removed, 13 added; db.php +49 lines, 0 removed. THE PLATFORM CARD RULES ARE OVERRIDDEN, NOT EDITED: .nft carries `width:25%; float:left` and .nft-data `text-align:right` -- a fixed four-column float grid from before there was a phone audience -- and both are overridden in the PAGE's inline CSS, so showcase.php renders the identical markup exactly as it did. Do not 'fix' them in flexbox.css until every caller has migrated. NEGATIVE-OFFSET HACKS ARE THE FIRST THING TO CHECK ON EVERY PAGE: flexbox.css claws back space with `position:relative; top:-Npx` in at least eight places -- #filter-nfts is -35px and #filtered-content is -40px, both tuned to close the gap under a bare one-line <h2>. Give a page a taller masthead and they stop closing a gap and start landing content ON TOP of something; here the filter covered the stat tiles and the grid covered the context line. Grep `top: -` before changing any page header. AND SAY REPEATED FACTS ONCE: the rate is per COLLECTION, so a filtered view was printing the same rate, project and collection on all 24 cards -- four of five lines identical, which is what made the grid read as a database report. Fields uniform across the page are hoisted to one context line under the title and dropped from the cards, leaving art and a name. SQUARE CORNERS: DHC Fighters and the Arena have none, and they are the pages that read as modern; the 2.5rem on .nft and 10px on .nft-data are the old platform's. New work is hard-edged -- and the radius is not all in one place: .nft is 2.5rem, .nft-data 10px and .content 10px, so squaring a page means overriding the CONTAINER too, not just the card. ALIGNMENT IS INHERITED, NOT SET: .main carries `text-align:center` for the whole column, so a new element that does not state its own alignment floats centred over a left-aligned h2. State it. NOTHING MAY CROP THE ART: .nft-image is `min/max-height:170px; overflow-y:hidden` with `.nft img { max-height:165px }` -- a fixed window that hard-clips any piece taller than it, which was survivable while the image was a thumbnail and is not once the art leads the card. my-nfts.php replaces it with a 1/1 box and object-fit:contain: uniform grid at 1,483 items, and a tall or very wide piece is letterboxed and shown WHOLE rather than cropped. Verified against 1:1, 1.59:1, 2.06:1 and a 1500x1674 tall piece in one grid.

STORE.PHP IS PAGE TWO, same pattern: getItemsData() added (identical filter, query and ORDER BY, plus the payment options, per-option balance, affordability and already-purchased flags -- that arithmetic is business logic and stays in db.php; only markup moved). getItems() is untouched and still has its one caller, so store.php reverts in a line. renderBuyButton() is still called FROM the page: it emits a form that posts a purchase and must not be duplicated into a template. Checked what the page inherits BEFORE building, which caught four of the five traps for free. THE FIFTH WAS SPECIFICITY: `.nft span { display:block }` is 0-1-1 and beats a bare `.st-meta` at 0-1-0, so new spans inside a .nft rendered as blocks and ran their text together ("Sinder Skullz2 left"). Prefix new card classes with #filtered-content. The store card also now says what a viewer can AFFORD -- a greyed button with the balance beside it, and a masthead count -- which the old page made you read every card to work out. |
| games-skull-swap.md (cascade) | A match left sitting on the board | cascadeTiles() in skullswap.php, checked by skullswap-cascade-harness.js. REPORTED FROM A REAL ROUND: "cleared a bunch of diamond bombs and the cascade left a match of 3 just sitting there". THE RE-CHECK WAS GATED ON `moved || matchCheckCount < 2` AND THAT COUNTER IS A GRACE ALLOWANCE, NOT A BRAKE -- it means "nothing moved, but look twice more anyway", a cheap net for a match that settles a beat late. Nothing stops the cascade chain because nothing RESTARTS it: the chain only continues when resolveMatches() finds a match and the handler that clears it calls cascadeTiles() again, so a check that finds nothing ends there regardless of the counter. Read it as a brake (easy to do) and the fix looks dangerous; read it as grace and the bug is obvious. THE BUG: every cascade incremented it, including productive ones, and only a check that found NOTHING reset it. A bomb chain runs a cascade per blast, so by the time the board settled the grace was spent -- and the one check that mattered never ran. The else branch reset the counter, scheduled nothing, and abandoned whatever was on the board. FIX: ask the board (`const pending = moved ? true : this.checkMatches().hasMatches`) instead of guessing from a counter, and let anything productive reset the grace so it is available when actually needed. checkMatches() only READS the board and is called only when nothing moved -- the one case where it changes the answer. REPRODUCIBLE ON DEMAND, which is what the harness pins: a full board that cannot fall, one three on it, matchCheckCount at 2. Before the fix it resolves at counts 0 and 1 and is abandoned at 2 and above. HARNESS TRAP, hit immediately: asserting "no match exists afterwards" is flaky in BOTH directions, because refills are random and a fresh three can appear while the loop is still legitimately working. The planted match uses an icon that exists nowhere else, and its absence is the assertion. AND WHAT THE TESTS CANNOT SEE: removing the counter's reset, or the counter from the condition entirely, changes nothing observable once `pending` is in front of them -- both were checked by hand rather than left to look covered. The idle-board test is still worth keeping: it is what would catch somebody making the scheduled callback re-enter cascadeTiles(), which WOULD spin. |
| site-header.php *(shared)*          | Public site navigation | site-header.php is the ONE public nav, included by homepage.php, by every public game landing page, and by skullpaper.php FOR ANONYMOUS READERS ONLY -- header.php's entire navbar sits inside `if(isset($name))`, so a stranger arriving at the Skull Paper from search got an EMPTY nav and no way back to anything; a member already has the platform navbar and two stacked bars would be worse than the bug. That page also needs `#skullpaper .sp-side { top: 62px }` when the public menu renders, because its contents sidebar is sticky at top:10px and would scroll under a sticky 51px bar -- the same class of collision as match3rpg.php's exit button. Checked: no id, class or function collisions with header.php or the platform CSS, and .container has no overflow, so sticky survives inside it. Included by (dhcgame, match3rpg, cryptcrawlgame, cryptconquestgame, skullracergame, guardiansgame). It used to be markup+CSS inside homepage.php alone, which is why a visitor landing on a game page had no way to reach the rest of the site. SELF-CONTAINED (own CSS under an `sh-` prefix) because those pages share no stylesheet -- each carries its own inline CSS. NO session, NO db.php: it is navigation, and homepage.php is included by WordPress. ROOT-RELATIVE LINKS, NEVER ABSOLUTE -- cookies here are host-only, so an absolute www. link in SHARED nav would log out everyone browsing the bare domain on every page it appears on; the homepage's old nav had absolute URLs and this move fixes them. STICKY, NOT FIXED: fixed is out of the layout, so every including page would need its own top padding, wrong the moment the bar wraps on a phone. That is why homepage.php's .hp-hero padding-top dropped 120->56 and 96->40 (it was reserving height for the old fixed bar) and why match3rpg.php's #m3-exit moved to top 62px -- it is z-index 99990 against the nav's 9990, so at 8px it covered the skull mark. Two optional knobs set BEFORE the include: $sh_on_home (keeps section links as in-page anchors) and $sh_links (the homepage passes its full 9; the default is the short set a game page wants). skullswap.php, cryptcrawl.php and cryptconquest.php are NOT in the list -- they include db.php and already wear the platform header. |
| platform.md (PWA stalls)           | Installed-app loading failures | service-worker.js, offline.html, header.php (#nav-loader), plus pwa-offline-harness.js. THE INSTALLED APP IS display:standalone, SO A DEAD NAVIGATION HAS NO WAY OUT -- no address bar, no reload, no back. Reported as "always closing the app and reopening it". Three things compounded: (1) **#nav-loader covered the whole screen on every internal link click and nothing ever took it down** -- its bar animated 12s then sat at 90% forever, with no cancel, retry or explanation. That was the biggest of the three and is pure client-side. It now shows a stall panel after STALL_MS (9s: under ~5s it fires on merely slow pages, over ~15s people have already force-quit), naming the likely cause from navigator.onLine / connection.effectiveType / downlink, with **Try again** and **Stay on this page** -- the page underneath never went anywhere. Going offline mid-wait short-circuits the timer (measured: panel in ~120ms instead of 9s). bfcache restore clears the timer or the panel can appear over a page that already loaded. (2) **the service worker was a no-op**, so a failed navigation gave a blank frame. It now intercepts navigations only and falls back to offline.html. **Scope is deliberately narrow**: GET only (a form POST can pay a reward -- never replay one), `mode === 'navigate'` only (sub-resources, fetch and ajax/ untouched), same-origin only; anything else gets no respondWith at all, i.e. the old behaviour. NAV_TIMEOUT_MS is 20s and is a FLOOR, not a deadline -- cutting off a slow-but-progressing page would make a bad connection worse. With no cached fallback it re-fetches rather than inventing a response. (3) offline.html is **entirely self-contained** -- no stylesheet, script, font or image, because it is shown exactly when fetching anything is what does not work. Inline SVG logo. The harness asserts that. STILL NOT CACHED, DELIBERATELY: every HTML response is per-player and live (balances, timers, raid state), and flexbox.css / the game JS are unversioned URLs, so a cached copy would survive a deploy while version.php's update banner told people to refresh into the old stylesheet. If asset caching is ever added it must be invalidated by that VERSION signal. pwa-offline-harness.js loads the real worker in a vm sandbox with faked caches/fetch and drives every branch; the loader was also driven in headless Chrome (normal load never shows the panel; stalled at 9.7s; cancel restores the page). |
| platform.md (Launchpad section)     | Post-login landing     | launchpad.php ($lp_sections registry = every tile; process-oauth.php:~198 redirects here, NOT profile.php; header.php top-level link). Nav-only + 3 cheap stats (getWallets/getCurrentDailyRewardStreak/getCurrentBalance) -- NO writes, NO game logic, because it is the first page after login and must never be what breaks. Start Here strip is conditional on those 3 stats and self-clears. Emoji icons, not images (FTP deploy). The staked-NFT view is my-nfts.php (was dashboard.php -- renamed because the name promised an overview it never gave). dashboard.php REMAINS as a permanent 301 stub and must not be deleted: 46 references plus bookmarks, Discord links and in-game "back to Skulliance" buttons still point at it. Its page identifier for filterNFTs()/renderVisibility() is now "my-nfts", and skulliance.php accepts BOTH that and the old "dashboard" for the #holdings anchor. dropship/dashboard.php is a DIFFERENT file and is untouched. TWO PATH CHECKS keyed off the old filename and broke silently in the rename -- skulliance.php gating verifyMembershipNFTs() on REQUEST_URI, and skulliance.js gating the NFT upload button on window.location.pathname. Both now accept my-nfts.php (and still the old name). Anything else that keys off a page NAME rather than a link will fail the same quiet way. Game exit buttons (match3rpg, skullswap x2, monstrocity, displayRound() in skulliance.js, and dropship/header.php) go to launchpad.php, not the NFT view -- Drop Ship was the last one still exiting to dashboard.php, from before it moved into this repo, and it is the only in-repo "back to Skulliance" button that ever pointed there; wallet-ajax.php still redirects to the NFT view after connecting a wallet, which is deliberate. |
| platform-dashboard.md (NFT panel)  | What one staked NFT is doing | my-nfts.php (#nftd panel), ajax/nft-dossier.php, lib/nft-dossier.php, plus nft-dossier-harness.php and nft-panel-harness.js. Clicking an NFT used to link straight out to a block explorer, which answers "does this exist on chain" and nothing about the thing the staker owns it for. ONE READ PER SURFACE, EACH TOLERANT OF ITS TABLE BEING ABSENT: these are the platform's older tables, a missing one returns false from query() and is reported as "nothing here" rather than taking the panel down. WHO MAY LOOK IS DECIDED IN THE ENDPOINT, ONCE -- the library does not know and must not, because a rule re-derived per query will disagree with itself; the test is the one the rest of the platform uses, the owner always and anyone else only when that owner's visibility is 2. **RAIDS ARE REACHED THROUGH THE SOLDIER**: raids_soldiers keys on soldier_id and only soldiers knows nft_id. Counted over EVERY soldier row for the NFT, with no LIMIT and no s.active filter -- the panel SHOWS the active soldier, but a soldier can be retired and replaced and the raids it fought still happened, so scoping the count the same way would drop a player's whole history the day their Skull died. INNER JOIN on raids, not LEFT: a raids_soldiers row whose raid is gone has no outcome to score. **A WIN DEPENDS ON WHICH SIDE IT FOUGHT ON.** raids.outcome is written from the ATTACKER's point of view -- '1' the raid succeeded, '2' it was repelled, '0' still running -- so '1' is a win for offense and a LOSS for defense, and counting outcome='1' as a win outright tells a defender they won every raid they lost. That is not a crash, it is a confident wrong number. Aggregated in PHP, not SUM(CASE...): the per-NFT row count is tiny and a GROUP BY is one more thing ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY can reject on a server whose mode we do not control. The harness LIFTS THE REAL AGGREGATION and runs it against made-up rows, with one fixture carrying every side/outcome combination -- the per-case checks each pass against a counter that only ever increments one field, so only the mixed fixture catches a tally written to the wrong key. Raids tile and record render only when total > 0 (a zero column is not information), an unresolved raid shows in "Right now" instead of the record, and `var r = d.raids || {}` keeps an older payload from taking the panel down. WHAT IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT: Skull Racer, Crypt Crawl, Crypt Conquest, Realm Guardians, Boss Battles and Monstrocity all key on user_id, not an NFT. obscura_runs HAS an nft_id and still does not qualify -- it is a transient pointer to the puzzle being solved, NULLed when it resolves, and obscura_scores carries no NFT, so there is no history to count. auctions.asset_id and raffles.asset_id are free-text fields on admin-created auctions, NOT links to nfts.id. Per-NFT earnings are not stored anywhere either: rewards come from collections.rate times what you hold, so "this NFT has earned X" would be a back-calculation, and the panel shows the rate instead. **A STAT LINE HAS TO NAME ITS STAT**: the raid record shipped as a bare .nftd-idle under a FIVE-tile grid, so "8 won, 5 lost" could have been qualifying missions, gauntlets or the nightly rate -- it is now .nftd-note with a RAIDS label, inline rather than a section heading, which between the grid and the artwork note would outweigh the sentence it introduces. **THE REALM AND THE DIAMOND SKULL ARE PICTURES.** A realm's picture IS its theme (images/themes/{theme_id}.jpg, the file realms.php and the raid cards draw), so the soldier query selects r.theme_id and a realm with no theme draws no thumbnail rather than a broken one. diamond_skulls.diamond_skull_id IS ITSELF AN nfts.id -- gallery.php has relied on that for as long as the feature has existed -- so the row joins nfts for a name and an image; it used to print the bare row id, a number the owner has never seen anywhere else. The image is resolved by the ENDPOINT, not the library, the same split the main image uses (getIPFS() is db.php's and the library does not include it), and the raw CID is unset before the payload goes out. **nft-panel-harness.js EXECUTES render()**, because whether a picture renders is not a question source text can answer: a structural check for "this block mentions ds.image and an img tag" PASSED with the thumbnail wired to `false`, since the string still appeared further down the same ternary. nft-dossier-harness.php shells out to it and reports it as one assertion, the arrangement digest-harness.php already uses for its geometry. The panel was also previewed against the real stylesheet, which is what caught the record sitting flush against the artwork note (.nftd-idle is margin:0 because it is used standalone elsewhere, so the gap belongs to the .nftd-note + .nftd-idle pairing). |
| platform-gallery.md (Music Videos) | The public page of music videos made with projects' art | musicvideos.php, lib/music-videos.php, the NFTs menu in header.php, plus musicvideos-harness.php. **THE COPY SAID THE WRONG THING FIRST**, and on a page about other people's work that matters: "films made by the artists for their own collections" is not what these are. The art is theirs; the videos are made using it. **THE FOLDER IS THE LIST**: videos arrive by FTP like every other piece of media here, so a hardcoded roster would mean a deploy to publish one and a page that is wrong the first time somebody uploads without saying. glob('videos/*.mp4') is the roster. **MATCHED TO A PROJECT BY FILENAME SLUG**, falling back to the currency slug, with MV_ALIASES for the ones that are neither: f.ART's video is projectvenus.mp4, named for its currency VENUS, and the project name slugs to 'fart'. A video that matches nothing is still SHOWN, titled from its filename -- a video the owner uploaded is one the owner wants seen, and dropping it would be the page disagreeing with the folder. **THE FAILURE IS SILENT IN THE WORST WAY**: an unmatched file renders perfectly, just with a filename where that artist's logo should be. No error, page looks finished, one artist's film is the only one without their mark on it -- which is why the harness drives the real mv_videos() against a fake mysqli carrying the owner's actual project export and a real temp directory. **THREE WAYS TO LABEL ONE**, in descending order of how much it is the project's own mark: the Monstrocity theme logo (images/monstrocity/<slug>/logo.png, found through monstrocity_theme_collections -> collections -> projects), then the currency icon, then the name. Today exactly one lands on the icon: f.ART, which has no theme. Both files are is_file()-checked, because a path that is not there prints a broken frame rather than falling through. The icon keeps the name beside it -- a glyph alone among eleven wordmarks reads as an image that failed to load. **THE GRID IS IMAGES AND THE PLAYER IS ONE VIDEO.** The first version put a <video> in every tile at preload="metadata" and seeked each to its own midpoint for a still. On the live page that opened more connections than a browser allows per host: most tiles sat on a spinner showing nothing, four reached the midpoint without painting a frame, one worked. The tiles are the project's REALM THEME artwork now -- images/themes/<project id>.jpg, which realms.php already keys by project id, so the file is named exactly the way this needs it -- and pressing one opens a single shared <video>. **CLOSING REMOVES THE SRC AND CALLS load()**, not just pause(): a paused video that still has a source keeps its buffer and on some browsers keeps filling it. The tiles are <button>s so the whole thing works without a mouse, and focus returns to the tile that opened the player. The films are SQUARE -- the first version reserved 16/9 and letterboxed every one of them -- so both the tile (4/3, the theme art's shape) and the player (1/1) say so, and the player's box width follows the viewport HEIGHT so a square never overflows. **AND THE PLAYER IS NEVER BIGGER THAN THE FILM.** Once loadedmetadata reports videoWidth/videoHeight the box is set to the file's own pixel width, scaled DOWN only when the window cannot hold it: Math.min(1, availW/w, availH/h), where the 1 is the entire rule and is one character away from being wrong in a way nobody would notice on a big monitor. The aspect is taken from the file too, so the first non-square one later is not stretched into a square. Driven in musicvideos-fit-harness.js against a stubbed DOM, because the Chrome here will not decode video and videoWidth is never anything but 0 in a page I can open. **AND THE TILE ART IS RESIZED ON THE SERVER.** The realm theme originals are 2000x1500 and up to 1.4MB; twelve of those behind a 380px tile is ten megabytes to look at a grid, and the page sat spinning. Worse, the video a visitor then clicked had to queue behind them for one of the browser's six connections, so the films looked broken when they were only waiting -- which is exactly why it "played better when logged in" and stalled in incognito: a normal window already had the art cached. mv_thumb() writes a 620px copy to images/themes/thumbs/ once (1004KB -> 91KB, 40ms, then free), rebuilds it if the source mtime moves, writes to a temp name and renames because two visitors can arrive at once, and returns the ORIGINAL on any failure -- no GD, unwritable directory, corrupt jpeg -- since that should cost the saving, not the picture. All but the first three tiles stay loading="lazy" on top of it. **AND THE PLAYER OPENS AT THE RIGHT SIZE, not at a guess it then snaps out of:** sizing only on loadedmetadata meant the modal opened at the CSS default and visibly jumped from 760px to 560. mv_video_dims() reads the width and height out of the mp4's own header -- the last eight bytes of tkhd, as 16.16 fixed, which holds for both versions of that box -- walking the atom tree rather than scanning for 'moov', because that string turns up inside compressed data. 0.05ms, and (0,0) for anything unreadable, where the browser's own numbers take over as before. **AND THE LOGO LOOKUP WAS WRONG FOR ELEVEN OF TWELVE PROJECTS.** It went monstrocity_theme_collections -> collections -> projects; that link table is the newer per-collection mapping and most themes have no rows in it, so the join returned nothing and everything fell through to a points icon. Nothing looked broken, which is the problem. monstrocity_themes.project_id is the column the admin writes and is what this reads now. **HARNESS LESSON, AGAIN:** harness_strip_comments() does NOT strip <?php /* ... */ ?> blocks, so the comment explaining why preload and playsinline are set satisfied a bare search for either word and those two assertions could not fail. Anchored on the <video tag. The currency fallback was also dead in test until a fixture named after a currency was added -- all twelve real videos match by name. |
| (no page) | The invitation sent to artists and projects | partner.php, lib/partner-flyers.php, plus partner-harness.php. Replaces explaining the platform down a DM with screenshots. PUBLIC for the same reason the Skull Paper is: its entire audience is logged out, so it must not include skulliance.php (which redirects anonymous visitors to error.php) or verify.php, and it carries site-header.php because header.php's navbar lives inside `if(isset($name))` -- a stranger would otherwise have no way off the page. NOT IN ANY NAV: it is sent, not browsed. **?for=Name PERSONALISES IT, INCLUDING THE UNFURL** -- the name reaches the h1, the title, the og:title, the og:description and the canonical, so the card that appears in a DM already says who it is for. Sanitised to a character class and capped at 48 before being reflected, not merely escaped on output: it lands in meta attributes and a URL, and a 400-character "name" would wreck both the hero and the card. Everything reads correctly with no parameter. **EVERY NUMBER IS READ FROM THE CODE THAT ENFORCES IT**, because a marketing page that promises what the platform does not do is worse than no page, and nobody re-reads one: the currency rule is asserted by RUNNING admin_currency_problem() at its boundaries (2 and 16, 1 and 17), the Monstrocity count is count(default-characters) x count(monstrocity_theme_subdirs()) rather than typed, and the chain list is diffed against admin-collections.php's $CHAINS so a fourth chain fails the harness until the page mentions it. The game count is diffed against the Skull Paper nav. **THE ROSTER IS LIVE** (SELECT FROM projects) because a hardcoded list starts lying the first time somebody joins; the marquee filters to icons that exist on disk, since a row of fallback skulls argues the opposite of the intended point. lib/partner-flyers.php holds the artist flyer list that WAS a literal inside homepage.php -- two copies of a list that grows every time an artist joins means one page is wrong, and it is whichever one nobody opened recently. The flyer set is a SUBSET of staking projects (not everyone has artwork), so the count comes from the database, never from that file. THE CARD: summary_large_image is ~1.91:1 and X crops anything else; skulliancelogo.png is 700x300 and letterboxed. It now serves images/partner-card.jpg (1200x630, 328KB) WHEN THAT FILE EXISTS and falls back to skulliancebackground.png otherwise -- the background is a 4.5MB PNG and some crawlers skip a large image, but a card pointed at a 404 is worse than a heavy one, so the optimised copy is opt-in by upload rather than by deploy. The name is NOT drawn into the image: there is no TTF in this repo (see dhc-card.php) and X renders og:title at full size beside the card anyway. TONE, corrected twice in review: the pitch is "you have been invited", not "bring your project to us" -- there is no application form and no listing fee, which is the actual policy and a stronger position; and the benefits section must NOT argue that art without a game attached gets sold, which insults the artist it is addressed to. It says the collection gives collectors somewhere to gather, not that the work needed justifying. Policy ids and Monstrocity art are on OUR side of the ask, not theirs. **NEVER CALL POINTS A CURRENCY ON THIS PAGE.** An artist reading "your own currency" hears "you want me to launch a token" -- a contract, a listing, a price chart, speculators, and a regulatory question nobody wants -- and the page lost people on the word alone. It says points, and a plain block headed "Points are not a token" states the facts flat before anything about what they are for: off chain, nothing minted, no contract, cannot be bought, sold, sent, traded, swapped, bridged or withdrawn, no value off the platform. That block comes BEFORE the icon spec, because somebody who is worried stops reading at the worry rather than after the filename rules. "currency" is also the DATABASE COLUMN and half the function names around it, which makes it the single most likely word to get typed back into the copy by whoever is editing the query directly above -- so the harness strips the PHP and the comments out of the markup and fails on any remaining occurrence. The per-phrase checks are SCOPED TO THE BLOCK: "no contract" matched "No contracts, no servers, no mod work" four sections away, so deleting the sentence that actually reassures anybody passed. **DHC HAS ITS OWN SECTION** as the depth argument -- traits drop from every game, tiers are read from how many of the 226 original Fighters wore each one, Fighters are assembled from earned parts, the Arena is Crew vs Crew where rarity does not decide a fight, and the Collection is public with its own share cards. Credited to Maxingo, as everywhere else. **AN ORIGIN SECTION COMES BEFORE THE ROSTER AND THE ASK**, because the page was written for people who already know what Skulliance is and most invitees do not -- a stranger shown "40 projects are already here" has no idea what they are a crowd for. It is not a history: the part that matters to an invitee is the REASON it keeps growing, which is that one collection cannot justify building staking, missions, realms, a marketplace and a dozen games and forty together can, so nobody on the platform is competing for the same collectors. Claims match overview.md and the homepage (six founding skull artists on Cardano, Oculus Orbus a collector and developer, partner staking opened after it worked, then other chains). The games row must NOT lead with DHC Arena: DHC has its own section further down and the first tile would be spent on something the page is about to cover properly. |
| marketplace-store.md *(new)*        | Free member claims     | store.php, ajax/item-edit.php, store-edit-harness.php (EDIT AFTER SUBMISSION. Two editors: user 1 on every listing and every field, and a PARTNER CREATOR on the listings credited to a project they own. OWNERSHIP IS BY PROJECT because there is nothing else -- `items` records no submitter, so the only link between a listing and a person is projects.discord_id, the same link renderItemSubmissionForm() uses to decide who may submit at all; a creator can own several projects, so it is a set. db.php storeItemEditRights() answers that ONCE and both the page and the endpoint ask it; a page that decides for itself which buttons to draw drifts from the endpoint that decides which writes to accept, and the endpoint is the one that matters. The endpoint checks BOTH ENDS of a move -- the stored project_id and the posted one -- so owning a listing today does not let anyone push it onto another artist's shelf. `featured` and `secondary_project_id` are admin-only and are OMITTED from a partner's UPDATE rather than rejected: reading `$_POST['featured'] ?? 0` would clear an Exclusive flag every time its owner fixed a typo. store-edit-harness.php pins all of that down by running the real endpoint in a subprocess against a fake connection -- verified to FAIL when the admin-field gate is removed. NOTE `include '../db.php'` resolves against the WORKING DIRECTORY (a path starting with a dot bypasses include_path), which is why it works under mod_php and why the harness runs with cwd=ajax/. The gate is in the endpoint, checked against the session; store.php's $st_admin/$st_mine only decide whether a button is drawn, and a button nobody can see is not a permission. db.php adminUpdateItem() whitelists the columns it will SET and intersects that list with SHOW COLUMNS FROM items -- not information_schema, which the cPanel user gets #1044 on -- because `secondary_project_id` and `featured` are read unprefixed out of an items+projects join and nothing in the repo proves which side owns them; an UPDATE naming a projects column would fail the whole statement. The modal reads its values off the card's own data-* attributes, so opening it costs no round trip, and it posts to ajax/item-edit.php rather than to store.php -- skulliance.php:404 creates an item on any POSTed `name`, so a form falling back to a normal submit would duplicate the listing it was meant to fix. Save reloads rather than repainting the card: price, currency options, affordability and the featured-first ORDER BY are all server-side. db.php's older updateItem() is dead code covering four of eight columns; left alone rather than widened. quantity 0 delists (the store query is `WHERE quantity != 0`), -1 is unlimited. |
| marketplace-auctions.md *(new)*     | Bid-based NFT sales     | auctions.php, db.php:9379-9577 |
| marketplace-raffles.md *(new)*      | Ticketed raffles        | raffles.php, db.php:9602-9828 |
| platform-activity.md (admin menu) | One list of admin pages | admin_tabs() in admin-lib.php, read by admin_chrome() AND by header.php's Admin dropdown, pinned in digest-harness.php. THE LIST WAS DUPLICATED and Daily Digest went into the tab strip while the dropdown kept its hand-written copy -- so the page existed and could not be reached, reported as "not in the dropdown admin menu". Same failure the platform has now had five times (four IPFS gateway lists, two chain-icon maps, two reward derivations, seven art-path constructions): two copies of a list are one list and one bug. header.php requires admin-lib.php inside its user-1 branch, which is free because that file echoes nothing and includes no db.php -- it says so in its own header. The harness asserts the dropdown is GENERATED, that no page filename is hard-coded in the nav beside it, and that every page the list names actually exists on disk. |
| platform-activity.md (daily digest) | A day of the platform as one poster | admin-digest.php, js/digest-builder.js, activity_digest()/activity_digest_src() in activity-lib.php, the 'digest' tab in admin_chrome(), plus digest-harness.php. IT EXISTS BECAUSE THE ACTIVITY TABLE DOES. The collage needs art from missions, raids, realms, Gauntlets, the Arena, Fighters, auctions and raffles, every player who turned up, and a tally per feature -- from the feature tables that is eight joins across eight unrelated schemas, several of which never kept the picture, only the ids it was built from. activity already holds all of it because it is written from the ANNOUNCEMENT, so this is three queries and a feature that starts announcing tomorrow is in the poster tomorrow with nothing added here. Inherits the feed's limits: history starts when the table did. **DRAWN IN THE BROWSER** like admin-flyers.php, and for its reasons plus one: there is NO TTF IN THIS REPO (see dhc-card.php, which went text-free for exactly that) and a poster whose job is shouting out players by name cannot be text-free. Also avoids Imagick compositing forty pictures some of which are 5000x5000 -- the memory profile that already spilled to disk once in admin-lib.php. No POST, no writes, so admin-harness.php's write-path checks do not apply, same as the flyer builder. **TAINTING IS THE WHOLE RISK AND IT FAILS LATE AND TOTAL**: a canvas that has drawn a cross-origin image without CORS throws on toBlob(), so a bad url costs the entire download, discovered at the end, after everything looked right. Two defences and both are needed. activity_digest_src() normalises OUR OWN urls to RELATIVE -- not cosmetic: the login cookie has no domain, so the admin may be on www.skulliance.io while the announce wrote the bare host, and those are different ORIGINS to a canvas; it keeps cdn.discordapp.com absolute (verified against the live CDN: access-control-allow-origin: *) and DROPS everything else, including IPFS gateways and protocol-relative //host/x which looks like a path and is not. In the JS, crossOrigin must be set BEFORE .src (after is silently ignored) and a failed load resolves null rather than rejecting, so one dead url costs one tile. The export is try-wrapped so a taint reports itself instead of a button doing nothing. Proven in a browser: a 1200x1500 poster exported 2.25MB with no throw. A HALF-OPEN RANGE ON created_at, never DATE(created_at) = -- the function form cannot use idx_created and would read every row in the table to answer "what happened today". Art is GROUPed by image_url (a board posting twelve results carries one picture twelve times) and /icons/ is excluded (a 128px currency glyph as a collage tile is the mistake the feed already made once). SEEDED BY THE DAY, so the same day always draws the same poster and a different day never draws the same one -- that is the brief, and it also stops the preview reshuffling on every control change. FOUR FIXES OFF THE FIRST REAL DAY (377 events), every one of them invisible in a synthetic fixture. (1) **PLAYERS GROUPED BY user_id, NOT author_name** -- the author LINE is not the person: the daily-reward announce writes "president_crypto · Day 6 of 7" and every other announce writes the bare username, so four of twelve faces in the shout-out row were duplicates. Joined INNER to users, so a cron announce with no actor drops out and the name/avatar are canonical rather than whatever the embed said. (2a) **SKULL RACER BOX ART EXCLUDED** -- three fixed promo images under /racing/images/, and they are AI-generated where every other picture on the platform is Maxingo's hand-drawn work; side by side in one collage the difference is the first thing you see, and the poster exists to market the hand-drawn work. Excluded by DIRECTORY so anything added there later is covered. Like every other art exclusion it is from the PICTURES only -- the tally still counts the races. (2) **TRAIT DROPS EXCLUDED, ASSEMBLED FIGHTERS KEPT** -- both announce down the dhcfighters channel so the channel cannot tell them apart; the URL can. A drop is <art>/250/<category>/<slug>.png, one piece of armour on a flat ground, and they are the most frequent announcement on the platform, so a day's collage filled with disembodied shoulder plates while the Fighters people built were crowded out. (3) **ONE LABEL PER CATEGORY** -- captioning every tile read DHC FIGHTERS five times, BOSS BATTLES three and MISSIONS twice down one poster. Art is grouped by channel, each group gets a contiguous block, the name is said once underneath on the poster's own ground (not over the art, where a gradient is never quite legible on pale pictures). THREE THINGS THAT BLOCK LAYOUT MUST DO, each learned by looking: every row is FILLED (5 tiles in 3 columns leaves a hole beside the last row, and a hole in a collage reads as a failed image -- items are spread across rows and each row sized to fill, so a short row is just wider pictures); the gutter BETWEEN blocks is wider than the gap inside one (with one gap everywhere the clustering was invisible and only the labels distinguished anything); and a category too narrow for its own name is DROPPED rather than ellipsised -- a real poster ended on "REAL…", and the tally line already names and counts every category anyway. Rounding error goes to the biggest block, never the last, which is usually the smallest. (4) **THE MASTHEAD IS THE REAL WORDMARK** (images/skulliancelogo.png, 700x300, same-origin so canvas-safe), falling back to type only if it fails to load. Also: the tally drops whole entries instead of ellipsising, after a live poster said "19 Daily Rewar…". **A CATEGORY'S MINIMUM WIDTH IS ITS MEASURED LABEL, NOT A NUMBER.** It was a flat 150px, which is wide enough for MISSIONS and not for REALM GUARDIANS -- a poster went out on X reading "REALM GUAR…". The minimum is now ceil(measureText(label)) + 6, the label font must be set BEFORE measuring (or it measures against the header's), categories are dropped ITERATIVELY until the survivors all fit their names (each one dropped also frees a gutter, which a fixed maxBlocks could not see), and the reclaim loop takes width off the widest block THAT STILL HAS ROOM ABOVE ITS OWN MINIMUM -- picking the single widest and giving up when it hit bottom overflowed the band by 147px with nine long names. THE GEOMETRY HAS ITS OWN HARNESS, digest-geometry-harness.js, because it has to RUN the layout rather than read it -- 85 layouts across four widths, seven caps and three day-shapes, asserting no cut label, nothing past the band, no zero-size tile, every row spanning its block, and every SURVIVING block drawing all of its own tiles. That last one is per block and not per poster: a whole category dropped for lack of label room is INTENDED, so a poster-wide drawn===planned flags 13 correct layouts, while a block that kept its place and quietly left art behind is the row-distribution bug and shows up nowhere else. digest-harness.php shells out to it and reports it as one assertion. HARNESS ANCHOR TRAP, HIT HERE: activity_channels() (the Activity page's filter dropdown) and activity_digest()'s tally both open with the identical `SELECT channel, COUNT(*) AS n FROM activity`, and activity_channels() sits EARLIER in the file -- so a plain strpos for that string landed on the wrong query and the assertion passed whatever the digest did. Anchor inside the function body, not across the file. AND THE PAGE FORGOT admin-css.php, which every admin page ends with -- admin_chrome() opens the .adm wrapper but does not carry its styling, so the whole panel rendered as unstyled text hard against the browser edge. digest-harness.php now asserts that for EVERY admin-*.php that calls admin_chrome(), because it is a silent page-wide break with no error anywhere. TWO THINGS MEASURED OFF THE FIRST RENDER rather than reasoned: the chrome was taking 44% of a wide poster and the collage was a strip, so header and footer are held to about a third; and the shout-out row's top was short by one avatar radius, which the wide format had slack to absorb and the TALL one clipped every name off the bottom edge -- peopleH and the py arithmetic must stay in step or the mosaic and the row disagree about where the floor is. |
| platform-activity.md (your own day) | The per-player digest modal | player-digest.php (included by header.php), ajax/player-digest.php, activity_player_digest() in activity-lib.php, plus player-digest-harness.php. Same argument as the platform poster: activity is written from the ANNOUNCEMENT, so every feature that announces is already in it and one that starts announcing tomorrow appears here tomorrow with nothing added. **DRAWN IN THE BROWSER** for the reason admin-digest.php is -- there is NO TTF in this repo (see dhc-card.php, which went text-free over it) and a poster whose job is naming a player and counting their day cannot be text-free. **TAINTING IS THE RISK AND IT FAILS LATE AND TOTAL**: a cross-origin image drawn without CORS makes toBlob() throw, so the symptom is the download button doing nothing after the poster rendered perfectly -- every src goes through activity_digest_src() server-side, crossOrigin is set BEFORE .src (after is silently ignored), a failed load resolves null so one dead url costs one tile, and the export is try-wrapped so a taint explains itself. **IT CAN ONLY EVER SHOW YOUR OWN DAY**: the user comes from the session and the date is computed server-side -- a ?user= or a client-supplied date would let anyone walk a player's whole history one request at a time, and the harness fails on either. The endpoint does NOT include skulliance.php: it is fetched from every page, and a logged-out visitor must get a clean {show:false} rather than error.php parsed as JSON. A day with no activity returns show:false -- a poster reading "you did nothing" is worse than silence. **SEEN-ONCE LIVES IN localStorage, NOT A COLUMN**, deliberately: a new users column is a migration somebody has to run by hand, and when that did not happen for monstrocity_theme_collections every write failed silently for weeks. The cost of this being wrong is seeing a nice poster twice on a second device. Every access is wrapped because storage throws in a private window, and the key is checked BEFORE the fetch so a player who already closed today's digest costs nothing per page. X CANNOT ATTACH A FILE, so Share STORES the poster (ajax/digest-share.php) and posts a link to digest.php?d=<token>, a public page whose og:image is that stored file -- one click, picture included. THE TAB IS OPENED ON THE CLICK and pointed at X once the upload lands: window.open() from a promise callback is no longer user-initiated and every browser blocks it. A failed upload still posts, with the site card, because a Share button that does nothing is worse than a post without a picture. WHAT MAKES THE UPLOAD SAFE: the filename is DERIVED from the session user and the server day (a client-chosen name is an arbitrary file write), the bytes are checked as a PNG and bounded on both size and dimensions (a 30000x30000 PNG is small on disk and gigabytes decoded) and then DECODED AND RE-ENCODED as JPEG rather than stored as sent, so nothing hidden in them survives; the write is atomic because two taps race. The token is NOT a secret and does not need to be -- a file only exists because its owner pressed Share, which published it -- and making it one would mean a secret stored somewhere, which on this host means a manual step, which is the failure mode that cost Monstrocity weeks. digest.php shape-checks the token to 20 hex before it touches a path, does not include skulliance.php (X would cache a card of error.php) and carries site-header.php so a stranger arriving from the post is not stranded. Posters are pruned after DSH_KEEP_DAYS on a share, BOUNDED to 40 deletions so one unlucky request never pays for a year of backlog -- no cron, because the directory only grows when somebody shares. The post leads with what they DID rather than a bare number, and budgets 280-24-tail like every other share here. **AND best.label IS A CHANNEL NAME, NOT A COUNTABLE NOUN** -- the card read "including N " + label.toLowerCase(), which is grammatical for Missions and Raids and nonsense for most of activity_channel_label()'s map: "including 4 skull swap", "including 1 raids", "including 5 dhc fighters", in a post whose job is advertising those features BY NAME. It reads "N of them in Skull Swap" now, which works for every label and keeps the capitals. The trim also DROPS WHOLE CLAUSES in order (streak, then the breakdown, then the card) rather than cutting one: "148 days running." had been coming out as "148...", which is under 280 and reads as a typo, so a length-only budget check passes it. The harness sweeps every label in the real map against every entry in ASKS -- 368 posts -- and asserts three separate things a length check cannot see: no bare count in front of a label, no flattened capitals, and every card still ending in a full stop. The harness RUNS shareText() through node: its long fixture needs SPACES in the label, because a single unbroken token is thrown away whole by the word-boundary trim and the post comes out short however wrong the budget is -- the same trap that let a missing url allowance through on partner.php. **THE SURPRISE CACHE pays the HABIT, not the post:** roughly one open in DHCF_CACHE_ODDS (12) drops DHCF_CACHE_DRAWS (3) traits on the 'digestcache' source, which is unlisted and shares the referral's placement_3 table. It hangs on OPENING the digest for a reason -- hanging it on Share would be paying for a post on X, which is the one thing that would put the invite scheme on the wrong side of the platform-manipulation rule the rest of it is carefully inside, so digest-share.php must stay free of it and the harness asserts that. **THE ROLL IS A DAY HASH, NOT A DIE**, and that is a security property rather than a style choice: the seen-once marker lives in localStorage (see above), so a per-request rand() could be re-rolled by clearing site data and reloading until it paid -- unlimited traits for anyone who noticed. dhcf_cache_day() hashes the player id and the day together under a fixed prefix, so the answer for a player on a day is fixed before they ask. The endpoint STILL checks for an existing digestcache award since CURDATE() before awarding, because the modal is opened more than once and a future change to the roll must not become a repeatable payout. It is awarded inside the player's OWN request, so dhcf_award() queues the reveal normally and no replay is needed, unlike the referral below. **MEASURED, not assumed:** 188 of 2400 synthetic users (7.8%) on a given day, stable across repeat calls, and a different set the next day. referral-harness.php owns these checks (13 mutations, all caught) -- including that the odds themselves stay reachable: the distribution bounds scale with DHCF_CACHE_ODDS and so pass just as happily at 1-in-500, which is a feature nobody would ever see, so the constant is separately bounded to 4..40. **AND THE JS PARSE IS NOW A CHECK, because php -l cannot see inside a <script> block:** a leftover pair of orphan lines from the share-handler swap passed php -l and left the modal dead on every page of the site. player-digest-harness.php extracts the script contents and runs node --check on them. **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THE CACHE SHOWED NOBODY A DIGEST AT ALL.** dhcf_cache_day() is defined in dhcfighters-config.php, which NOTHING in this endpoint reaches -- db.php requires it only from inside a function -- and the first draft called it in the same condition that then required the library. An undefined function is a fatal, the endpoint's own shutdown handler converts a fatal into {show:false}, and {show:false} is the correct answer on a quiet day: the modal would simply have stopped appearing for everybody, with nothing in the response saying why. The require now precedes the call and the call is function_exists-guarded, and the harness asserts the ORDER rather than the presence of both -- the same lesson as dhc_shot_src() and dhca_public(): the piece was right and the seam it plugged into was not. **THEN A PLAYER USED IT AND FOUND FOUR THINGS AT ONCE, every one invisible from the code.** (1) window.open('', '_blank', 'noopener') RETURNS NULL -- that is what noopener means, the opener gets no handle -- so the tab this design opens on the click to dodge the popup blocker could never be pointed anywhere. It sat on about:blank and the code fell through to navigating the page the player was already on. Opened without noopener now, with tab.opener severed instead: the same protection with a handle left over. (2) THE POSTER WAS 1080x1350 INTO A summary_large_image CARD, which crops to about 1.91:1, so what went out on X was a band through the middle with the entire art collage cut off. It is 1200x630 now with the art in the larger half, still inside the 200-2400 x 200-3000 the upload accepts. (3) <a download> DOES NOT WORK ON AN iPHONE -- iOS Safari ignores the attribute on a blob: url and navigates to the blob, taking the player off the page and putting nothing in the camera roll. It goes through navigator.share({files}) now, and the blob is built SYNCHRONOUSLY with toDataURL+atob rather than toBlob, because share() needs the click's transient activation and a promise callback is a later task with none left. (4) THE FOOTER AND THE STREAK WERE PRINTED ON TOP OF THE ARTWORK: the old layout grew downwards and clamped the footer back up with Math.min(gy + 36, H - 120) when it ran out of canvas, and a clamp is not a layout -- it guarantees a collision instead of an overflow. Fixed positions in a fixed-height column now, art bounded to its own panel. **AND markSeen() RAN ON DISPLAY**, so anything that took the player off the page before they had finished -- which on an iPhone was the Download button itself -- burned the digest for the day with no way back. The keys are queued and written on Close, which is what the modal's note has always promised; Share and Download deliberately do not dismiss. **THE POST CALLED THE STREAK "4 days running"**, which reads as that much activity every day for four days, and the owner asked what his own post meant. It says "4 day daily reward streak" now -- and "login streak" was the WRONG FEATURE as well as the wrong wording: users.streak is getCurrentDailyRewardStreak() in db.php, the daily reward CLAIM streak, which incrementDailyRewardStreak() also resets to 0 at seven, so it never reads above 6. The poster said it too and was changed with it; and the four asks were shortened to pay for the extra characters: the longest ask sets how much is left for the player's own numbers, and at 93 it was trimming the streak off a quarter of all posts. Worst case 278/280, streak surviving all 84 cases that have one. **player-digest-geometry-harness.js EXISTS BECAUSE NONE OF THIS WAS TESTABLE BY READING.** It lifts draw() and runs it against a recording 2d context over 4032 layouts, asserting nothing lands outside the card and no text lands on anything else. Two of its own first findings were the recorder rather than the poster: cover() draws oversized and clips, so ignoring clip() reported an 813px image on a 646px panel, and a flat descender under every baseline reported "112" colliding with its own caption -- descent is only assumed now for strings that contain one. A third was the FIXTURES: every channel label was short enough that the row truncation was never exercised, and a long one added at position eight was sliced off before five rows were drawn. 6 mutations, all caught, including the exact clamped-footer bug that shipped. **AND THEN: THE INVITE DOES NOT LAND ON /staking/ AT ALL.** The post links to digest.php, so that is the page an arrival from X meets, and I had fixed the call to action on index.php -- the wrong page. On digest.php the buttons sat UNDERNEATH a full-width poster on a 760px column, which on a phone is most of a screen on its own, so the arrival landed on a stranger's artwork with nothing to press in sight. The offer and the buttons come first for an invited arrival now; an ordinary visitor still gets the picture first, because for them it is the interesting thing and no ask has been set up. Both are printed from one closure each, so the two orderings cannot drift. **TWO MORE ON THE SAME HOP:** the claim button linked to index.php and DROPPED THE ?r=, and index.php keyed its whole invited treatment on $_GET['r'] -- so the arrival who came the ordinary way, through the poster, was the one who never saw the offer mentioned on the page built to convert them. The button carries the code now AND index.php reads REF_COOKIE as well, which is what ref_credit() will actually pay on and therefore the right thing to ask. **THE POSTER img HARDCODED width="1080" height="1350"**, which is what it used to be; posters shared before the change are still that shape and still served, so neither pair is right for both and a wrong one reserves a box of the wrong shape. getimagesize() on the stored file, which is on the same disk. **AND IT SAID "WHOEVER SHARED THIS GETS THREE AS WELL" with their username and avatar drawn into the picture directly above the sentence.** ?r= decodes to exactly one user id -- the account that will actually be paid -- so the coyness was pretending not to know something the page was already showing. It names them now, in the heading, the offer and the caption. **NAMED ONLY WHEN THE POSTER IS REALLY THEIRS:** ?d= and ?r= are independent parameters, so a hand-made url can pair one person's artwork with another person's code, and that would caption a stranger's day with whatever name the url asked for. dsh_token_belongs_to() recomputes the hash for every day a poster could still exist for and answers whether any of them match -- about 0.07ms, no table, and where it says no the generic wording stands. It is a function rather than three lines in the page precisely so the harness can RUN it: owner yes, stranger no, id 0 no, non-token no, DSH_KEEP_DAYS yes, 400 days no. Nothing new is disclosed by naming them: the name is already in the image, and the image exists only because its owner pressed Share. **TWO HARNESS FAULTS CAUGHT BY MUTATION, both the same shape as ix-stats and ix-cta earlier:** 'is $e($dg_from) present anywhere' passes with two of its three uses escaped, so the check counts instead -- every mention is either a control use or an escaped print and nothing else. 10 more mutations, all caught. **THEN THE OWNER REWROTE THE POST and it came to 362 characters against 280.** His version is first person ("I did 112 things"), names the streak as daily reward claims, says DHC FIGHTER traits rather than DHC traits, and ends "Claim your 3 traits from @skulliance below" instead of a bare handle. All of that is kept; what paid for it was the asks, cut from 93 to 85 characters, because the longest ask sets how much is left for the player's own numbers. Worst case 279/280 across 1680 combinations with all three of his facts surviving every one -- at the first attempt the claim line's extra 38 characters were trimming the streak off 75% of posts. **"29 MONSTROCITY LEVELS" IS WHAT HE ASKED FOR AND IS NOT IMPLEMENTED:** it needs a unit noun per channel, and the label map has none. Inventing one means asserting what each feature's announcements represent, and a wrong guess is a false claim in a public post, so it still reads "29 of them in Monstrocity", which is true for every label. Worth doing properly if the units are ever written down. **AND THE POSTER DREW EIGHT PICTURES WHERE NINE TILES EXACTLY** -- 3x3 at the same 173px tile that eight gets as 3/3/2 with a hole in it. ACTIVITY_PDIGEST_ART already fetches twelve, so the ninth was being thrown away by a slice in draw() rather than never collected. The geometry harness now reads the cap out of draw() and fails if it does not fill its own grid. **AND THE iOS FIX THEN BROKE THE DESKTOP:** gated on navigator.canShare() alone, desktop Chrome and Safari answer yes too, so a button labelled Download image opened a share sheet offering to message the picture to somebody's contacts. The sheet is for iOS ONLY now -- UA sniffed, deliberately, because the thing that needs detecting cannot be feature-detected: iOS Safari reports 'download' in an anchor and then ignores it, and iPadOS reports itself as MacIntel so maxTouchPoints is what separates it from a desktop Mac. The harness DRIVES saveImage() under six user agents against a stub canvas and asserts the route each takes (desktop/Windows/Android download, iPhone/iPad sheet), because reading the source is exactly what let the sheet ship to the desktop. **THAT TEST'S FIRST RUN WAS A LIE:** node has its own read-only navigator, so `global.navigator = {...}` is silently ignored and all six platforms saw node's user agent -- it reported the desktop answer everywhere and looked like a page bug. Object.defineProperty, not assignment. |
| platform-activity.md (invites) | The referral built on the digest share | lib/referral-lib.php, the 'referral' entry in DHCF_GAMES, ref_capture() in db.php, ref_credit()/ref_replay() in skulliance.php, plus referral-harness.php. **NO NEW TABLE, AND THAT IS NOT A SHORTCUT**: dhc_trait_drops already records (user_id, source, source_detail, awarded_at), which is who-invited-whom and when -- once-per-friend is EXISTS source_detail='invited:<id>' and the daily wall is dhcf_award()'s own per-source cap. Avoiding a CREATE TABLE matters because nothing here creates tables and monstrocity_theme_collections failed silently for weeks when one was never run. **placement_3, NOT run**: three draws from run (55% common) is three commons and nobody bothers twice; placement_3 is 25/30/27/15/3, the table a seven-day streak pays, which is the right comparison. placement_1 stays reserved for topping a board. THREE EACH -- the arrival's three are the OFFER, not generosity, because the post says "get 3 yourself" and anything less makes the post a lie. Cap 60 draws, about twenty invites a day. **THE ORDERING IS THE WHOLE FEATURE**: skulliance.php writes last_login on the way in, so it is READ FIRST and passed to both callers -- read it after and every returning player looks active, no dormant invite ever pays, and nothing errors. **A COOKIE, NOT THE QUERY STRING**: signing in leaves for Discord and returns through process-oauth.php, so ?r= is gone by the time anyone is logged in to credit; captured in db.php before any output, SameSite=Lax because the arrival is a click from X. **THE INVITER IS NOT IN THE REQUEST**: dhcf_award() queues a reveal only for the session user (deliberately -- a raid resolved by the defender must not show them the attacker's trait), so the inviter gets a DM at the time and ref_replay() rebuilds the reveal from the ledger on their next login. dhcf_drop_payload() was SPLIT OUT of dhcf_award() so a replayed drop is the same shape as a live one. The unseen queue went 5 -> 9 because an invite pays three at once. **NOTHING IS PAID FOR POSTING** -- the traits land on a sign-in, which is a conversion here, not a like or a repost on X; a scheme that pays for the post itself and proves it by collecting a permalink is on the other side of that line, and this must not cross it. The POST LEADS WITH THE PLAYER'S OWN NUMBERS and the ask rotates over four phrasings keyed on sharer+day: the invite used to lead, which meant every post opened with the same byte-identical sentence, and near-identical content across many accounts is what X's spam policy covers -- with the players' accounts actioned, not ours. The ask names WHO QUALIFIES, which is not fine print: without it every active follower clicks for nothing and the post reads as bait. **AND THE FIRST VERSION PAID NOBODY WHO ARRIVED THE OBVIOUS WAY.** An invite is /staking/?r=<code>, /staking/ is index.php, and index.php contained NO PHP AT ALL -- 14 lines of static HTML with one Discord button. So ref_capture() never ran there, the cookie was never set, and by the time the arrival came back from the OAuth hop nothing recorded who invited them: they signed in perfectly normally and the invite paid nobody, with no error on any page. db.php does the capture for every other page on the platform, which is exactly why this was invisible -- the ONE page the link actually points at is the one page that does not include db.php. It now requires referral-lib.php and captures before the first byte (setcookie() after output returns quietly, the same silent failure moved). The session is resumed there only when a session cookie already exists, or every crawler hitting the login page writes an orphan session file. **AND THE BARE INVITE URL WAS BUILT TWICE** -- once for the Discord message, once as the share's fallback -- and the fallback omitted ?r= entirely, so every post that fell back (tainted canvas, failed upload) carried a link crediting nobody. One inviteUrl() builds it now, and the harness asserts the string appears exactly ONCE in the modal: two copies is how one of them forgets. VERIFIED OVER REAL HTTP with php -S, not by reading the file -- a valid code sets skl_ref, junk and absent codes set nothing, and the login button still renders. |
| (no doc page - public marketing) | The staking root, which is the page every invite lands on | index.php, homepage-data.php (hp_project_icons() and the hp_stat_* figures), site-header.php, lib/founding-artists.php, plus index-harness.php. **IT WAS FOURTEEN LINES OF STATIC HTML.** A dark background and one Discord button: no art, no pitch, no mention of what Skulliance is. Every poster drawn, every report-card post and every three traits promised on each side was spent delivering a cold stranger to that, because the public pitch lives on the WordPress homepage at skulliance.io, which the invite link never touches. **THE CALL TO ACTION IS STILL THE DISCORD BUTTON**, unchanged and at both ends of the page, surrounded by the reasons to press it; the two free games are named in prose as evidence, never rendered as a second button, and the harness asserts nothing else carries the primary style. The OAuth url is byte-identical and asserted so -- the client id, the registered redirect and the scope list are Discord's, not ours to tidy. **NO db.php, DELIBERATELY:** this is the most-hit cold page on the platform and a crawler's first stop, and homepage-data.php exists exactly so a public page can carry live figures without it (raw credentials, five-minute file cache, a stale value served when the database is unreachable). site-header.php is likewise self-contained. **THE FIGURES ARE ALL OR NOTHING:** hp_stat_chains() falls back to 1 where every other figure falls back to 0, so an unreachable database rendered exactly one tile reading "1 chain" -- which argues against the page it is on. The headline figure has to be real or no strip appears. **THE FLYERS ARE NOT THE ASSET AND THIS COST 42MB TO FIND OUT:** the 27 partner flyers are 1-3MB each at 1500px and a marquee renders them into 112px tiles. homepage.php had already pulled its own flyer wall above the fold for this reason and left a note saying to bring it back only with resized sources; the note was right and I rebuilt the same wall anyway. hp_project_icons() returns the currency icons instead, 6-8KB each, white on transparent, at the 150px they are specified at. **AND A MARQUEE CANNOT BE LAZY** -- its tiles arrive by transform rather than by layout, so loading="lazy" leaves most of the track permanently blank, which is what the first render showed. Eager is only affordable because they are icons; the screenshots below the fold stay lazy. **auto-fit STILL MAKES A TRACK AS WIDE AS ITS OWN MINIMUM** when the container is narrower, so minmax(380px,1fr) was a 380px column inside a 360px phone: measured at 400px of content in a 360px column, exactly 360 once the floor became min(380px,100%). **HARNESS LESSON, TWICE IN ONE FILE:** strpos($html, 'ix-stats') and substr_count($html, 'ix-cta') both match the page's OWN <style> block, so both assertions passed whatever the markup did. Scoped to '<ul class="ix-stats">' and 'class="ix-cta"'. 11 mutations, all caught. |
| platform-activity.md *(new)*        | Platform activity feed  | feed.php (WAS activity.php -- the name moved to the new ranking page), activity-lib.php, activity-schema.md, the write inside discordmsg() in webhooks.php, activity_prune() from the nightly block in verify.php, nav link in header.php, plus activity-harness.php (120 assertions, every one verified to FAIL under mutation). **IT IS A LOG, NOT A QUERY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** The obvious build merges the feature tables by date; it cannot work, because a large share of what Skull Bot announces is ASSEMBLED AT ANNOUNCE TIME AND STORED NOWHERE -- a monthly standing, a raid summary, a trait roll written out in words exist as a sentence in an embed and as nothing else. So the row is written where the sentence already exists, inside discordmsg(), which covers all 100+ call sites at once and puts a new feature in the feed the day it ships. The cost is that HISTORY STARTS EMPTY and no faithful backfill exists. **THE WRITE IS INSIDE THE 2xx BRANCH**, not next to the json_encode(): a post rejected for a rotated webhook (401), a deleted one (404), an over-length embed (400) or rate limiting (429) reached NOBODY, and a feed whose premise is "this was announced" must not carry it. An unconfigured channel never gets there at all -- the enclosing `if ($webhook != "")` already skipped it. **alertAdmin() IS EXCLUDED, BY TWO FLAGS.** Those are ops failure reports with an @ping down the DEFAULT webhook, and the page is readable by every member. `$skl_alerting` (static, so it is still true inside the nested call) covers the alert raised from discordmsg()'s own failure branch; `$GLOBALS['skl_ops_alert']`, set by alertAdmin() in a try/finally, covers alertAdmin() called from anywhere else. The finally matters: a leaked flag silently kills the whole feed for the rest of the request. **TWO ACTOR IDS.** $actor_id defaults to null = resolve from `$_SESSION['userData']['user_id']`, which is right for nearly every announce because the player's own request is what triggers it (cron correctly records 0; an announce made about player X from an admin's request mis-attributes, which costs one row in one filter). $actor_id2 CANNOT be inferred and must be passed -- dhca_announce() is the platform's only two-sided announce and passes ($attId, $defId). THREE SCOPES, and the third is not the second: `mine` is user_id only, `involving` is user_id OR user_id2. An Arena defender never made a request, so collapsing them would file losses they did not initiate under their own activity. **NESTED ANCHORS BROKE THE CARD AND IT IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL.** activity_format() turns a Discord `[text](url)` into an <a>, and the card was itself an <a>; the parser closes the outer one early, so the card's whole body was hoisted OUT of its link. Caught in the browser (one card's .ac-link had a single child where every other had three), not by reading. The card is now a div with an empty `.ac-stretch` overlay (position:absolute; inset:0; z-index:1) and `.ac-desc a` at z-index 2, so in-text links still go where they say. **text-align needs declaring on .ac-link, not just .ac-card**: flexbox.css has a bare `a { text-align: center }` and an element selector beats a value inherited from the card, so the first fix corrected only the unlinked cards. activity_format() is the XSS BOUNDARY for the page -- htmlspecialchars() runs FIRST and the markdown is applied to the escaped string, so the only tags in the output are the ones it builds; link hrefs are restricted to http(s) and re-escaped, and card hrefs are checked the same way. TRUNCATION IS mb_-AWARE because titles are full of emoji and a half-cut character is invalid UTF-8 that costs the whole row on utf8mb4; it is done in PHP rather than left to MySQL because STRICT MODE MAKES AN OVER-LENGTH VALUE AN ERROR, NOT A TRUNCATION. A MISSING TABLE IS THE EXPECTED PRE-DEPLOY STATE, not a failure: mysqli_report is OFF so query() returns false, every call is @-suppressed (display_errors is ON and this runs inside the request carrying a player's own result back to them), the page renders an empty wall, and the nightly prune reports "no table yet" rather than alerting. Retention ACTIVITY_RETAIN_DAYS = 120. **THE IMAGE SLOT HOLDS TWO DIFFERENT KINDS OF THING.** Reported: the daily-reward cards were a giant white blob. db.php:943 is the ONLY announce on the platform that puts an icon in the image slot -- `icons/<currency>.png`, 120-128px, a flat glyph meant to be seen at 24px -- and it renders correctly in a Discord embed, so it is fixed in the FEED, not by changing a working announce. activity.php detects the ornament directory server-side (`~/icons/[^/]+$~`) and gives it an emblem treatment: 56px, contained, on its own slim band, which also reads as what it is instead of pretending to be art. acShot() is the belt-and-braces for anything under 200px natural width, because this feed absorbs new announcements automatically BY DESIGN and a future small image should not wait for someone to notice; it also sweeps `complete` images on DOMContentLoaded, since a cached image can finish before the onload handler is attached. THE FRAME IS SQUARE (aspect-ratio 1/1, cover). The old `max-height: 240px` cover crop was slicing the top and bottom off art that is MOSTLY ALREADY SQUARE -- realm scenes, Fighter renders, Monstrocity bosses -- which was the actual thing hiding the picture. cover not contain, on the owner's call: square sources crop to nothing, and contain would letterbox every card to accommodate the minority that are taller (mission art runs portrait, buffy-bot-monster.gif is 555x778, and is cropped to its middle). onerror hides the WRAPPER, not the <img>, or the emblem band's padding and border survive as an empty stripe. TWO FOLLOW-UPS FROM LIVE. (1) "Icons have a weird dark border" -- the artwork frame sets `background: #07111d` so a photo has a ground to load against, and these currency icons are TRANSPARENT PNGs, so that ground painted a hard dark square around the glyph; the emblem rule has to undo it with `background: none`. (2) The emblem band takes `flex: 1` so it absorbs whatever height the card has left instead of being a short strip above a void -- these are the shortest cards on the wall, so the leftover space is largest exactly here, and absorbing it centres the mark. A CARD WITH NO ART GETS 22 LINES, NOT 7 (.ac-card.ac-noart). The clamp exists to stop a long description crowding out a 290px image; on an imageless card it did the opposite -- a "Start All" that launched 9 missions listed 4 and trailed off above half a card of nothing. GRID STRETCH IS DELIBERATE: a text-only card beside one with art has empty space below its text, and `align-items: start` was tried and reverted -- it gives natural heights and leaves ragged holes under the short cards instead, trading the flaw rather than fixing it. Real masonry fixes it and costs the reading order (CSS columns fill top-to-bottom per column, so card seven lands at the top of column two), which a newest-first chronology cannot afford. **"INVOLVING ME" IS STRICTLY PASSIVE (user_id2 = me), NOT A SUPERSET.** It shipped as `user_id OR user_id2`, the conventional "everything about me" reading, and in use the two tabs were nearly the same list -- almost everything a player is party to they also started, so the second tab reprinted the first and the few rows worth seeing were buried. Strictly passive, the three tabs PARTITION the feed and the tab contains only what the player would otherwise never have known about. CONSEQUENCE FOR CALLERS: the actor goes in user_id and the counterparty in user_id2, and which way round now MATTERS -- the old OR absorbed a reversed pair silently, this hides the row from one player and misfiles it for the other. activity.php has a scope-specific empty state for it, because empty is a normal state here and a blank panel reads as broken. **EVERY TWO-SIDED ANNOUNCE MUST PASS BOTH IDS.** Reported after launch: "Involving me is displaying notifications I initiated, not the ones that involve me." The filter was correct; the DATA was not -- only ranked Arena passed a second id, so every other multiplayer event recorded one participant and the other side never saw it, making "Involving me" a slower "My activity". Now wired: Gauntlet victory/defeat (runner + the owner of the NFT fought -- gauntletResolveEncounter()'s own comment already calls third-party inclusion the POINT of the Discord ping), Arena live challenge and live match (host + guest, dhcarena-live.php), auction sold/delivered/timed-out and raffle drawn/delivered/timed-out. THE TWO MARKETPLACE CRONS HAVE NO SESSION, so activity_actor() returns 0 and an unattributed announce belongs to nobody -- not even to "My activity" -- which is why even the single-party ones (no bids, no tickets, minimum not met) now name the creator; the harness asserts discordmsg( count == attributed count in both files so a new announce cannot be added unattributed. Watch the variable scope: in BOTH crons phase 1 has `$prev_bidder`/`$winning_uid` (the live pick) and phase 2 has `$winner_id` (the id phase 1 PERSISTED) -- they are different variables in different scopes and the wrong one silently attributes to the wrong person. **NOT FILTERED BY users.visibility**, on the owner's explicit call ("if it goes to discord I think it's safe to mirror to activity") -- but note platform-dashboard.md tells players that setting controls whether "activity appears publicly", so the docs and the behaviour disagree. user_id/user_id2 are stored, so honouring it is one WHERE clause in activity_recent(), no migration. `general`, `member`, `elite`, `innercircle` are dead channels and will simply never appear. **MENTIONS ARE RESOLVED, NOT FLATTENED.** activity_format() first replaced every `<@id>` with the words "a member", which on a live Gauntlets card produced "a member was defeated by a member (mato_b13)" -- two different players erased from a sentence whose entire content was which of them won. activity_mention_names() now looks up `users.discord_id -> username` for a WHOLE PAGE in one query (a leaderboard or Gauntlets run posts a dozen announcements naming the same few people; per-card lookups would be dozens of round trips for one answer), and it is called AFTER the +1 row is popped so the undrawn card pulls no names. "a member" survives as the fallback for an id with no account here. The username is player-supplied and is substituted by a callback running AFTER htmlspecialchars(), so it is escaped separately -- that is a hole if anyone inlines it. **THE CHANNEL SELECT MUST DECLARE ITS OWN COLOURS**: the rule set only padding and font-size at first, leaving everything to the user agent, so on a phone it was the one white rounded system box on a dark page. Values are flexbox.css's own dropdown convention (#filterLeaderboard / .dropdown): #0d1e2e ground, #D6DDDE text, square. `#ac-filters select option` is styled SEPARATELY because a select's background does not inherit into its popup list on Windows or Android. The segmented control is square for the same reason and its padding is 8px so both controls come out 33px. |

| platform-collections.md *(new)*     | Policy registry         | collections.php |
| platform-leaderboards.md *(new)*    | All leaderboards        | leaderboards.php, db.php:4194-5637. AVG LEVELS ARE FORMATTED: the all-time Monstrocity board ranks on AVG(level) and MySQL returns an AVG as a DECIMAL, so the hub card read "Lvl 28.0000" and the row under "Avg Level" matched it; the monthly board uses MAX(level), an integer, which is why only one of the two ever looked wrong. lbAvgLevel() rounds to one decimal and drops a trailing '.0' (28.0000 -> 28, 27.6667 -> 27.7). number_format() runs FIRST so the string always contains a '.', which is what stops the rtrim pair turning "20" into "2" -- the harness pins that case explicitly. Used by both the hub card and the board row so they cannot disagree. WHY THE SWITCHER WAS INVISIBLE ON DESKTOP AND FINE ON A PHONE: flexbox.css pulls #filtered-content up 40px and #filter-nfts (Find a Project) a further 35 and right-aligns it -- rules shared with store/my-nfts/showcase/collections, so they cannot be changed there. The far right of that row was therefore ALREADY occupied, and margin-left:auto put the toggle underneath the project <select>. Measured live: toggle at x1543 y154-178, #filter-nfts y136-166, elementFromPoint at the toggle's centre returned the select. On a phone the media query stacks them, so it showed. Fixed by scoping `#filtered-content{top:0}` and `#filter-nfts{top:0}` to this page, dropping margin-left:auto so the toggle sits next to the back link, and giving .lb-subhead position:relative+z-index:1. Content now begins 14px below the subhead (its own margin) instead of 26px above its bottom edge. BOARD PICKER: renderLeaderboardPicker() puts a grouped <select> of all 23 boards at the right of the same row, so changing BOARD no longer needs the hub either. It targets leaderboardShortestPeriod() -- Weekly, else Monthly, else All-Time -- because the switcher is adjacent, which keeps it one entry per board. No hub entry: the back link is that. THE TWO DROPDOWNS ARE ONE PAIR: filterLeaderboard() used to emit #filter-nfts inside the results panel with its own label style and flexbox.css's #filterLeaderboard (190px wide, 30px tall, 20px bold, 8px radius) -- next to a board picker that looked nothing like it -- and it rendered a BLANK box on a board page because no option matched. It emits .lb-pick markup now, both sit in a .lb-picks wrapper at the right of the subhead, and one rule styles both.
