That rule must name `.lb-pick select#filterLeaderboard`: a bare `.lb-pick select` (0-1-1) loses to flexbox.css's id (1-0-0). Defaults: the selected option is the current project when $filterby is numeric, otherwise a "Choose..." placeholder -- never blank and never appearing to claim whichever project sorts first. The Leaderboard Hub option is gone (the back link in the same row is the better affordance), and #filterLeaderboardForm needs display:none or the hidden form is a zero-size flex ITEM that still collects the row's 18px gap. Labels are "Games:" and "Projects:" (was "Find a Project:"). filterLeaderboard() is called ONLY from leaderboards.php, so none of this is shared. PERIOD SWITCHER ON A BOARD PAGE: opening All-Time and then wanting Monthly meant going back to the hub and finding the card again -- two navigations to change one word, and no way to compare periods. renderLeaderboardPeriods($filterby) prints the sibling periods next to the back link, driven by leaderboardBoardFor(), which reverse-looks-up the slug in $SKULLIANCE_BOARDS -- THE SAME 'periods' map the hub cards are built from, so the two cannot drift: add a period to the catalogue and both grow it. 23 boards, 20 of them multi-period. A one-period board renders NOTHING (on the hub the row is always rendered so card heights match; on a board page a lone inert "All-Time" is a label pretending to be a control). Slugs are compared AS STRINGS because one of them is numeric -- '15' is the delegations board -- and $filterby arrives as a string from the query and as an int elsewhere; that cast, not PHP-7 loose-compare folklore, is what the harness pins (since PHP 8, 0 == 'activity-ath' is already false). leaderboards-harness.php evals the catalogue and both functions straight out of db.php and checks every period slug round-trips to its own board. |
| platform-analytics.md *(new)*       | Public transparency dashboard | analytics.php, ajax/analytics-content.php, ajax/analytics-trends.php, plus analytics-metrics-harness.php. ANALYTICS HAD FALLEN A YEAR BEHIND: the cards covered three games and the trend chart offered 14 metrics, while the platform had shipped eleven games. Now 23 trend metrics in three <optgroup>s (Platform / Realms & Missions / Games) and a card for every game, plus a Specialty Games row for Drop Ship and Oculus Lounge. ONE DEFINITION OF A PLAY, THREE PLACES. db.php's Activity `$sources` (~line 7730) decides what counts: completed delves only for cryptcrawls/cryptconquests (`status IN ('won','lost')`), resolved encounters for Gauntlets (`outcome != 'pending'`), one row per fallen realm for guardians_scores, SUM(attempts) rather than COUNT(*) for the two match-3s, runs not solves for Obscura (`active = 0`). ajax/analytics-trends.php and ajax/analytics-content.php mirror it exactly; change one, change all three. DATE COLUMNS ARE NOT UNIFORM AND NOT GUESSABLE: skull_racer_runs `created_at`, gauntlets_encounters `resolved_date`, dhc_arena_battles `started_at`, dhc_fighters `created_at`, dhc_trait_drops `awarded_at`, nfts/realms/missions/raids `created_date`, everything else `date_created`. Each is taken from that table's own CREATE in the repo (db.php for the racer, obscura-lib.php, guardians-lib.php, dhcarena-schema.md, dhcfighters-schema.md) or from the Activity board's per-source `$w_*` filters. Guessing wrong gives an EMPTY CHART, not an error. `$metrics` entries are now a raw FROM clause, not a bare table name (Gauntlets needs the `ge` alias), so the backticks came off -- safe because `$metric` is checked against the map's keys before any of it reaches a query. A FAILED QUERY NO LONGER LOOKS LIKE AN UNPLAYED GAME: the endpoint returns an `error` key and logs the SQL, and the page prints a message over the canvas instead of drawing an empty chart. NULL periods are dropped (history from before a table got its date column, per db.php's migration note). DROP SHIP AND OCULUS LOUNGE HAVE NO TREND, DELIBERATELY: own database via dropShipDbConnection(), and `results` has NO date column at all -- a run is filed against a game ROUND. Their cards show This Round / All Time instead, and the section is skipped entirely if that database is unreachable rather than rendering a row of zeros. UNDATED ROWS ARE A THIRD STATE, AND THE ONE THAT BIT: `guardians_scores` has 39 sieges live and a NULL `date_created` on every one of them, so All Time drew an empty chart that read as a game nobody plays. guardians-lib.php documents the column as `DATETIME NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` and guardiansFinishRun()'s INSERT does not name it, so the live column does not match the doc -- it was added without the default. THE SAME NULL BREAKS THE MONTHLY REALM GUARDIANS LEADERBOARD, which is the exact failure db.php's migration note warns about: all-time looks fine while the dated views quietly report zero. FIX: `ALTER TABLE guardians_scores MODIFY COLUMN date_created DATETIME NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP;` -- new sieges stamp, the 39 historical rows stay NULL because there is no honest source for when they happened. Until then the endpoint counts the NULL bucket into an `undated` key instead of dropping it, and the page says "39 recorded, none of them dated" and prints an under-chart note when a partial gap would silently undercount a line. Only All Time can see these: every other range filters `DATE(col) >= ...`, which NULL fails. TWO-CARD ROWS USE .ana-row-2: the DHC pair and the Specialty pair sat in a 3-column grid at two-thirds width with a hole where the third card would be. analytics-metrics-harness.php cross-checks the <option> list, metricLabels, `$metrics`, `$SKULLIANCE_BOARDS` (so a new game cannot ship without a trend -- exemptions are an explicit list with reasons) and the Activity `$sources` fragments, and verifies each date column against its CREATE where the repo has one. |
| platform-profile.md *(new)*         | Profile + streak cal    | profile.php  GAMES PANEL: profile-lib.php profile_game_record(), plus profile-harness.php. The page predates nine of the platform's games and showed none of them. One panel, not nine sections -- each game has its own page and leaderboard already. Columns are taken from the existing check*Leaderboard() queries in db.php rather than guessed, so they match the live schema by construction (dhc_fighters, dhc_arena_battles, guardians_scores, cryptcrawls, cryptconquests, gauntlets+gauntlets_encounters, obscura_scores, skull_racer_runs). EVERY query is guarded: mysqli_report(MYSQLI_REPORT_OFF) at db.php:49 means a missing table returns false, not an exception, and profile.php is PUBLIC and takes a username from the query string -- a game not yet deployed must not be able to blank it. A game with no plays returns null and is dropped, so the grid shows what someone HAS done. Harness covers the empty player, zero-vs-absent, a false-returning table not taking the others down, plural/singular, formatting, and that a guest issues no queries at all. STYLING: 29 border-radius declarations squared; 50% kept on avatars (header.php and leaderboards.php are round, squaring only this page would read as a mistake) and 2px on the loader rail (gallery/leaderboards convention). |
| platform-wallets.md *(new)*         | Multi-wallet            | wallets.php, db.php:575-611 (getWallets), db.php checkAddress (returns 'added'/'exists_mine'/'exists_other'/'claimed'), wallet.js (connect flow), wallet-ajax.php, header.php (the global wallet modal -- `renderWalletConnection()` in skulliance.php is now an empty stub). **Lucid is constructed with NO provider (`Lucid.new(undefined, "Mainnet")`) and that is load-bearing.** Passing a Blockfrost provider makes `Lucid.new()` fetch live protocol parameters and hand the cost models to the CML WASM pinned in lucid-cardano@0.8.7, which no longer accepts as many cost-model entries as Cardano has: `Uncaught (in promise) CostModel operation 166 out of bounds. Max is 166`, for every wallet, on every attempt. Nothing here builds a transaction - `selectWallet()`'s `address()`/`rewardAddress()` only decode what the CIP-30 extension already returned - and every line touching protocol parameters sits behind `if (provider)` in that build, so dropping the provider skips the broken path rather than patching around it. Do not reintroduce a provider to "fix" anything; upgrade Lucid instead. This also removed a Blockfrost API key that was sitting in readable client-side source (the server's key is separate, from config). `dropship/wallet.js` had the identical break and the identical fix. Also: `rewardAddress()` returns **null**, not `""`, when a wallet has no stake key - the old `!= ""` check therefore passed stake-less wallets straight through. **connectWallet() must keep its try/catch and must open the modal BEFORE its first await**: it had neither, so a locked extension, a dismissed approval popup, an unreachable Blockfrost and an undelegated wallet all produced the same symptom -- clicking a wallet icon did nothing whatsoever. Multi-wallet has always worked; a "disconnect" button is NOT needed to add a second wallet, and there is deliberately no remove-wallet feature (removing one would unstake its NFTs). `renderWalletConnection($page)` renders the **Connect a Wallet** button and had been reduced to an empty stub, on the reasoning that the header modal covered it - but the only thing opening that modal was an unlabelled icon in the top nav, so wallets.php listed your wallets, said "connect as many as you want", and offered no control. It now always renders on the wallets page and elsewhere only for accounts with **no** wallet yet (which is what its other call sites - points, crafting, missions, realms - existed to prompt). The button depends on `wallet.js` being loaded unconditionally by header.php:73; if that ever becomes conditional, the button becomes a dead click. |
| platform-transactions.md *(new)*    | Ledger                  | transactions.php, db.php:4020-4081 |

---

## Verified Constants (from code, confirmed by grep)

### Daily rewards (db.php:806-830)
- Streak point tiers (RANDOM): day 1→1, 2→3, 3→5, 4→10, 5→15, 6→20, 7→30 (total 84).
- Daily consumable awarded per streak day: 1→Random Reward, 2→25% Success, 3→Fast Forward,
  4→50% Success, 5→75% Success, 6→Double Rewards, 7→100% Success.

### Membership Discord role IDs (skulliance.php:145-212)
- Base 949930195584954378 · Elite 949930360681140274 · Inner Circle 949930529841635348.

### assignRole() and the role-sync burst (role.php, driven from skulliance.php:151-195)
- **THE ROLE LIST IS A LOGIN SNAPSHOT.** `skulliance.php` drives role removal from
  `$_SESSION['userData']['roles']`, filled once at OAuth and never refreshed. It is the
  same snapshot problem documented for the Oculus VIP gate above, in a second place.
- **IT CALLS ONCE PER ROLE.** Up to 7 for the Ritual guild (1235869893664964608) on every
  `dashboard.php` / `my-nfts.php` load. For somebody who has LEFT that guild the snapshot
  still lists their old roles, so all 7 fire, all 404, and the list is unchanged next load.
  That produced both reported symptoms: endless `10007 Unknown Member` alerts, and a 429
  from 7 authenticated calls to one route in milliseconds - us rate-limiting ourselves.
- **A per-request `static $notMember`, keyed `guild|user`**, drops that to one call. It does
  not persist across requests, so a stale snapshot still costs exactly one 404 per page load.
  Fixing that properly means refreshing the snapshot or pruning the session list - not done.
- **10007 / 10013 are logged, never alerted.** The person is not in that guild; no admin can
  fix it. 10004 Unknown Guild (bot removed) and 10011 Unknown Role still alert, because those
  break roles for everyone.
- **429 is a wait, not a failure.** `retry_after` comes back in the body, routinely under a
  second for this route; retried once, and only when the wait is <= 2s, because this runs
  inside a page load. A longer wait means give up and say so.
- **THE HINT USED TO BLAME THE TOKEN FOR EVERYTHING.** "Check the bot token" was the default,
  so a 429 sent you to rotate a working credential. The default now says what is actually
  known, and 403 / 429 / 10004 each have their own.
- `SKULL_DISCORD_API` makes it testable; production never defines it. `role-harness.php`
  points it at a local `php -S` **router** (not files - the endpoint has no extension and
  answers PUT/DELETE, which the built-in server 405s on a plain .php) and drives: left-guild,
  the burst, the 429 retry including that it really waits, the hints, and success staying silent.
- No `curl_close()` here either - same reason as dropship/role.php.

### DHC Arena: the shield ceiling and the cascade stall (dhcarena-engine.php)
- `DHCA_SHIELD_CAP = 0.50` - max shield as a share of the Fighter's own maxHp. There was
  no cap and nothing removes shield except damage absorbed and a Break hit, so it banked
  for a whole battle. Measured before the fix over 300 simulated battles: median 0,
  **p99 802, worst 8117**. After: p99 202, max 287. Median turns unchanged at 27.
- **THE SHIELD EVENT MUST ANNOUNCE THE GAIN THAT LANDED, NOT THE ONE OFFERED.** The client
  does `shield += e.v` to move the bar as the wave plays, so reporting the uncapped figure
  floats its bars above the server's numbers for the rest of the battle. The harness's
  "fx covers every bar" check catches this - but only since it was made to EXIT NONZERO;
  it printed its divergence count and carried on, so it had been passing on a mutation
  that did exactly this.
- **THE CASCADE STALL, which was the real bug.** `dhca_resolve_wave()` drops any cell
  holding a not-yet-live bomb from the clear, so arming a bomb does not delete the gem
  under it. When EVERY cell of a matched group carries one, the whole match is dropped,
  the board is untouched, and `dhca_matches()` returns the identical match next pass - so
  it resolves again, to the guard at `$chain <= 40`, chain multiplier climbing to 14.65x.
  `dhca_play()` now breaks when `$b['_cleared']` is empty.
- **THE TELL WAS THE DISTRIBUTION, NOT THE SYMPTOM.** Reported as an opponent repeating a
  match with ~700 shield. What identified it was the ladder: two players with BEST CHAIN
  of exactly **x40** while everyone else sat x5-x9. Chains run 2..12 and then jump straight
  to 40 with nothing between, because 40 was never a cascade - it was the guard.
- **THE REPRODUCING SEEDS ARE LOAD-BEARING.** The runaway is ~0.5% of battles, so the first
  version of the check ran 60 arbitrary battles, passed, and went on passing with the fix
  removed. `$shRunaway` in dhcarena-harness.php holds eight seeds swept from 3000 battles
  against the pre-fix engine; each is a live reproduction. Note `crew()` takes `&$seed` and
  MUTATES it, so a seed captured after the call is not the one that produced the battle.
- Ladder reads `MAX(b.best_chain)`, so one glitched battle pollutes a player's entry
  permanently. x40 rows in `dhc_arena_battles` predate the fix and are not achievable now.

### Monstrocity themes are a TABLE now (monstrocity-themes-lib.php, monstrocity-schema.md)
- 49 themes used to be a `const themes = [...]` literal inside monstrocity.php. They are rows
  in `monstrocity_themes`. **Nothing creates the table** - run the SQL in monstrocity-schema.md
  by hand, same as dhcarena-schema.md.
- **project_id / collection_id are NULLABLE AND MOSTLY NULL.** Many themes are for projects
  that are not on Skulliance, so there is nothing to point at. `project_name`/`title` carry the
  display strings. Do not make the FKs required.
- **VISIBILITY IS TWO BOOLEANS, NOT A TIER.** The old `<?php if($dev)` block was nested inside
  `<?php if($innercircle)`, so the one dev theme (`j2`) needs BOTH. A single tier column would
  show it to a dev who is not Inner Circle - a state the old code could never produce.
- **THE GATE IS monstrocity-themes-harness.php** and it compares against
  `monstrocity-themes-snapshot.php`, a frozen copy of the original literal. That snapshot is a
  FIXTURE: never edit it to make a test pass, and never add new themes to it. New themes are rows.
- **THE GATE IS NO LONGER BYTE-IDENTITY, AND THE SNAPSHOT WAS NOT REGENERATED.** Themes are now
  ordered alphabetically by project within each group (`monstrocity_sort_themes()`), because
  sort_order put every new theme at the BOTTOM of its group - Happy Hoppers landed after
  everything else. That is a deliberate output change, so the gate became SET identity: the same
  themes, in the same groups, visible to the same people, with order asserted separately. Editing
  the snapshot to agree would have thrown away the only record of what the literal held.
- **The admin list uses the same comparator**, so the panel lists themes in the order players
  see them. It was in sort_order, which put every new theme at the bottom - the same reason a new
  theme was hard to find in the picker.
- **The default game is PINNED FIRST** in its group; "Monstrocity" would otherwise sort under M
  among the partner projects. One line in the comparator if that is ever wrong.
- Comparison is case-insensitive (a lowercase project name must not sort after "Zenith"), then
  title, then sort_order as a final tiebreaker so ties stay deterministic.
- The comparison is byte-identical for 2 of the 4 visibility combinations and
  **identical-ignoring-whitespace** for the other 2. The difference is 15-18 bytes of indentation
  the `<?php if ?>` tag left in its own output. Because that comparison is blind to whitespace,
  the harness ALSO pins exact newline/group/item counts per combination - without those, a
  mutation collapsing every separator newline passed.
- **THE STUB OBEYS THE SQL.** Its first version sorted and filtered in PHP whatever it was asked,
  so reversing the ORDER BY and deleting `active = 1` both passed. It now honours those two
  clauses and records the query.
- **DEPLOY ORDER DOES NOT MATTER**, because an empty result falls back to the snapshot and logs.
  Worst case of pulling before running the SQL is yesterday's theme list, not an empty picker on
  a public game page. Still run the SQL first.
- monstrocity.php now includes db.php (it previously had NO database connection at all) and its
  `session_start()` is guarded, because db.php starts one conditionally and a second call is an
  E_WARNING printed into the page.
- `monstrocity-test.php` is a dead Grok-era backup carrying its own drifted copy (40 themes vs 49).
  Deliberately NOT wired to the table. A deletion candidate, not a consumer.
- **COLLECTIONS ARE MANY PER THEME, so they are `monstrocity_theme_collections`, not a column.**
  That multiplicity is exactly why some themes carry a LIST of policy ids. Attach collections and
  the policies are read from them; attach none and the legacy `policy_ids` string stands. Nothing
  is attached at migration time, which is why every byte comparison still passes.
- **A FAILED OR MISSING LINK TABLE MUST NOT BLANK THE POLICIES.** A theme with no policy ids
  matches nobody's NFTs and silently stops recognising holdings. The override only applies when
  the derived list is non-empty, and the harness drives a connection that fails just that query.
- **NOTHING IN THE CODEBASE CREATES `monstrocity_theme_collections`** -- it is a manual step out
  of monstrocity-schema.md, and that is the whole feature's silent failure mode. Until it is run,
  mysqli returns false for every query against it WITHOUT THROWING: the admin's DELETE and INSERT
  both fail, the page still says "Saved.", and every tick is discarded with the boxes empty on
  reopen. Combined with clearing the policy_ids textarea -- the natural thing to do once you
  believe the collections have taken over -- the theme matches nobody and says nothing about why.
  `monstrocity_theme_links_table()` (a cached `SHOW TABLES LIKE`) now gates it, and the admin
  reports it ON THE FORM as well as on the save, so the edit is not made twice. A PARTIAL write
  is reported too: a derived list that is a SUBSET of what was ticked reads on the game as "some
  of my NFTs are missing", which is harder to diagnose than none of them.
- **`c.policy IS NOT NULL AND c.policy <> ''`, both stated.** `x <> ''` evaluates to NULL for a
  NULL row, which is not TRUE, so a NULL policy was already excluded -- by three-valued logic
  rather than by statement, and reading the query it looked like only the empty string was. They
  are different mistakes with different fixes; monstrocity-theme-check.php reports them apart.
- **THE PROJECT FILTER WAS READ AS A SELECT-ALL, AND SAVED A THEME MATCHING NOBODY.** It was one
  unlabelled checkbox, "Show every project's collections", sitting directly above a list of
  checkboxes -- which is where a select-all lives. Ticked, nothing selected, policy_ids cleared
  because the collections were believed to have taken over: every warning in the save needs
  collections to BE ticked, so all of them stayed quiet. Now a labelled radio pair (a two-state
  choice, not a thing you switch on) with the bulk action it was mistaken for beside it as
  buttons -- Tick all shown acts on the filtered set, Untick all clears everything including
  hidden rows, or a link would exist that the admin can neither see nor remove. Both carry
  `type="button"`; a bare <button> in a form submits it. And a save that ends with NO policy ids
  from either source now warns, which is the guard that catches this whatever the route in.
- **admin-css.php IS INCLUDED LAST, SO IT WINS EVERY TIE.** `.adm label{display:flex;
  flex-direction:column}` and `.adm button{background:#00c8a0;font-weight:bold}` are both (0,1,1)
  and beat a bare class: unscoped, the new filter radios stacked their dot above their text, the
  two bulk tools rendered as filled mint primaries louder than Save, and every collection row had
  had its checkbox on the line above its name all along. None of it is visible in the source --
  caught by previewing against the real stylesheet IN THE REAL ORDER (page <style> first,
  admin-css.php after), which is the whole reason that order matters in a preview. The harness
  asserts each of the three is scoped under .adm, anchored on the rule's brace: a plain prefix
  match hit ".adm .mt-radio input" and let the rule that actually fights .adm label go unscoped.
- `monstrocity-theme-check.php` is the diagnostic: per theme, the attached collections and each
  one's policy, derived versus legacy, **what the shipped `monstrocity_themes()` actually hands
  the game** (not a second opinion about it), and -- given a username -- how many NFTs that player
  would be offered next to where the rest of their NFTs really live. It also flags orientations,
  which are POSITIONAL against the policy list and silently reassign when the derived list is a
  different length from the string they were written for.
- **TWO QUERIES, NOT A GROUP_CONCAT.** A GROUP_CONCAT join needs a GROUP BY that ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY
  can reject, and truncates at group_concat_max_len (1024, about 17 policies) SILENTLY. Two plain
  reads merged in PHP have neither failure mode.
- The stub dispatches on which table the SQL names. Returning themes for every query made the
  collection-override path untestable - it would never see a link row. And `lastSql` is useless
  with two queries: assert against the one that names the table you mean.

### Monstrocity theme admin (admin-monstrocity.php, Phase 2)
- Registered in `admin_tabs()` as `monstrocity`. Purpose is ADDING themes, not editing the 49
  that exist, so the list is deliberately plain and the create form is where the work went.
- **THE SLUG BECOMES A DIRECTORY**, so it is validated hard (lowercase/digits/hyphen, no leading
  or trailing hyphen, which also excludes `.` and `..`) and the harness asserts the ORDER:
  validation runs before `@mkdir`. Presence of a validator proves nothing if the folder is made first.
- **THE SLUG IS IMMUTABLE ONCE CREATED.** Changing it would orphan every image under
  `images/monstrocity/<slug>/`. Renaming belongs to Phase 3, where art is being moved anyway.
- **POLICY IDS ARE LENGTH-CHECKED (56 hex).** A truncated paste is the error that produces a
  theme matching nobody, with nothing in any log to say so.
- Visibility nesting is enforced in validation too: dev-only without Inner Circle is refused,
  because the old conditionals could never produce it.
- Collection links are REPLACED wholesale on save; the form submits the complete set, so a diff
  would only be a way to get it subtly wrong. Link order is the policy-id order.
- Uploads reuse `adm_accept_upload()` -> `admin_write_image()` (Imagick, memory-budgeted,
  GIF-aware, type taken from file content not filename). Background and logo are capped on the
  long edge with NO forced aspect ratio - a background is chosen to read either side of the board.
- Creating a theme makes `base/`, `leader/` and `battle-damaged/` so Phase 3 has somewhere to write.
- The list's "Art on disk" column counts real files per folder, so what is still missing is
  visible without opening an FTP client.
- **LIST OR FORM, NEVER BOTH.** The form started as a 420px column beside the list and grew to
  seventeen controls. One URL, two exclusive views: no `?edit`/`?new` shows the list.
- **BLANK PROJECT NAME MEANS INHERIT.** `monstrocity_display_project()` resolves it at save, so
  the stored row always holds a usable label even if the project is unlinked later. Typing a name
  overrides. Validation only demands a name when NO project is linked.
- **THE COLLECTION LIST FILTERS TO THE SELECTED PROJECT**, with a "show every project" escape and
  a count of what is hidden. A TICKED collection is never hidden - not because hiding would unlink
  it (a checked box in a `display:none` label is still submitted; only `disabled` stops that) but
  because a theme can span projects and you cannot save what you cannot see.
- `.mt-back`, `.mt-field label`, `.mt-cols label` and `table.mt a.slug` all restate `text-align`
  to counter `dist/flexbox.css`'s global `a { text-align:center }`. The harness asserts this
  CONDITIONALLY, so the guard drops if that global ever goes.
- **ANCHOR WARNING FOR ANYONE EXTENDING THE HARNESS HERE.** The list cell that reports policies
  contains its own inline `<?php else:` and `<?php endif; ?>`. Both of the obvious anchors for
  "is the form inside the else branch" therefore match the WRONG occurrence, and the check passed
  with the branch separator deleted. It anchors on the unique `FORM` banner and uses `strrpos`
  for the endif.

### Monstrocity character art (Phase 3: admin-monstrocity-art.php, ajax/monstrocity-art.php, monstrocity-art-lib.php)
- **THE `active` FLAG IS THE COMMIT.** Build a theme switched off, fill the board, switch it on.
  That is why no staging layer exists: each slot saves straight to the live folder and the theme
  simply is not in anyone's picker yet.
- **NOTHING IS BATCHED.** Every upload, flip and clear is one request against one slot, so a
  failure costs that slot. There is deliberately no "save all" and the harness asserts its absence.
- **THE BROWSER RESIZES BEFORE SENDING** (stepwise halving on a canvas, then one final draw). A
  source NFT is tens of MB, larger than post_max_size, and arrives as NO POST AT ALL - the exact
  failure that wiped the theme form. After resizing, ~200KB goes over the wire and a timeout stops
  being possible. Stepwise rather than one jump: a single large downscale aliases on detailed art.
- **DAMAGE IS GENERATED, NEVER UPLOADED**, by HSL(hue 0, saturation 100%, lightness preserved).
  Verified against 25 shipped base/damaged pairs across 5 themes at **0.234/255 mean error** - the
  same image the owner was making by hand. A base regenerates its damaged variant on every write
  and every flip, which is what stops the pair drifting apart (the live library has pairs that did).
- **MONSTROCITY_DAMAGE_WEAK = 20.** Below it the red does not read on that art and the slot wants
  different art instead. Calibrated from the live library: working themes score 50-80, invisible
  ones under 10. This is the ONE place the owner's eye is actually required.
- Resize is **500 wide, aspect preserved, never upscaled**. 11 of 45 themes are not square.
- A MISSED DROP MUST NOT NAVIGATE THE PAGE. A window-level dragover/drop guard cancels the default
  outside a slot; the workflow is dragging one file at a time out of a Finder window, so missing
  is routine and the default action is to walk away from a half-filled board.
- The endpoint takes the theme's folder from the DATABASE, never the request, and the character
  must be one of the 14. Both are paths; neither is client-supplied.

### Monstrocity art board: finishing a theme
- **THERE IS NO SAVE BUTTON AND THE BOARD HAS TO SAY SO.** Reported from real use: 28 squares
  filled, everything already on disk, and no way to tell. A banner states it outright, and the
  only genuinely pending thing - the `active` flag - is a button right there.
- **ACTIVATION IS REFUSED ON AN INCOMPLETE SET**, counted from the DISK by `mart_missing_slots()`,
  not from what the page last drew. A tab left open since before a square was cleared cannot
  publish a theme with a broken image in it. Both base AND leader are required for all 14.
- **"Flip both" is two single-slot requests**, not a batch endpoint. The rule that one failure
  costs one square holds for it too, and the harness asserts no batch flip action exists.
- Escape the FIELDS, not the separator: `htmlspecialchars($a . ' &mdash; ' . $b)` turns the `&`
  into `&amp;` and prints the entity as literal text. It shipped reading "HAPPY HOPPERS &MDASH;".

### Monstrocity logos: one box, 300x125 (mart_fit_logo)
- **THE PAGE RENDERS `max-width:300px; height:auto`** (monstrocity.php:124), which caps WIDTH and
  leaves height free. That gives three different wrongs: 400x125 draws at 300x94 and looks small,
  200x400 draws full height and towers, 119x128 draws tiny. All three were being fixed by hand.
- **MEASURED FROM THE SHIPPED LOGOS: 44 of 49 are exactly 125 tall**, widths 119-400 (plus the
  default theme's 1024x392 outlier). The invariant the design wants is that every logo occupies the
  SAME BOX, so uploads are now letterboxed into 300x125 with transparent padding and centred. That
  makes max-width a no-op and the rendered height always 125.
- **CONTAIN, NEVER COVER OR STRETCH.** A logo is type and a mark; cropping or distorting either
  ruins it. A 200x400 source draws about 63px wide inside the box, not 300.
- **THE PADDING MUST BE EXPLICITLY TRANSPARENT.** `imagecreatetruecolor()` starts OPAQUE BLACK, so
  skipping the alpha fill gives every logo a black slab that looks almost right on a dark page and
  is impossible to unsee afterwards. The harness checks a corner pixel's alpha.
- Small art IS enlarged rather than left tiny, but the caller is told, because the only fix for a
  soft logo is a bigger source.
- Existing logos are NOT rewritten. Padding a 232x125 to 300x125 renders identically (it was
  already centred by `margin:0 auto`), so there is nothing to gain from touching them.
- The theme form shows the current logo on a checkerboard with its dimensions, and flags it when
  it is not the standard box.

### Monstrocity bosses (admin-monstrocity-boss.php, bosses table)
- Columns: `id, name, health, max_health, strength, speed, tactics, size, powerup, project_id,
  collection_id, theme, orientation, extension`. **THERE WAS NO CREATE PATH AT ALL** - bosses were
  hand-inserted in phpMyAdmin; the only writes in the codebase were health resets.
- **A BOSS WITH A MISSING project_id OR collection_id DOES NOT ERROR, IT DISAPPEARS.**
  ajax/get-bosses.php INNER JOINs both, so a bad id silently drops the row from the game with
  nothing logged. That is the single worst thing this form can produce and it is refused hardest.
- **ART IS ONE FLAT FOLDER shared by every theme**: `images/monstrocity/bosses/<name>.png` plus
  `bosses/battle-damaged/<same>`. Filename derived from the NAME, same rule as characters.
- **500 wide, aspect preserved** - measured from uptown (500x593) and ronin (500x500), same as
  character art, so mart_resize() needed no special case.
- **The red formula holds for bosses**: uptown generated vs shipped is 0.098/255. ronin is 2.97,
  with 85% of pixels inside 5/255 and the outliers on grey source pixels where the shipped art went
  saturated red and the formula gives pale pink - the same older-settings drift as the 18 themes.
  Not a reason to change the formula.
- **RENAMING A BOSS MOVES ITS ART** (`mart_rename_boss`), unlike a theme slug or a character name.
  A boss name is free text somebody will want to correct, and the filename is derived from it.
- `theme` is still a SLUG STRING, not a FK to monstrocity_themes. The form validates it against
  real themes and the list flags "no such theme", but the column was left alone - converting it is
  a separate migration with its own byte-identical gate.
- Powerups are the only four the game implements: Minor Regen, Regenerate, Boost Attack, Heal.
  Anything else is a boss with no ability.
- **ORDER IS PROJECT, COLLECTION, STRENGTH, SPEED, TACTICS, id** - one decision, two
  implementations:
  `monstrocity_boss_order()` in PHP and the ORDER BY in ajax/get-bosses.php, which
  `monstrocity_boss_order_sql()` generates the canonical spelling of. **The harness extracts the
  game's clause and compares it EXACTLY** - a prefix match let a shortened helper pass.
- **THE GROUPING IS NOT OPTIONAL.** I removed project/collection from the ordering on the
  reasoning that the picker's project dropdown made contiguity unnecessary. WRONG: the picker
  opens UNFILTERED, so without grouping every project's bosses interleave by difficulty and the
  list is a jumble. Restored, and the harness now asserts that a WEAK boss in project 2 still
  sorts after a STRONG one in project 1.
- The original mismatch was the admin ignoring the grouping, not the game having it. The tune
  screen draws the same project+collection runs the picker does, numbers rows within a run, and
  the arrows stop at run edges - swapping stats across a boundary would move neither boss where
  it looked like it was going.
- **ESCALATION IS JUDGED INSIDE A RUN.** Comparing the first boss of one collection against the
  last of another reports a dip no player ever experiences.
- Three stats give enough resolution to place a boss exactly without inventing a sort column, and
  they are the same three the arrows move. Sharing a strength is now FINE; only a full tie on all
  three is undefined, and that is what `monstrocity_boss_set_problems()` reports.
- **THE TUNE SCREEN SHOWS ONE THEME, THE GAME LISTS THEM ALL.** A theme's bosses are a SUBSEQUENCE
  of one global climb, so row 3 of 5 can be 11th in game. The "In game" column prints that, or the
  panel looks like it disagrees with the game again.
- **DUPLICATE carries everything except the name and the art.** Both are keyed by the name, so
  copying them would point two bosses at one image and the second save would replace the first's.
  Strength is bumped by one so the copy lands AFTER its source rather than tying with it.
- **THE BATCH TUNER (`?tune=<theme-slug>`) IS FIELDS ONLY.** It saves a whole theme's stats in one
  pass because a ladder only makes sense read together, but it never touches a file - the
  one-request-per-image rule the art board lives by is untouched, and the harness brace-matches
  the batch block to prove `$_FILES` never appears inside it.
- Each batch row is validated against the STORED row merged with the submitted numbers; validating
  a half-row would reject it for fields the tuning screen never showed. A row that fails is
  skipped and named, not written.

### TRAP: skulliance.php's store handler used to eat any form with a `name` field
- `skulliance.php` is included by EVERY page, and its store-item submission handler engaged on
  `isset($_POST['name'])` ALONE. Any form anywhere with a field called "name" was therefore
  treated as a store submission. The Monstrocity boss form raised "Please fill out all the fields
  in the item submission form" on a save that had already succeeded.
- **The alert was the mild end of it.** A form posting name, image_url, price, quantity and
  project_id together would have CREATED A STORE LISTING and announced it to Discord from an
  unrelated page.
- The guard now requires the store's own fields to be PRESENT. store.php sends all of them, so its
  empty-field alert is unchanged - `isset()` is about existing, the check inside is about filled in.
- admin-harness.php reads the guard condition out of the file and asserts each field is in it, and
  separately asserts that NO `admin-*.php` posts the full store field set.

### Monstrocity bosses: building a ladder without round trips
- Saving used to land back on the boss just finished. **Save & copy** redirects to a new form
  prefilled from the one just saved (strength +1), which is the loop a ladder is actually built in;
  **Save & new** goes to a blank one. Both are redirects, so a refresh cannot resubmit the boss.
- Only on a CLEAN save. If the art was refused, that message is the only way to find out, and
  redirecting past it would lose it.

### THE FILENAME RULE (monstrocity_char_slug + monstrocitySlug in monstrocity.php)
- Was `strtolower` + spaces-to-hyphens, in PHP and spelled out SIX TIMES in the game's JS.
  "Dr. Vape" became `dr.-vape` and the file is `dr-vape.png`, so the admin reported the art
  missing **and the game had never found it either** - that boss was showing a broken image in
  play. The panel only made a pre-existing bug visible.
- **APOSTROPHES ARE DELETED; EVERYTHING ELSE SEPARATES.** Confirmed against the live server, which
  is the only authority on what these files are called:
  `Dr. Vape -> dr-vape`, `Ivy V. -> ivy-v`, **`Jack O'Treat -> jack-otreat`** (the apostrophe
  VANISHES - treating it as a separator gives jack-o-treat, which does not exist),
  `Jack-O-Treat -> jack-o-treat` (a real hyphen does separate).
- The two implementations MUST agree. The harness drives both against the same list of awkward
  names, including the 14 characters, whose slugs are unchanged by the new rule.

### A SCREEN MAY ONLY REFUSE WHAT IT SHOWS (monstrocity_validate_boss_stats)
- The batch tuner ran the FULL boss validator over the stored row merged with the submitted
  numbers. Result: one boss saved and thirteen errors reading "Orientation must be Left or Right"
  from a form that has no orientation field, with nothing the user could do about any of them.
- The batch validates ONLY max_health, strength, speed, tactics, size and powerup - exactly what
  it displays. A pre-existing problem in another field belongs to the single-boss form, which
  does show it. The harness asserts a blank orientation/extension/name/theme/project/collection
  cannot block a batch save, and that what IS shown is still checked.

### The boss list: 124 bosses, grouped, and not decoding them all
- **THE LIST WAS DECODING EVERY BOSS'S ART TO DRAW ITSELF.** `mb_art()` loaded BOTH PNGs in full
  and sampled ~22,500 pixels for a damage score - about 11ms per boss locally and several times
  that on the host. With 124 bosses that is seconds of image work before anything renders, every
  load, for a number that belongs on the per-theme and per-boss screens. The score is OPT-IN now
  (`mb_art($slug, $ext, true)`); the list passes nothing and pays only for `getimagesize()`.
- Thumbnails carry `loading="lazy"`, and the list is nested `<details>`: project closed by
  default, collection open inside it, so one click reaches the bosses. The grouping matches the
  picker's own project-then-collection order.

### TRAP: admin-harness.php's `$PAGES` was a hardcoded list of four
- The per-page checks (admin_require, admin_chrome) ran over four pages named when there were
  four. SIX MORE were added since and none were covered, which is how
  admin-monstrocity-boss.php shipped passing `admin_chrome('monstrocity')` - the Bosses page lit
  the Monstrocity tab and nothing caught it.
- There is now a sweep over `glob('admin-*.php')`, so a new page is covered the day it exists. It
  asserts the gate, the layout call, that the tab key is real, and that a page owning a tab
  highlights its own. A page may borrow another's tab (the art board has none of its own).
- It uses `harness_strip_comments()`, because several of those pages contain `accept="image/*"`
  and the inline stripper eats live code from the slash-star in it.

### A BARE CID IN METADATA WAS LOSING ITS FIRST SEVEN CHARACTERS (verify.php)
- `$ipfs = substr($image, 7, strlen($image));` ran unconditionally, assuming everything reaching
  it was an `ipfs://` URI. CIP-25 permits a BARE CID and older collections use one, so
  `QmQtVpbUTXB29t...` was stored as `UTXB29tNsdza...` - a CID no gateway resolves, rendering the
  skull placeholder forever with nothing anywhere saying why. Found on the Happy Hoppers Club
  Posters, whose on-chain metadata is `"image": "QmQtVpbU..."` with no scheme.
- Now strips the prefix only when `stripos($image, 'ipfs://') === 0`. Strictly additive: an
  `ipfs://` value takes the identical path. **Rows written before this stay corrupt** - find them
  with the SELECT in the commit message; they need re-verifying, not editing.
- This affects EVERY chain and collection, not just Monstrocity.

### Monstrocity resolves NFT images the way My NFTs does (monstrocityImageChain)
- **THE THEME'S `ipfs_prefixes` COLUMN IS DEAD. Nothing reads it.** Storing a gateway per theme
  was the wrong shape: it pinned all 49 themes to ipfs.io, and it made a gateway going bad a data
  migration instead of a one-line change in lib/ipfs-gateways.php. The column is left in the
  table; do not reintroduce a reader for it.
- The chain is: **locally cached file -> each measured gateway -> the skull.** The cached copy is
  first because it is the only candidate not involving a gateway at all, and it is the whole
  reason My NFTs was reliable while the game was not. `ajax/get-nft-assets.php` ALREADY returned
  `localUrl` - the game simply was not using it as more than a single fallback.
- `monstrocityNextSrc()` WALKS the chain on error. Previously one failed gateway was the end of it.
- **IT IS AN INLINE `onerror`, DELIBERATELY.** These images are built as template strings with an
  onerror attribute, and an inline handler pre-empts a document-level listener entirely - the
  trap that stopped the avatar repair firing on the leaderboards. A listener here would never
  run, and the harness asserts one is not added.

### THE GAME WAS FETCHING FROM A GATEWAY THAT REFUSES US (monstrocity.php)
- `https://ipfs.io/ipfs/` was hardcoded in two places AND is the stored `ipfs_prefixes` of all 49
  themes. **ipfs.io answers 429 to this server** - measured 2026-10-05 in lib/ipfs-gateways.php
  and confirmed again on a Happy Hoppers poster: filebase 200 in 2.3s, ipfs.io 429, dweb.link 429.
  A failed image plus the browser retrying it is what "stuttering" looks like.
- `getIPFS()` in db.php has avoided ipfs.io for a while and prefers a LOCAL CACHED COPY first,
  which is why My NFTs worked and the game did not - the game never used it.
- The game now reads `ipfs_gateways()` and drops known-dead hosts from whatever a theme names, so
  a theme cannot pin itself to a gateway that will not answer. New themes default to NO prefix.
- **`ipfs_gateways()` is required INSIDE `ensureNFTImageCached()` in db.php**, so any page echoing
  the list must require lib/ipfs-gateways.php itself or hit an undefined function.
- A harness HEAD request to filebase returns 0; it does not answer HEAD. Use a ranged GET.

### TRAP: a `::before` marker is a FLEX ITEM, and space-between centres the middle one
- The boss list's project headings use a `::before` triangle instead of the default disclosure
  marker. A pseudo-element on a flex container IS a flex item, so the summary had THREE -
  marker, project name, boss count - and `justify-content:space-between` did exactly what it
  says: first at the start, last at the end, **middle one centred**. The project name sat in the
  middle of a very wide row and read as deliberately centred text.
- `flex-start` plus `margin-left:auto` on the count gives what space-between was there for.
  `text-align:left` is stated anyway, since flexbox.css centres bare anchors platform-wide.
- The harness asserts space-between is not used on a summary carrying a marker.

### TRAP: a `<select>` that does not reflect the stored value REWRITES IT
- Reported as size and powerup "not loading from the database" on the boss tune screen. A strict
  `===` against the allowed list matched nothing for a value stored with different case, so the
  browser showed the FIRST option.
- **THAT IS A SILENT DATA REWRITE, not a display bug.** The batch form submits every field, so one
  press of "Save all" would have written "Small" over every boss stored as "small".
- `monstrocity_select_options()` matches case- and space-insensitively, and when a value genuinely
  is not in the list it KEEPS it as a selected option labelled "(not a known value)" rather than
  swapping it. Validation then refuses it on save and names the boss - a human decides, nothing is
  rewritten quietly. Every select on both Monstrocity forms goes through it.
- Companion to the no-`value=` trap below: one submits the wrong value, the other displays the
  wrong value. Both end in the database.

### Boss reordering: arrows move the BOSS, the ladder keeps its curve
- Order is strength, so reordering meant working out which numbers produce the sequence you
  wanted. The arrows swap the whole difficulty block (health, strength, speed, tactics) with the
  neighbouring row, so the curve stays exactly as tuned and stays monotonic BY CONSTRUCTION.
- **Size and powerup do NOT swap.** They belong to the boss, not the rung - a Large boss does not
  become Small by being fought earlier.
- Purely client-side until "Save all", so a reorder is abandonable by leaving the page. The
  harness asserts no fetch happens from the arrow handler.

### TRAP: an `<option>` with no `value=` submits its DISPLAY TEXT
- Reported as "Extension must be one of: png, gif, jpg, mov" while PNG was selected. The boss
  form's extension options showed `.png` with a leading dot and carried no `value=`, so the
  browser posted `.png` and validation refused the thing the user had plainly chosen.
- The page's other selects worked only BY ACCIDENT, because their text equalled their value. All
  twelve options now carry an explicit value, and the harness requires `<option` and
  `<option value=` counts to match in both Monstrocity forms - that catches the whole class
  rather than this one label.

### TRAP: GD dies mid-request on a large source image (mart_too_big)
- GD allocates ~4 bytes per pixel and a resize holds source and destination at once, so a
  4500x4500 PNG needs about 190MB. Over memory_limit PHP does not fail politely - it dies
  mid-request, which on ajax/monstrocity-art.php is a blank reply the page reports as "not JSON".
- `mart_too_big()` reads the dimensions FROM THE HEADER, before any decode, and refuses with the
  size, the budget and what to export instead. The harness asserts the check runs before
  `imagecreatefromstring`, because afterwards the memory is already gone.
- `mart_load()` returns null and `mart_load_why()` carries the reason, so a refusal does not come
  back as "that is not an image" to somebody who uploaded a perfectly good one.
- Boss art posts the ORIGINAL file (unlike the character board, which resizes in the browser
  first), so the boss form also carries the dropped-POST detection and prints both limits.

### HARNESS TRAP: the comment stripper ate `accept="image/*"` (harness-strip.php)
- The one-line `preg_replace('!/\*.*?\*/!s', ...)` that every harness used inline treats the MIME
  wildcard in `accept="image/*"` as a block-comment opener and deletes to the next real close -
  **4KB to 9KB of LIVE CODE** on admin-monstrocity.php, admin-monstrocity-art.php and
  admin-flyers.php. Assertions ran against a mangled file; two failed for that reason alone and
  the rest passed without ever seeing the code they name.
- `harness_strip_comments()` only treats a block comment as one when its opener is the first
  non-whitespace on the line. **Other harnesses in this repo still use the naive inline version -
  any that read a file containing `image/*` are suspect.**
- Related: do not put a literal block-comment close, or a `];`, inside a comment that something
  parses. Both have already truncated an extraction in this feature.

### Crafting (db.php:3947-3990)
- Craft: burn equal parts of all 6 core points → DIAMOND (1:1 per point type).
- Shatter: DIAMOND → equal parts of all 6 core points.
- Burn: 100 CARBON = 1 DIAMOND. (NOTE: the "minimum batch of 1,000" claim from the old
  GitBook is NOT enforced in code - code only requires multiples of 100. Harmonized in docs.)

### Realms locations (db.php) - location_id / project_id 1-7, all cap at level 10
- 1 Portal: raids_allowed = portal_level; soldiers per raid scale with it.
- 2 Armory: nightly gear drops (L1 = 1; L2+ = rand(1, min(10, level))).
- 3 Tower: garrison up to 10 trained soldiers; TowerScore = (garrison/10)*10.
- 4 Barracks: trains soldiers; training time = (11 - level) * 24 hours; deployment cap = min(100, barracks_level*10).
- 5 Factory: nightly consumable drops (level = items/day).
- 6 Crypt: resurrects dead soldiers; time = (11 - level) * 24 hours.
- 7 Mine: CARBON = level * 100 per night.
- Upgrade cost = next_level * 100 project points (3x cost if paying with non-core points).
- Raid offense = ceil((Armory + Barracks + Crypt + BarracksScore)/4); defense = ceil((Tower + Factory + Mine + TowerScore)/4).
- NOTE: old GitBook "3%/9% loot" wording is not directly verifiable in code; keep loot
  description qualitative until the exact endRaid loot formula is confirmed.

### Monthly/weekly reward pools (db.php)
- Missions monthly LB: 100,000 CARBON / rank (db.php:4466).
- Realms/Raids monthly LB: 1,000,000 CARBON fair-share (db.php:4606).
- Streaks LB: 10,000 CARBON (db.php:4902).
- Monstrocity monthly LB: 30,000 CLAW + 30,000 CARBON / rank (db.php:5510-5511).
- Skull Swap weekly LB: 25,000 CARBON (db.php:5020).
- Gauntlets weekly LB: 25,000 CARBON (db.php:5264).
- Boss Battles weekly LB: CLAW/CARBON split by damage (db.php:5139-5258).
- Share on X (db.php `shareOnXUrl()` / `shareOnXButton()` + per-game `*ShareText()` builders,
  defined just above `checkActivityLeaderboard()`): finish-screen share buttons on all 6 games.
  Uses X's PUBLIC Web Intent endpoint (`x.com/intent/post`) -- NOT the paid API. No developer
  account, no OAuth, no tokens, no per-post charge. If anyone later proposes "upgrading" this to
  the API, note that as of 2026 post creation costs $0.015, or **$0.20 if the post contains a
  link** -- a 13x surcharge this design avoids entirely.
  Imagery is free too: `url=` points at each game's PUBLIC page, which must carry BOTH `og:image`
  AND `twitter:card=summary_large_image` or X degrades to a small thumbnail. skullswap.php had
  og:image but no twitter:card and was fixed here. Targets: guardiansgame.php, cryptcrawlgame.php,
  **monstrocity.php DOES NOT INCLUDE db.php.** It is standalone -- its own `session_start()`, no
  header.php, no db.php -- so NO helper defined in db.php can be called from it. Calling
  `guestSignupPrompt()` there shipped a fatal on an undefined function that truncated the page
  mid-markup: Monstrocity AND boss battles rendered nothing, with an empty console, because no
  script ever reached the browser. Its guest prompt is inlined instead and must be kept in step
  with db.php's copy by hand. Check this include before using any shared helper in a game file;
  skullswap.php, cryptcrawl.php and cryptconquest.php all DO include db.php.
  cryptconquestgame.php, skullracergame.php, match3rpg.php (Monstrocity's public page -- NOT
  monstrocity.php), skullswap.php. **These must stay outside skulliance.php's login gate** -- X
  would follow a redirect to error.php and the card would silently collapse to a bare link.
  Two implementations by necessity: PHP (`shareOnXButton`) for the server-rendered finish modals
  in cryptcrawl-render.php and cryptconquest-render.php, and inline JS for the client-side
  ones (monstrocity.php, skullswap.php, racing/index.html, and guardians.php's `showDefeat()`)
  where the result isn't known server-side. Realm Guardians shares WAVES HELD, not the wave
  reached, because that is what its board ranks on -- the post and the leaderboard must not tell
  different stories about the same run. Its mid-run nuke modal deliberately has NO share button;
  only the defeat modal does. Both append @skulliance and budget 24 chars for the t.co-wrapped URL against
  the 280 limit. Verified by a harness (31 assertions) covering URL shape, every target page
  having the card tag AND no login gate, per-game text, the 280 budget, and button markup.
- Drop Ship / Oculus Lounge boards (db.php `checkDropShipLeaderboard()` + `dropShipDbConnection()`):
  these two live in Drop Ship's SEPARATE database on the same MySQL server. Oculus Lounge is a
  Drop Ship RESKIN, so both are rows in one `results` table split by project_id -- **1 = Drop
  Ship, 4 = Oculus Lounge** (DROPSHIP_PROJECT_* constants). One function, SIX boards: each game
  has $mode 'weekly' / 'ath' / 'xp', mirroring Drop Ship's own checkLeaderboard /
  checkATHLeaderboard / checkXPLeaderboard. 'weekly' is Drop Ship's "current game" -- renamed
  because its rounds pay out on the same cadence as everything else here; it looks up
  `SELECT id FROM games WHERE active=1 AND project_id=N` (Drop Ship reads that from its session,
  which doesn't exist on this side) and lists individual RUNS, not a per-player aggregate, so one
  player can hold several places -- matching Drop Ship's own board.
  They sit in their own **Specialty Games** group, which is what puts them on a row of their own;
  `$fixed_cols` in renderLeaderboardHub forces that section to 2 columns
  (`.lb-hub-grid--cols-2`, collapsing to 1 under 560px).
  Podium backdrop: many of these players predate Skulliance and have no realm theme, so
  `renderPodium()` takes a 4th `$fallback_image` arg and these boards pass the game's own art
  (`$DROPSHIP_BACKDROPS`, both URLs curl-verified 200 -- images deploy by FTP outside this repo).
  Theme still wins when the leader has one.
  `dropShipDbConnection()` opens a second mysqli with Drop Ship's credentials and the include is
  FUNCTION-SCOPED on purpose -- both credential files define $servername/$username/$password/
  $dbname, so a top-level include would clobber Skulliance's and break $conn. Drop Ship does the
  same thing in reverse in `dropshipConnectedStakeAddresses()`. Connection is statically cached
  and a failure is sticky, so a down database isn't retried per board.
  **Read-only, deliberately.** Drop Ship is quarantined: it reaches into Skulliance to award
  MOON/DREAD and nothing came back the other way until this. Keep it to SELECTs -- there's a test
  asserting no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/REPLACE/TRUNCATE appears in the function. If that direction
  is ever unwanted, the alternative is Drop Ship pushing its top scores into
  leaderboard_snapshots on its own cron.
  **Many Drop Ship players predate Skulliance and have no account here.** They are still ranked
  -- hiding the actual best players because they never signed up would make the board wrong --
  they just get no profile link. Matching is by discord_id; matched players display their
  SKULLIANCE username, unmatched their Drop Ship one.
- Leaderboard nav split: `filterLeaderboard()` in skulliance.php (called ONLY from
  leaderboards.php:~460) lists **projects only** now -- All Projects, Core and Partner.
  Delegations was dropped from it too since it has a hub card; `?filterby=15` still resolves via
  the numeric project branch, so the card's link is unaffected. The ~31 board options were removed; boards live on the hub. Do NOT add a
  board back to that selector: mixing 31 boards with 36+ projects is what made it useless for
  both jobs. Consequence to preserve: since boards are no longer listed there, the
  selector's first entry is `<option value="hub">` and the "&larr; All Leaderboards" link in
  leaderboards.php (printed under the `<h2>`, suppressed on the hub itself). Those two are the
  ONLY routes from a board back to the others -- removing both strands every board page. `?filterby=15` (Delegations) is reachable via the numeric project branch, not a named
  case -- it has no dispatch `case` and does not need one.
- Leaderboard hub (db.php `$SKULLIANCE_BOARDS` / `renderLeaderboardHub()` /
  `refreshLeaderboardSnapshots()`): card grid shown at leaderboards.php with no filter, or
  `?filterby=hub`. `$SKULLIANCE_BOARDS` is the single registry of subject -> periods; its period
  values are the EXISTING `?filterby=` strings, so old links and game finish-screen buttons are
  unaffected -- this is a navigation layer, not a re-route.
  **REQUIRES A MIGRATION** (page works without it, cards just show "No leader yet"):
  `CREATE TABLE leaderboard_snapshots (board VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL, rank_pos TINYINT NOT NULL,
  username VARCHAR(255) NULL, discord_id VARCHAR(32) NULL, avatar VARCHAR(255) NULL,
  score VARCHAR(64) NULL, updated_at DATETIME NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
  PRIMARY KEY (board, rank_pos));`
  Then a cron on `rewards.php?leaderboardsnapshot=1` (hourly is fine). That endpoint is
  READ-ONLY for players: refreshLeaderboardSnapshots() calls only DISPLAY variants, never
  $rewards=true, so it never pays out, resets a reward flag or posts to Discord.
  **FACTIONS IS THE ONLY BOARD THAT DOESN'T RANK PLAYERS** -- it ranks projects, and pushes
  `['faction'=>true,'project_id','project_name','currency']` with no username/discord_id/avatar
  (renderPodium() special-cases it the same way via `$is_faction`). Verified that every other
  board pushes the standard user shape. The snapshot normalises it into the existing columns
  rather than adding one: name column holds the project name, avatar column holds a ready-made
  `icons/<currency>.png` path, discord_id is left EMPTY -- and empty discord_id is exactly what
  the renderer uses to decide whether the avatar column is a Discord hash or a direct path. Any
  future non-player board needs the same treatment or its card silently reads "No leader yet".
  It captures each board's top 3 by buffering the board's own output and keeping the global
  `$leaderboard_top3` -- so a card can never disagree with the board it links to, since there is
  one implementation of each ranking. Its dispatch map is deliberately NOT shared with
  leaderboards.php's switch (that one also routes reward runs and project ids; coupling them
  would put payout paths one edit away from an unattended cron). NOTE `delegations` is not a
  board -- `?filterby=15` is a PROJECT id routed through checkLeaderboard(); there is no
  checkDelegationsLeaderboard(). Emoji icons, not images, because images deploy by FTP outside
  the repo and 16 missing icons would ship a broken-looking page.
- Skull Racer weekly LB: 50,000 CARBON x2 -- a Races board and a Laps board, each with its own
  50,000 pool, so one driver topping both takes 100,000 (db.php SKULL RACER block, end of file).

### Games constants
- Gauntlets (db.php:9877-9888): hand size 6, win at 3 wins (no loss = "sweep"), 100 points/win.
  Consumables: 100/75/50/25% Success = +4/+3/+2/+1% win chance; FF swaps card; Double Rewards 2x; Random Reward redirects points.
  Matchup (db.php:9916-9931): circular chain 6>1, 5>2, 4>3, 2>4, 1>5, 3>6. Strong 70%, weak 30%, neutral/same/Diamond 50%. Partner NFTs wildcard to random core.
- Skull Swap (ajax/save-swap-score.php): 25 matches/game, max score 25,000, min 60s anti-cheat.
- Monstrocity: 28 campaign levels, 35+ NFT themes; character traits health/strength/speed/tactics/size/powerup.
- CLAW is a real point type (Monstrocity/Boss reward), separate from CARBON/DIAMOND.
- Skull Racer (db.php SKULL RACER block, racing/index.html): 3 laps/race, 1,000 CARBON flat
  per finished race (SKULLRACER_CARBON_PER_RACE) on top of the weekly LB pool above. Leaderboard
  ranks by each user's own best (lowest) total_time, not wins -- opposite direction from every
  other game's leaderboard here. Server-side sanity floor (SKULLRACER_MIN_TOTAL_TIME=300s,
  SKULLRACER_MIN_LAP_TIME=100s) rejects an obviously fabricated submission without re-simulating
  the run; derived from racing/index.html's own trackLength/maxSpeed constants and must be
  updated by hand if those ever change (same manual-sync caveat as common.js's SPRITES object,
  see racing/common.js's own comment on that one). "skullracer" Discord channel not yet
  configured in credentials/webhooks_credentials.php -- see webhooks.php's function_exists guard.
  TWO leaderboards off one function: `checkSkullRacerLeaderboard($conn, $weekly, $rewards, $mode)`
  where $mode is 'race' (ORDER BY best_time) or 'lap' (ORDER BY best_lap). Filters
  `skullracer` / `weekly-skullracer` / `skullracer-laps` / `weekly-skullracer-laps` in
  leaderboards.php -- note each new filter must ALSO be added to the two `$filterby != ...` guard
  lists (~line 218 and ~362) or it falls through to the "treat it as a project id" branch and the
  page breaks. Selector options live in skulliance.php (~line 1020), not leaderboards.php.
  **Payout ordering is load-bearing**: rewards.php?skullracer=1 calls the function twice ('race'
  then 'lap') against the same reward=0 rows, THEN calls resetSkullRacerRuns() once. The reset
  used to live inside the function; if it goes back there the first board's reset flips every row
  to reward=1 and the second board pays nobody, presenting as "no lap times this week". One cron
  covers both -- do NOT add a second crontab entry. Verified with a harness that extracts the
  real function from db.php, stubs updateBalance/discordmsg/etc, and asserts both orderings,
  6 payouts across 2 boards, 2 distinct Discord posts and exactly one reset.
  Counts toward platform Activity leaderboards (db.php `checkActivityLeaderboard()`, source
  `'racer'`, weight 5, alongside crawl/conquest/mission). No status filter, unlike those two:
  every row in `skull_racer_runs` is already a finished race that passed the SKULLRACER_MIN_*
  floors, since skullRacerFinalizeRun() is the only thing that inserts and it only runs on
  completion. Unlike crawl/conquest this source needs NO migration -- `skull_racer_runs` has had
  `created_at` since its original CREATE and the INSERT writes it explicitly, so monthly/weekly
  filtering works immediately. Note the column is `created_at`, NOT `date_created` like most
  tables here, hence its own `$w_sr` filter var rather than reusing one of the shared ones.
  Time display: skullRacerFormatTime() in db.php is a hand port of formatTime() in
  racing/index.html (~line 2366) -- dot-separated M.SS.T, tenths TRUNCATED not rounded, minutes
  part omitted under 60s. Same manual-sync caveat as common.js's SPRITES object: change one and
  you must change the other. Verified byte-identical against the JS on 9 values including its
  float quirks (104.1 -> "1.44.0", 59.9 -> "59.8" -- the truncation can read a tenth low; the
  game has always done this and both sides now do it identically). Before this, the leaderboard
  and the race-finished Discord post printed raw seconds ("104.1s") while the game printed
  "1.44.0" for the same lap, which got reported as a wrong-data bug when the data was fine.
  Best Lap on the leaderboard is MIN(sr.fastest_lap) GROUP BY user -- already the all-time best
  lap across every race, NOT the best lap of the best race.
  Collision punt (COLLISION PUNT block + puntCar(), racing/index.html): hitting a car throws it
  forward and sideways, scaled by CLOSING speed (impactSpeed - car.speed), not the player's raw
  speed -- PUNT_MIN_DV_FRAC=0.10 of maxSpeed is the threshold below which nothing happens.
  Player recoil is deliberately unchanged: the recoil formula reads the car's speed, so the
  collision handler snapshots carSpeedAtImpact BEFORE calling puntCar() and recoils off the
  snapshot -- swap that order and punting quietly makes your own crashes cheaper. Cars carry
  baseSpeed/puntTimer/puntDx (set in resetCars()); updateCarOffset() returns 0 outright while
  puntTimer > 0, which is what suspends its "steer back on if off road" recovery long enough for
  a car to actually leave the track. Existing leaderboard times stay comparable: the change
  raises the floor (fewer repeat collisions) without lifting the ceiling, since a record run
  avoids traffic rather than punting through it.
  Client-side only (racing/index.html): crest jump is a screen-space sprite offset that never
  touches steering/speed, but DOES suppress the car-collision loop while `jumping` is true (added
  so a well-timed jump can save a boost run from a car in your lane) -- off-road sprite collision
  is untouched by it, only traffic cars. Boost pads sit on the 4 longest genuine
  straightaways (real curve===0 runs, excluding the start/finish straight), random lane each
  race, BOOST_SPEED=18000 (displays as exactly "180" via the existing speed HUD formula) held
  until that straightaway ends. BOOST_SPEED deliberately exceeds maxSpeed's own "at most one
  segment per frame" collision-detection invariant -- accepted as a rare/minor trade-off rather
  than restructuring the movement loop, see that block's own comment in racing/index.html.
  Jump ramps (resetJumpRamps(), runs after resetBoostPads() since it reads pad.padStart/
  straightEndIndex): one per boost pad, same lane, placed ~40% of the way down the remaining
  straight. Triggers the identical jump state machine as a hill crest (playerSegment.jumpRamp
  check in update()), gated on speedPercent >= JUMP_MIN_SPEED_FRAC but deliberately NOT on
  jumpCooldownTimer the way the natural crest branch is -- that cooldown used to be shared across
  both, and one of the 4 ramps sits right after addBumps2()'s run of 8 natural crests on the same
  boosted straight, so landing off one of those bumps could leave the cooldown still counting down
  by the time you reached the ramp and block its supposedly-guaranteed trigger outright. Distance
  to the ramp from the last bump is fixed, not time -- covering it FASTER (boosted) left LESS real
  time for that cooldown to expire, not more, so it reproduced as "never triggers boosted, works
  unboosted" rather than intermittently. Safe to bypass: JUMP_MIN_SPEED_FRAC alone requires enough
  speed that JUMP_DURATION's airtime always covers well past RAMP_LENGTH*segmentLength, so there's
  no realistic way to still be on the ramp's own segments by the time you land and could re-fire.
  Drafting (DRAFT_* constants/draftTimer/draftActive in racing/index.html): sustained lane-overlap
  with a car across a DRAFT_WINDOW_SEGMENTS-segment lookahead for DRAFT_BUILD_TIME seconds adds
  DRAFT_SPEED_BONUS to the effective top-speed cap (ignored while boostActive, which already
  exceeds it); breaks instantly on losing the overlap or on any crash.
  Brake lights (Render.player()'s `braking` param in racing/common.js, passed `keySlower` from
  racing/index.html's own render() call): swaps to a _BRAKE-suffixed sprite for whichever angle/hill
  variant would otherwise be drawn (PLAYER_LEFT -> PLAYER_LEFT_BRAKE, etc.) -- looked up by name
  string (`if (braking) name += '_BRAKE'`) rather than a parallel if/else chain, so it's one flag
  applied after the existing steer/updown branch picks the base name, not a second copy of it. The
  6 new _BRAKE sprites (images/sprites/player_*_brake.png) are pixel-identical to their normal
  counterparts except the two tail-light housings recolored brighter (found via connected-component
  detection on the exact 3 tail-light red/orange tones, NOT a blanket color swap -- the skull grille's
  eye-glow highlight and a roof-beacon dot happen to reuse one of those same 3 tones, and a blanket
  swap brightened those too before this was caught). Solid on/off with the brake key, not a timed
  flash/strobe. This is the first sprite ADDED to images/sprites/ since the original art replacement
  (see SPRITE-REPLACEMENT-BRIEF.md) rather than one of the existing 34 being edited -- ran an actual
  `rake resprite` (SpriteFactory's :packed layout reflows EVERY sprite's x/y when the file set
  changes, not just appends new ones at the end) and did the full by-hand re-sync of common.js's
  SPRITES object this project's own resprite-sync convention requires (see that var's own comment)
  -- every coordinate in it changed, not just the 6 new entries.
  Gamepad (gamepadIndex/pollGamepad() in racing/index.html): standard Gamepad API, polled once a
  tick from update() (no press/release events for held buttons/axes, only connect/disconnect) and
  written straight into the same keyLeft/keyRight/keyFaster/keySlower flags keyboard/touch already
  use -- a third input source, not a separate code path downstream. D-pad (buttons[14]/[15]) or left
  stick (axes[0], STICK_DEADZONE=0.25) steers; (Square) buttons[2] or R2 buttons[7] is gas; (Cross)
  buttons[0] or L2 buttons[6] is brake -- Square/Cross over the triggers specifically so a thumb on
  the face buttons can rock straight between them for quick on/off braking, triggers left live too
  as alternates. Standard mapping button indices (W3C spec), same as any other browser gamepad
  support -- PS4/PS5 controllers register under that mapping on current iOS/Android/desktop browsers,
  not anything sniffed or negotiated here.
  Detection does NOT rely solely on gamepadconnected/gamepaddisconnected -- reported "finicky",
  root-caused to that event being a genuinely unreliable way to learn a controller exists: a
  controller already paired at the OS level before this exact page load, or various browser/OS
  combinations, are real ways for the qualifying first press to fire without the event ever reaching
  this page, leaving gamepadIndex null and every subsequent press going nowhere. gamepadTakeOver(i)/
  gamepadRelease() are the actual state-change functions now (both idempotent -- safe to call
  redundantly); the connect/disconnect LISTENERS still call them as a small optimization (instant
  takeover the moment the browser confirms one, vs. up to one tick/~16ms later), but pollGamepad()
  itself is what's actually authoritative: whenever gamepadIndex is null, it scans every
  navigator.getGamepads() slot for ANY pad with gamepadHasInput() (any button pressed or axis past
  STICK_DEADZONE) EVERY tick, and takes over the instant one matches -- "a player pressing a
  controller button interrupts whatever mode the game is in" literally, not contingent on the event
  having fired first. Releasing is deliberately NOT symmetrical with that -- a quiet poll is never
  treated as a disconnect, only the explicit gamepaddisconnected event is. See the "DO NOT infer a
  gamepad disconnect from a quiet poll" entry below for what happened when it briefly was.
  Genuinely CANNOT be fixed from here: pressing a DualSense/DualShock's PS/Home button while
  connected to an iPhone opens Game Center system-wide, for every app and every website alike --
  Apple intercepts that specific button at the OS level, unconditionally, before any page's JS ever
  sees it. Reported as part of the same "finicky" bug report (losing the game, having to back out of
  Game Center, controls dead on return) but it isn't the same bug -- it's what the OS does with that
  one button on ANY MFi-class controller, full stop, and no web-facing API exists to change or
  suppress it. The robustness work above is what actually helps the RECOVERY side of that sequence
  (pressing any OTHER button once back on the page re-takes-over immediately, same as a fresh
  connect) -- the Game Center detour itself isn't this codebase's to solve.
  NO <audio>/<video> ELEMENTS ON THIS PAGE, EVER -- this is the single most important thing to know
  about audio here, and it is load-bearing, not tidiness. On iOS a PLAYING HTMLMediaElement creates a
  system "Now Playing" session, and while one exists the OS routes a connected game controller's
  buttons to the MEDIA REMOTE (play/pause/skip) rather than delivering them to the page's Gamepad
  API at all. In the installed PWA that presented as the controller working for exactly one press
  and then going dead -- and it was identified only when a player noticed that opening the media
  player that had popped up and PAUSING the music handed control straight back. Several consecutive
  rounds of gamepad-polling "fixes" (stored slot index, release-on-quiet-poll, stateless re-scan)
  were chasing that symptom; none of them could ever have worked, because the input was never
  reaching the page. Toggling mute from the controller broke it the same way for the same reason
  (flipping .muted on the <audio> element re-asserted the session), which is why THAT looked like a
  separate bug too. Everything is Web Audio now -- engine, pass-by, collision, lap, and finally music
  (Game.playMusic() in racing/common.js) -- and an AudioContext creates no Now Playing session, so
  there is nothing for iOS to hand the controller to. Music is fetch()'d + decodeAudioData'd one
  track at a time rather than base64-embedded like the SFX (~3MB each, and a decoded AudioBuffer is
  raw PCM), trading a short silent gap at each track change for the controller working at all;
  mute is a GainNode value, not .muted, so a muted track keeps playing silently and unmuting resumes
  in place. v1-v3/dev.html still have an inert <audio id='music'> element in their markup -- nothing
  reads it any more, but don't take that as license to add one back here.
  KNOWN, ACCEPTED SIDE EFFECT of all of the above: on iPhone the game is now silent when the ringer
  switch is set to silent. That is a real behaviour change, not a bug to hunt -- <audio>/<video>
  playback on iOS ignores the mute switch (which is why video sites play on silent), while Web Audio
  respects it, so moving everything to Web Audio inherited that. It cost a live debugging session
  ("nothing starts audio now... the phone was on silent") before it was spotted, hence this note.
  There IS a WebKit API that would override it -- `navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'` (Safari
  16.4+) -- but 'playback' is precisely the category meant for media that belongs in Now Playing,
  i.e. the exact thing whose session was hijacking the controller in the first place. Assume trying
  it re-breaks controller input until someone has actually tested it on a device; the ringer switch
  is a far cheaper thing to live with than that bug coming back. The other known workaround (playing
  a silent looping <audio> element to promote the audio session category) is a non-starter here for
  the same reason -- it's a playing HTMLMediaElement, which is the thing that must not exist.
  Audio unlocking must KEEP RETRYING, not fire once (Game.startMusic() in racing/common.js). An
  AudioContext starts 'suspended' and iOS only honours resume() from inside a genuine user-gesture
  call stack -- a gamepad button press very likely isn't one there (same platform family as iOS
  reserving the controller's PS button system-wide). A controller-only player therefore fires the
  first-interaction hook, has resume() quietly refused, and sits in silence forever if nothing
  retries -- reported exactly that way the moment music moved off <audio>. So startMusic() is
  idempotent (starting the playlist at most once) but re-attempts resume() every time it's called,
  and is wired to click/touchstart/keydown listeners that stay attached, plus gamepadTakeOver() in
  racing/index.html. Whichever gesture the platform does accept wins; the rest are no-ops. A source
  started while still suspended simply begins the moment the context resumes, so nothing is lost by
  starting the track before the unlock lands.
  DO NOT store a gamepad slot index (pollGamepad() in racing/index.html). Two separate revisions
  tracked "which slot is the active controller" (gamepadIndex) and both produced the same PWA bug:
  the stored index goes stale -- iOS shuffles or empties slots, re-reports the same pad at a
  different position, or hands back a Gamepad object that stops updating -- and every later poll then
  reads a dead slot forever while the controller is still physically connected and sending input.
  Reported as "it recognizes the first button press, then locks me out", recoverable only by bouncing
  out to Game Center and back (i.e. by forcing iOS to re-hand the page a fresh pad). There is no
  index now: every tick re-scans navigator.getGamepads() from scratch, taking any pad with live input
  first (which also makes "a controller press takes precedence" literally true, and picks the RIGHT
  pad when several are paired) and otherwise the first pad merely present (so releasing every button
  coasts, rather than silently handing the car back to auto-gas mid-straight). `gamepadActive` exists
  only to run the takeover/release side effects once -- it is never used to FIND the pad.
  DO NOT infer a gamepad disconnect from a quiet poll (pollGamepad()'s `if (!gp) return;` in
  racing/index.html). navigator.getGamepads() transiently returns null/empty entries for a frame or
  two entirely on its own -- the ORIGINAL version of that line already documented this ("browsers
  keep the slot but null the entry between the connect event and first poll, sometimes") and simply
  skipped the tick. A later "robustness" pass changed it to call gamepadRelease() there, reasoning
  that a vanished slot meant a vanished controller. It did not: every transient blip became a full
  handover back to auto-gas mid-race (keyFaster = true, hint text flipped, gamepadIndex cleared),
  and re-acquiring then required a FRESH button press with live input, since the takeover scan only
  fires on gamepadHasInput(). Reported as constant, unpredictable dropouts -- "the controller
  doesn't disconnect from the phone, it just disconnects from the game" -- and correctly identified
  by the user as a regression against code that had worked reliably. ONLY the explicit
  gamepaddisconnected EVENT releases now; that's an actual statement from the browser rather than
  something inferred from one quiet frame. The takeover scan (which only ever ADDS detection, never
  removes control) was kept.
  Options/Start -> mute (buttons[9], edge-detected against optionsWasPressed in pollGamepad()). Was
  pulled once and is back: toggling it used to kill controller input instantly ("I can toggle the
  audio with the controller but the second I do, I lose control"), which read as this binding's
  fault, but wasn't -- mute flipped .muted on the old <audio> music element, re-asserting iOS's Now
  Playing session and handing the controller to the media remote (see the no-<audio> entry above).
  With music on Web Audio there is no media element to poke. It calls Game.toggleMute() directly
  rather than dispatching a synthetic .click() on #mute (which is how it reused that element's two
  listeners before) -- same end state, no DOM events fired from inside the input poll loop. That
  function returns the new muted state, which the caller assigns to engineMuted so the Web Audio
  SFX stay in step with the music gain; tapping the icon still goes through the click listener,
  which updates engineMuted its own way.
  Gamepad audio unlock (Game.onFirstInteraction() in racing/common.js, shared by every page in
  racing/ that calls it -- index.html/v4.final.html, dev.html, v1-v3 -- not just the live game):
  that function's own browser-autoplay-unlock listener only covered
